- **Failure or attack sequence:** P1 is scoped assuming a small extension; in reality the ingress→origin resolver, its HTTPS validation, its reachability/before-Apply gate, and its freshness semantics are all new. Without it, `public` exposure mode either cannot ship or ships as another hand-pasted URL — recreating the exact copied-URL failure mode the proposal exists to remove. For scenario 31 specifically: the plan digest binds 'resolved endpoint identities and origins' and Apply requires 'non-expired... endpoint preconditions', but 'non-expired' is never defined (no TTL, no mandated re-resolution at Apply), and with no resolver there is nothing to re-check an origin against.
- **Impact:** The affected exposure mode is the only path to real remote webhooks; underscoping it jeopardizes P2's public-mode acceptance and leaves the approval-invalidation-on-ingress-change invariant unenforceable.
- **Required change:** Reclassify as NEW host work in P1: define EnvironmentIngress→origin resolution for a (service, endpoint) pair, HTTPS enforcement, and an explicit Apply precondition: re-resolve the origin at Apply admission and fail with provider.plan.stale if it differs from the plan-bound origin; define observation/endpoint freshness (TTL or re-resolve-always).
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance: approve a plan bound to origin A, change the environment ingress hosts to B, run Apply — it must deny with provider.plan.stale, zero broker requests sent, and a receipt recording the stale-precondition denial; re-plan shows an UPDATE of the webhook URL to B.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 11, 12

#### ADV-011 — secret lifecycle / trust boundary (contradiction)

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P0 (threat model approval), P1 (input classification schema, manifest schema, permission matrix)
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** The proposal claims raw credential and one-time secret bytes never reach the provider process: 'Runtime/build credentials that must become configuration outputs are moved host-to-host: resolver to approved secret sink. They do not transit the provider process' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:486-488) and, for one-time captures, 'It never sees the captured bytes' (528-529). Brief invariant 14 restates this.
- **Evidence:** core/docs/provider-plugins.md:866-868 contradicts it: 'The host resolves secret references. The provider receives opaque credential handles for brokered requests, not raw credential values. The host may expose a secret value to the provider only when it must transform or project that value and the operation manifest explicitly allows it.' This creates a manifest-gated path where raw secrets DO transit the provider process, with no named action, risk level, classification constraint, or production denial anywhere in the canonical action table (395-403), the credential-handle section (846-861), or the security controls list (1392-1422).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** An implementer (or a provider author writing its manifest) uses the 866-868 clause to declare a 'transform' need for STRIPE_RUNTIME_API_KEY; the host resolves op://dogfood/stripe-runtime/credential and hands raw bytes to the third-party provider binary. A compromised provider now holds a raw runtime credential in memory, can encode it into any allowed brokered request body to api.stripe.com (an approved origin), and exfiltrates it — exactly the attack the credential-handle/broker architecture exists to prevent. The permission test matrix (581-601) has no case covering this path because no action name exists for it.
- **Impact:** The central trust boundary of the design is ambiguous in the normative text. Two engineers implementing P1 from this document can build incompatible and unequally safe systems; the safe reading and the unsafe reading are both textually supported.
- **Required change:** Either delete the sentence at 866-868, or replace it with a precise carve-out: name the only classes eligible for direct provider exposure (e.g., PUBLIC and SENSITIVE non-credential inputs; never values with purpose MANAGEMENT/RUNTIME/BUILD/WEBHOOK_VERIFICATION), give the path an explicit canonical action with critical risk, require it to appear in the plan and receipt, and state that production admission denies it. Update the negative test matrix with 'manifest declares transform-exposure + production = deny'.
- **Acceptance test:** A conformance provider whose manifest declares a transform-exposure for a RUNTIME-purpose credential is rejected at manifest validation; a Tier-0 test proves no RPC in the frozen v0 proto can carry a raw secret value into the provider process for any input classified SECRET/CREDENTIAL_HANDLE.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 24

#### ADV-012 — protocol lifecycle / output timing (contradiction)

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (proto and coordinator), P2 (Stripe), P3 (Sentry — blocking for projection-only conformance)
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** The proposal specifies when configuration projection happens — but in three mutually inconsistent ways.
- **Evidence:** (a) core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1122 makes PROJECT_OUTPUT an ordered plan action, implying execution during Apply of the approved plan; Apply preconditions include 'a prepared output and secret transaction' (~1173). (b) The per-operation authorization section defines a separate fourth moment, 'Commit: authorize and atomically commit each public configuration or secret-sink projection... The provider agent cannot perform this commit' — host-performed, distinct from Apply dispatch. (c) The setup flow lists '7. apply only after confirmation; 8. write projection' (1494-1495) as sequential steps, matching the brief's summary lifecycle 'Validate -> Observe -> Plan -> Approve -> Apply -> Project -> Doctor' (brief:273-274), which shows Project as a stage after Apply. The RPC table (1044-1054) has no projection RPC at all, and the management-modes section (806-816) says observe-mode bindings 'project configuration' without saying which mechanism runs when no mutating Apply exists.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Sentry v0.1 is observe/project-only (1697-1701) and the cross-provider matrix requires 'projection-only/no mutation: yes' for all three providers (1917). If projection is a PROJECT_OUTPUT plan action, Sentry needs plan approval and an Apply invocation containing only PROJECT_OUTPUT actions — but nothing says observe mode produces plans or calls Apply. If projection is a host phase after Apply, the plan-digest binding of 'output target and current digest' and the PROJECT_OUTPUT action are redundant and the conformance step 'verify output projection and state' (Tier 2 step 8) has no defined trigger. P3 cannot be implemented against a frozen v0 protocol without privately resolving this, defeating the freeze.
- **Impact:** The single most-used code path (every provider projects; only some mutate) has no authoritative sequence. This is precisely the question the brief's Lane 4 asks ('whether outputs belong in Plan, Apply, Observe, or a separate projection call') — the proposal answers it three different ways instead of once.
- **Required change:** Pick one model and state it normatively. Recommended: every binding operation, including observe-mode, produces a plan; PROJECT_OUTPUT actions are host-executed (the provider only computes output values, returned from Apply — or from Plan for projection-only flows); the host's Commit moment executes PROJECT_OUTPUT actions after all provider checkpoints, under the plan digest's output-target CAS. Rewrite setup steps 7-8 and the brief-summary lifecycle to match, and state explicitly which RPC an observe-mode binding's projection flows through.
- **Acceptance test:** The Tier-2 conformance suite runs a projection-only provider (no mutating actions) end to end and the documentation's lifecycle diagram, setup steps, plan action list, and per-operation authorization moments all describe the observed sequence identically.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 24

#### ADV-013 — secret sink contract vs one-time capture durability (contradiction)

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (sink interface), P2 (Stripe signing secret), P5 (production sink)
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** The proposed secret-sink contract 'Prepare(binding, outputs) -> transaction; Put(name, secret bytes, metadata) -> opaque reference; Commit() -> references; Rollback()' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:999-1004) is a transaction whose durability point is Commit and which supports Rollback.
- **Evidence:** The one-time capture rules require the opposite semantics: the broker 'persists them directly into the already prepared and authorized secret sink target' (520-522) and 'checkpoints the persisted reference before allowing another mutation' (525) — i.e., durable at Put, mid-Apply, before any Commit; and 'The host must never silently roll back a successfully persisted one-time secret merely because a later public-configuration projection fails; preserving the only recoverable copy is more important than presenting a false cross-system transaction' (534-538) — i.e., Rollback must not apply to these values. The projection-writer invariant 12 'restore the prior output set if local validation fails' (984) adds a third pressure: in local v1 the sink IS the ignored *.secret.env file family (994-996) and the projection examples put captured secrets (STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET) in billing.secret.env (940-943), so 'restore the prior output set' can textually mean rolling back the very file the capture rules forbid rolling back.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Crash between broker Put and sink Commit after Stripe returned the webhook signing secret: if Put buffers until Commit, the only copy of the secret is lost while the remote webhook exists — the exact disaster the capture design exists to prevent (Stripe does not return the secret again; recovery requires endpoint replacement). Conversely, if Put is immediately durable, an Apply failure that triggers sink Rollback() has undefined behavior — either it deletes the only copy (same disaster) or it silently violates the transaction contract implementers coded against. Projection-writer restore after failed validation can likewise clobber the just-persisted secret file.
- **Impact:** The sink interface is defined in P1 and named a P0 open decision ('define the sink interface in P1'); freezing it with self-contradictory semantics forces a breaking interface change when the first UNCERTAIN recovery path is implemented, and risks real secret loss in the Stripe P2 slice.
- **Required change:** Specify two durability classes in the sink contract: (a) one-time captures — durable and fsynced at Put, exempt from Rollback, referenced by the UNCERTAIN checkpoint; (b) re-derivable projections (existing references, public values) — transactional, rolled back on failure. State explicitly that projection-writer restore (invariant 12) never touches sink-owned one-time secret files, and define what the checkpoint at 525 records (sink reference + fingerprint + operation/attempt ID).
- **Acceptance test:** A Tier-2 conformance test kills the host between broker capture and sink Commit, restarts, and proves the captured secret reference is durable and discoverable from the UNCERTAIN checkpoint; a second test proves Rollback() after a failed Apply leaves the one-time secret intact while removing staged re-derivable outputs.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 24

#### ADV-014 — Permission model / host wiring (reuse overstatement)

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P0, P1
- **Verification:** CONFIRMED — Keep HIGH — evidence is directly source-proven and the fix (rescope reuse table/P1 as new CLI work incl. principal acquisition and PDP-backend connectivity, plus a fail-closed acceptance test) must land before P1 is scoped/accepted; not BLOCKER because it requires no protocol or trust-boundary redesign and the exit gate at 2059-2060 already forces eventual discovery.
  - Verifier reasoning: Every cited evidence item re-verified: the CLI's only agent-load path uses WithoutSandbox()+WithoutPrincipal() (supervisor.go:142-143); no file in cli/ constructs policy.Principal, SaasPDP, CeilingPDP, GatewayEvaluator, or calls WithPermissionsCallback/WithProductionAdmission outside one test and Mind-daemon permit *verification* (prepared_mutation.go:477, which builds no PDP); the only production composition of the full stack is core/toolbox/session/session.go:294-296,336 and even it receives the principal as a caller-supplied option (line 244) — no principal-acquisition flow exists anywhere CLI-usable. Additionally, the only production PermissionsBackend implementation is server-side in module-saas-starter/host/host.go:444; the CLI has no client to it, and the proposal never mentions login/principal acquisition at all. The proposal's reuse framing (296-361 "existing authorization stack end to end") is true only of core primitives, while P1's CLI/host item verbs distinguish "implement" (broker, state, receipts) from "compose existing" for the entire authz path (2025-2027), making the under-scoping failure sequence reachable for an issue author. Caveat weakening but not refuting the finding: the P1 exit gate at 2059-2060 already requires every action be "attributable to a bound principal and admitted through the existing Codefly PDP", and 2039 adds production admission gates — so the finding's claim that only a trivially-met gate exists is overstated; however exit gates fire at phase acceptance, not scoping, and the requested fail-closed negative test (apply refuses to start without principal/PDP) is genuinely absent.
  - Verifier evidence: cli/pkg/engine/supervisor.go:142-143 (WithoutSandbox+WithoutPrincipal); cli/pkg/gateway/prepared_mutation.go:477,498-499 (verify-only, no PDP); core/toolbox/session/session.go:244,294-296,336-344 (only production composition; principal caller-supplied); core/agents/manager/loader.go:653-656 (explicit choice required); module-saas-starter/host/host.go:444 (only production PermissionsBackend, server-side); core/docs/provider-plugins.md:296-361,2025-2027,2039,2059-2060
- **Claim challenged:** "Reuse Codefly's existing permission stack end to end" and the reuse table row 'plugin sandbox and PDP … manifest ceiling, principal binding, operation admission' imply the CLI host already binds principals and consults a PDP; the coordinator merely 'composes' existing primitives (proposal:296-357, 2026-2027).
- **Evidence:** The only production wiring of principal+PDP+scoped-auth+callback is core/toolbox/session/session.go:294-336 (used by core/toolbox/conformance/host/harness.go and Mind) and cli/pkg/gateway/prepared_mutation.go (Mind daemon, VerifyEd25519). The codefly CLI's own agent supervisor loads every agent with manager.WithoutSandbox()+manager.WithoutPrincipal() (cli/pkg/engine/supervisor.go:142-143). No file under cli/ constructs a policy.Principal, a policy.SaasPDP, or a policy.GatewayEvaluator for provider/human-CLI use (rg over cli/ --type go). manager.Load itself requires an explicit WithPrincipal|WithoutPrincipal choice (loader.go:654-656).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Issue authors read 'reuse the stack end to end', scope the foundation issue as thin composition, and discover mid-implementation that the entire host-side authorization path (principal acquisition from login/CI, PDP construction, gateway evaluation, callback server, production admission) must be built for the CLI. The 'no provider-specific authorization path' exit gate (proposal:2027) is trivially met while the actual gate — every provider action attributable to a bound principal and admitted through a PDP — has no CLI substrate at all.
- **Impact:** Under-scoped foundation issue; false confidence that authorization is 'mostly done'. Until the CLI host wires a PDP, the default state is fail-OPEN (no enforcement), not fail-closed.
- **Required change:** Rewrite the reuse table and P1 to state that the CLI provider coordinator's principal acquisition, PDP composition (BuildPDP), gateway evaluation, permissions-callback server, and WithProductionAdmission wiring are NEW work built on existing primitives. Add a P1 acceptance test proving `codefly provider apply` runs with a bound, validated, non-expired principal and a live PDP, and refuses to start without them.
- **Acceptance test:** In a CLI integration test, launch a provider operation with no principal source configured and assert admission fails closed (no agent spawned, no HTTP); with a bound principal, assert PrincipalFrom(ctx) is non-nil at every provider RPC and every mutation records a DecisionEvent with a non-empty principal_id.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 5, Lane 2 (policy)

#### ADV-015 — Per-plan-action authorization / confused deputy

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P0, P1
- **Verification:** CONFIRMED — Keep HIGH/HIGH — the fix (manifest request descriptors + host-side action/resource derivation in the broker) is additive design specification that must land before the HTTP-broker/authorization phase (provider-plugins.md:2027-2030), but it does not invalidate earlier phases, so BLOCKER would overstate it.
  - Verifier reasoning: Every cited evidence item holds under independent inspection: (1) core/policy/callback.go:286-298 takes action/resource verbatim from the plugin body and overrides only the principal (262-274); callbackAuthorizer.Authorized (380-388) forwards plugin-chosen pairs; (2) core/policy/doc.go:216-221 claims only principal non-impersonation; (3) GatewayEvaluator/session (gateway.go:141-147, session.go:464,497-500) derive the resource host-side but mint one token per CallTool RPC — there is no existing per-outbound-HTTP-request derivation primitive; (4) Stripe docs confirm update = POST /v1/webhook_endpoints/{id}, so method alone cannot classify action risk. Reachability under the proposal as written: provider-plugins.md:440-442 mandates a re-check against the approved plan but never specifies that the broker derives the canonical action/resource from (method, path-template, origin) via manifest request descriptors; line 1431 lists a caller-supplied "operation action" field; the manifest schema (707-741) has no per-action method/path descriptors (only secret-captures uses that vocabulary, for capture not authorization); and the plan whose labels are re-checked is itself provider-produced, so a compromised provider can label a mutation as a low-risk action and pass both plan admission and the broker re-check. Intended-outcome statements (truth table line 589 "create grant cannot update or delete"; "operation-aware method policy" line 662; intersection line 330-334) assert the property without the mechanism, and the brief itself (lines 1003-1005) lists "whether the host broker must independently derive rather than trust those claims" as an unanswered challenge — confirming the proposal does not settle it. Minor caveat that does not refute: the callback fallback is proposal-restricted to read-only local dev (459-461), but the production scoped-token path still mints Action/Resource from provider-asserted plan labels, so the confused-deputy gap persists. The required change (host derivation from manifest-declared request descriptors; ignore provider-asserted action/resource for authorization, mirroring principal non-impersonation) is the exact missing specification.
  - Verifier evidence: core/policy/callback.go:286-298 and 380-388 (plugin-asserted action/resource forwarded verbatim; only principal overridden at 262-274); core/policy/doc.go:216-221 (principal-only security property); core/policy/gateway.go:141-147 and core/toolbox/session/session.go:464,497-500 (host-derived resource, one token per RPC); core/docs/provider-plugins.md:440-442, 447-457, 501-509, 589-593, 662, 707-741, 1430-1432; core/docs/provider-plugins-adversarial-review-brief.md:1000-1005 (open challenge: "whether the host broker must independently derive rather than trust those claims"); https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/update (POST /v1/webhook_endpoints/{id} confirmed)
- **Claim challenged:** 'Broker execution: before every outbound request, re-check that the action/resource is in the approved plan and authorize the exact origin, method, path template, credential purpose, and idempotency identity' and 'the host broker must independently derive rather than trust' provider action/resource claims (proposal:442, brief Lane 5).
- **Evidence:** The existing plugin→host authorization channel (callback.go) overrides ONLY the principal from the spawn-time binding; the action and resource are taken verbatim from the plugin's request body: handleAuthorize builds PDPRequest{Tool: req.Action, Args:{resource: req.Resource}} (callback.go:286-298) and callbackAuthorizer.Authorized sends whatever (action, resource) the plugin passes (callback.go:380-389). The doc.go security note only claims principal non-impersonation (doc.go:216-221), not action/resource integrity. GatewayEvaluator's host-derived resource path (gateway.go:142-147, session.go:497) exists for Toolbox CallTool but mints exactly one token per RPC, not per outbound HTTP request.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A compromised provider requests broker execution while calling the callback (or any self-authorization) with action='provider.observe' (low risk, no approval) even though the request it hands the broker performs POST /v1/webhook_endpoints/{id} (an update) or DELETE (verified: Stripe update=POST /v1/webhook_endpoints/:id, delete=DELETE /v1/webhook_endpoints/:id — https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints). If the host authorizes on the provider's claimed action rather than deriving it from the approved plan + the actual HTTP method/path, an observe grant becomes an unapproved mutation.
- **Impact:** Unapproved remote mutation/deletion under a low-risk grant; defeats invariant 8 ('every remote mutation authorized against an exact action and resource') and invariant 22.
- **Required change:** The broker must map each outbound (method, path-template, origin) to a canonical action+resource using the manifest's declared request descriptors and the approved plan — never using a provider-asserted action/resource — and authorize that derived pair. Provider-supplied action/resource fields must be ignored for authorization, mirroring the principal-non-impersonation property.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance test: a provider that declares provider.observe but emits a request descriptor whose method/path map to provider.update|delete is denied at the broker with a derived-action mismatch, and no request reaches the vendor.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 5, Lane 2 (policy)

#### ADV-016 — Lane 1/23 — origin model

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P0, P1 (protocol/manifest schema), P3 (Sentry self-hosted)
- **Verification:** CONFIRMED — Keep HIGH — the P2 Stripe slice works with fixed manifest origins so implementation can start, but origin admission must be defined in the v0 protocol before it freezes (v0 freezes before the affected Sentry phase P3, :1983).
  - Verifier reasoning: The internal contradiction is real and unresolved anywhere in the proposal. The security model commits to a packaged-manifest-derived exact origin allowlist with subset enforcement (broker allowlist :650; "Manifest ceiling ... maximum origins" :1393; "runtime advertisement must be a subset of the manifest" :1395; host validates advertised "exact allowed API origins" against the packaged manifest :1088/:1095; broker "exact origins" :1401), and the only manifest schema shown declares literal origins (api-origins: [https://api.stripe.com] :707-708) with no pattern or user-origin-permission mechanism. Simultaneously, v1 scope commits to user-supplied API bases: Stripe input STRIPE_API_BASE (:860, :1583), Sentry "API base or regional origin" (:1673) with self-hosted marked a "required design case" (:1926, :30) and a P3 deliverable (:2095), Resend "API base" (:1739). A packaged manifest of literal origins cannot enumerate a customer's self-hosted origin, so either the broker rejects it (advertised capability unusable) or the implementation admits input/binding-supplied origins ad hoc, voiding the "manifest-derived exact origin ceiling" claim. No other section closes the gap: :1970 is a one-line migration note, not a protocol definition; the plan-digest "origins" (:1155) and scoped-auth caveat "endpoint origin" (:457) refer to callback origins per the endpoint/ingress section (:1320-1373), not the API base; negative test :593 ("provider cannot choose a different origin") has an undefined baseline for self-hosted bases; and observed Sentry state even includes a provider-returned "regional origin" (:1687) as a plausible tainted origin source. The current sentry.sh confirms self-hosted --api-base is existing product behavior the provider must replace. Minor citation errata (finding's :1405-1406 should be :1393/:1401; script lives under module-saas-starter/ not module-saas-starter/) do not affect the substance.
  - Verifier evidence: core/docs/provider-plugins.md:650, :707-708, :1393-1395, :1088-1096, :1401, :860, :1583, :1673, :1739, :1926, :2095, :30, :1970, :1155, :457, :1320-1342, :593, :1687, :1983; module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/sentry.sh:37,69-71,150-151
- **Claim challenged:** The HTTP broker 'derives exact origin ceilings from the packaged manifest' and the manifest lists fixed api-origins (e.g. only https://api.stripe.com).
- **Evidence:** core/docs/provider-plugins.md:649 ('a provider-manifest-derived exact origin allowlist'), :707-708 (manifest api-origins), :1405-1406; contradicted by v1 provider inputs that accept a user-supplied API base: Stripe :1583 ('API base, normally https://api.stripe.com'), Sentry :1673 ('API base or regional origin') and P3 deliverable :2095 ('regional/self-hosted API base handling'), Resend :1739; current scripts likewise accept --api-base (module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/sentry.sh:69-73, 150-152).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A founder configures self-hosted Sentry with --api-base https://sentry.internal.corp. Either (a) the broker rejects every request because the origin is not in the packaged manifest — the advertised v1 Sentry capability is unusable — or (b) the implementation quietly accepts provider/binding-supplied origins, at which point a compromised provider can request any origin and the 'exact origin ceiling' security claim is void (credential exfiltration to attacker origin via a crafted request descriptor).
- **Impact:** Internal contradiction between the security model and a committed v1 feature; whichever way it is resolved ad hoc, either the product breaks or the trust boundary breaks.
- **Required change:** Define origin admission in the v0 protocol before freeze: the manifest declares default origins plus, per credential purpose, whether a user-supplied origin is permitted; user-supplied origins are host-validated inputs bound into the plan digest and scoped-authorization caveats and shown in the plan; the broker enforces the admitted origin set, never a provider-supplied one.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance test: a binding with a policy-admitted self-hosted origin succeeds through the broker; a provider request descriptor naming any origin not in the admitted set is denied and receipted; changing the admitted origin after approval invalidates the plan digest.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 1, 23

#### ADV-017 — Lane 1 — product scope vs isolation machinery

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P0, P1, P5
- **Verification:** CONFIRMED — Keep HIGH — the missing P0 decision must be resolved before P0/P1 acceptance per the proposal's own exit-gate criterion, but the finding's 'months of critical path' cost estimate should be discounted in the report because roughly half the listed apparatus (UDS, sandbox, PDP, receipts, Ed25519 v2) is existing-infra composition and digest locking is already staged to P5.
  - Verifier reasoning: All cited evidence re-derived and holds: every named provider and identity example is publisher codefly.dev; no third-party publisher, marketplace, or external-author requirement exists anywhere except the P5 author guide/scaffold; the P0 decision list (:1987-1998) contains no first-party-only vs third-party-publication decision despite P0's own exit gate requiring that no unresolved decision can change protocol shape; and P1 (:2005-2062) does bundle sandbox, authenticated UDS, broker, handles, capture, and conformance as one foundation package with isolation as an exit criterion. The proposal even calls providers 'third-party binaries' (:299, :658) without defining the third party, and has no publisher-trust/registry-review/artifact-revocation design — confirming the trust posture rests on an undeclared product decision, and that the third-party branch is under-specified exactly as the finding says. Reachability holds in its essential form. Discounts that do NOT refute: (a) authenticated UDS, sandbox policy (core/policy/permissions.go SandboxPolicy.Apply exists), scoped auth incl. Ed25519 v2 (core/policy/scoped_auth_v2.go), and PDP composition are explicit reuse, so the 'months of hardening' cost is overstated for those items; (b) the conformance agent serves protocol freezing regardless of trust posture; (c) digest locks and Ed25519 public verification are already deferred (P0 :1998, P5 :2155, :352) as the finding's required change requests. The genuinely new enforcement-depth items (broker generality, credential handles, declarative one-time capture, manifest/catalog ceiling) remain P1 prerequisites whose required depth is driven by the undeclared decision, so the core scope challenge and the required P0 decision stand.
  - Verifier evidence: core/docs/provider-plugins.md:674, 695-704 (codefly.dev publisher); :2177-2189 (all-first-party validation cohort); :2161 (P5 author guide); :299, :658 ('third-party binaries' asserted, never defined); :1987-2003 (P0 decisions lack publisher-scope decision); :2005-2062 (P1 bundles sandbox/UDS/broker/handles/capture/conformance, isolation exit gate); :352, :687-688, :1998, :2155 (digest lock / Ed25519 v2 already deferred); core/policy/permissions.go:48-174 and core/policy/scoped_auth_v2.go (sandbox and scoped-auth infra already exist, i.e. reuse not new build); provider-plugins-adversarial-review-brief.md:490 (invariant 2), :834-835 (severity definitions)
- **Claim challenged:** The full untrusted-third-party apparatus (new agent kind, sandbox, authenticated UDS, manifest/catalog ceiling, HTTP broker, credential handles, declarative one-time capture, conformance CI, distribution/digest locking) is required foundation work for the stated product goal.
- **Evidence:** Every provider named in the proposal is first-party: publisher codefly.dev in all identity examples (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:672-676, 695-704) and all 3 initial + 5 later-validation providers (:2177-2189) are Codefly-authored; no third-party publisher, marketplace, or external-author requirement appears anywhere except the P5 'provider author guide and scaffold command' (:2161). The isolation cost is justified solely by invariant 'provider binaries are treated as potentially buggy or compromised' (brief invariant 2). Missing evidence: any product statement that external parties will publish providers in the plannable horizon.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** P1 (explicitly one coordinated core+CLI package, :2005-2062) carries the protocol, broker, handles, capture, sandbox, and conformance simultaneously; if third-party publication is not a near-term requirement, months of the critical path are spent hardening a boundary between Codefly and code Codefly itself writes, reviews, signs, and ships — while the user-visible value (safe Stripe/Sentry/Resend setup) is deliverable by the same trusted host with far less machinery. Conversely, if third-party IS required, the proposal under-specifies registry trust, review, and revocation for external publishers.
- **Impact:** P1 size, sequencing, and risk are driven by an undeclared product decision; the roadmap cannot be honestly sized or the five issues cut until it is made. This is a scope challenge, not a request to weaken invariant 2 — defense-in-depth against first-party supply-chain compromise can be retained at lower enforcement depth (e.g. defer v2 Ed25519 public verification, artifact-digest locks, and full broker generality to P5) without changing user-facing invariants.
- **Required change:** Add to P0 an explicit decision: 'first-party-only providers through GA' versus 'third-party publication is a goal'. If first-party-only, re-tier P1: keep the protocol phases, state, projection writer, plan digest, and PDP composition; stage sandbox/broker/handle/capture generality as hardening increments with named exit criteria instead of foundation prerequisites. If third-party, add the missing publisher-trust design to P0.
- **Acceptance test:** P0 exit gate contains a written decision on third-party publication with the resulting P1 work-package list; each P1 security layer is traceable to either a user-facing invariant or the third-party decision.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 1, 23

#### ADV-018 — Lane 16 — retry ownership and ambiguous-outcome classification

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (broker + protocol), P2/P4 (Stripe/Resend acceptance)
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** The proposal claims retries are safe because failures are 'classified retryable and permanent' by the provider, the broker normalizes Retry-After, Apply uses 'a stable operation ID across transport retries', and uncertain outcomes are 'never blindly retried' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md, Apply and 'Partial and uncertain apply' sections; goal 7).
- **Evidence:** core/proto/codefly/base/v0/failure.proto:158-159 defines `retryable` as 'the same operation may succeed without user changes' and :169-170 `retry_after` — with no field distinguishing 'request never delivered' from 'delivered, outcome unknown' (TIMEOUT=16 and NETWORK_FAILED=26 both cover post-send loss). The proposal assigns rate-limit/error translation to the provider agent AND rate-limit normalization to the broker (responsibility split in 'Host/provider responsibility boundary' and 'HTTP broker requirements') but never names the single layer that executes retries. Scoped authorization for mutations is 'MaxUses: one for mutations and secret persistence' ('Per-operation authorization'), while Apply simultaneously promises transport-retry tolerance via a stable operation ID. toolbox-web has no retry layer today (rg 'retry|backoff' over toolbox-web: no matches), so the retry loop is NEW code with no specified owner.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Broker sends POST /webhooks (Resend webhook create, no idempotency key declared in the proposal for Resend); TCP connection drops after send; broker surfaces Failure{code: TIMEOUT, retryable: true, retry_after: 1s}; whichever layer honors `retryable` (provider loop, coordinator, or future generic broker retry) re-sends → duplicate remote webhook, violating brief invariant 24 and the no-diff-second-run invariant 31. Conversely: Stripe keyed POST gets a transient 429; the retry needs the same scoped authorization but MaxUses=1 is exhausted → mutation stuck, operation degraded to UNCERTAIN under ordinary packet loss, requiring manual recovery for a routine transient.
- **Impact:** Duplicate remote mutations (billing webhooks firing twice into the product) or false UNCERTAIN outcomes on every flaky network — both destroy the reliability contract the whole plan/apply design exists to provide, and the fix touches the broker request/response schema, the Failure usage contract, and scoped-auth semantics simultaneously.
- **Required change:** Specify in P1, normatively: (1) only the host coordinator retries; provider agents and any generic broker layer never auto-retry; (2) the broker stamps every failure with delivery status {NOT_SENT, SENT_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN, RESPONSE_RECEIVED} and `retryable=true` is forbidden for SENT_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN unless the request carried a declared vendor idempotency key still inside its retention window; (3) a bounded per-plan-action retry budget and backoff honoring normalized Retry-After; (4) a keyed transport retry of the same plan action either reuses the same scoped-authorization use or the host mints a per-attempt token bound to the same plan-action digest — pick one and state it; (5) broker owns transport-class mapping (429/5xx/timeout → FailureCode); providers may refine but never raise retryability.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance suite: broker fixture simulates timeout-after-send on a non-idempotent POST → operation terminates UNCERTAIN, receipts show zero automatic re-sends; second fixture: idempotency-keyed POST receives 429 then 200 → exactly one logical mutation, retry within budget, single approval, second plan is empty.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 16, 17, 18

#### ADV-019 — Lane 16/18 — read-only RPCs have no enforced HTTP-method ceiling

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (manifest schema + broker), affects all provider phases
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** Security control 9 asserts 'Validate, Observe, Plan, Doctor, and UpgradeState cannot mutate remote state' and invariant 21 'Observe and Plan do not mutate remote state' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md, 'Required controls').
- **Evidence:** The only per-request broker authorization the proposal specifies is step 3 of 'Per-operation authorization': 'before every outbound request, re-check that the action/resource is in the approved plan' — Observe and Doctor have no plan. The provider manifest declares method/path patterns globally (the Stripe manifest must include POST/DELETE on /v1/webhook_endpoints for Apply), and credential handles bind 'allowed method and path templates', but no rule maps operation kind (observe/doctor) to a non-mutating method set. Nothing in the 'HTTP broker requirements' list states that observe/doctor-scoped handles exclude mutating methods.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Compromised provider binary runs during `codefly provider doctor` (advertised low-risk, auto-allowed by policy). It submits a request descriptor POST /v1/webhook_endpoints to api.stripe.com — origin, method, and path are all inside the packaged manifest because Apply needs them; the doctor operation's management credential handle is valid. If the host minted that handle with the manifest's full method set, the broker injects the credential and the 'read-only' Doctor creates a remote resource with no plan, no approval, and no ownership record.
- **Impact:** Invariants 21/22 become advisory against exactly the threat the design claims to defeat (compromised provider binary); an unplanned, unowned remote mutation from a diagnostics command is a direct trust-boundary failure.
- **Required change:** Add a normative broker rule in P1: credential handles minted for validate/observe/plan/doctor/upgrade-state operations carry only method/path templates the manifest marks non-mutating (default: GET/HEAD); any read that requires an unsafe method (POST-based search/GraphQL) must be explicitly declared `read-only: true` per path template in the manifest and flagged in security review. The broker denies, receipts record the deny, and conformance covers it.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance provider issues a manifest-allowed POST during Observe and during Doctor → broker denies both with PERMISSION_DENIED, a deny receipt exists, remote fixture shows zero mutations.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 16, 17, 18

#### ADV-020 — One-time secret capture / HTTP broker (Resend signing-secret shape variance)

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (broker/capture contract), acceptance gates in P2 and P4
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** Proposal claims the broker's declarative JSON-Pointer capture keeps one-time secrets out of the provider process: capture triggers 'For a matching successful response' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:516) with declared method/path/content-type/pointer (provider-plugins.md:500-513), and the Resend section says the provider must 'treat a returned signing secret as SECRET; tolerate its absence' (provider-plugins.md:1774-1775).
- **Evidence:** provider-plugins.md:516 ('For a matching successful response, the broker: ...') defines behavior only for pointer matches; no clause defines behavior when a secret-shaped value appears at a different pointer. Resend shape variance is documented reality: official create example returns top-level signing_secret (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/webhooks/create-webhook), while the starter script already defends against two historical shapes — module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/resend.sh:206 extracts '.signing_secret // .data.signing_secret'. The verify docs prose (https://resend.com/docs/webhooks/verify-webhooks-requests) and API examples disagree about which endpoints return the secret, and Resend has no API version header ('Currently, there's no versioning system in place' — https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/introduction), so response shapes can change under the provider without any pinning defense. The Tier-1 cassette rule 'fail if a secret-shaped field remains' (provider-plugins.md:1855) protects only recorded cassettes, not the live response handed to the provider.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Manifest declares capture at POST /webhooks pointer /signing_secret. Resend ships an unversioned response change nesting the field at /data/signing_secret (a shape the vendor has used before, per resend.sh:206). Broker finds nothing at the declared pointer, concludes 'absent', and passes the full raw body — containing whsec_... — to the provider process. Provider incorporates it into canonical observation; it lands in provider state, plan diffs, diagnostics, and any live-recorded cassette. Invariant 18 (one-time secrets never enter provider state/logs/plans/cassettes) is violated with no error raised; the operation reports success and 'secret absent, supply a reference'.
- **Impact:** Silent exfiltration of the webhook signing secret into every surface the design promises to protect, on a code path that reports success; a capture miss is indistinguishable from genuine absence, so the documented absence-tolerance (provider-plugins.md:1774-1777) actively masks the leak.
- **Required change:** Specify capture-miss semantics in the P1 broker contract: (a) alongside pointer captures, the manifest declares secret token pattern classes for the provider (Resend: whsec_[A-Za-z0-9_=-]+, re_[A-Za-z0-9_]+); (b) the broker scans every response body — before logging, recording, or provider delivery — for declared pattern classes outside declared capture pointers and fails closed (response withheld, operation UNCERTAIN/BLOCKED, sanitized metadata checkpointed); (c) 'captured', 'genuinely absent', and 'present-but-undeclared-location' become distinct broker results (see capability/result model in the lane artifact).
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance fixture replays a recorded POST /webhooks response with signing_secret relocated to /data/signing_secret against a manifest declaring /signing_secret: the operation must terminate BLOCKED/UNCERTAIN, the provider process must never receive the whsec_ bytes (asserted by scanning provider-side observation/state/logs), and the receipt must record a capture-mismatch outcome distinct from 'absent'.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 15

#### ADV-021 — Observe-path secret exposure and missing secret-retrievability capability/result model (Resend)

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (capture/result protocol), P4 (Resend live qualification of actual retrieve/list behavior)
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** Proposal claims Observe is read-only and safe ('Read-only plan boundary: Validate, Observe, Plan, Doctor, and UpgradeState cannot mutate remote state', provider-plugins.md:1407-1408), frames one-time capture as a mutation-response mechanism ('Some APIs return a secret only in the successful mutation response', provider-plugins.md:495), and lists observed resend.webhook fields including 'signing-secret presence when the API supplies it' (provider-plugins.md:1762).
- **Evidence:** Current official docs show GET /webhooks/{webhook_id} returning the raw secret in the example response: '"signing_secret": "whsec_xxxxxxxxxx"' (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/webhooks/get-webhook), and the verification page states 'Calls to create, retrieve, or list webhooks will also return the signing secret in the response body' (https://resend.com/docs/webhooks/verify-webhooks-requests). The list example omits it (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/webhooks/list-webhooks), as do the update (PATCH) and delete examples. The capture example manifest covers only a POST (provider-plugins.md:506-513), and sink preparation is specified for 'an API call that may return a one-time secret' in the Apply/mutation context (provider-plugins.md:532-533). Nothing defines capture or redaction on GET paths during Observe/Doctor/Import.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Provider performs a routine Observe or Doctor of an existing binding: broker forwards GET /webhooks/{id}; the response contains the raw whsec_ secret; no capture declaration matches a GET, so the secret reaches the provider process and canonical observation, then provider state and any diagnostics — during a phase the operator was told is read-only and which required no secret-sink authorization. Conversely, the legitimate recovery flow the docs enable (re-reading a lost secret via retrieve) has no home: capture-to-sink during Observe would be a sink write inside a 'read-only' phase with no provider.project.secret authorization, contradicting invariant 33 for denied/observe-only operations.
- **Impact:** Either every Resend Observe leaks a webhook signing secret into provider-visible surfaces, or the proposal cannot express the one documented recovery path for a lost secret; both directions break stated invariants (18, 21/33). The Resend acceptance criterion 'signing-secret present/absent behavior is recorded against the real API' (provider-plugins.md:1810) is untestable without defining which result states exist.
- **Required change:** Adopt an explicit capability/result model (P1 protocol): manifest declares per resource type secret-retrievability capability = RETRIEVABLE | CREATE_ONLY | UNKNOWN_QUALIFY_LIVE (Resend v0.1 ships UNKNOWN_QUALIFY_LIVE); every secret-bearing response location is declared per-path including GETs, with per-phase disposition — Observe/Doctor: REDACT_REPORT_PRESENCE (broker strips the field, returns presence+fingerprint only, no sink write); explicit secret-recovery/import action (requires provider.project.secret approval): CAPTURE_TO_SINK. Broker per-operation result = CAPTURED | ABSENT | PRESENT_UNDECLARED (fail closed) | REDACTED_PRESENT | SINK_FAILED(UNCERTAIN). The Resend plan for a webhook whose secret is unknown emits an explicit recovery action or MANUAL_ACTION, never an implicit read-time capture.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance: (1) Observe of an existing resend.webhook replayed from a cassette whose retrieve response contains signing_secret yields observation with presence+fingerprint only and no whsec_ bytes anywhere provider-side; (2) a planned secret-recovery action with provider.project.secret approval captures the same response's secret directly to the sink and the projected email@1 secret reference resolves; (3) the same action without approval is denied with no sink write and no remote call.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 15

#### ADV-022 — Pagination, uniqueness, and lost-response recovery for Resend webhooks (scenario 37/12-analog)

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (broker pagination + completeness in plan digest), P4 (Resend qualification)
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** Proposal claims 'webhook create/update/observe converges' and duplicate-safe behavior for Resend (provider-plugins.md:1809, 1766-1767), and that the broker 'injects stable idempotency keys where declared' as the general lost-response defense (brief summary; provider-plugins.md broker section).
- **Evidence:** Official docs: GET /webhooks is cursor-paginated with limit/after/before and has_more, 'You can only use either after or before parameter, not both' (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/webhooks/list-webhooks); default and maximum page sizes are not documented. Idempotency keys are supported ONLY 'on the POST /emails and the POST /emails/batch endpoints' with 24-hour retention (https://resend.com/docs/dashboard/emails/idempotency-keys) — POST /webhooks has no idempotency support, so a lost create response is only recoverable by listing and matching. The proposal contains no pagination contract in the broker request-description or Observe sections and no observation-completeness state; duplicate-endpoint recovery is specified for Stripe but not Resend. Existing evidence of the failure mode: module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/resend.sh:169-181 reads a single unpaginated GET /webhooks page and matches '[.data[] | select(.endpoint == $endpoint)][0]', ignoring has_more.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Team account has more webhooks than one page (or a mid-pagination 429 from the team-shared 10 req/s budget truncates listing). Observe returns a partial set that omits the existing Codefly-owned webhook; Plan emits CREATE; Apply creates a duplicate endpoint receiving duplicate Svix deliveries. Separately: create response is lost after the remote create succeeded; with no idempotency key on POST /webhooks, a blind retry creates a second webhook, and the reconciliation path (list-and-match) inherits the same pagination hole — plus the new webhook's one-time create-response secret is gone, forcing the retrieve-based recovery of the previous finding.
- **Impact:** Violates invariant 31 (second identical run produces no diff) and invariant 24 (ambiguous outcomes not blindly retried); duplicate webhooks double-deliver events to the starter and are invisible without complete listing; the P4 exit gate ('webhook create/update/observe converges') cannot be met.
- **Required change:** Before protocol v0 freeze: (a) broker request descriptions support declared cursor pagination (param names, cursor field, has_more field, bounded max pages); (b) Observe results carry a completeness flag bound into the plan digest — Plan over incomplete observation is rejected; (c) Resend lost-create recovery is specified as observe-complete-list → match by endpoint+events → single match = checkpointed adopt-as-uncertain-own, multiple matches = MANUAL_ACTION (no URL-based auto-adoption, per the stated ownership rule); (d) live acceptance qualifies default/max page size, which is undocumented.
- **Acceptance test:** Cassette with 2+ pages of webhooks where the owned endpoint appears only on page 2: Observe must return it and the second plan must be empty; cassette truncating pagination with a 429 mid-listing: Plan must refuse with incomplete-observation, not emit CREATE; conformance kill of the create call after send: recovery must find the created webhook via complete listing and never issue a second POST /webhooks.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 15

#### ADV-023 — agent kind routing / installation / release discovery

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (foundation); blocks P2 distribution
- **Verification:** CONFIRMED — Keep HIGH/HIGH — must be fixed in the P1 foundation phase before any provider release is published (brief line 835); not BLOCKER because it needs a centralized-mapping fix, not a protocol or trust-boundary redesign.
  - Verifier reasoning: Every cited source line checks out verbatim, and the kind-to-directory mapping is demonstrably already inconsistent for an existing kind: toolboxes get three different subdirs (build.go installs to services/, FindLocalLatest scans modules/, Agent.Path resolves toolboxes/), which proves hand-edited switch drift is a real failure mode, not hypothetical. The failure sequence is reachable exactly as described: ResolveLatest scans the wrong local subdir, falls back to PinToLatestRelease which calls toGithubSource hardcoding service-<name> for every kind; manager.Load silently auto-downloads from GitHub when LookPath misses. The proposal as written does not prevent it — its extension list (lines 1035-1036: "agent path, local latest resolution, build, list, versions, publish, and CI") omits the GitHub release-source mapping, while its own P2-P4 phases name the release repos provider-stripe/provider-sentry/provider-resend. So under the proposal, provider release resolution deterministically queries codefly-dev/service-<name> — wrong by the proposal's own repo naming — and silently shares a service's release stream whenever a same-named service repo exists. The only softening caveat: absent a same-named service-<name> repo, the failure is loud (download error) rather than silent wrong-artifact; this narrows one branch of the impact but does not refute the finding, since the wrong-repo mapping and the local build/install misrouting (build to services/, Path to providers/, silent remote fetch) are deterministic. Required change (centralized kind->subdir and kind->release-source mapping plus exhaustive-switch test) is well targeted at the actual defect.
  - Verifier evidence: core/resources/agent.go:160-177 (4-kind Path switch); core/agents/manager/manager.go:78-85 (FindLocalLatest defaults to "modules"); cli/cmd/agents/build.go:514-519 (defaults "services", no toolbox branch); cli/cmd/agents/versions.go:496-506 (has toolboxes, defaults modules); core/agents/manager/downloader.go:27-32 (Repo: "service-%s" for all kinds) + downloader_url.go:15 and manager.go:188 (both consumers); core/agents/manager/loader.go:670-707 (silent GitHub auto-download on LookPath miss); core/docs/provider-plugins.md:243-245, 1032-1036 (routing list omits release-source mapping) vs :2068, :2094, :2124 (repos named provider-stripe/provider-sentry/provider-resend); toolbox-web/agent.codefly.yaml (kind: codefly:toolbox, proving live three-way drift for an existing kind)
- **Claim challenged:** Reuse table rows claim provider support needs only 'add PROVIDER / codefly:provider routing' and 'include provider kind in path and inventory switches' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:243-245, 1032-1038).
- **Evidence:** Kind-to-directory mapping is duplicated and already inconsistent across four sites: core/resources/agent.go:160-177 (Path: services/applications/modules/toolboxes, error on unknown); core/agents/manager/manager.go:80-86 (FindLocalLatest: services/applications, silent default to "modules" — toolboxes already misrouted); cli/cmd/agents/build.go:514-519 (default "services", only application/module branches — no toolboxes); cli/cmd/agents/versions.go:498-502 (has toolboxes). GitHub release resolution hardcodes the repo name as service-<name> for every kind: core/agents/manager/downloader.go:27-32 (toGithubSource: Repo: fmt.Sprintf("service-%s", p.Name)).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A provider binding pinned to version 'latest' calls ResolveLatest -> FindLocalLatest, which scans ~/.codefly/agents/modules/<publisher> (wrong directory), finds nothing, and falls back to PinToLatestRelease, which resolves 'latest' from the GitHub repo codefly-dev/service-stripe — the same repo a *service* agent named 'stripe' would use, since kind is absent from the repo mapping. A provider and a service with the same publisher/name silently share one release stream. Separately, `codefly agent build` on a provider directory installs the binary under agents/services/, resources.Agent.Path looks under agents/providers/, LookPath fails, and manager.Load silently auto-downloads a different artifact from GitHub instead of using the locally built one.
- **Impact:** Wrong-artifact resolution and silent local/remote artifact divergence for the exact plugin class that holds credential handles and performs remote mutations; supply-chain confusion between service-* and provider-* release repos; local dev iteration ('codefly agent build' then run) silently broken for providers.
- **Required change:** Make the P1 foundation issue include a single centralized kind->subdir and kind->release-source mapping (extend resources.Agent or a registry keyed by AgentKind) consumed by Path, FindLocalLatest, build, versions, and toGithubSource, with an exhaustive-switch test that fails when a kind is added without routing. Give provider agents a kind-distinct GitHub repo naming scheme (e.g. provider-<name>) before any release is published.
- **Acceptance test:** Unit test: for every registered AgentKind, Path/FindLocalLatest/build-install/versions/toGithubSource agree on one location and one release source; a provider agent built locally with `codefly agent build` is resolved by manager.Load without triggering any store/GitHub fallback; a provider and service sharing publisher/name resolve to different release repositories.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 2 (non-policy)

#### ADV-024 — artifact verification on auto-download

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P2 (Stripe distribution), P5 (GA)
- **Verification:** CONFIRMED — Keep HIGH/HIGH. Not a BLOCKER per the brief (line 834): the fix (release checksum manifest verification or disabling the GitHub fallback for kind codefly:provider) is additive and requires no protocol/trust-boundary redesign; but it must land before P2, the first phase that distributes a provider binary (provider-plugins.md:2064). Confidence HIGH is warranted: directly proven by source per brief line 841.
  - Verifier reasoning: Re-derived every claim independently. (1) Evidence holds: manager.Download (core/agents/manager/downloader.go:59-149) performs a plain http.Get on the GitHub release URL and extracts/installs the binary with zero checksum, digest, or signature verification (only zip-slip and size-cap defenses); the OCI path does verify sha256, but against the registry's own just-fetched manifest (store.go:184,189-243), confirming the 'transfer integrity, not pinned identity' caveat; loader.go:675-707 auto-invokes Download inside manager.Load whenever the binary is missing and neither AGENT_NIX_FLAKE nor AGENT_REGISTRY is set (the default developer configuration), then exec.Command's the result at loader.go:714. (2) Reachable under the proposal as written: provider-plugins.md:686 explicitly extends 'the same local, Nix, OCI, and GitHub resolution order' to codefly:provider agents; the artifact-digest lock is explicitly deferred (line 687-688 'a later agent lock file', line 1998 P0 'decide artifact digest lock timing', line 2155 P5 deliverable), and the Required Controls list (1391-1420) contains no install-time verification control for the GitHub fallback despite the threat model stating 'a provider binary may be buggy or compromised' (line 1381). No other section prevents the failure sequence. (3) The reuse-table claim at line 245 ('OCI digest verification') is accurate only for the OCI path and therefore overstates verified distribution for the default developer path, exactly as the finding says.
  - Verifier evidence: core/agents/manager/downloader.go:59-149 (unverified http.Get + extract + chmod); core/agents/manager/downloader_url.go:14-26 (GitHub URL construction); core/agents/manager/loader.go:675-714 (auto-fallback to Download and immediate exec); core/agents/manager/store.go:184,189-243 (OCI digest verified against registry manifest only); core/docs/provider-plugins.md:245 (reuse-table digest claim), :686-688 (same resolution order applies to providers; digest lock deferred), :1381 (compromised-binary threat model), :1396-1397 (pinned-agent control scoped to production/pre-GA), :1998 and :2155 (digest lock deferred to P5), :2064 (P2 = first distributed provider)
- **Claim challenged:** Reuse table row 'agent release discovery … pinned versions, local-first development, OCI digest verification' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:245) implies verified distribution; security model requires 'Pinned agent: production uses exact versions and, before general availability, artifact digests' (provider-plugins.md:1396-1398).
- **Evidence:** OCI pulls verify the blob sha256 against the registry manifest (core/agents/manager/store.go:189-242). The GitHub release fallback performs a plain http.Get and tar extraction with no checksum, digest, or signature verification of any kind (core/agents/manager/downloader.go:58-80; rg for checksum/sha256/verify in downloader.go returns nothing). This unverified path is auto-invoked inside manager.Load whenever the binary is missing locally and Nix/OCI stores are not configured (core/agents/manager/loader.go:675-707).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A developer runs `codefly provider setup billing`; the provider binary is absent locally; Load silently downloads and executes an unverified tarball over HTTPS from a GitHub release. Any compromise of the release asset (or the repo-name collision in the previous finding) yields arbitrary code execution in the process that is subsequently handed credential handles, the permissions-callback socket, and brokered vendor access.
- **Impact:** The 'provider binaries are treated as potentially compromised' threat model is undermined at install time: the digest-verification claim in the reuse table holds only for the OCI path, while the default fallback path for developers is unverified. OCI digest verification also only proves transfer integrity against the registry's own manifest, not a pinned identity.
- **Required change:** Before P2 (first provider distributed): either disable the GitHub auto-download fallback for kind codefly:provider (fail with an explicit install command) or add release checksum verification (published sha256 manifest verified before extraction). The P5 artifact-digest lock file remains the production gate, but local dogfood must not silently execute unverified downloads.
- **Acceptance test:** With a tampered GitHub release asset, manager.Load of a provider agent refuses to execute the binary; with no local binary and no verified source, `codefly provider setup` fails with an explicit remediation instead of silently downloading.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 2 (non-policy)

#### ADV-025 — sandbox enforcement per OS (macOS read model)

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (security envelope), affects P2-P4 dogfood claims
- **Verification:** CONFIRMED — Keep HIGH/HIGH — directly proven by source (confidence HIGH), and it fits "must be resolved before the affected phase (P2-P4 local dogfood) is accepted" rather than BLOCKER, since the fix is an additive sandbox deny-read extension or a text downgrade, not a protocol redesign. Two sub-claims should be dropped from the finding as unsupported (provider state holds sink references only per control 15; sibling $TMPDIR yields no tokens, which live in CODEFLY_AGENT_TOKEN env).
  - Verifier reasoning: I tried to refute this on four fronts and it survived all of them.

(1) Evidence check — every citation holds. core/runners/sandbox/sandboxexec.go:98-147 does begin `(version 1)` + `(allow default)` and then deny-lists only writes (`(deny file-write*)` + allowlist), the four home secret roots (`.ssh`, `.aws`, `.config/codefly/secrets`, `.gnupg`, lines 121-122), and network per policy. Reads are otherwise ambient: lines 164-170 explicitly emit read paths as inert profile comments ("readPaths are advisory on macOS under this model: file-read* is already broadly allowed"). The "best-effort hardening, not a security boundary on its own" comment is at lines 175-177 as quoted. NetworkLoopback emits `(allow network* (local ip "localhost:*"))` / `(allow network* (remote ip "localhost:*"))` (lines 142-144) — wildcard port, all of loopback, not the assigned UDS paths (UDS is a separate, correctly narrowed regex allowlist at 154-159). sandbox.New() selects sandboxexec on darwin (sandbox.go:95-101), and validateProductionAdmission rejects only BackendNative (loader.go:610-615), so BackendSandboxExec is admitted as "enforcing".

(2) Proposal citations hold too. provider-plugins.md:1399-1401 control 5 is "Sandbox: provider has no workspace writes and external network is denied"; :638-646 asserts "no direct workspace write access; no direct secret-store access"; :465-491 and :527-530 assert the provider "cannot exchange a management handle for raw credential material" and "never sees the captured bytes"; :247 is the "plugin sandbox and PDP → manifest ceiling" reuse row; the example manifest at :730-733 sets `read_paths: [] / write_paths: [] / network: loopback`.

(3) Reachability — nothing in the proposal prevents it, and no mitigation exists elsewhere. `rg -i 'macos|seatbelt|bwrap|read confinement|not a security boundary|limitation|does not enforce'` over the entire 95 KB proposal returns nothing: there is no OS-differentiated caveat anywhere. policy.SandboxPolicy (core/policy/permissions.go:48-53) has only ReadPaths/WritePaths/Network/UnixSockets — there is no deny-read field at all, so the manifest's empty `read_paths` cannot express read confinement on macOS even in principle; the finder's fix (a) genuinely requires new API surface. The target is concrete, not hypothetical: /Users/antoine/development/deus/codefly/module-saas-starter/configurations/local-dogfood/ contains real plaintext secret files (internal-auth.secret.env present) and the templates carry exactly the raw management credentials the handle model claims to withhold — billing.secret.env.example has STRIPE_API_KEY / STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET, identity.secret.env.example has IDENTITY_CLIENT_SECRET / IDENTITY_MANAGEMENT_API_KEY, and its own comment calls it a "local plaintext escape hatch". core/configurations/secrets.go supports these workspace-resident plaintext secret files as a first-class local mechanism. A provider spawned with `read_paths: []` on macOS reads all of them; 0600 mode bits (proposal :258) do not help because the provider runs as the same uid. The one-time-capture machinery is bypassed the same way whenever the approved sink is a workspace or ~/.codefly path (provider state root is documented at :1248 as ~/.codefly/providers/...).

(4) Best refutations I could mount, and why they fail to demote it: (a) `network: loopback` blocks direct external exfiltration — true, but the broken claim is confidentiality ("no direct secret-store access", "cannot exchange a handle for raw credential material"), which does not depend on an exfil channel, and exfil remains plausible by smuggling stolen bytes into a broker-permitted request body/metadata field on an allowlisted origin (the broker bounds size/method/origin, not content). (b) Control 5's own wording says "no workspace *writes*", so that one sentence is not literally violated — but :641-642 and the credential-handle section make the stronger confidentiality claim, so the doc as a whole does overclaim. Two sub-claims are weaker than the finder states and should be trimmed rather than credited: provider state is required by control 15 (:1417) to hold "secret sink references only", so reading it yields little; and per-spawn bearer tokens are passed via CODEFLY_AGENT_TOKEN env (loader.go:766-780), not files in $TMPDIR, and outbound UDS to a sibling's socket is denied unless path-allowlisted — so "read $TMPDIR of sibling spawns" is not a credential path. Neither trim touches the decisive workspace-secret read.
  - Verifier evidence: core/runners/sandbox/sandboxexec.go:100-102 ((version 1)/(allow default)), :112-124 (write allowlist + only 4 home read-denies), :142-144 (`(allow network* (remote ip "localhost:*"))`), :164-170 (macOS readPaths emitted as inert comments), :175-177 ("best-effort hardening, not a security boundary on its own"); core/runners/sandbox/sandbox.go:95-101 (darwin → sandboxexec); core/agents/manager/loader.go:610-615 (only BackendNative rejected), :766-780 (token via env); core/policy/permissions.go:48-53 (no deny-read field exists); core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1399-1401, :638-646, :465-491, :527-530, :730-733, :1248, :1417; core/docs/provider-plugins-adversarial-review-brief.md:1015-1031 ("Prove or refute the claim that a compromised provider cannot reach an unapproved origin or read workspace/secrets"), :834-835 (severity table); module-saas-starter/configurations/local-dogfood/billing.secret.env.example (STRIPE_API_KEY, STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET), identity.secret.env.example (IDENTITY_MANAGEMENT_API_KEY), internal-auth.secret.env (real, non-.example file present); core/configurations/secrets.go (workspace plaintext secret files supported)
- **Claim challenged:** The provider agent has 'no workspace writes and external network is denied' plus the credential-handle model's premise that a provider process cannot read raw secrets (provider-plugins.md:1399-1401, 637-646); reuse row 'plugin sandbox … manifest ceiling' (provider-plugins.md:247).
- **Evidence:** The macOS backend profile begins '(allow default)' and only deny-lists writes, network (per policy), and four secret roots (~/.ssh, ~/.aws, ~/.config/codefly/secrets, ~/.gnupg): core/runners/sandbox/sandboxexec.go:98-147. Its own comment states the read policy is 'best-effort hardening, not a security boundary on its own' (sandboxexec.go:176-177). Neither the workspace, ~/.codefly (agents, provider state, execution journal), nor $TMPDIR of sibling spawns is read-denied. NetworkLoopback re-allows all of localhost ('localhost:*', sandboxexec.go:142-144), not just the assigned UDS paths. Production admission rejects only BackendNative (core/agents/manager/loader.go:610-615), so BackendSandboxExec passes admission as 'enforcing'.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** On macOS local dogfood (the primary target of P2-P4), a compromised provider binary simply reads billing.secret.env / *.secret.env in the workspace, the provider state directory under ~/.codefly, or any other home file outside the four deny-listed roots — bypassing the entire credential-handle and one-time-capture machinery without touching the broker. With NetworkLoopback it can also connect to any localhost port (other agents' loopback gRPC, local Postgres), not only its own sockets.
- **Impact:** The design's central local-dogfood security claim ('raw management credentials are not exposed…', 'one-time secrets never enter the provider process') is not enforced by the reused primitive on macOS; the documentation promises more than Codefly can enforce, which the brief explicitly flags as a review target.
- **Required change:** In P1: (a) extend the provider spawn's sandbox policy with explicit read-deny subpaths for the workspace root, ~/.codefly, and the temp parents of sibling spawns on macOS (sandbox-exec supports deny file-read* subpath rules, already used for the four secret roots), or (b) downgrade the written macOS local-dogfood guarantees to 'write+network confinement only' and state that read confinement holds only under bwrap; production admission for providers should then require BackendBwrap. Do not ship the current text unchanged.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance test on macOS: a test provider that attempts to read <workspace>/configurations/*/billing.secret.env and ~/.codefly/providers/... receives EPERM/denial under the provider sandbox profile; a test that dials a localhost port other than its assigned sockets is denied or the documented claims are amended to exclude this case.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 2 (non-policy)

#### ADV-026 — Lane 6 - macOS sandbox does not confine filesystem reads

- **Severity:** HIGH _(reported as BLOCKER; adjudicated down by the verification pass)_  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P0 threat model; P1 foundation exit gate ('a provider binary cannot directly write workspace files or reach an unapproved external origin' must be extended to reads and cannot be signed off on macOS today)
- **Verification:** CONFIRMED — HIGH (not BLOCKER): per the brief's ladder (provider-plugins-adversarial-review-brief.md:834-835), the fix is additive sandbox-profile hardening plus doc/test gating before P1 exit — no protocol or trust-boundary redesign — matching HIGH's 'must be resolved before the affected phase is accepted'; the finding's own required change ('before P1 exits') concedes this.
  - Verifier reasoning: Independently re-derived every load-bearing citation. The macOS Seatbelt profile in core/runners/sandbox/sandboxexec.go is (allow default) with deny only on file-write* and four read paths (.ssh, .aws, .config/codefly/secrets, .gnupg); the .config/codefly/secrets entry is dead (Codefly home is ~/.codefly per core/resources/global.go, and the string appears nowhere else in core/ or cli/), and readPaths are explicitly advisory comments (lines 161-169). Therefore a provider agent spawned with the proposal's own manifest (read_paths [], write_paths [], network loopback — proposal lines 730-733) can read the entire workspace including configurations/<env>/*.secret.env (real layout confirmed in core/configurations/secrets_test.go:289; v1 local secret sink writes such files per proposal line ~994) and all of ~/.codefly including other bindings' provider state (proposal line 1248). This directly falsifies, on macOS, the architecture claim 'no direct secret-store access' (lines 641-642); a compromised provider binary is in-scope by the proposal's own threat model (line ~1382) and invariant 14 mandates reusing this sandbox. No other proposal section closes the gap — the document never mentions macOS/Seatbelt/darwin read semantics. Exfiltration via broker to the provider's manifest-allowlisted origin or via diagnostics is available. CI never exercises the sandbox e2e at all (no workflow passes -tags sandbox_e2e; the loopback test is additionally darwin-tagged; go.yml runs ubuntu-latest only). Two imprecisions do not change the verdict: 'invariants 12/13' is a mis-cite (relevant invariants are 11 and 14), and webhook secrets are forbidden in ~/.codefly provider state (proposal lines 1276-1277) — but they land in equally readable workspace *.secret.env files via the v1 file sink, so the stated impact stands.
  - Verifier evidence: core/runners/sandbox/sandboxexec.go:100-104 ((allow default), (deny file-write*) only), :120-124 (four-path read deny incl. dead .config/codefly/secrets), :161-169 (readPaths advisory on macOS); core/resources/global.go:57 (~/.codefly); core/runners/sandbox/doc.go:29-32 (not hostile-binary defense); core/docs/provider-plugins.md:641-642 (no direct secret-store access), :730-733 (manifest sandbox spec), :941 (billing.secret.env), :994 (v1 sink writes workspace *.secret.env), :1248 (~/.codefly/providers state root), :1396-1397 (control #5); core/configurations/secrets_test.go:289; core/toolbox/launch/sandbox_e2e_loopback_darwin_test.go:1 (//go:build sandbox_e2e && darwin); core/.github/workflows/go.yml:23 (ubuntu-latest; no workflow passes -tags sandbox_e2e)
- **Claim challenged:** Proposal invariants 12/13 and control #5 ('Sandbox: provider has no workspace writes and external network is denied', line 1396) plus the architecture claim that the provider has 'no direct workspace write access' and 'no direct secret-store access' (lines 638-646) are presented as enforced by Codefly's existing sandbox.
- **Evidence:** core/runners/sandbox/sandboxexec.go:99-102 emits '(version 1)' then '(allow default)' and only '(deny file-write*)'; the sole read restrictions are lines 119-124 (deny $HOME/.ssh, .aws, .config/codefly/secrets, .gnupg). Lines 162-170 state read paths 'are advisory on macOS under this model: file-read* is already broadly allowed'. Codefly home is ~/.codefly (core/resources/global.go:57), the proposal's provider state root (proposal line 1248); projected secret files live in the workspace (proposal line 941; real layout in core/configurations/secrets_test.go, e.g. configurations/local/auth.secret.ref.env). '$HOME/.config/codefly/secrets' appears nowhere else in core/ or cli/. core/runners/sandbox/doc.go:27-32 states the package 'is NOT a defense against a hostile binary that's actively trying to escape'.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A provider agent spawned on macOS with the proposal's own manifest (lines 730-734: read_paths [], write_paths [], network loopback) can read: the entire workspace including every configurations/<env>/*.secret.env and *.secret.ref.env; all of ~/.codefly including agent caches and every other binding's provider state under ~/.codefly/providers/<workspace-sha>/...; and every other same-user file (~/.npmrc, ~/.docker/config.json, other repos). It then exfiltrates via a request body to its own manifest-allowlisted origin, or returns the bytes inside a diagnostic string.
- **Impact:** On the primary dogfood platform there is no mechanism enforcing 'a provider cannot read workspace/secrets'. One malicious provider release reads every secret the provider system itself persists, including other bindings' one-time webhook secrets, defeating credential handles, purpose separation and the secret-sink design before any of them are exercised.
- **Required change:** Before P1 exits: (a) deny file-read* by default on macOS with an explicit allowlist, or at minimum deny the concrete Codefly surfaces - the resolved workspace's configurations/**, ~/.codefly/** and the projection output directory - and delete the dead '.config/codefly/secrets' entry; (b) state per-OS in the proposal which mechanism enforces the read claim, and gate 'production mutation ready' on macOS read confinement existing; (c) add darwin tests asserting a sandboxed agent cannot read a workspace *.secret.env or a file under ~/.codefly; (d) run sandbox_e2e on a macOS runner (CI is ubuntu-latest only, .github/workflows/go.yml:23, and the loopback e2e is darwin-tagged so it never executes).
- **Acceptance test:** darwin sandbox_e2e: launch a fixture agent with read_paths [], write_paths [], network loopback; the fixture attempts to read (1) <workspace>/configurations/local/billing.secret.env, (2) $HOME/.codefly/providers/<sha>/local-dogfood/billing/state.db, (3) $HOME/.ssh/id_ed25519. All must fail with EPERM, and the test must run in CI on macOS.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 6, 7
