Absent: any method/path policy (all of GET/HEAD/POST/PUT/DELETE/PATCH to any path, server.go:149), forbidden-header enforcement — caller-supplied headers including Authorization are set verbatim (server.go:234-240), proxy-variable defense (default http.Transport honors HTTP(S)_PROXY), IP-literal/DNS-rebinding checks, credential injection, idempotency-key injection, rate-limit normalization, request-ID extraction, body classification, and any cassette recorder. Structurally, these guards execute inside the plugin process itself (toolbox-web IS the plugin), and toolbox-web imports core (toolbox-web/server.go:12-14), so core/CLI cannot import it — the broker must be written host-side from scratch.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** P1 is scoped as if the broker is an extraction task. An implementer who 'extracts' server.go as the seed inherits an agent-controlled-header, proxy-honoring, any-path fetch as the credential-bearing broker — exactly the confused-deputy shape the design forbids — or discovers mid-P1 that the broker (origin/path templates, credential handles, one-time capture, redirect+rebinding defense, cassettes, sanitization) is an unbudgeted new security-critical component.
- **Impact:** Material under-scoping of the single most security-critical new component; risk of wrong trust-side reuse. The reusable part of toolbox-web is a design pattern (~60 lines of allowlist/redirect logic), not code.
- **Required change:** Reclassify this row as NEW (host-side broker in core or cli) with only conceptual reuse of toolbox-web's allowlist/redirect tests as behavioral references; make the broker its own reviewed P1 deliverable with an explicit threat-model checklist (header stripping, proxy neutralization, redirect+IP-literal policy, parse-before-log, capture-before-record). Correct the proposal sentence quoting 'most of the HTTP guard behavior'.
- **Acceptance test:** P1 issue text budgets the broker as new code with its own security review; broker tests prove caller-supplied Authorization/cookie/proxy headers are stripped, non-declared method/path is refused, and redirects crossing origins drop credentials — none of which toolbox-web tests cover today.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 2 (non-policy)

#### ADV-082 — execution receipts wiring

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Claim challenged:** Reuse row 'execution receipts | executionreceipt, CLI execution journal/runtime | admitted/started/terminal facts, UNCERTAIN, signed immutable receipts' (provider-plugins.md:257) and 'every mutation produces complete signed receipts' (P1 exit gate, provider-plugins.md:2057-2059).
- **Evidence:** The library is real and strong: core/executionreceipt/receipt.go (Prepare/Attest/Verify, Ed25519, SchemaV1), core/proto/codefly/execution/v1/execution.proto (EXECUTION_STAGE_COMPENSATED=5, EXECUTION_STAGE_UNCERTAIN=6, operation_id/attempt_id at lines 49-52), cli/pkg/executionjournal/journal.go (bbolt outbox, verifier-required Open, 0600/symlink rejection at journal.go:82-111). But the only production wiring is the gateway daemon behind an explicit --governed-execution flag with required authority JWKS/issuer flags (cli/cmd/daemon_execution.go:15-45); no ordinary CLI command emits receipts, and the attestor key lifecycle (cli/pkg/executionattestor) is gateway-scoped (attestorFileName 'gateway-ed25519-v1.json', cli/pkg/executionruntime/runtime.go:31-34).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** The provider coordinator runs as a plain CLI command; without new producer/attestor/key wiring it emits no receipts at all, or P1 quietly couples `codefly provider apply` to running the governed-execution daemon — neither is stated in the proposal.
- **Impact:** PARTIAL/UNWIRED reuse presented as available capability; the P1 exit gate ('complete signed receipts') hides a real design decision: who signs receipts for a plain CLI invocation, with which key, stored where.
- **Required change:** P1 must specify the provider receipt producer explicitly: reuse executionreceipt+executionjournal libraries, add a CLI-embedded attestor identity (or an explicit dependency on the gateway runtime), and define key storage/rotation for non-daemon runs. Update the reuse row to 'library REUSE AS-IS; CLI producer wiring NEW'.
- **Acceptance test:** `codefly provider apply` on a machine with no gateway daemon produces verifiable Ed25519-signed ADMITTED/STARTED/terminal receipts in a documented journal location, and `executionreceipt.Verify` passes against the published producer identity.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 2 (non-policy)

#### ADV-083 — agent spawn environment inheritance

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Claim challenged:** Provider agents get 'only the provider input subset and credential handles required for the current operation' (provider-plugins.md:644-646).
- **Evidence:** manager.Load builds the child environment as cmd.Env = os.Environ() plus additions (core/agents/manager/loader.go:722-731). The provider process therefore inherits the operator's full shell environment: HTTP(S)_PROXY, GITHUB_TOKEN/GH_TOKEN (read by the loader itself at manager.go:24-26), cloud credentials, OP_* session vars, etc.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A compromised provider reads ambient tokens directly from its own environment, or uses inherited proxy variables to shape any network path it does have (macOS loopback, future native egress) — no broker bypass needed for exfiltrable material that was never part of the operation.
- **Impact:** The spawn-time minimization claim is untrue under the reused loader; every ambient secret in the founder's shell becomes provider-readable.
- **Required change:** P1: provider spawns use an allowlisted environment (explicit CODEFLY_* control-plane variables plus PATH/HOME/TMPDIR as needed) instead of os.Environ() inheritance — a small LoadOption (WithCleanEnv) on the existing loader.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance provider dumps its environ; the test asserts no variable outside the documented allowlist (and specifically no *_PROXY, GITHUB_TOKEN, OP_*) is present.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 2 (non-policy)

#### ADV-084 — codefly agent ci conformance reuse

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 test infrastructure
- **Claim challenged:** Reuse row 'agent conformance | codefly agent ci | build/package/standalone verification | provider protocol conformance mode and fixtures' (provider-plugins.md:259) presents provider conformance as an added mode.
- **Evidence:** cli/cmd/agents/ci.go:427-428 hard-fails on any kind other than codefly:service ('agent conformance currently requires kind codefly:service'); the conformance gates are Builder.Create/generated-service shaped (ci.go:37-43, 569); even toolboxes — the previously added kind — are unsupported by the gate today.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** P1/Tier-2 testing assumes `codefly agent ci` can verify providers after a 'mode' flag; in reality the whole gate pipeline (scaffold, workspace gate, fixture model) is service-specific and a provider gate set (manifest/catalog subset, offline-RPC no-network, plan/apply/no-diff, receipt sequence) is new work sharing only the report schema and packaging steps.
- **Impact:** Test-infrastructure under-scoping in P1; risk that provider conformance is cut when the 'small extension' turns out to be a new gate family.
- **Required change:** Classify as EXTRACT/GENERALIZE: P1 budgets a provider conformance gate family reusing the report schema (agentCIReportSchemaVersion), source/release gates, and standalone-build machinery, with new provider-protocol gates replacing the generated-service gate.
- **Acceptance test:** `codefly agent ci` on a provider agent runs provider-specific gates and produces a report.json; running it on a provider today is proven (in the issue) to fail at ci.go:427, documenting the required generalization.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 2 (non-policy)

#### ADV-085 — Lane 7 / scenario 35 - response bounds must fail closed, not truncate; JSON parsing needs explicit limits

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 broker plus one-time capture; P2 Stripe signing secret
- **Claim challenged:** The broker enforces 'bounded methods, request/response sizes, and timeouts' (line 653), 'smaller default body caps for provider APIs' (1439), and capture 'bounds and parses the response before it can be logged or recorded' (line 522).
- **Evidence:** The reuse precedent truncates instead of failing: toolbox-web/server.go:251-260 reads MaxBodyBytes+1 (4 MiB, line 21) and on exceed returns the first 4 MiB with truncated: true (256-260), pinned by toolbox-web/server_test.go:142-164. Response headers are flattened with no size limit (262-268). No redirect-count, header-size, JSON-depth, duplicate-key or value-length limits exist anywhere. The proposal specifies no behavior for 'capture declared but response unparseable/oversize after a successful mutation' and no parser limits.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A vendor or mis-deployed self-hosted origin returns a 6 MiB response to POST /v1/webhook_endpoints. Under precedent semantics the broker truncates: the JSON is invalid, the declared pointer /secret cannot be extracted, and the endpoint exists remotely with its only signing secret discarded. Variants: duplicate 'secret' keys (last-wins in encoding/json, first-wins in some JSON Pointer implementations) make capture ambiguous; a 100 MB string value or 10000-deep nesting consumes host memory/CPU inside the trusted process; a non-string secret (number/object/null) has undefined capture behavior; invalid UTF-8 or embedded NULs flow into the secret sink.
- **Impact:** An unrecoverable one-time secret requiring manual dashboard remediation, or host-side resource exhaustion, triggered by data the host does not control - and reached in the worst variant where the remote effect happened and the recoverable copy was discarded.
- **Required change:** Fail closed on any response exceeding the per-operation cap (no truncation) with small caps (e.g. 256 KiB default, declared overrides); cap response header bytes and redirect count explicitly; for capture-declared requests parse with an explicit limits profile (max depth, max tokens, max string length, reject duplicate keys, require the pointed value to be a JSON string of declared max length) and treat any violation as a failed request returning only safe metadata, never the raw body. Record the failure as UNCERTAIN with a typed diagnostic naming the manual recovery.
- **Acceptance test:** Cassette-driven tests: oversize response fails with a typed diagnostic and no truncated body reaches the provider; duplicate-key response refuses capture; non-string pointed value refuses capture; deeply nested or huge-string response is rejected within a fixed memory ceiling; and in every case where the mutation was already sent the receipt shows UNCERTAIN with the manual recovery action.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 6, 7

#### ADV-086 — Lane 7 - redirects with injected credentials, and retries versus vendor idempotency windows

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 broker plus cassette recorder; P2 Stripe idempotency; P4 Resend rate-limit/retry mapping
- **Claim challenged:** 'Redirect revalidation' (line 652), 'redirect denial when credentials would cross origins' (1442), and 'injects stable idempotency keys where declared' (1441) with retry classification left to the provider (1180-1186).
- **Evidence:** Go forwards Authorization/WWW-Authenticate/Cookie on redirects to exact-host or subdomain matches and strips them otherwise (net/http Client.Do, pkg.go.dev/net/http). The precedent's CheckRedirect permits any allowlisted host (toolbox-web/server.go:78-84), tested only for the off-allowlist case (server_test.go:166-198), so with a multi-origin manifest a credential minted for origin A can be replayed to a subdomain of A. Stripe: 'You can remove keys from the system automatically after they're at least 24 hours old' and 'We generate a new request if a key is reused after the original is pruned' (docs.stripe.com/api/idempotent_requests); idempotency applies to POST only and reusing a key with different parameters errors. Sentry documents trailing-slash paths and prioritizes 'usability over correctness' for verbs (docs.sentry.io/api/requests/), so 3xx handling may be operationally required - unverified here, LOW confidence on the need.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Redirect: a self-hosted or regional origin returns 302 to a sibling subdomain; a broker that re-injects the credential per hop (the natural implementation, since injection is host-side) delivers the credential to a host never present in the approved plan digest. Retry: a transient failure is retried 25 hours later with the same derived idempotency key; Stripe treats it as a new request and a second webhook endpoint is created, producing exactly the 'existing same-URL endpoint is an unmanaged conflict' state the design tries to avoid (line 1659).
- **Impact:** Credential exposure to an unapproved host, and duplicated remote resources presented as a successful retry.
- **Required change:** Never follow redirects on credentialed requests - return the 3xx as data with Location exposed but not followed; allow opt-in following only for declared idempotent GETs to the same origin and declared path prefix, re-deriving credentials and recording each hop in the receipt. No generic retry layer for non-idempotent methods: a retry is legal only when the plan action declares an idempotency identity, the vendor supports it for that method, and the attempt is inside a declared freshness window (under 24h for Stripe); otherwise the outcome is UNCERTAIN and requires observation. Cassettes must normalize host-injected non-deterministic headers (Idempotency-Key, request IDs, dates) in the matcher and canonicalize form-encoded Stripe bodies (sorted keys, array index syntax).
- **Acceptance test:** (a) A credentialed request receiving 302 to a subdomain is not followed and no Authorization is emitted on a second hop; (b) an idempotent GET redirect within the same origin/path-prefix is followed and both hops appear in the receipt; (c) a retry beyond the declared idempotency window is refused and the operation marked UNCERTAIN; (d) replaying a recorded Stripe create twice with different operation IDs matches the same cassette entry.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 6, 7

#### ADV-087 — Purpose separation / projection and runtime injection

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (writer/commit authorization), P3 Sentry acceptance
- **Claim challenged:** Management, runtime, build, and browser credential purposes 'remain distinct' end-to-end, and 'the Sentry setup credential is not automatically copied into SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN'.
- **Evidence:** ConfigurationValue has only key/value/secret — no purpose, browser-exposure, or provenance field (core/proto/codefly/base/v0/configuration.proto:8-15); the proposal itself says outputs are converted to these existing messages with 'SECRET maps to secret=true'. All secret values of a consumed configuration are injected into a service's process env together (core/resources/environment_variables_manager.go:490-512), into cmd.Env for native runs (core/runners/base/native_runner.go:315-323) and container env for docker (core/runners/dockerrun/docker_runner.go). Enforcement in the proposal is 'the provider must warn' plus 'production policy may reject' — advisory.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Sentry provider projects SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN (BUILD purpose) into error-tracking.secret.env as the proposal's own example shows. Every runtime service that consumes the error-tracking configuration receives the org:ci token in its process environment; purpose metadata was discarded at conversion, so no consumer-side check exists. Worse, a buggy/compromised provider can map the MANAGEMENT credential handle's reference into the runtime output; the stated acceptance test ('setup token absent from runtime output') cannot catch this without the host comparing resolved reference identity, which is nowhere specified.
- **Impact:** Invariants 15/16 are enforced only at the moment of projection by convention, and not at all at consumption; a build token in runtime env or a management key projected as runtime survives every specified control.
- **Required change:** Specify host-side enforcement at output commit: resolve each projected secret reference and compare its identity/fingerprint against the declared purpose of the input it came from; refuse MANAGEMENT->RUNTIME/BUILD projection absent explicit allow-shared-credential. Split build-only secrets into a distinct configuration name (or add purpose to ConfigurationValue) so runtime services never receive them.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance provider attempts to project the management-purpose handle's reference under a runtime-purpose output key; host output commit denies with a receipt. Sentry dogfood: dump runtime service environ; assert no SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN present while the build consumer receives it.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 8, 9

#### ADV-088 — Secret minimization / resolver reuse

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Claim challenged:** Reuse of the configuration loader and secret references needs only a 'writable secret-sink contract' extension, while resolving 'only the named credential required for one operation'.
- **Evidence:** secretResolution.resolveConfiguration resolves every value marked Secret in a loaded configuration in one pass and caches raw values by URI for the load (core/configurations/secrets.go:232-255, 202-216). There is no per-key or per-purpose selective resolution API.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** codefly provider observe loads input-configuration providers/stripe; the loader resolves both STRIPE_MANAGEMENT_API_KEY and STRIPE_RUNTIME_API_KEY via `op read`, materializing the runtime key in host memory (and prompting 1Password unlock) for an operation that needs only the management credential. Contradicts the proposal's required control 7 ('resolve only the named credential required for one operation').
- **Impact:** Broader-than-needed secret materialization in host memory on every operation; unnecessary secret-store access events; larger crash-dump exposure.
- **Required change:** Add selective resolution (resolve named keys on demand) to the loader or a provider-specific resolution path; acknowledge it as new work in the reuse table (currently understated).
- **Acceptance test:** Instrument the resolver in a test: `codefly provider observe` on a two-credential input configuration performs exactly one backend resolution (management), zero for runtime.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 8, 9

#### ADV-089 — Secret sink contract / orphan recovery

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (contract), P5 (production sink)
- **Claim challenged:** The Prepare/Put/Commit/Rollback sink contract plus UNCERTAIN classification is sufficient to recover from partial failure between remote mutation, sink persistence, state commit, and receipts.
- **Evidence:** Proposal 'Secret sinks' section defines only Prepare(binding, outputs) -> transaction, Put -> opaque reference, Commit, Rollback; no lookup, no deterministic addressing, no retry semantics. Scenario language says the broker 'checkpoints the persisted reference before allowing another mutation' but does not order sink Commit against provider state commit, and the journal (cli/pkg/executionjournal/journal.go:432,796-808) records stages, not sink contents.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Production apply: Stripe webhook created, secret Put+Committed to the production sink, then the process crashes before the state/checkpoint write (scenario 39). On rerun, the plan sees a remote endpoint with no known secret reference; nothing in the contract lets the host discover the already-persisted secret, so it either creates a duplicate sink entry, overwrites, or forces an endpoint REPLACE — rotating a production secret unnecessarily. Conversely, if Put succeeds but Commit is undefined-durability, the only recoverable copy may be lost.
- **Impact:** Uncertain outcomes involving production secrets are not actually recoverable as promised; operators face orphaned or duplicated production secrets with no reconciliation path.
- **Required change:** Extend the sink contract with deterministic addressing (key derived from workspace/environment/binding/output name), a Lookup operation, defined Commit durability, and idempotent Put-or-adopt on retry; require the sink reference checkpoint to be journaled before declaring the plan action complete and define its order relative to state commit.
- **Acceptance test:** Crash-injection conformance: kill the host between sink Commit and state commit; rerun must find the persisted secret via Lookup, adopt its reference into state, and produce a no-diff second plan without touching the remote resource.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 8, 9

#### ADV-090 — Sentry client-keys response carries legacy secret material into observation/cassettes

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (broker sanitizer supports declared secret fields on read responses), P3 (Sentry manifest and conformance)
- **Claim challenged:** The proposal's sentry.client-key observed resource lists only 'client key ID; active state; public DSN; safe rate-limit metadata' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1690-1695) and generic Observation 'must not include arbitrary provider response bodies' (provider-plugins.md:1114), implying the keys response is safe to handle.
- **Evidence:** The official list-client-keys endpoint (GET /api/0/projects/{org}/{project}/keys/, scopes project:read|write|admin) returns key objects containing a `secret` field and a `dsn` object with `secret` (legacy secret DSN) alongside `public` (https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/list-a-projects-client-keys/). The proposal gives Resend an explicit hygiene rule — 'never log a webhook list response' (provider-plugins.md:1778) — but states no analogous rule for the Sentry keys endpoint, and the one-time-capture machinery only sanitizes manifest-declared captures, not undeclared secret fields in read responses (provider-plugins.md:502, and scenario 36 of the brief). The current script already persists the full keys response to disk (module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/sentry.sh:170-180 writes keys.json to a temp dir) and selects .[0].dsn.public one pointer away from .[0].dsn.secret.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** During Observe, the broker records a cassette of the keys endpoint; the cassette (or a debug log, or provider state that copies the observed key object) now contains the legacy secret DSN and key secret. A cassette committed for deterministic replay ships working credential material (brief scenario 27); a provider that selects the wrong JSON field projects dsn.secret instead of dsn.public.
- **Impact:** Secret-material leakage through exactly the surfaces (cassettes, state, logs) the proposal promises are clean; violates intended invariant 18 without any component technically misbehaving, because no rule declared the field secret.
- **Required change:** Add Sentry-specific acceptance requirements mirroring the Resend rule: the keys-endpoint response is never logged or recorded unsanitized; the broker/response-classification layer (provider-plugins.md:1435) must carry a manifest-declared strip/deny list for known secret fields on read endpoints (keys[].secret, keys[].dsn.secret), applied before logging, metrics, error bodies, and cassette write; observation schema for sentry.client-key must be a closed allowlist of fields.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance: record a cassette against a fixture keys response containing secret and dsn.secret; assert the on-disk cassette, provider state, receipts, and diagnostics contain neither value; assert Observe output schema rejects any field outside the declared allowlist.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 14

#### ADV-091 — Deterministic client-key selection and DSN drift

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P3 (Sentry), doctor section
- **Claim challenged:** The proposal claims deterministic observation and a no-diff second run (intended invariant 31) for Sentry, but the Sentry section never defines how one client key is selected when a project has several, none active, or a paginated list.
- **Evidence:** core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1690-1695 (client-key resource has isActive and multiple keys are possible) and :1110 (generic 'ambiguous' support) with no Sentry selection rule; official docs confirm cursor pagination and multiple keys with isActive and rateLimit per key (https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/list-a-projects-client-keys/); the current script hardcodes the first array element regardless of active state (module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/sentry.sh:180 `.[0].dsn.public`); official docs also state DSNs can be rotated and revoked (https://docs.sentry.io/concepts/key-terms/dsn-explainer/).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A project has two keys (e.g. one rotated-out but still listed, one active). Run 1 observes key A and projects its DSN; the user rotates keys in the Sentry UI; run 2 observes key B first (or key A now inactive) and produces a projection diff with no desired-state change — or worse, the projection keeps a revoked DSN and the app silently drops all events until someone inspects the Sentry dashboard, which is exactly the operator experience the product statement promises to eliminate.
- **Impact:** Breaks the second-run-no-diff invariant, and creates a silent total loss of error tracking after routine key rotation with a green doctor unless doctor checks key active status.
- **Required change:** Specify in the Sentry provider schema: (a) if the binding supplies an explicit client-key ID, use it; (b) otherwise require exactly one active key, and return AMBIGUOUS with a MANUAL_ACTION/BLOCKED plan when zero or multiple active keys exist; (c) doctor must verify the projected DSN still corresponds to an active key (rotation/revocation drift check).
- **Acceptance test:** Cassette tests: two-active-key fixture yields AMBIGUOUS not an arbitrary pick; inactive-only fixture yields a blocked plan with remediation; rotation fixture (projected DSN absent from active keys) makes doctor fail with a drift diagnostic; identical fixture twice yields byte-identical observation and NOOP plan.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 14

#### ADV-092 — Protocol-freeze coverage — Sentry contributes nothing to the mutation contract

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P2 (freeze criteria), P3 (Sentry), P5 (declaring the contract stable)
- **Claim challenged:** The proposal claims the protocol is 'designed against three providers before the protocol is frozen' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:23-24) and intended invariant 35, with Sentry as the counterexample that 'prevents a webhook-shaped abstraction' (provider-plugins.md:29-32).
- **Evidence:** Sentry v0.1 is observe/project only (provider-plugins.md:1697-1701); the cross-provider matrix shows managed create/update = 'webhook' for Stripe and Resend and 'not required' for Sentry, write-once/secret output 'no' for Sentry (provider-plugins.md:1912-1928). Thus every CREATE/UPDATE/REPLACE/DELETE, idempotency, checkpoint, UNCERTAIN, and one-time-capture path is validated exclusively against webhook-endpoint-like resources before freeze. Separately, the deferral rationale for project creation ('team ownership, default alert-rule... decisions', provider-plugins.md:1699-1701, 2281-2285) is stale versus the current API: POST /api/0/organizations/{org}/projects/ requires no team, auto-creates a personal team for the caller, exposes default_rules (default true), and needs org:write when member project creation is disabled (https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/create-a-project-for-an-organization/).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** The mutation half of the protocol freezes shaped around webhook CRUD: single-resource, synchronous, secret-returned-at-create, URL+events as the whole desired state. The first non-webhook mutation (Sentry project creation with its auto-personal-team side effect and org:write conditional scope, or any provider with asynchronous provisioning) then forces protocol changes after freeze — the exact failure mode the three-provider sequencing was supposed to prevent.
- **Impact:** False confidence in the freeze criterion; Sentry's simplicity hides mutation-lifecycle deficiencies rather than testing them. This does not invalidate ordering Sentry second — its projection/origin/classification pressure is real — but the freeze claim overstates coverage.
- **Required change:** State honestly in the freeze criteria that mutation semantics are validated only against webhook-shaped resources in v0; before declaring the mutation contract stable, either (a) design-review (not implement) Sentry project creation and client-key creation/rotation against the current API as paper pressure tests — noting the auto-created personal team as a side-effect resource the plan model must be able to represent, and the conditional org:write scope as a policy-visible scope escalation — or (b) explicitly scope the frozen v0 mutation contract to synchronous single-resource lifecycles. Update the project-creation deferral rationale to the current API shape.
- **Acceptance test:** The protocol-freeze checklist document enumerates which RPC/plan features were exercised by at least two providers; mutation features exercised only by webhook resources are either marked provisionally-stable or backed by a written non-webhook design study (Sentry project creation) showing no protocol change is needed.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 14

#### ADV-093 — Dual durable stores without ordering contract

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Claim challenged:** 'Every externally visible effect is checkpointed' (brief invariant 25; provider-plugins.md:1183, 1273) plus execution receipts (provider-plugins.md:1189-1193) yields consistent durable evidence.
- **Evidence:** Provider state is one bbolt store per binding (provider-plugins.md:1248-1252); receipts live in a second, separate bbolt journal (cli/pkg/executionjournal/journal.go:74-98; cli/pkg/executionruntime/runtime.go:120-127 places it under ~/.codefly/execution/gateways/<sha>). bbolt cannot transact across files. The proposal never specifies write ordering between state checkpoints and receipt appends, nor which store is authoritative when they diverge, nor where provider receipts actually live. The journal already enforces terminal-requires-durable-STARTED (journal.go:219-224) and offers IncompleteAttempts for the restart sweep (journal.go:432-470), so the primitives for a defined ordering exist.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Crash between a provider-state action checkpoint commit and the corresponding receipt append (or vice versa): state says the webhook was created, journal has STARTED with no terminal (or a terminal receipt exists with no matching state checkpoint). Without a specified ordering and reconciliation rule, recovery code cannot know which artifact to trust, and audit evidence diverges from operational state.
- **Impact:** Ambiguous crash recovery and unreliable audit trail for exactly the operations the receipts exist to attest.
- **Required change:** Specify in P1 the canonical ordering — ADMITTED receipt, STARTED receipt, pre-send state checkpoint, remote effect, post-effect state checkpoint, terminal receipt — declare provider state authoritative for remote facts and the journal authoritative for attribution, and require every provider CLI entry point to run an IncompleteAttempts-style sweep (appending UNCERTAIN terminals) on the binding before accepting new work. Also state explicitly where provider receipts are stored relative to provider state (surviving state deletion is desirable).
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance kills the coordinator at each inter-store boundary; after restart, the sweep produces exactly one terminal receipt per interrupted attempt, and the next plan's proposed actions are consistent with the surviving state checkpoints in every kill window.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 10

#### ADV-094 — State schema downgrade and mid-operation agent replacement

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1; verified per provider in P2-P4
- **Claim challenged:** UpgradeState (provider-plugins.md:1228-1239) covers agent/state version skew.
- **Evidence:** provider-plugins.md:1229-1233 covers only 'planning with newer code' over older state. No text defines behavior when an older agent encounters newer state, even though both signals exist: agent advertises its state schema version (provider-plugins.md:1092) and state records its schema version (provider-plugins.md:1264). Backups exist only per-upgrade-step (provider-plugins.md:1239); general backup/restore is deferred to P5 (provider-plugins.md:2153-2156). Whether pending/partial/uncertain checkpoints (provider-plugins.md:1273) are part of the versioned state schema — needed when a crash-interrupted operation is recovered by a different agent version — is unstated. Diagnostic code provider.schema.incompatible already exists (provider-plugins.md:1574).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** User pins agent 0.2.0, state upgrades to schema 3, then rolls back the binding to agent 0.1.0 (or two machines share dotfiles/state at different agent versions). The old agent receives schema-3 prior state in ProviderContext. Unspecified behavior: silent misparse could drop ownership records or misread checkpoints — precisely what UpgradeState forbids upward ('cannot discard remote IDs... silently', provider-plugins.md:1236-1237) but nothing forbids downward.
- **Impact:** Silent ownership/state corruption on a routine version rollback; the recovery path (restore from the pre-upgrade backup) is unspecified until P5.
- **Required change:** State the fail-closed rule in P1: the host refuses any operation when state schema version exceeds the agent's supported version, emitting provider.schema.incompatible with remediation (upgrade agent, or restore the automatic pre-upgrade backup — which therefore must be retained and restorable in P1, not P5). Declare checkpoints part of the versioned state schema so UpgradeState migrates in-flight recovery data.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance: upgrade state to N+1, then invoke the schema-N agent; every RPC that receives prior state fails closed with provider.schema.incompatible and unmodified state; restoring the automatic backup returns the binding to a workable schema-N state.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 10

#### ADV-095 — Lock scope, hold duration, and apply-start CAS

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Claim challenged:** 'bbolt or an equivalent fsync-backed transactional store and an inter-process lock' (provider-plugins.md:1251-1252) plus freshness checks (control 11) sufficiently specify concurrency.
- **Evidence:** The single sentence at provider-plugins.md:1251-1252 is the entire locking spec. Plan digest binds prior-state and observed-state digests (provider-plugins.md:1153-1154) and control 11 says apply rejects changed digests, but nothing states the digest check happens atomically under the lock at apply admission, whether the lock is held across the (minutes-long) remote Apply, or what a concurrent process experiences. Existing patterns diverge: bbolt open fails after a 500ms timeout (journal.go:98 with Options{Timeout}), localservice polls flock in a loop (cli/pkg/localservice/lock_unix.go:27-45), cliupdate uses gofrs/flock (cli/pkg/cliupdate/state.go:14-26). bbolt's file lock is exclusive per open handle, so a coordinator that holds the DB open during Apply blocks even read-only observe/doctor on the same binding.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Two processes: A applies plan P1 (state advances), B applies P2 built on the old prior-state digest. If B's digest check reads state before acquiring the write lock (TOCTOU), B executes a stale plan — precisely brief scenario 20. Separately, a held-open bbolt handle makes `codefly provider doctor` fail with an opaque 'timeout' during any long apply, or, with no timeout, hang.
- **Impact:** Either a correctness hole (stale plan executes) or an operability hole (spurious lock failures with unhelpful errors), depending on unstated implementation choices.
- **Required change:** Specify in P1: per-binding (or per-workspace-store) advisory lock via flock; Apply acquires the lock, then re-reads and CASes prior-state and output digests inside the same critical section before the first remote effect, and holds the lock through terminal checkpointing; read-only commands use a shared lock or lock-free snapshot reads; lock contention maps to a stable diagnostic naming the holder PID/operation. OS-released flock on crash covers the crash-while-locked case.
- **Acceptance test:** Tier-0 concurrency test: interleave two apply processes at every ordering; exactly one succeeds, the other fails with provider.plan.stale before any remote effect; a concurrent doctor during apply returns a lock-held diagnostic, never a hang or raw bbolt timeout string.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 10

#### ADV-096 — Receipt reuse dependency for local dogfood

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Claim challenged:** Provider operations reuse execution receipts as-is with only 'provider operation kinds and remote resource references' added (provider-plugins.md:257).
- **Evidence:** cli/pkg/executionrecorder/recorder.go:123 requires Journal, Attestor, and Authority; the Authority verifies a Work Context token (recorder.go:46-59, 184-191), and ExecutionReceiptV1.work_context with tenant_id is required (core/proto/codefly/execution/v1/execution.proto:62-64); executionruntime.Open demands AuthorityJWKS and AuthorityIssuer (cli/pkg/executionruntime/runtime.go:91-96). A purely local founder running `codefly provider setup` has no SaaS authority issuing Work Context tokens. AuthorityFunc (recorder.go:51-59) makes a local-authority adapter feasible, so this is a small extension, but it is unnamed work, and 'signed execution evidence' for local dogfood depends on it. Confidence MEDIUM: not every CLI wiring path that might already mint local Work Contexts was traced.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** P1 integrates receipts assuming reuse-as-is; local dogfood then either cannot produce receipts (violating 'every mutation produces complete signed receipts', provider-plugins.md:2058) or bypasses the recorder with an ad-hoc unsigned path, undermining the evidence model.
- **Impact:** The proposal's durable-evidence claims for local dogfood rest on an unwired dependency: either receipts silently disappear from the flagship dogfood path (P2 exit gates become unverifiable) or an unsigned side channel is invented under schedule pressure, forking the evidence model the design forbids forking.
- **Required change:** Name the work in P1: a local principal/authority adapter (or explicit local assurance mode) so the recorder's admission contract is satisfied in dogfood without a remote authority, with production admission still requiring the real authority.
- **Acceptance test:** On a machine with no SaaS session, `codefly provider apply` for a local binding produces a verifiable Ed25519-signed ADMITTED/STARTED/terminal receipt chain in the journal, attributable to the local principal.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 10

#### ADV-097 — Idempotency retention window and lost-response recovery

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (checkpoint message carries idempotency identity + timestamps), P2 (Stripe window policy and metadata stamping)
- **Claim challenged:** Proposal (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1222-1226) says 'Stripe idempotency keys reduce creation ambiguity, but they do not replace state and observation. A key may expire' — but defines no retry window policy, no checkpoint content enabling window computation, and misses that in-window replay re-delivers the one-time secret.
- **Evidence:** Official semantics: keys accepted on all POSTs; 'You can remove keys from the system automatically after they're at least 24 hours old'; replay 'works by saving the resulting status code and body of the first request… Subsequent requests with the same key return the same result'; reuse with different params errors; concurrent duplicates are not saved and 'You can retry these requests' (all https://docs.stripe.com/api/idempotent_requests). Therefore a same-key retry within the window returns the original create body including `secret`, and beyond ~24h the same key may be pruned, making the identical POST create a duplicate endpoint. The current script sends no Idempotency-Key at all (module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/stripe.sh:176-193).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Scenario 38: Apply crashes after POST send; operator retries the operation 30 hours later. The broker re-injects the same operation-derived key; Stripe has pruned it; a second webhook endpoint with the identical URL is created; state holds no remote ID for either; the account creeps toward the 16-endpoint cap (https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks) and the next Observe finds two same-URL endpoints, which per invariant is an unmanaged conflict requiring manual untangling — and the second endpoint's secret was captured while the first's is unreachable via API.
- **Impact:** Ambiguous-outcome recovery, the central reliability story of the proposal, is under-specified for the exact vendor mechanism it cites; the safe recovery affordance (in-window replay re-yields the secret for capture) is left unused, forcing avoidable MANUAL_ACTION or replacement.
- **Required change:** (1) Checkpoint the idempotency key identity and original request send-time before dispatching any mutation, so the host can decide replay safety; (2) define the retry policy: same-key automatic replay permitted only within a conservative window (e.g. ≤20h from send-time, since 24h is a lower bound on pruning eligibility, not a guarantee), with the broker's secret-capture applying identically to replayed bodies; (3) beyond the window: mandatory re-Observe, discovery via the Codefly metadata ownership stamp (webhook endpoints support metadata, https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/create — the provider must stamp binding/operation identity at create), then explicit import of the stamped endpoint plus MANUAL_ACTION 'reveal secret in Workbench' (https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks: 'Click to reveal') or a planned replace.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance: kill the host between request send and response commit; recovery within the window must replay the same key, capture the same secret into the sink, and converge with no duplicate endpoint; recovery simulated past the window must refuse automatic retry, surface the metadata-stamped duplicate in the next plan as import-or-replace, and never emit a second CREATE with a fresh key silently.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 13

#### ADV-098 — Restricted-key practicality for the setup/runtime split

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P2 (Stripe), P5 (production policy templates)
- **Claim challenged:** Proposal (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:880-887, 1640-1643) assumes a 'management key: account inspection and webhook management' and a 'runtime restricted key' for 'approved customer, checkout, portal, subscription, and billing operations'.
- **Evidence:** Restricted keys exist with rk_test_/rk_live_ prefixes and per-resource None/Read/Write permissions, but creation is documented Dashboard-only ('To create a new restricted key, click Create a restricted key', https://docs.stripe.com/keys/restricted-api-keys); neither https://docs.stripe.com/keys nor the restricted-api-keys page publicly enumerates the permission categories — Webhook Endpoints, Checkout Sessions, Customer portal, Subscriptions, and Account read are not confirmed grantable from documentation alone; insufficient permission yields an invalid-request error at call time. Also unverified: whether GET /v1/account (needed by Observe) succeeds under a webhook-scoped management RAK.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** P2 ships a documented least-privilege recipe ('create a management RAK with Webhook Endpoints: Write') that a founder cannot reproduce because the category names/coverage differ in the Dashboard, or Observe fails mid-setup because the management RAK cannot read /v1/account, producing a confusing auth diagnosis; alternatively the docs over-promise that Codefly can provision these keys when both RAKs are irreducibly manual dashboard actions.
- **Impact:** The credential-separation story — a headline differentiator of the design — cannot be validated from primary docs and risks shipping wrong founder-facing instructions; the setup flow must budget for two MANUAL_ACTION key-creation steps it currently does not model.
- **Required change:** Treat the exact RAK permission set for both roles as a live-acceptance qualification item (record which categories are required for GET /v1/account, webhook CRUD, checkout/portal/subscription runtime calls); model RAK creation as explicit MANUAL_ACTION steps in `setup`; document the sandbox-scoped full sk_test_ key of a dedicated sandbox as the sanctioned local-dogfood management credential, reserving RAK discipline for live accounts.
- **Acceptance test:** Live acceptance on a dedicated sandbox: mint the documented management RAK and runtime RAK per the shipped instructions, run full setup/observe/plan/apply/doctor with the management RAK only and the product smoke suite with the runtime RAK only; record the minimal working permission sets as cassette-adjacent fixtures and fail CI if the shipped docs drift from them.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 13

#### ADV-099 — testing-cassettes

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 test infrastructure; consumed by P2-P4
- **Claim challenged:** Tier 1 cassettes 'fail if a secret-shaped field remains' and provide deterministic request matching, reusing Codefly's cassette testing guidance.
- **Evidence:** No recorder/replayer/sanitizer implementation exists in core/, cli/, or toolbox-web/ Go code (rg 'cassette|go-vcr|Cassette' -g '*.go' returns only unrelated hits); the pattern is documentation-only, citing the external mind-server repo (core/docs/testing.md:135-150). The proposal specifies broker-injected idempotency keys ('injects stable idempotency keys where declared') and cassette match keys including 'canonical path/query/body hash' and 'safe headers' (Tier 1, provider-plugins.md:1841-1857) without defining how operation-derived idempotency keys, timestamps, and request IDs are normalized so replay matching is stable across runs, and without defining what 'secret-shaped' means.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** (a) A cassette records an injected idempotency header derived from a fresh operation ID; on replay the new operation ID produces a different header/body hash, the match misses, and the suite flakes or (worse) a permissive matcher matches the wrong interaction. (b) The sanitizer's failure criterion is a regex guess; a vendor introduces a new secret field shape (exactly scenario 36) that passes the check and a live token lands in Git (scenario 27).
- **Impact:** Flaky or falsely-green Tier-1/Tier-2 suites; committed secrets in cassettes.
- **Required change:** Specify in the foundation issue: (1) match keys exclude broker-injected headers and normalize operation/attempt-derived values via stable substitution; (2) the sanitizer's deny-set is derived from machine-readable sources — manifest secret-capture declarations, credential purposes, and per-provider secret-pattern catalogs — not ad-hoc regexes, and an unknown-looking high-entropy field fails closed pending review; (3) the recorder is a named deliverable with its own tests, not 'guidance reuse'.
- **Acceptance test:** Recording the same logical operation twice with different operation IDs produces byte-identical committed cassettes; injecting a novel 'whsec_'-like field into a recorded response makes sanitization fail the recording run rather than committing it.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 19, 20, 21, 22

#### ADV-100 — migration-import

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P2 (Stripe migration), pattern reused in P4
- **Claim challenged:** State adoption step 4: 'propose an import plan for exact remote IDs' for script-created resources.
- **Evidence:** scripts/setup/stripe.sh:168-176 selects an existing webhook by URL match ('[.data[] | select(.url == $url and .status == "enabled")][0].id') and persists nothing but the signing secret into billing.secret.env (stripe.sh:222-226); no remote ID, account ID, or state is recorded anywhere. The proposal correctly demands exact-ID import (provider-plugins.md:1955-1962) but never specifies where the operator obtains the ID or how multiple same-URL endpoints are presented.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Operator runs migration; Observe finds two same-URL endpoints (one stale from an earlier dogfood run — the script always creates a new one when none is enabled). With no candidate-selection UX specified, the operator either guesses an ID from the Stripe dashboard (adopting the wrong endpoint whose signing secret does not match the locally stored one, breaking webhook verification silently) or abandons migration.
- **Impact:** Wrong-resource adoption producing signature-verification failures diagnosed only at runtime; migration friction pushes users back to --force script flows.
- **Required change:** Specify the import UX: Observe lists all same-URL candidates as unmanaged conflicts with remote ID, created date, event set, and status; import requires typing the exact ID; after adoption, doctor must verify the projected signing secret verifies a Stripe test event (or is explicitly re-provisioned) before ownership is marked.
- **Acceptance test:** With two same-URL endpoints in the sandbox, 'codefly provider import billing stripe.webhook-endpoint <id>' succeeds only for an explicitly typed ID, refuses URL-based selection, and doctor flags a secret/endpoint mismatch after adopting the endpoint whose secret differs from the local file.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 19, 20, 21, 22

#### ADV-101 — migration-ownership

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (projection writer), exercised in P2 migration
- **Claim challenged:** The projection writer 'attaches ownership metadata containing binding, provider, plan digest, contract version, and output hashes' and 'never overwrites an unowned, differing configuration silently'.
- **Evidence:** provider-plugins.md:990-999 (writer invariants) never states where ownership metadata is persisted. During the compatibility period, legacy scripts remain executable (provider-plugins.md:1936-1939) and write configuration files directly with install -m 600 plus a --force override (provider-common.sh:146-175), with no knowledge of ownership metadata.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** If ownership metadata lives beside the configuration files (sidecar file or header comment), a legacy script rerun with --force rewrites the file and either deletes the sidecar (provider now sees unowned config — recoverable) or leaves stale metadata claiming ownership of content the provider never wrote (provider CAS check passes on the stale digest? or worse, a digest mismatch is misattributed to drift). The failure mode is ambiguous precisely because the storage location is unspecified.
- **Impact:** Undefined coexistence behavior in the exact window migration is designed for; possible misattributed ownership.
- **Required change:** Specify: ownership metadata (output digests, plan digest, contract version) is stored only in the provider state directory (~/.codefly/providers/...), never in the workspace; the CAS check compares the live file hash to the state-recorded digest, so any external rewrite (script, editor) deterministically surfaces as 'unowned/differing' requiring explicit adoption.
- **Acceptance test:** After a provider projection, rerun the legacy script with --force; the next 'codefly provider plan' reports the output as externally modified/unowned and refuses to overwrite without explicit re-adoption; no state file in the workspace tree changed.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 19, 20, 21, 22

#### ADV-102 — migration-shim

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P2, P4 (script migration deliverables)
- **Claim challenged:** Scripts become thin shims: 'scripts/setup/stripe.sh -> codefly provider setup billing --env local-dogfood', with 'the shim may translate old safe flags to provider input sources'.
- **Evidence:** stripe.sh flags --provision-webhook (maps to a binding-level webhook lifecycle change, not an invocation flag), --skip-remote-validation (no equivalent — provider setup always observes), --force (replaced by displayed plan/adoption per provider-plugins.md:986-988), --webhook-origin (host ingress resolution replaces it). provider-common.sh is sourced by all eight scripts while only three migrate in scope.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A CI job or runbook invoking 'stripe.sh --skip-remote-validation --force' gets either silently different semantics (a remote Observe now runs, requiring network and credentials where none were needed) or an opaque failure; alternatively a permissive shim silently drops --force and hangs waiting for interactive plan approval in CI.
- **Impact:** Broken existing automation during the compatibility window; support burden; users bypass to raw scripts.
- **Required change:** Define per-flag shim policy in the P2/P4 migration deliverables: translatable flags (--api-key-file, --env-file, --workspace) map to input sources; untranslatable flags (--provision-webhook, --skip-remote-validation, --force) hard-fail with the exact replacement command/binding edit printed; shims must be non-interactive-safe (--yes only where policy allows, per invariant 10). Non-migrated scripts keep the real provider-common.sh unchanged.
- **Acceptance test:** Invoking the stripe shim with each legacy flag combination in a non-TTY environment either performs the documented equivalent or exits non-zero with the exact replacement instruction; no shim path performs a vendor API call or writes an output file itself.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 19, 20, 21, 22

#### ADV-103 — testing-conformance-fixture

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Claim challenged:** P1 exit gate: 'a neutral conformance provider can validate, observe, plan, apply one idempotent resource, project a public+secret configuration, rerun with no diff, import, disconnect, and explicitly destroy.'
- **Evidence:** provider-plugins.md:2052-2062. The proposal never states what remote endpoint the neutral conformance provider observes/mutates: a live vendor is unavailable (it is vendor-neutral by definition), and the cassette infrastructure records real APIs — of which the conformance provider has none.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** The implementer necessarily builds a local fixture HTTP server for the conformance provider; without a stated policy this is either (a) built ad-hoc and undocumented, becoming an unreviewed de facto reference semantics for idempotency/pagination/error behavior, or (b) challenged in review as violating 'no mocks', stalling the P1 gate.
- **Impact:** Ambiguous P1 acceptance; risk that conformance semantics drift from real-vendor semantics validated only later in P2-P4.
- **Required change:** Specify: the conformance provider targets a Codefly-owned loopback reference server that is a normative contract artifact (defining idempotency, 429/retry-after, pagination, one-time-secret-return, and lost-response behavior the protocol must handle), reviewed with the protocol, and explicitly not a simulation of any real vendor; broker enforcement runs against it exactly as against real origins (its origin appears in the conformance manifest).
- **Acceptance test:** The conformance suite runs with no external network (verified by sandbox denial), exercises one-time-secret capture, lost-response recovery, and 429 handling against the reference server through the production broker, and the reference server's behavior spec is a committed reviewed document.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 19, 20, 21, 22

#### ADV-104 — testing-platform-matrix

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1; re-verified in P5 compatibility runs
- **Claim challenged:** P1 exit gate: 'a provider binary cannot directly write workspace files or reach an unapproved external origin', proven by conformance and capture fixtures.
- **Evidence:** provider-plugins.md:2060-2062, 2043-2046. The proposal's test tiers never state on which operating systems the sandbox-dependent assertions (external network denial, workspace-write denial, no-network offline RPCs) must run; sandbox backends differ materially between macOS and Linux (mechanism verification is Lane 6, but the test-plan gap is independent of which mechanisms exist).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Conformance runs green on developer macOS (one sandbox backend); production/CI runs on Linux where the backend and its escape surface differ (or is absent in a container); the P1 exit-gate claim is asserted for an environment it was never tested on. Additionally, 'offline' RPCs (Validate/Plan/UpgradeState) need a stated enforcement check — e.g., the host asserts zero broker requests during those RPCs — else 'offline' is a doc promise.
- **Impact:** The central isolation claim of the design is certified by tests that do not cover the deployment platform.
- **Required change:** Require the conformance tier to run on at least Linux (CI, containerized and non-containerized if backends differ) and macOS, with per-platform assertions for external-network denial and workspace-write denial; add a host-side conformance assertion that Validate/Plan/UpgradeState issue zero broker requests and receive zero credential handles.
- **Acceptance test:** CI matrix shows the sandbox-denial conformance tests executing and passing on linux/amd64 and darwin/arm64; a deliberately misbehaving test provider attempting a direct dial and a workspace write fails conformance on both platforms; a Plan-time broker request fails conformance.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 19, 20, 21, 22

#### ADV-105 — roadmap-operations

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P2, P3, P4
- **Claim challenged:** P2-P4 exit gates depend on live acceptance against 'dedicated sandbox/test accounts' with cleanup (Tier 3), but the issue structure contains no provisioning work.
- **Evidence:** provider-plugins.md:1879-1894 (Tier 3 requirements), 2078 ('add cassette and live sandbox acceptance suites'), 2130-2133 (Resend live qualification is a required deliverable: 'qualify signing-secret retrieval behavior against current API and record it'); recommended issues 2-4 (provider-plugins.md:2199-2203) list no account provisioning, credential storage, or cleanup-automation tasks. Resend domain verification requires operator-owned DNS records (inference from the proposal's own domain-prerequisite framing at provider-plugins.md:1749-1754; exact lead time unverified — LOW confidence on duration, HIGH on the dependency existing).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Issue 4 (Resend) reaches its acceptance phase and blocks on creating an account, verifying a domain via DNS propagation, and wiring opt-in CI credentials — days of calendar time appearing mid-issue; the signing-secret ambiguity that the whole Resend design hinges on cannot be qualified until then.
- **Impact:** Schedule slip concentrated at exit gates; temptation to substitute hand-written fixtures for the required live qualification, undermining the no-mocks policy exactly where it matters most (Resend secret-shape ambiguity).
- **Required change:** Add explicit prerequisite tasks to each provider issue (or a shared ops task): dedicated Stripe sandbox account, Sentry test organization/project, Resend account + DNS-verified test domain, CI secret channel for opt-in live runs, and scripted cleanup verification; start the Resend domain verification when Issue 5 (Stripe) starts.
- **Acceptance test:** Before each provider issue is marked started, its tracking issue links working (opt-in) live-acceptance credentials and a green cleanup dry run against the dedicated account; the Resend signing-secret qualification cassette is recorded before the provider's plan semantics are finalized.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 19, 20, 21, 22

#### ADV-106 — state-recovery

- **Severity:** MEDIUM  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (sink contract), exercised in P2
- **Claim challenged:** 'The signing secret returned at creation is a write-once secret output' (Stripe), with outputs classified 'stable, computed, or write-once'.
- **Evidence:** provider-plugins.md:1615-1616, 924. Scenario: external deletion of the owned webhook (brief scenario 24) forces recreation, which yields a new signing secret that must replace the previously persisted 'write-once' sink value; the proposal defines no replacement semantics for write-once outputs, and separately forbids rolling back a persisted one-time secret (provider-plugins.md:536-539).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** User deletes the webhook in the Stripe dashboard; next plan proposes CREATE; Apply captures the new secret, but the sink target already holds the old write-once value. Depending on unspecified semantics the sink either refuses the write (operation becomes UNCERTAIN with a live webhook whose secret was never persisted — the worst state) or silently overwrites (destroying the only copy of a value that might still be needed if the operator adopted the wrong resource).
- **Impact:** A routine external-deletion recovery path dead-ends in UNCERTAIN or silently destroys secret history; webhook verification breaks until manual intervention.
- **Required change:** Define write-once as write-once-per-remote-resource-generation: a new plan that creates/replaces the resource authorizes a sink replacement bound to the new plan digest, the prior reference is retained (versioned or tombstoned) in state for audit, and the projection atomically switches to the new reference only after sink commit.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance scenario: delete the fixture resource out-of-band, rerun plan/apply; the new secret persists under a new sink generation, projection updates, receipts show old and new fingerprints, and a subsequent plan is empty.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 19, 20, 21, 22

### LOW (26)

#### ADV-107 — Lane 3 / partial-kind-addition precedent (JOB)

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Claim challenged:** Implicit proposal assumption: adding an enum value plus registration completes a kind.
- **Evidence:** basev0.Agent_JOB = 2 exists (core/proto/codefly/base/v0/agent.proto:15) and const JobAgent = AgentKind("codefly:job") exists (core/resources/job.go:17-18), but no RegisterAgent call for it exists anywhere (rg over core/cli: only MODULE/SERVICE/APPLICATION/TOOLBOX at module.go:28, service.go:31, application.go:21, toolbox.go:25), and Path() has no job branch — LoadAgent of a JOB proto yields an Agent with empty Kind via the unchecked map read at core/resources/agent.go:69.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Historical proof that kinds ship half-wired without any test failing; the same fate awaits PROVIDER without an exhaustiveness gate. Also agentKinds map misses return zero values silently (agent.go:69, 244) instead of erroring.
- **Impact:** Silent empty-kind Agents propagate to path resolution and keying (Unique(), Key()) with a bare 'codefly:' prefixless identity.
- **Required change:** Add an exhaustiveness test (every proto Kind either registered with full routing or explicitly rejected) and make agentKinds misses in LoadAgent/AgentFromProto return errors; include in the P1 foundation issue.
- **Acceptance test:** Test enumerating basev0.Agent_Kind values fails if any value lacks either a registration with complete routing answers or an explicit rejection entry; LoadAgent with JOB currently must error rather than return empty Kind.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 3, 4

#### ADV-108 — Lane 4 / RPC size bounds and cancellation

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Claim challenged:** Proposal: 'Observation returns canonical, bounded state' and ProviderContext carries deadlines — but no message-size bounds or repeated-field limits are specified for any provider RPC, and cancellation semantics are defined only for the accepted-mutation case.
- **Evidence:** provider-plugins.md specifies body caps only for the HTTP broker ('smaller default body caps for provider APIs'); nothing bounds the Observe/Plan/Apply gRPC messages the potentially compromised provider returns to the host; buf.validate is already used for field constraints in existing protos (core/proto/codefly/base/v0/agent.proto:31-33) so the mechanism exists.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A compromised provider returns a multi-GB Observe response or a plan with 10^6 actions; the host coordinator OOMs or hangs while holding the state lock, before any policy decision applies.
- **Impact:** Availability of the coordinator and lock starvation; trivially preventable in v0.
- **Required change:** Set explicit gRPC max receive sizes on the host connection to provider agents and buf.validate bounds on repeated fields (actions, resources, diagnostics, outputs) in provider.proto; define cancellation behavior for each phase (safe abort for Validate/Observe/Plan/Doctor/UpgradeState; checkpoint-then-UNCERTAIN for in-flight Apply actions).
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance provider returning an over-limit Observe payload is rejected with a typed failure and no state/lock corruption; cancellation during a brokered mutation yields UNCERTAIN with the last durable checkpoint.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 3, 4

#### ADV-109 — Lane 12 / callback endpoint identity

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Claim challenged:** Callback reference `service: auth-sidecar, endpoint: rest, path: /v1/billing/webhook` is a complete endpoint identity.
- **Evidence:** Endpoint identity in Codefly is module+service+endpoint (core/proto/codefly/base/v0/endpoint.proto:20-24 requires name, service, AND module); the starter contains auth-sidecar under two module trees (module-saas-starter/module/services/auth-sidecar and modules/saas-starter/services/auth-sidecar), and the deterministic port hash includes the module (core/network/port.go:117-126). Path ownership validation is feasible against RestRouteGroups (endpoint.proto:73-101) but the binding schema as shown cannot disambiguate the module.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** In a workspace with the same service name in two modules, resolution is ambiguous or silently picks the wrong module, producing a valid-looking but wrong callback port.
- **Impact:** Ergonomic/correctness gap in the binding schema; cheap to fix now, awkward after the schema is frozen.
- **Required change:** Add optional `module:` to the callback reference with a documented default (single-match rule that errors on ambiguity, mirroring cli/cmd/endpoint behavior at cli/cmd/endpoint/endpoint.go:65-76); require the path to match a declared RestRouteGroup of the target endpoint or be explicitly declared novel.
- **Acceptance test:** Binding validation errors with an ambiguity message when two modules contain the named service; path not present in the service's REST routes yields a warning naming the declared groups.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 11, 12

#### ADV-110 — stale existing-capability claim / duplicate builder (spot-check)

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (core/proto and SDK helpers)
- **Claim challenged:** 'Provider agents use a provider-specific advertisement helper. The existing `services.Advertisement` assumes Builder and Runtime and should not be stretched to mean external provider' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1038-1040).
- **Evidence:** core/agents/services/advertisement.go:24-25 already defines a `CapabilityOnly` mode that 'suppresses BUILDER/RUNTIME for plugins that expose only an' additional capability (used by capability-only plugins; see also lines 50-54 where BUILDER/RUNTIME are conditionally appended). The stated justification for a parallel helper is therefore factually stale. The workspace convention (CLAUDE.md) additionally mandates that every plugin builds AgentInformation through the core Advertisement builder and forbids hand-building AgentInformation.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A parallel provider-specific advertisement builder duplicates capability/backend assembly; the two builders drift (e.g., backend-resolution or catalog-digest inputs change in one), and manifest/catalog subset validation (744-746) compares catalogs produced by divergent code paths, yielding false mismatch failures or missed mismatches.
- **Impact:** Duplicate implementation and a violated repo-wide convention; low severity because the fix is a small extension either way.
- **Required change:** Extend services.Advertisement with the EXTERNAL_PROVIDER capability under CapabilityOnly (or an analogous mode) instead of a parallel helper, or correct 1038-1040 to justify the new helper on real grounds (e.g., provider-specific catalog fields) and require it to wrap, not replace, the core builder.
- **Acceptance test:** Exactly one code path in core assembles AgentInformation capabilities for all agent kinds including provider, and the provider conformance manifest/catalog comparison consumes it.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 24

#### ADV-111 — action vocabulary completeness (state-mutating operations without actions)

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (action vocabulary, admission wiring)
- **Claim challenged:** The canonical action table (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:395-403) covers every authorizable provider operation.
- **Evidence:** `UpgradeState` is an RPC (1054) that rewrites provider-owned state, 'produces before/after digests and a receipt' (1238-1240), yet has no action in the vocabulary, so its admission requirement is unspecified. Similarly the receipts example uses the operation name `provider.apply` (1190-1193) which the text explicitly says 'is not sufficient as an authority grant' (405-408) — correct in intent, but the vocabulary section never states which identifiers are operation names (receipts/dispatch) versus authority actions (admission), and `destroy`/`setup` composites likewise have no stated mapping.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** An implementer either invents `provider.upgrade-state` ad hoc (diverging vocabularies across host and manifest schemas) or runs state upgrades unadmitted; audit queries filtering by canonical action miss state rewrites.
- **Impact:** Vocabulary gap only; no unsafe behavior is mandated, but the permission matrix (581-601) cannot cover an unnamed action.
- **Required change:** Add the state-upgrade admission story (either a `provider.upgrade-state` action with low/medium risk, or an explicit statement that host-invoked offline upgrades are admitted under the dispatch operation with receipts) and add one sentence distinguishing operation names from authority actions.
- **Acceptance test:** Every RPC and CLI verb in the document maps to a named admission action or an explicit exemption, and the permission test matrix includes the upgrade path.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 24

#### ADV-112 — management-mode semantics vs high-risk import (clarity)

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (binding validation), P3 (Sentry, first observe-mode provider)
- **Claim challenged:** `observe` mode 'validates and reads remote state; can import or reference existing resources; projects configuration; cannot create, update, or delete remote objects' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:806-814).
- **Evidence:** IMPORT is a plan action (1123) and `provider.import` is risk 'high' (399) because it changes ownership state and future destroy scope; the brief's summary describes observe as 'inspect and project without remote mutation' (brief:214). Allowing a high-risk, plan-carried, approval-requiring action inside the mode positioned as the safe/read-only tier is not a remote-mutation contradiction (import mutates only local state) but blurs the mode boundary the rest of the document leans on (e.g., 'managed: supports plan/apply' at 816-818 implies observe does not plan/apply — yet import requires 'an explicit remote identity and a plan' at 1294-1295).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** An operator selects observe mode believing it cannot change anything Codefly tracks, imports a resource, and later destroy (after flipping to managed) includes a resource they believed was merely observed; or an implementer disables plan/apply machinery for observe-mode bindings, making the documented import capability unreachable.
- **Impact:** Semantics/clarity only; resolvable with one sentence.
- **Required change:** State that observe-mode bindings still run the plan pipeline restricted to IMPORT/PROJECT_OUTPUT/NOOP actions, that import in observe mode carries the same high-risk approval, and that imported-under-observe resources are deletable only after the mode is changed to managed.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance: an observe-mode binding accepts an IMPORT+PROJECT_OUTPUT plan with approval, rejects CREATE/UPDATE/DELETE plans at plan admission, and the mode table text matches that behavior.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 24

#### ADV-113 — Revocation and per-request re-check during long Apply

- **Severity:** LOW _(reported as HIGH; adjudicated down by the verification pass)_  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Verification:** REFUTED — Downgrade from HIGH to LOW: at most an editorial request to name, in the broker/P1 section, the concrete revocation mechanism (per-request PDP re-evaluation and/or TRL consultation in the broker) and to state a bound on positive-cache staleness; the normative behavior and the required test the finding demands are already in the proposal.
- **Claim challenged:** 'Revocation is checked at every brokered sub-operation and output commit rather than only when the agent is spawned … Long plans must tolerate a grant being revoked between actions: stop, checkpoint, emit receipts' (proposal:562-566).
- **Evidence:** There is no per-outbound-request authorization primitive. GatewayEvaluator.EvaluateAndMint mints one ScopedAuthorization per CallTool (gateway.go:109-200); the plugin-side ReplayTracker enforces MaxUses but performs no revocation lookup, and the TokenRevocationList (hardening.go:65-137) is consulted only inside core/toolbox/policyguard/guard.go — which providers do not use. SaasPDP never caches denies (pdp_saas.go comment 21-23; cache stores only allows at :195-200), so revocation IS visible IF the host re-evaluates per action — but that re-evaluation loop does not exist for providers.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Scenario 8: a human's grant is revoked after Plan but during a multi-action Apply. If the host minted one token/decision at dispatch with a TTL long enough to span Apply (proposal recommends short TTL but Apply of several webhooks can exceed it or reuse one decision), remaining actions proceed because nothing re-checks revocation between actions.
- **Impact:** Revoked authority continues to perform remote mutations until token expiry; violates invariants 5, 8, 26.
- **Required change:** Specify that Apply re-invokes the PDP (SaasPDP or gateway EvaluateAndMint, both of which see revocations because denies are uncached and the callback fails closed) before each mutating action and each output/secret commit, and consults the TokenRevocationList; on deny/revocation it stops, checkpoints, and emits a partial/denied receipt. This is NEW host logic, not reuse.
- **Acceptance test:** Integration test: revoke the principal's grant (backend Decide flips to deny; TRL.Add(tokenID)) after action 1 of a 3-action plan; assert actions 2-3 do not execute, an UNCERTAIN/partial receipt is written, and no further vendor request is sent.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 5, Lane 2 (policy)

#### ADV-114 — Safe policy-log/receipt evidence

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Claim challenged:** 'Every authorization decision should expose safe correlation fields … policy decision path without tokens, credentials, raw provider bodies, or secret outputs' (proposal:568-581).
- **Evidence:** SaasPDP.failClosedDecision embeds err.Error() verbatim into PDPDecision.Reason (pdp_saas.go:297-311) and RecordDecision logs decision_reason verbatim (observability.go:195). Principal.AsIdentity copies the raw credential into the identity map under 'principal_token' (principal.go:211-213), which flows through PDPRequest.Identity.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** If provider vendor error text is mapped into a PDP deny Reason, or if the provider's identity map is logged, a bearer token or vendor error body could reach policy logs/receipts.
- **Impact:** Potential secret/PII leakage into audit surfaces.
- **Required change:** Specify that provider→PDP denial reasons carry only neutral codes (never vendor bodies), and that receipts/logs redact principal_token and never include provider request/response bodies. Add a test asserting no secret-shaped field appears in any DecisionEvent or receipt for provider operations.
- **Acceptance test:** Log-scrub test over a full provider Apply asserting no token/DSN/webhook-secret/vendor-body substring appears in policy logs, metrics labels, or receipts.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 5, Lane 2 (policy)

#### ADV-115 — Lane 1 — management-mode semantics
