- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (docs/CLI), P2
- **Claim challenged:** 'observe' mode 'can import or reference existing resources' while being the no-remote-mutation mode.
- **Evidence:** core/docs/provider-plugins.md:811-815; import is separately classified as a high-risk action (provider.import, :399) that claims ownership and mutates local state and future destroy scope.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** An operator reasons 'observe mode cannot change anything', imports a resource, later flips the binding to managed, and destroy now targets a resource adopted while 'observing'; the mode name promised passivity that the ownership ledger did not honor.
- **Impact:** Semantic blur in the operator mental model; no remote mutation occurs, so severity is low.
- **Required change:** Either restrict provider.import to managed mode, or document explicitly that observe-mode import changes ownership/destroy scope and surface it in the plan as a high-risk local action.
- **Acceptance test:** Plan output for an observe-mode import displays an ownership-change warning and requires the same approval as managed-mode import.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 1, 23

#### ADV-116 — Lane 23 — managed-service boundary

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P0 (non-goals), post-GA
- **Claim challenged:** The provider abstraction can eventually cover object storage and managed Postgres ('object storage/cloud database: decide where external provider plugins end and environment managed-service deployment begins').
- **Evidence:** core/docs/provider-plugins.md:2185-2187 defers the decision; provider projections are configuration values only (:915-931) and providers have 'no application endpoint' (:38-40); a managed database is a service-graph dependency whose endpoint other services must resolve, which only service agents provide today (agent kinds in core/proto/codefly/base/v0/agent.proto:12-26).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** If a future team implements 'provider-postgres', application services would consume a database through env-var projection instead of graph endpoints, bypassing dependency ordering, readiness, and endpoint resolution — corrupting exactly the runtime semantics the proposal protects by excluding providers from the graph.
- **Impact:** Scope-creep trap rather than current defect; the honest answer is that managed data services are not providers.
- **Required change:** Promote the deferred question to a stated non-goal with the answer: capabilities that introduce a service-graph endpoint belong to service/environment machinery, not codefly:provider; a provider may at most observe/project credentials for an externally managed instance.
- **Acceptance test:** Non-goals section names the graph-endpoint criterion as the boundary test for future provider candidates.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 1, 23

#### ADV-117 — Lane 1 — naming

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 docs
- **Claim challenged:** 'provider' is the right name despite existing 'secret provider' terminology and Terraform's different provider semantics.
- **Evidence:** core/docs/provider-plugins.md:137-158 defines a normative glossary separating external provider agent from secret provider; Terraform providers are resource-type factories over a resource language (https://developer.hashicorp.com/terraform/plugin/framework: providers are 'the top level abstraction that define the available resources and data sources'), whereas a Codefly provider binding is a configured integration instance with projection.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Engineers arriving from Terraform expect resource blocks and a state graph; docs saying 'provider' for both Codefly bindings and op:// secret backends invite support confusion, but the glossary and 'external provider' prefix mitigate it.
- **Impact:** Residual documentation-discipline cost only.
- **Required change:** No material finding: accept the name; enforce the 'external provider' long form in user docs and CLI help where secret backends are nearby.
- **Acceptance test:** CLI help for `codefly provider` and secret-backend docs cross-link and disambiguate the two meanings.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 1, 23

#### ADV-118 — Lane 18 — metrics label/cardinality contract undefined until P5

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (contract), P5 (implementation)
- **Claim challenged:** P5 delivers 'metrics for provider operation count/latency/failure/drift without secret or tenant-cardinality leaks' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md, P5 deliverables).
- **Evidence:** The existing baseline is safe by construction — PDPMetrics is a label-free in-process counter set (core/policy/observability.go:25-100) — but the proposal names the non-goal (no cardinality leaks) without a label contract, and provider operations naturally tempt labels like binding name, workspace hash, remote resource ID, or vendor account ID.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** P5 implementer adds a per-binding, per-remote-id latency histogram; a workspace with many bindings/resources explodes series cardinality, and account IDs/binding names (which users often name after customers) leak tenant identity into the metrics backend.
- **Impact:** Metrics backend cost/da­mage and tenant-identifying leakage; cheap to prevent now, expensive to strip later.
- **Required change:** Fix the label allowlist in the P1 design doc even though implementation is P5: {provider name, action, outcome, FailureCode, protocol version}. Explicitly forbid binding names, workspace hashes, environment names beyond a coarse class (local/prod), remote IDs, and account IDs as metric labels; anything per-binding belongs in receipts, not metrics.
- **Acceptance test:** Metrics unit test asserts the exported series label keys are exactly the allowlist; a fixture with 50 bindings produces no additional label values.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 16, 17, 18

#### ADV-119 — Lane 17 — command surface larger than necessary

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Claim challenged:** The 13-command surface (list, show, schema, add, setup, validate, observe, plan, apply, doctor, import, disconnect, destroy) is the right operator surface (core/docs/provider-plugins.md 'Commands').
- **Evidence:** show ≡ list BINDING; schema is a read-only projection of GetProviderInformation reachable as a flag; add is subsumed by first-run setup (which must handle the binding-absent case anyway); validate and observe are phases of plan that CI selects — five of thirteen commands duplicate reachable behavior. The existing CLI precedent (doctor) composes modes via flags (--json, --service, --timeout) rather than commands.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** No hard failure; discoverability and doc surface cost: founders face 13 verbs where 8 suffice, and each extra command needs its own JSON contract, exit codes, and doc page kept stable forever.
- **Impact:** Permanent maintenance and documentation surface; larger stable-contract area to keep compatible.
- **Required change:** Adopt the 8-command surface detailed in the 'special' artifact: list (absorbs show, --schema), setup (absorbs add, adds --dry-run), plan (absorbs validate via --validate-only and observe via --refresh-only), apply, doctor, import, disconnect, destroy — keeping the two destructive verbs distinct per invariant 23.
- **Acceptance test:** CLI integration tests demonstrate every workflow from the proposal's 13-command list (including CI validate-only and observe-only) is expressible in the 8-command surface with deterministic JSON and documented exit codes.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 16, 17, 18

#### ADV-120 — Broker request normalization details required by Resend

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (broker), P4
- **Claim challenged:** Proposal's broker normalizes 'request IDs, retry-after, and rate-limit metadata' (brief summary) and the Resend acceptance maps 429 to retryable+retry-after (provider-plugins.md:1811).
- **Evidence:** Confirmed feasible: Resend documents ratelimit-limit, ratelimit-remaining, ratelimit-reset, and retry-after headers per IETF draft-ietf-httpapi-ratelimit-headers-06, default 10 req/s per team across ALL keys (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/rate-limit). Also: 'All requests require a User-Agent header; omitting it results in a 403 error' (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/introduction), and the 10 req/s budget is shared between provider management traffic and the application's production email sending on the same team.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A broker that strips or omits User-Agent gets blanket 403s misdiagnosed as credential failure; aggressive Observe pagination consumes the same team budget the production app uses to send email, so a setup run can rate-limit live sending.
- **Impact:** Operability: confusing failures and possible production email throttling during provider operations.
- **Required change:** Broker sends a stable Codefly User-Agent; Resend provider honors ratelimit-remaining with a conservative concurrency of 1 and bounded pagination; document that management traffic shares the team sending budget.
- **Acceptance test:** Cassette asserts every brokered Resend request carries the Codefly User-Agent; conformance asserts sequential (non-concurrent) pagination with backoff on retry-after.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 15

#### ADV-121 — Script-to-shim migration inherits unsafe key sharing

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P4 (migration deliverable)
- **Claim challenged:** Proposal: 'migrate scripts/setup/resend.sh to a shim' (provider-plugins.md:2133) after plugin parity.
- **Evidence:** module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/resend.sh:230 projects the single RESEND_API_KEY — the same key that just performed webhook management (resend.sh:167-209) — into email.secret.env as the runtime key; the script has no full_access/sending_access concept anywhere. The proposal's plugin inputs correctly separate 'full-access management credential handle' from 'sending-only runtime credential handle or explicit shared-key opt-in' (provider-plugins.md:1740-1741).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A 'parity' shim that maps the script's single --api-key onto both plugin credential inputs silently converts today's unsafe behavior into an approved-looking plugin run without the explicit allow-shared-credential opt-in surfacing in plan and receipt (provider-plugins.md:905-907).
- **Impact:** Migration quietly launders an over-privileged runtime credential through the new system, defeating the credential-separation upgrade that motivates the Resend provider.
- **Required change:** Shim mapping must translate a single supplied key into management-credential + explicit allow-shared-credential opt-in (visible in plan/receipt), and print the sending-key remediation; parity is behavioral, not flag-for-flag.
- **Acceptance test:** Running the shim with one key produces a plan and receipt that both record shared-credential opt-in, and doctor flags the shared key with create-sending-key remediation.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 15

#### ADV-122 — doctor diagnostic-code namespace collision

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (doctor integration)
- **Claim challenged:** Provider doctor sections will add namespaced codes such as provider.auth.rejected, provider.config.missing (provider-plugins.md:1561-1575).
- **Evidence:** The existing doctor workspace JSON contract already uses provider_* codes meaning secret providers: codeProviderNotConfigured, codeProviderExecutableMissing, codeProviderAuthRequired, codeProviderResolutionFailed (cli/cmd/doctor_workspace.go:38-41), under a versioned schema whose code renames are declared breaking (doctor_workspace.go:25-28).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Automation matching on codes sees 'provider_authentication_required' (secret backend: run `op signin`) next to 'provider.auth.rejected' (Stripe key invalid) — two unrelated remediation paths under one operator-facing vocabulary; the proposal itself defines 'secret provider' as a distinct term (provider-plugins.md:149) but the machine contract does not reflect the distinction.
- **Impact:** Operator/automation confusion and a future breaking rename once the collision is noticed after codes are frozen.
- **Required change:** Before freezing provider diagnostic codes in P1, namespace them distinctly (e.g. external-provider.* or binding.*) or migrate the secret-backend codes under the versioned schema bump that P1 already touches.
- **Acceptance test:** doctor JSON schema review shows no code string where the 'provider' token is ambiguous between secret backends and external provider bindings.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 2 (non-policy)

#### ADV-123 — Lane 6 - orphaned permission-callback sockets and predictable socket naming

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 foundation (provider session lifecycle) or an independent core cleanup
- **Claim challenged:** core/agents/manager/loader.go:582-585 documents 'Orphaned sockets only happen on host crash; restart cleans them up'.
- **Evidence:** The sweeper removes only directories prefixed 'codefly-uds-' whose owner PID is dead (core/agents/manager/sockets.go:34-65, prefix check at line 44). The callback socket is a bare file named codefly-perms-<pid>-<unixnano>.sock created directly in os.TempDir() (core/policy/callback.go:152-156) with 0600 (167-172), removed only on Close; nothing sweeps that prefix.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** After an ungraceful CLI exit stale codefly-perms-*.sock files accumulate in /tmp indefinitely, leaking PID and timestamp metadata and confusing diagnostics, so the documented cleanup claim is false. The name uses PID plus nanotime rather than crypto-random, making the path guessable by any same-user process - mitigated by 0600, by the sandbox's narrow unix-socket grant, and by the callback binding the spawn-time principal as the authoritative subject (core/agents/manager/loader.go:836-843).
- **Impact:** Operational untidiness plus a documentation inaccuracy in a security-relevant file; no authorization bypass, since the decider and bound principal remain the ceiling.
- **Required change:** Extend SweepStaleAgentSockets to remove stale codefly-perms-<pid>-*.sock files with dead owners, or place the callback socket inside the per-spawn 0700 UDS directory so the existing sweeper covers it; use crypto/rand for the name; correct the comment; and surface stale counts in doctor (CountStaleAgentSockets already exists, core/agents/manager/sockets.go:69).
- **Acceptance test:** Create a codefly-perms file attributed to a dead PID, run the sweeper, assert removal; assert a live process's socket is untouched; assert the provider session places its callback socket under the per-spawn 0700 directory.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 6, 7

#### ADV-124 — Fingerprints

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Claim challenged:** 'Safe fingerprint' of secrets can be recorded in state, plans, and observation.
- **Evidence:** Proposal uses 'safe fingerprint' throughout (state contents, capture step 5, output fields) without defining the construction. No fingerprint code exists in core/configurations/ or core/executionreceipt/.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** If implemented as an unkeyed hash (SHA-256 of the secret), any state/receipt reader can confirm a guessed secret offline; for low-entropy secrets (user-chosen values, short tokens) this enables practical brute force from a non-secret artifact that is deliberately widely visible.
- **Impact:** State/receipts/plans become an oracle for secret confirmation, weakening the 'no secrets in state' invariant at the margin.
- **Required change:** Define fingerprint as HMAC-SHA-256 with a per-workspace random key stored beside provider state (0600), truncated; document that fingerprints compare only within one workspace.
- **Acceptance test:** Unit test: fingerprints of identical secrets differ across workspaces; state files contain no unkeyed digest of any test secret.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 8, 9

#### ADV-125 — Cassette leak response and hidden input

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1/P2
- **Claim challenged:** Cassette sanitization ('fail if a secret-shaped field remains') plus review prevents live secrets in Git; hidden interactive input is an existing safe source.
- **Evidence:** Proposal Tier 1 cassette rules rely on secret-shape detection and review; no rotation/incident runbook exists anywhere in the document. Repo has no hidden-prompt primitive: no term.ReadPassword usage in cli (only terminal raw-mode for the TUI, cli/cmd/terminal.go:129; plain prompt in cli/pkg/cli/communicate/prompt.go); today's hidden input lives in bash (`read -r -s`, module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/stripe.sh:206). Only one LLM cassette exists in the workspace (cli/cmd/codefly-help/main_test.go:74-89); the broker recorder/sanitizer is entirely new.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A Sentry-style token with no recognizable prefix passes the secret-shape heuristic, is committed in a cassette, and there is no defined response: no rotation step, no history purge procedure, no ownership. Separately, P1 estimates 'hidden prompting' as reuse when it is new (small) Go work.
- **Impact:** Slow or absent response to a real leak in Git history; minor scope underestimate for P1.
- **Required change:** Drive the sanitizer primarily from manifest-declared secret fields and injected-header positions (deterministic), keep the shape heuristic as backstop, add gitleaks-style scanning in CI, and write a leak runbook (rotate first, then purge). Add term.ReadPassword-based hidden prompt to the host as named P1 work.
- **Acceptance test:** Recorder test: a response containing an unprefixed 40-hex token declared in the manifest as secret is replaced with a typed placeholder; CI scan fails a deliberately poisoned cassette.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 8, 9

#### ADV-126 — Build-token (org:ci) scope verification is not implementable as written

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P3 (Sentry), live acceptance tier
- **Claim challenged:** Sentry acceptance requires 'optional build token requires org:ci policy' and 'wrong region, project, or scope is diagnosed' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1725-1731), implying the provider/doctor can verify a supplied build token's scopes.
- **Evidence:** org:ci is confirmed as the documented scope 'for CI and deployment workflows, including uploading source maps, creating releases, and managing code mappings' (https://docs.sentry.io/api/permissions/). But the starter expects an Organization Auth Token (sntrys_ prefix, module-saas-starter/configurations/local-dogfood/error-tracking.secret.env.example) and official docs state org tokens have a fixed, non-customizable 'limited set of permissions', are shown only once, and cannot have scopes edited (https://docs.sentry.io/account/auth-tokens/); no documented endpoint enumerates an arbitrary token's effective scopes. Whether an sntrys_ org token can even perform the project:read GETs the setup path uses is not documented on the pages consulted (open question; the current script projects the same token it used for discovery, sentry.sh:161,203, so dogfood evidence conflates the two purposes).
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Doctor's 'credential scope diagnostics' for the build token either silently passes an under-scoped token (build later fails deep inside next.config.mjs source-map upload) or the provider attempts a mutation (creating a throwaway release) to probe scopes, violating the read-only doctor invariant.
- **Impact:** Unverifiable acceptance criterion; degraded diagnosability for the one credential whose failure surfaces latest (at release build time).
- **Required change:** Define the qualification method explicitly: accept token-type evidence (sntrys_ prefix implies the fixed CI permission set) plus at most a read-only probe, and record 'scope unverifiable without mutation' as a first-class doctor result; qualify in live acceptance whether org tokens can serve the project:read discovery path at all, since that determines whether setup and build tokens can ever be the same artifact.
- **Acceptance test:** Live acceptance run with (a) an sntrys_ org token and (b) a personal token with org:ci: doctor reports token type, purpose fit, and verifiability honestly in both cases without performing any remote mutation.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 14

#### ADV-127 — Agent package deletion protection

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1/P5
- **Claim challenged:** 'Deleting the agent package while active bindings or resources remain should be blocked or loudly diagnosed' (provider-plugins.md:1316-1318).
- **Evidence:** No mechanism is specified; nothing links installed agents under ~/.codefly/agents/providers/ to state directories under ~/.codefly/providers/<sha>/ (which are keyed by workspace path hash and thus not enumerable per agent without scanning all state roots). Crossplane lesson cited at provider-plugins.md:205-206.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** User uninstalls provider-stripe while a binding owns a live webhook; later destroy/disconnect cannot run; state and remote resource are stranded until the exact agent version is reinstalled — and nothing tells the user which version that was except a field inside state they cannot easily read.
- **Impact:** Recoverable but confusing orphaned-resource situations; 'should be' language will not survive implementation triage.
- **Required change:** Schedule concretely (P1 or P5): agent uninstall scans provider state roots for references to the agent identity and requires --force with an explicit list of affected bindings; `codefly provider list` surfaces bindings whose agent is not installed.
- **Acceptance test:** Uninstalling an agent referenced by any binding's state fails with the binding list; --force succeeds but doctor subsequently reports the missing-agent binding.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 10

#### ADV-128 — Secret fingerprints and support-bundle safety

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Claim challenged:** State stores 'secret sink references and safe fingerprints' (provider-plugins.md:1271) and support bundles are safe (provider-plugins.md:2165).
- **Evidence:** 'Safe fingerprint' is never defined (also provider-plugins.md:925). A plain unsalted sha256 of a secret is an offline verification oracle: anyone holding a candidate secret (or brute-forcing a low-entropy one) can confirm it against state, receipts, or a support bundle. Webhook signing secrets are high-entropy so practical risk is low, but the contract does not restrict fingerprints to high-entropy values.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A support bundle containing plain-hash fingerprints lets a recipient confirm guessed or leaked-elsewhere secret values, weakening 'attributable without exposing secrets' (brief invariant 34).
- **Impact:** Marginal information leak; cheap to prevent now, awkward to rotate later since fingerprints are persisted in immutable receipts.
- **Required change:** Define fingerprint = truncated HMAC-SHA256 with a per-workspace random key stored only in the state root (excluded from support bundles), or truncated hash of (random salt || secret) with the salt kept out of bundles.
- **Acceptance test:** Given a state export and a support bundle plus knowledge of the true secret, an offline attacker cannot confirm the secret matches the recorded fingerprint without the workspace fingerprint key.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 10

#### ADV-129 — Webhook endpoint quota and shared-sandbox hygiene

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P2
- **Claim challenged:** Proposal acceptance (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1652-1664) covers cleanup and dedicated-sandbox isolation but nowhere accounts for Stripe's endpoint quota.
- **Evidence:** 'You can register up to 16 webhook endpoints on Stripe' (https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks). Multiple developers sharing one sandbox (brief scenario 19) each provisioning a public endpoint, plus duplicates accumulated from failed recoveries (finding on scenario 38), consume the cap.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** The 17th create returns an error mid-Apply for a reason unrelated to the plan; without a quota-aware diagnostic the failure maps to a generic vendor error and the remediation ('delete stale Codefly-stamped endpoints') is not surfaced.
- **Impact:** Operability annoyance with a confusing failure mode in exactly the shared-sandbox dogfood setup the project promotes.
- **Required change:** Doctor's remote section reports endpoint count vs the 16 cap and flags Codefly-stamped endpoints not present in any local state; map the quota error to a specific diagnostic code with that remediation.
- **Acceptance test:** Cassette test: a create response representing the quota error maps to a `provider.remote.quota-exceeded`-style diagnostic naming the 16-endpoint limit and listing stamped stale endpoints from the preceding Observe.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 13

#### ADV-130 — Script-to-plugin deltas (migration accuracy)

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P2 migration
- **Claim challenged:** Proposal claims the script lifecycle maps cleanly into the plugin (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1933-1976) — broadly true, but three concrete deltas are unstated.
- **Evidence:** (1) stripe.sh:135-136 accepts only `^sk_test_[A-Za-z0-9_]+$`, which rejects legitimate rk_test_ restricted management keys the proposal itself recommends; (2) stripe.sh:167-174 effectively adopts an existing endpoint by URL match (requires the operator's secret but takes the endpoint as satisfied) — under the proposal this exact situation must instead become an unmanaged conflict plus explicit import (provider-plugins.md:1658-1661, 1961-1963); (3) stripe.sh sets no metadata/idempotency-key/api_version on create, so script-created endpoints carry no ownership stamp and adoption during migration can only proceed by explicit endpoint-ID import, never stamp matching. Also: the script's error message 'Stripe cannot reveal its signing secret' (stripe.sh:174) is API-accurate but operationally wrong — Workbench can reveal endpoint secrets ('Click to reveal', https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks), which is the correct MANUAL_ACTION remediation.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** Migration tooling that assumes stamp-based recognition finds nothing to recognize on script-created endpoints; shim-era users with restricted keys are rejected by the legacy regex; operators are told a recoverable secret is unrecoverable and mint needless replacements.
- **Impact:** Minor but real friction and one incorrect remediation message inherited into the plugin if copied verbatim.
- **Required change:** State in the P2 migration notes: script-created endpoints are import-by-ID only; host key classification must accept rk_test_/rk_live_ alongside sk_ prefixes; replace 'cannot reveal' remediation with the Dashboard-reveal MANUAL_ACTION.
- **Acceptance test:** Migration test: a state-adoption run against a script-provisioned sandbox proposes import by endpoint ID with the existing secret reference, and the missing-secret path prints the Workbench reveal instruction rather than declaring the secret unrecoverable.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lane 13

#### ADV-131 — migration-compatibility

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** HIGH  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1 (binding schema), documented in P2 migration
- **Claim challenged:** Provider bindings are added to environment configuration without stated old-CLI behavior.
- **Evidence:** All resource YAML loads through permissive yaml.v3 Unmarshal (core/resources/marshalling.go:62-69, LoadFromBytes); no KnownFields/strict decoding anywhere in core/resources (rg). An older CLI reading a workspace with provider-bindings silently drops the field.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** A teammate on an older CLI opens the workspace: no error, no doctor warning — billing/email/error-tracking configuration is simply absent or stale, and 'codefly run' proceeds with fail-closed defaults; the developer debugs the product instead of the version skew.
- **Impact:** Confusing degraded behavior during rollout; support burden. (Silent-ignore is at least safe: no misinterpretation, no remote effect.)
- **Required change:** Add a minimum-CLI-version marker alongside provider-bindings (or a workspace feature declaration) that new CLIs write and old doctors already surface, or document the skew in the migration guide with a doctor check in the new CLI that reports bindings invisible to older versions.
- **Acceptance test:** An older CLI build running 'codefly doctor workspace' against a bindings-bearing workspace produces at least a visible unknown-feature warning (or the migration doc explicitly records the silent-ignore behavior and the new CLI's doctor lists bindings with the required minimum version).
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 19, 20, 21, 22

#### ADV-132 — testing-enforcement-criteria

- **Severity:** LOW  **Confidence:** MEDIUM  **Protocol-breaking if deferred:** no  **Affected phase/issues:** P1
- **Claim challenged:** Tier 1 replays 'through the Codefly broker' and destroy 'deletes only resources allowed by delete-owned' via exact identity.
- **Evidence:** provider-plugins.md:1841-1843 (replay through broker), 448-451 (broker re-check authorizes 'exact origin, method, path template'), 478-481 (handle binds 'allowed method and path templates'). Path templates admit any resource ID; exactness comes only from the plan-action resource — the host must derive the concrete request path (e.g. DELETE /v1/webhook_endpoints/{approved-id}) from the approved resource rather than trusting the provider's descriptor, and broker enforcement must be proven active during cassette replay. Both are implied, neither is an acceptance criterion.
- **Failure or attack sequence:** An implementation satisfies the letter of the spec by template-matching the provider-supplied path during Apply; a buggy/compromised provider substitutes a different resource ID within the approved template and deletes an unplanned resource (scenario-34 adjacent). Separately, a replay harness that bypasses broker checks 'for determinism' certifies enforcement that never ran.
- **Impact:** The exact-resource guarantee and the replay-with-enforcement guarantee silently weaken to template granularity / no enforcement.
- **Required change:** Add two acceptance criteria to the foundation issue: (1) for plan-bound mutations the broker constructs or verifies the concrete path from the approved resource ID; a descriptor with a mismatched ID is denied and receipted; (2) Tier-1 replay executes the identical broker policy code path, proven by a replay test where an out-of-policy recorded request is rejected at replay time.
- **Acceptance test:** Conformance: provider submits DELETE for template-valid but unapproved ID → broker denies, receipt records caveat-mismatch; cassette replay of a redirect-to-foreign-origin recording fails at replay with the same error class as live.
- **Lane/reviewer:** Lanes 19, 20, 21, 22

## 5. Factual corrections

Inaccurate capability claims, stale vendor claims, inconsistent examples, and broken references — listed independently of severity. Parenthetical labels name the reviewing lane group.

- **(review brief)** The brief's repository map designates `module-saas-starter-six-integrations/` as the authoritative merged starter reference and instructs reviewers to prefer it over `module-saas-starter/`, which it warns "may be behind." **That directory does not exist in the workspace.** The six-integrations work is already merged into `module-saas-starter` main (PR #70, merge commit `8706324`, the current HEAD), so the warning is inverted: the ordinary worktree *is* the merged reference. All starter citations in this report were normalized and spot-verified at `87063240faab` (see the note under the pinned-revision table). Fix the brief's repository map before it is reused for another review.

- (product-abstraction) The motivation claim that founders today must 'manually copy dynamic local ports' (brief 'What Codefly wants' list; echoed in the proposal's why-now narrative) is inaccurate for the current merged scripts: they resolve the callback origin through `codefly endpoint auth-sidecar --type rest` (module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/provider-common.sh:104-114; workos.sh:187-191) and validate it. The pain existed historically but is not the current baseline being replaced.
- (product-abstraction) The Rejected Alternatives claim that scripts 'duplicate credential handling, HTTP policy, endpoint resolution ... doctor' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:2302-2306) is overstated intra-starter: all eight scripts source one shared implementation, provider-common.sh (190 lines), for exactly those behaviors. The duplication argument is valid only across starters/modules; within the starter the lifecycle is already factored once.
- (product-abstraction) The proposal's implicit claim that current scripts keep management credentials out of runtime is the reverse of reality, which strengthens (not weakens) its case and should be cited as evidence: sentry.sh:199-204 writes the setup SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN into error-tracking.secret.env consumed at runtime; stripe.sh:219 writes the management-capable sk_test key as runtime STRIPE_API_KEY; workos.sh:346-349 projects one API key as both IDENTITY_CLIENT_SECRET and IDENTITY_MANAGEMENT_API_KEY.
- (product-abstraction) Internal contradiction (also filed as a finding): 'a provider-manifest-derived exact origin allowlist' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:649, manifest api-origins :707-708) versus user-supplied API bases accepted as v1 inputs for all three providers (:1583, :1673, :1739) and the P3 deliverable 'regional/self-hosted API base handling' (:2095).
- (product-abstraction) Internal contradiction (also filed as a finding): 'provider-specific signature algorithms ... remain in the provider agent' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1443-1444) is unsatisfiable together with the credential-handle rule that the provider never holds raw secret bytes (:465-484), for any signature computed over the request with the secret.
- (reuse-audit) provider-plugins.md:658-662 states 'The existing Web toolbox supplies most of the HTTP guard behavior.' Incorrect: toolbox-web/server.go contains only an exact-hostname allowlist (207-210), redirect hostname recheck (76-85), a 30s timeout and 4MiB body cap (21-26, 251-260); it passes caller-supplied headers including Authorization verbatim (234-240), enforces no method/path policy (149), uses the proxy-honoring default transport, and contains no credential injection, idempotency, rate-limit normalization, sanitization, or cassette code. The broker is new host-side code; toolbox-web contributes a pattern, not an implementation.
- (reuse-audit) provider-plugins.md:260 reuse row 'cassette testing | core testing guidance | record real external API responses and replay deterministically'. No cassette/VCR infrastructure exists anywhere in core, cli, or toolbox-web source (rg for cassette/govcr/go-vcr across *.go finds nothing); github.com/dnaeon/go-vcr v1.2.0 appears only in cli/go.sum as an unused transitive entry (no Go file imports it). The recorder, replayer, and sanitizer are entirely new.
- (reuse-audit) provider-plugins.md:257 reuse row 'execution receipts | executionreceipt, CLI execution journal/runtime'. The library exists (core/executionreceipt/receipt.go; core/proto/codefly/execution/v1/execution.proto with UNCERTAIN/COMPENSATED stages and operation_id/attempt_id), but the only production wiring is the gateway daemon behind the opt-in --governed-execution flag with mandatory authority JWKS/issuer flags (cli/cmd/daemon_execution.go:15-45). Ordinary CLI commands emit no receipts today; provider commands need new producer/attestor wiring.
- (reuse-audit) provider-plugins.md:245 reuse row 'agent release discovery … OCI digest verification'. Accurate only for the OCI store (core/agents/manager/store.go:189-242, digest from the registry's own manifest — transfer integrity, not pinned identity). The GitHub release fallback — the default when Nix/OCI env vars are unset — downloads and extracts with no verification (core/agents/manager/downloader.go:58-80) and is auto-invoked inside manager.Load (loader.go:675-707). Additionally the GitHub repo mapping hardcodes 'service-<name>' for every agent kind (downloader.go:30).
- (reuse-audit) provider-plugins.md:243/1036 'add PROVIDER / codefly:provider routing … include provider kind in path and inventory switches' understates the state of routing: the kind->directory switch exists in at least four mutually inconsistent copies — core/resources/agent.go:160-177 (has toolboxes), core/agents/manager/manager.go:80-86 (FindLocalLatest lacks toolboxes, silently defaults to modules/), cli/cmd/agents/build.go:514-519 (lacks toolboxes, defaults to services/), cli/cmd/agents/versions.go:498-502 (has toolboxes). The previously added kind (toolbox) never received complete routing, demonstrating the inventory task is error-prone without centralization.
- (reuse-audit) provider-plugins.md:1399 'Sandbox: provider has no workspace writes and external network is denied' is enforceable as written only on Linux/bwrap. The macOS backend is allow-default on reads with four deny-listed secret roots and describes its read policy as 'best-effort hardening, not a security boundary on its own' (core/runners/sandbox/sandboxexec.go:98-147, 176-177); the workspace, ~/.codefly, and sibling temp dirs are readable, and NetworkLoopback permits all localhost ports (sandboxexec.go:142-144). Production admission rejects only BackendNative (core/agents/manager/loader.go:610-615), so sandbox-exec passes as 'enforcing'.
- (reuse-audit) provider-plugins.md:747 'This mirrors toolbox manifest-ceiling validation' — confirmed accurate: resources.Toolbox.ValidateToolCatalog rejects advertised tools not covered by manifest permissions (core/resources/toolbox.go:199-218), and ValidateForProduction rejects empty sandbox/permissions blocks (toolbox.go:186-197).
- (reuse-audit) provider-plugins.md:969 'The existing configuration loader is read-only' — confirmed accurate: core/configurations exposes no write/save functions (rg for 'func (Write|Save|Persist)' returns nothing); the projection writer is genuinely new, as the proposal states.
- (reuse-audit) provider-plugins.md:1246-1248 state-location claim confirmed: the workspace-sha isolation pattern exists at cli/pkg/executionruntime/runtime.go:63-80 (DefaultStateDir: sha256 of canonical workspace path under ~/.codefly/execution/gateways/), and journal privacy hardening at cli/pkg/executionjournal/journal.go:82-111 — but its helpers (ensurePrivateDirectory, rejectUnsafeFile) are unexported and must be extracted or duplicated for provider state.
- (reuse-audit) provider-plugins.md:1041-1043 'The existing services.Advertisement assumes Builder and Runtime and should not be stretched' — confirmed: core/agents/services/advertisement.go builds Builder/Runtime-capability AgentInformation; a provider-specific advertisement helper is correctly scoped as new.
- (agentkind-protocol) Protocol version numbering is internally inconsistent: the provider manifest example declares `protocol-version: 1` (provider-plugins.md, 'Provider manifest' YAML) while the proto file is codefly/services/provider/v0/provider.proto, the RPC table is titled 'the proposed v1 surface', and the roadmap says P2 'freezes protocol v0' / 'provider v0 is frozen by Stripe'. Pick one scheme (proto package v0, manifest protocol-version integer matching it) before P1.
- (agentkind-protocol) Reuse-table row 'agent release discovery — GitHub/OCI/Nix stores — OCI digest verification' overstates existing integrity: only the OCI blob path verifies a sha256 against its (mutable-tag) manifest (core/agents/manager/store.go:157-189); the GitHub path performs no checksum or signature verification at all (core/agents/manager/downloader.go:59-70, extractTarGz:156-226; ValidURL:34-43 checks only host==github.com), and no artifact digest is recorded or re-verified at load time (no digest handling in core/agents/manager/loader.go).
- (agentkind-protocol) Reuse-table row 'agent build and local install — route provider binaries to agents/providers' implies existing kind-aware install routing; in fact `codefly agent build` routes every kind except application/module into agents/services/ (cli/cmd/agents/build.go:514-519), and on this host toolbox binaries (web__*, nix__*) are consequently installed under agents/services/codefly.dev while resources.Agent.Path() expects agents/toolboxes/ (core/resources/agent.go:168-169) — the claimed routing pattern to extend is itself currently inconsistent.
- (agentkind-protocol) The proposal's 'Agent information' section says to add 'provider routing in agent path, local latest resolution, build, list, versions, publish, and CI' but omits the four distribution-naming sites that also hardcode service-: GitHub repo naming service-<name> (core/agents/manager/downloader.go:30), release asset naming service-%s_… (core/agents/manager/downloader_url.go:17), OCI single-layer naming service-{name} (store.go:40-46), and Nix bin/service-{name} (store.go:438); it also omits that the OCI repository path agents/{publisher}/{name} (store.go:85-87) has no kind segment.
- (agentkind-protocol) The statement that the packaged provider.codefly.yaml 'is packaged with the release' describes future work as if the release format supported it: current release artifacts in every store are a bare single binary with no manifest slot; even toolbox manifests exist only in source checkouts (core/resources/toolbox.go:108-131 LoadToolboxFromDir).
- (agentkind-protocol) Minor: the proposal cites `agent.v0.Capability.EXTERNAL_PROVIDER` as an addition; the current Capability.Type enum (core/proto/codefly/services/agent/v0/agent.proto:50-62) ends at EXECUTION_EXPORTER = 4, so the addition is value 5 — additive and compatible, but note providers implementing the generic Agent service will advertise through AgentInformation whose remaining fields (backends, toolchains, protocols, validation) are service-shaped and should be absent/ignored for providers, consistent with the proposal's own advice not to stretch services.Advertisement.
- (permissions) Reuse table row 'plugin sandbox and PDP … manifest ceiling, principal binding, operation admission' (proposal:247) and 'compose these primitives' (proposal:360) overstate CLI readiness: no cli/ code binds a policy.Principal or constructs a PDP; the CLI agent supervisor uses manager.WithoutPrincipal()+WithoutSandbox() (cli/pkg/engine/supervisor.go:142-143). The stack is production-wired only in core/toolbox/session and cli/pkg/gateway (Mind daemon).
- (permissions) proposal:353 'manager.WithScopedAuthSecret … use the existing Ed25519 v2 format when host/plugin separation requires public verification' — WithScopedAuthSecret installs the v1 HMAC per-spawn secret (loader.go:513-546, env CODEFLY_SCOPED_AUTHZ_SECRET at loader.go:813-814). v2 Ed25519 (MintEd25519/VerifyEd25519, scoped_auth_v2.go) is a separate key-distribution mechanism not selected by WithScopedAuthSecret; the doc conflates the two.
- (permissions) proposal:356 lists 'policy.PDPDecision.RequireApproval and escalation' as a primitive to reuse for pausing high-risk actions, implying it fires today. No core PDP emits RequireApproval (observability.go:224 'no PDP returns this'; rg confirms none in non-test code); it is produced only by the saas-starter backend and consumed by the Mind agent SDK. A human-CLI approval path does not exist.
- (permissions) proposal:353 'WithPermissionsCallback … without allowing the provider to … impersonate another principal' is correct for the principal, but the same callback trusts plugin-supplied action and resource (callback.go:286-298). The doc should state that action/resource must be host-derived, not merely that principal is protected.
- (permissions) The manifest example (proposal:712-724) declares provider.create/update/delete on resource `provider:stripe/*`. Given globMatch only supports a single trailing '*' (permissions.go:489-498), this ceiling prefix-matches every workspace/environment/binding/type/remote-id, contradicting the least-privilege intent stated at proposal:411-428.
- (sandbox-broker) Proposal line 660 ('The existing Web toolbox supplies most of the HTTP guard behavior') and lines 1424-1426 ('The Web toolbox already enforces exact hostname matching, redirect revalidation, HTTP(S)-only URLs, timeouts, and response caps. Extract or reuse those primitives') materially overstate reusable behavior. The guard is ~40 lines running INSIDE the untrusted plugin process (toolbox-web/server.go:76-85, 200-210, 251-260), its allowlist is supplied through the plugin's own environment variable (toolbox-web/cmd/toolbox-web/main.go:22-24), and the plugin holds network: open (toolbox-web/toolbox.codefly.yaml:26). It is not an enforcement boundary and nothing meaningful can be extracted; the broker is new host-side code.
- (sandbox-broker) Proposal reuse table line 255 ('Web toolbox | toolbox-web | exact-host allowlist, redirect checks, timeouts, response caps') omits that toolbox-web permits http:// (server.go:204-206), sets any caller-supplied header verbatim including Authorization and Accept-Encoding (server.go:234-240), truncates oversize bodies instead of failing (server.go:251-260), performs no DNS/IP validation, and inherits environment proxies via http.DefaultTransport (server.go:76; GOROOT/src/net/http/transport.go:46-57).
- (sandbox-broker) Proposal lines 638-646 and control #5 (line 1396) state the provider has 'no direct workspace write access' and that external network is denied, implying read confinement. On macOS the sandbox does not confine reads at all: '(allow default)' with only file-write denied plus four home subdirectories (core/runners/sandbox/sandboxexec.go:100-124), and read paths are explicitly documented as advisory there (sandboxexec.go:162-170).
- (sandbox-broker) The macOS Seatbelt profile's secret deny-list entry '$HOME/.config/codefly/secrets' (core/runners/sandbox/sandboxexec.go:121) protects a path nothing in core/ or cli/ uses. Codefly's home is ~/.codefly (core/resources/global.go:57) and workspace secrets live at configurations/<env>/*.secret.env (core/configurations/secrets_test.go), neither of which is denied.
- (sandbox-broker) Proposal control #7 ('Secret minimization: resolve only the named credential required for one operation', line 1399) and line 866 ('The provider receives opaque credential handles ... not raw credential values') are contradicted by the spawn path: core/agents/manager/loader.go:722 sets cmd.Env = os.Environ(), and bwrap is invoked without --clearenv (core/runners/sandbox/bwrap.go:107-138), so every provider inherits the operator's full environment including CODEFLY_SCOPED_AUTHZ_SECRET (loader.go:817).
- (sandbox-broker) Proposal reuse rows for 'authenticated plugin transport' and 'plugin sandbox and PDP' (lines 246-247) imply existing wiring. Sandbox and production admission are wired only for toolboxes; all other agent load paths pass WithoutSandbox (core/services/agent.go:148, cli/pkg/engine/supervisor.go:142, core/sdk/sdk.go:168). UDS is opt-in with a single caller (core/agents/manager/loader.go:297, 416; core/toolbox/session/session.go:276) - correct classification is PARTIAL/UNWIRED.
- (sandbox-broker) Proposal invariant 11 and control #1/#2 imply the manifest ceiling bounds network capacity. It does not: the manifest's sandbox block is self-declared and applied verbatim (core/policy/permissions.go:174-206), and production admission accepts any non-native backend plus any non-empty policy (core/agents/manager/loader.go:611-615, core/resources/toolbox.go:186-197) - proven by toolbox-web/manifest_test.go:13-17 admitting a network: open manifest for production.
- (sandbox-broker) Internal contradiction: the broker requirements assume 'allowed method/path patterns from the provider manifest' (line 1437), but the normative provider manifest example (lines 695-742) contains no method, path, query or body declarations - only api-origins, permissions, sandbox, projections and resource-types.
- (sandbox-broker) Internal contradiction: api-origins is described as an exact manifest ceiling (lines 707-708, 1393) while providers accept an operator-supplied API base or regional origin (Sentry line 1673, STRIPE_API_BASE line 860) and Sentry self-hosted/regional origins are a required design case (lines 33, 1926). The proposal never states which source wins or how a self-hosted origin is admitted.
- (sandbox-broker) Proposal line 393 classifies provider.observe as low risk. With an origin-only ceiling and a Stripe management key, observe permits unbounded reads of customers, invoices and charges; risk classification must account for read scope, not just mutation.
- (sandbox-broker) Stale/incomplete vendor handling: Stripe idempotency keys are pruned after at least 24 hours and 'We generate a new request if a key is reused after the original is pruned' (https://docs.stripe.com/api/idempotent_requests). Proposal lines 1224-1226 acknowledge keys 'may expire' but the design specifies no retry freshness window, so a generic retry layer can silently duplicate a webhook endpoint.
- (sandbox-broker) core/toolbox/launch/launch.go:240-247 comments that 'buildSandbox only returns nil now under the explicit CODEFLY_SANDBOX_PERMISSIVE=1 escape hatch'; that environment variable exists nowhere in the repository, and buildSandbox actually returns nil for any manifest with an empty sandbox policy and no canonical_for entries (launch.go:199-206, 240-247).
- (sandbox-broker) core/agents/manager/loader.go:582-585 states orphaned callback sockets are cleaned up on restart. The sweeper handles only the 'codefly-uds-' directory prefix (core/agents/manager/sockets.go:44), so codefly-perms-<pid>-<nanos>.sock files (core/policy/callback.go:152-156) are never swept.
- (sandbox-broker) Proposal reuse row 'cassette testing | core testing guidance' (line 260) implies existing tooling. There is no cassette implementation in core/ or cli/ - searching for 'cassette' returns documentation only; this is new implementation.
- (sandbox-broker) Proposal line 1442 ('redirect denial when credentials would cross origins') understates the requirement: Go already strips Authorization on cross-domain redirects but forwards it to subdomain matches (https://pkg.go.dev/net/http, Client.Do), so a broker that re-injects credentials per hop must deny redirects on credentialed requests entirely rather than relying on cross-origin detection.
- (secrets) Verified correct (no correction, recording as confirmation): the proposal's claim 'Codefly currently has a read-only 1Password resolver' is accurate — core/configurations/secrets.go:137-144 executes only `op read --no-newline`; no write path exists anywhere in core/configurations/.
- (secrets) Reuse-table row 'Web toolbox — exact-host allowlist, redirect checks, timeouts, response caps' is accurate but materially incomplete as a reuse basis: toolbox-web currently accepts caller-supplied headers including Authorization (toolbox-web/server.go:234-240 sets any header from tool arguments), declares sandbox `network: open` (toolbox-web/toolbox.codefly.yaml), enforces its allowlist inside the plugin process (server.go:31-39), and uses the default http.Transport (proxy-env-honoring, no custom TLS/redirect policy beyond host check). The reusable surface for a host broker is roughly the exact-host match, redirect revalidation closure, timeout, and 4 MiB cap (server.go:21,26,76-85,102-108,251-260); credential injection, method/path policy, header denial, response classification, capture, and cassettes are all new.
- (secrets) Proposal Resend section says current documentation is 'internally inconsistent' about signing-secret retrieval. The verification page currently states plainly: 'Calls to create, retrieve, or list webhooks will also return the signing secret in the response body' (https://resend.com/docs/webhooks/verify-webhooks-requests), and the create response example shows `signing_secret` (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/webhooks/create-webhook). The list-example omission was not reproducible from the fetched content. More important than the inconsistency claim: retrievability-on-read makes observe-path redaction mandatory, a conclusion the proposal does not draw.
- (secrets) The proposal's Sentry flow ('setup token discovers project and DSN') omits that the client-keys list response also contains legacy secret material: `secret` and `dsn.secret` fields alongside the public DSN (https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/list-a-projects-client-keys/, response fields), with required scope project:read/write/admin. The 'browser-safe public DSN' framing is correct for the DSN itself but the discovery response is not secret-free.
- (secrets) Stripe webhook-endpoint create returns the `secret` field populated ('Returns the webhook endpoint object with the secret field populated', https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/create). Whether retrieve/list also return it was not confirmed by the fetched page — the proposal's recovery options for a lost secret (scenario 14) implicitly assume it is unrecoverable; this must be qualified in Stripe live acceptance rather than assumed (open question, confidence LOW).
- (secrets) P1 lists 'hidden prompting and file-based secret ingestion' under host-owned reuse boundary, but no hidden-input primitive exists in the CLI today: no term.ReadPassword usage (terminal raw mode at cli/cmd/terminal.go:129 is the TUI, not secret input; cli/pkg/cli/communicate/prompt.go is a visible prompt). Hidden input currently exists only in bash (`read -r -s`, module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/stripe.sh:206). Small but new Go work.
- (secrets) The current scripts the plugin replaces write raw secret-bearing vendor responses to disk: stripe.sh stores the full webhook-create response (containing whsec_) at ${SETUP_TEMPORARY_DIR}/webhook-created.json and the assembled billing.secret.env in the same mktemp dir (module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/stripe.sh:177-221), protected only by umask 077 + EXIT trap rm (provider-common.sh:130-132). Useful as the honest baseline the broker design improves upon; the proposal describes script behavior but not this on-disk exposure.
- (state-recovery) provider-plugins.md:1248 labels the state path segment 'canonical-workspace-path-sha256' and line 1244 says to 'use the execution runtime's workspace-isolation pattern', but the referenced implementation is not canonical in the symlink sense: cli/pkg/executionruntime/runtime.go:66-71 uses only filepath.Abs + filepath.Clean and never filepath.EvalSymlinks, so /tmp/ws and /private/tmp/ws (macOS) or any symlinked checkout hash to different state directories. The word 'canonical' overstates the reused behavior.
- (state-recovery) provider-plugins.md:257 lists execution receipts as reuse with only 'provider operation kinds and remote resource references' as the required extension. This omits a hard dependency: the recorder refuses to construct without a Work Context Authority (cli/pkg/executionrecorder/recorder.go:123) and every receipt requires a verified work_context with tenant_id (core/proto/codefly/execution/v1/execution.proto:62-64); executionruntime.Open requires AuthorityJWKS and AuthorityIssuer (cli/pkg/executionruntime/runtime.go:91-96). A local-authority adapter for dogfood is unnamed required work.
- (state-recovery) provider-plugins.md:258 cites 'inter-process locks' among existing durable-state patterns. The existing locks are: bbolt's own exclusive file lock with a 500ms open timeout (cli/pkg/executionjournal/journal.go:98), a polled flock in localservice (cli/pkg/localservice/lock_unix.go:27-45), and gofrs/flock in cliupdate (cli/pkg/cliupdate/state.go). None of these provides the plan/apply CAS-under-lock or shared-read semantics the provider lifecycle needs; the row is a pattern precedent, not reusable behavior.
- (state-recovery) provider-plugins.md:1294-1295 states 'One remote object cannot be bound to multiple local addresses in the same state backend' while provider-plugins.md:1248 defines the state backend per binding; under the stated layout the invariant's scope collapses to a single binding, where double-binding is impossible anyway — as written the sentence guarantees nothing across bindings or environments (see HIGH finding).
- (config-endpoints) Reuse-table overstatement — 'environment ingress: Environment.Ingress public host mapping / resolve a public HTTPS origin for remote webhooks' (core/docs/provider-plugins.md reuse table): Environment.Ingress (core/resources/environment.go:79-84, field :134) is consumed only by orchestration environment cloning (cli/pkg/orchestration/environment.go:44-47) and gitops kustomize bundling (cli/pkg/gitops/module_bundle.go:142,166). No (environment, service, endpoint)→public-origin resolver exists; the shipped scripts require the operator to pass --webhook-origin by hand (module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/provider-common.sh:123-127). This row is NEW host implementation, not 'reuse with extension'.
- (config-endpoints) Sentry projection example contradicts Sentry's official DSN classification and the loader's reference-only rules: the proposal writes SENTRY_DSN/SENTRY_ORG/SENTRY_PROJECT/SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT into 'error-tracking.secret.env or error-tracking.secret.ref.env' (provider-plugins.md, Example Sentry projection). Sentry: 'DSNs are safe to keep public because they only allow submission of new events' (https://docs.sentry.io/concepts/key-terms/dsn-explainer/). A plaintext DSN in a .secret.ref.env file is rejected by the loader ('plaintext is not allowed; use a supported provider reference', core/configurations/secrets.go:66-78, enforced at core/configurations/local_reader.go:377-385).
- (config-endpoints) The same Sentry example places the BUILD-purpose SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN inside the `error-tracking` configuration name that runtime services consume (the starter frontend declares error-tracking at module-saas-starter/module/services/frontend/service.codefly.yaml:14-18 and receives all its secret values via cli/pkg/orchestration/runner.go:273), contradicting the proposal's own statements 'build token is projected only to the build/release configuration that needs it' and the P3 exit gate 'setup token is absent from runtime output by default'.
- (config-endpoints) The binding callback example (`service: auth-sidecar, endpoint: rest, path: ...`) omits the module, but Codefly endpoint identity is module+service+endpoint (core/proto/codefly/base/v0/endpoint.proto:20-24) and the port hash includes the module (core/network/port.go:117-126); the six-integrations starter contains auth-sidecar under two module trees (module/services/ and modules/saas-starter/services/), so the example as written is ambiguous in the reference workspace itself.
- (config-endpoints) Writer invariant 6 'fsync and atomically rename the complete output set' is stated as an existing-pattern extension but is not implementable as written for a multi-file output set inside the shared configurations/<profile>/ directory (POSIX rename is per-file; the behavioral seed setup_install_pair uses two separate non-atomic install calls, provider-common.sh:146-174).
- (config-endpoints) Accurate claims verified for the record: 'The existing configuration loader is read-only' is TRUE (core/configurations/ contains only read/resolve paths; no writer exists); the 1Password resolver is read-only via `op read` (core/configurations/secrets.go:126-144); Resend CLI behavior matches the proposal ('registers a temporary webhook, streams events, and cleans up on exit', https://resend.com/docs/cli); Stripe webhook HTTPS requirement matches ('Registered webhook endpoints must be publicly accessible HTTPS URLs', https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks).
- (stripe) core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1594-1600 — `stripe.account` observed fields include 'sandbox/live mode', but the Stripe Account object has no livemode attribute (https://docs.stripe.com/api/accounts/object). Mode is only derivable from key prefixes (https://docs.stripe.com/keys) — which the provider cannot see under the credential-handle model — or from livemode on other objects (e.g. webhook endpoints). The observation claim is unimplementable as written.
- (stripe) core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1503-1521 (human plan example) — shows a webhook UPDATE with 'STRIPE_API_VERSION: 2025-… → 2026-02-25.clover' and 'secret: unchanged'. Stripe's webhook api_version is settable only at creation and is absent from update parameters (https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/create, https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/update); a pinned-version change is a REPLACE that produces a new signing secret. The example depicts an impossible vendor transition.
- (stripe) core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1614-1615 — 'The signing secret returned at creation is a write-once secret output' is confirmed for the API ('Only returned at creation', https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/object), but the proposal nowhere notes that the Dashboard/Workbench can reveal and roll endpoint secrets (https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks: 'Click to reveal', 'Invalidate secret key' with up to 24h dual-secret overlap). This materially changes lost-secret recovery from 'replace endpoint' to 'MANUAL_ACTION: reveal in dashboard', and the roll flow (two secrets active simultaneously) is relevant to rotation design.
- (stripe) core/docs/provider-plugins.md:772 and :1516 — example API version '2026-02-25.clover': Stripe's current version at review time is '2026-07-29.dahlia' (https://docs.stripe.com/api/versioning). Cosmetic, but examples should carry a plausibly current pin.
- (stripe) module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/stripe.sh:174 — error text 'Stripe cannot reveal its signing secret' is API-accurate but operationally misleading: the secret is revealable in Workbench. If migrated verbatim into provider remediation text it becomes an incorrect claim.
- (stripe) core/docs/provider-plugins.md:229 (vendor table row 'remote API version': Stripe 'explicit Stripe API version should be pinned') — confirmed correct: per-request Stripe-Version header and endpoint-creation api_version both documented (https://docs.stripe.com/api/versioning), and idempotency claims at :1222-1226 are confirmed correct in direction (POST-only keys, ≥24h retention, param-mismatch errors) per https://docs.stripe.com/api/idempotent_requests.
- (sentry) core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1523-1525 states the public Sentry DSN is 'redacted in generic output unless an explicit --show-sensitive policy permits display'. Official Sentry docs state 'DSNs are safe to keep public because they only allow submission of new events... they do not allow read access to any information' (https://docs.sentry.io/concepts/key-terms/dsn-explainer/). Treating the DSN as sensitive contradicts both the vendor and the proposal's own browser projection at :951 and :1709.
- (sentry) core/docs/provider-plugins.md:955-961: the example places the backend public DSN, SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT, SENTRY_ORG, and SENTRY_PROJECT in error-tracking.secret.env alongside SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN. None of the first four is a secret; under the proposal's own rule that 'SECRET maps to secret=true' (:927) this example encodes over-classification. It mirrors the current script (module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/sentry.sh:198-204) rather than the proposal's classification model.
- (sentry) core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1699-1701 and :2281-2285 justify deferring Sentry project creation partly on 'team ownership' decisions. The current official endpoint is POST /api/0/organizations/{organization_id_or_slug}/projects/ with no team in path or body; a personal team is auto-created for the caller with Team Admin role, default_rules is a boolean body parameter (default true), and org:write is required when the organization disables member project creation (https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/create-a-project-for-an-organization/). Deferral remains justified, but by the auto-created-team side effect and conditional scope escalation, not by a required team parameter.
- (sentry) Confirmed (no correction needed, recorded for the corrections ledger): 'Sentry API keys are legacy' (provider-plugins.md:1711) matches official docs ('API keys are a legacy means of authenticating... disabled for new accounts', https://docs.sentry.io/api/auth/); project:read is the documented minimum scope for both project retrieval and client-key listing (https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/retrieve-a-project/, https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/list-a-projects-client-keys/); org:ci is the documented scope 'for CI and deployment workflows, including uploading source maps, creating releases, and managing code mappings' (https://docs.sentry.io/api/permissions/); the rate-limit row 'per caller+endpoint limits and concurrency headers' (provider-plugins.md:230) matches the documented per-caller-per-endpoint fixed windows and X-Sentry-Rate-Limit-Limit/Remaining/Reset/ConcurrentLimit/ConcurrentRemaining headers (https://docs.sentry.io/api/ratelimits/); 'API is currently v0 and may be regional' (provider-plugins.md:229) matches the documented v0 API and us.sentry.io/de.sentry.io regional hosts (https://docs.sentry.io/api/).
- (sentry) Nuance the proposal omits: the client-keys list response includes legacy secret material (keys[].secret and keys[].dsn.secret) directly beside keys[].dsn.public (https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/list-a-projects-client-keys/); the proposal's Resend section has an explicit 'never log a webhook list response' rule (:1778) but no equivalent Sentry keys-response rule.
- (sentry) Open question (LOW confidence, needs live qualification): whether an Organization Auth Token (sntrys_, fixed non-editable permission set per https://docs.sentry.io/account/auth-tokens/) can perform the project:read GETs used for discovery. The starter example expects sntrys_ for SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN and the current script uses one token for both discovery and projection (sentry.sh:161,203), so existing dogfood evidence conflates setup and build purposes rather than proving each token type against each endpoint.
- (resend) CONFIRMED (sharpened): proposal provider-plugins.md:82-84 and :1770-1772 claim Resend docs are internally inconsistent about signing-secret return. Exact current state: verification prose says 'Calls to create, retrieve, or list webhooks will also return the signing secret in the response body' (https://resend.com/docs/webhooks/verify-webhooks-requests); create example returns signing_secret (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/webhooks/create-webhook); retrieve example returns signing_secret (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/webhooks/get-webhook); list example omits it (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/webhooks/list-webhooks); update (PATCH) and delete examples omit it. So only LIST is contradictory (prose yes, example no); retrieve is unambiguous in current docs. The proposal should not describe retrieve as ambiguous — the open question is list, plus whether examples reflect live behavior (live qualification still required).
- (resend) STALE SCRIPT CLAIM (proposal is right to flag it): module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/resend.sh:184 fails with 'its signing secret cannot be retrieved; supply --webhook-secret-file' without attempting GET /webhooks/{id}; current official retrieve docs show signing_secret in the response. The proposal's requirement not to hard-code this assumption (provider-plugins.md:1766-1768) is correct.
- (resend) CONFIRMED: full_access ('Create, delete, get, and update any resource') vs sending_access ('Can only send emails'), and domain_id 'Restrict an API key to send emails only from a specific domain' applying only when permission is sending_access (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/api-keys/create-api-key). Proposal provider-plugins.md:1782-1784 is accurate.
- (resend) CONFIRMED with addition: proposal vendor table (provider-plugins.md:230) says Resend has a 'default team-wide request rate with 429' — correct: 10 requests/second per team across all keys. ADDITION the proposal omits: Resend officially documents ratelimit-limit, ratelimit-remaining, ratelimit-reset, and retry-after response headers per IETF draft-ietf-httpapi-ratelimit-headers-06 (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/rate-limit), which directly supports the acceptance criterion '429 maps to retryable failure and retry-after' (provider-plugins.md:1811). No per-endpoint limits are documented — the limit is team-scoped only.
- (resend) CONFIRMED: 'current docs say no explicit version header yet' (provider-plugins.md:229) — 'Currently, there's no versioning system in place. We plan to add versioning via calendar-based headers in the future' (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/introduction).
- (resend) CONFIRMED: Svix verification with svix-id/svix-timestamp/svix-signature headers; at-least-once delivery ('may be delivered more than once in rare cases'); ordering not guaranteed; dedup by svix-id; retry schedule 5s/5m/30m/2h/5h/10h (https://resend.com/docs/webhooks/introduction, https://resend.com/docs/webhooks/verify-webhooks-requests). Proposal provider-plugins.md:895 and the dogfood acceptance (duplicated/out-of-order events) are well-grounded; the starter's durable receiver referenced by the acceptance is real (svix verifier and SvixID dedup: module-saas-starter/module/services/accounts/code/pkg/email/resend_webhook.go:13,29,45,50; migration 84_resend_delivery_events.up.sql).
- (resend) PARTIALLY INCORRECT/AMBIGUOUS: cross-provider matrix row 'local forwarder | Stripe CLI | n/a | Resend CLI' (provider-plugins.md:1923) implies transport parity. The CLI docs example is 'resend webhooks listen --url https://hostname.tailnet-name.ts.net --events email.received' with 'Any tunnel can be used, such as Tailscale Funnel, ngrok, or localtunnel' (https://resend.com/docs/cli), i.e. bring-your-own tunnel; the CLI 2.0 announcement instead claims the CLI 'handles the tunnel' (https://resend.com/blog/resend-cli-2). Conflicting primary sources; how the temporary webhook's signing secret is exposed is documented nowhere. Must be live-qualified in P4, not assumed.
- (resend) OMISSION: Resend DOES support idempotency keys, but only on POST /emails and POST /emails/batch, header 'Idempotency-Key', 24-hour retention, with 409 invalid_idempotent_request / concurrent_idempotent_requests semantics (https://resend.com/docs/dashboard/emails/idempotency-keys, https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/errors). Consequence for the proposal: broker idempotency-key injection cannot protect POST /webhooks (management mutations) — lost-response webhook creation must be reconciled by complete listing.
- (resend) ADDITION: error-name-based mapping is mandatory — restricted_api_key is 401 ('This API key is restricted to only send emails') while invalid_api_key is 403 (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/errors), inverted from common 401/403 convention; and every request requires a User-Agent header or Resend returns 403 (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/introduction).
- (resend) CONFIRMED: domain verification is a hard sending prerequisite ('You must add and verify at least one domain to send and receive emails') with statuses not_started, pending, verified, partially_verified, partially_failed, failed, temporary_failure (https://resend.com/docs/dashboard/domains/introduction); domain object exposes region (us-east-1 | eu-west-1 | sa-east-1 per invalid_region error), capabilities, and per-DNS-record status (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/domains/get-domain). Proposal's resend.domain observed fields (provider-plugins.md:1749-1754) are implementable as specified.
- (resend) CONFIRMED: all eight event types the script subscribes (resend.sh:190-192) remain valid — email.sent, email.delivered, email.delivery_delayed, email.failed, email.bounced, email.complained, email.opened, email.clicked — among a larger current set including email.received, email.scheduled, email.suppressed, domain.*, contact.*, suppression.* (https://resend.com/docs/webhooks/event-types).
- (resend) UNVERIFIED DETAILS requiring live qualification (labeled open questions, confidence LOW): exact HTTP status of POST /webhooks (script accepts 200 or 201, resend.sh:204 — docs example does not state it); whether the webhook endpoint URL must be HTTPS (proposal provider-plugins.md:1793 asserts it; create-webhook docs say only 'The URL where webhook events will be sent'); default/max page size of GET /webhooks; whether live list/retrieve responses actually match the documented examples regarding signing_secret.
- (reliability-ux-doctor) Proposal reuse-table row 'workspace doctor — CLI doctor — reuse: environment/configuration/secret validation and stable report; required extension: provider installation, auth, remote health, drift sections' understates the change: the existing command's documented contract is strictly read-only and 'must not start agents, containers, or services' (cli/cmd/doctor_workspace.go:92-95 and help text :684-686), and its stable-code JSON contract requires a schema-version bump for code changes (:24-27, :46). Adding provider sections that spawn agents and call vendor APIs is a contract revision (schema v2 + opt-in), not an additive extension as the table implies.
- (reliability-ux-doctor) The proposal's proposed doctor diagnostic codes (provider.config.missing, provider.auth.rejected, …) collide with the existing shipped doctor codes provider_not_configured / provider_executable_missing / provider_authentication_required / provider_resolution_failed (cli/cmd/doctor_workspace.go:37-40), where 'provider' means SECRET provider (1Password backend). The proposal's own terminology table ('secret provider … is not an external provider agent') flags exactly this overloading but the code namespaces do not resolve it; one doctor report would contain both families with contradictory meanings of 'provider'.
- (reliability-ux-doctor) Confirmed accurate (no correction needed, recorded for completeness): the structured-failure reuse claim matches source — FailureCode taxonomy, retryable, retry_after, diagnostics all exist in core/proto/codefly/base/v0/failure.proto:12-175; the Web toolbox claim matches source — exact-host allowlist with redirect revalidation (toolbox-web/server.go:78-82), 30s default timeout (:23-26,77), 4 MiB body cap (:17-21,251-257); and toolbox-web today contains NO retry, Retry-After, or rate-limit handling (rg over toolbox-web: zero matches), consistent with the proposal listing rate-limit normalization as new broker work. The proposal's Resend claim 'default team-wide request rate with 429' matches current official docs: 10 requests/second per team across all API keys, 429 on excess (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/introduction).
- (testing-migration-roadmap) Reuse-table row 'agent conformance | codefly agent ci | Reuse: build/package/standalone verification | Required extension: provider protocol conformance mode and fixtures' (provider-plugins.md:259) overstates reuse: the conformance stage is hardcoded to kind codefly:service (cli/cmd/agents/ci.go:427-428), codefly:module already requires --skip-conformance (ci.go:422-425), and the mechanism (Builder.Create service generation + workspace 'ci run --all' gate, ci.go:554-579, 680-694) is inapplicable to a leaf-protocol provider. Only the manifest/source/build/audit/drift stages and child-environment isolation (ci.go:476-509) are reusable; provider conformance is a new harness.
- (testing-migration-roadmap) Reuse-table row 'cassette testing | core testing guidance' (provider-plugins.md:260): no cassette recorder, replayer, or sanitizer implementation exists in core/, cli/, or toolbox-web/ Go code (rg 'cassette|go-vcr|Cassette' -g '*.go' matches only unrelated identifiers); the pattern exists solely as documentation referencing the external mind-server repository (core/docs/testing.md:135-150). The entire Tier-1 mechanism is new implementation.
- (testing-migration-roadmap) The permission-primitives table (provider-plugins.md:342-358) lists manager.WithPrincipal / WithScopedAuthSecret / WithPermissionsCallback / WithProductionAdmission as primitives to 'reuse'; they exist in core (core/agents/manager/loader.go:485, 546, 589, 603) but have zero call sites anywhere in cli/ (rg across cli/ -g '*.go'), so no current Codefly command path exercises them — the CLI-side composition is entirely new integration, not reuse of wired behavior.
- (testing-migration-roadmap) The contract identifiers codefly.dev/configuration/billing@1, email@1, and error-tracking@1 (provider-plugins.md:199, 767, 1638, 1715, 1797) resolve to nothing: rg for 'output-contract|codefly.dev/configuration' across core/ and cli/ returns no code; the starter defines only informal env-file conventions (scripts/setup/stripe.sh:215-221; module-saas-starter/setup_scripts_test.go:180-190). The proposal presents bindings validating against these contracts without any deliverable that creates the contract registry.
- (testing-migration-roadmap) Proposal statement 'The safe idempotency and --force checks in provider-common.sh should become the behavioral seed' (provider-plugins.md:986-988) is accurate about the mechanism (setup_install_pair at provider-common.sh:146-175 compares against defaults and refuses differing files without --force) but the scripts have no ownership metadata, remote-ID persistence, or state — the seed covers file collision only, not the ownership/adoption model layered on top (stripe.sh:168-176 persists no created-webhook ID; only the secret reaches billing.secret.env at stripe.sh:222-226).
- (testing-migration-roadmap) Environment/workspace YAML loading is permissive (yaml.v3 Unmarshal via core/resources/marshalling.go:62-69; no KnownFields/strict decoding anywhere in core/resources), so the proposal's implicit assumption that older CLIs will handle provider-bindings gracefully resolves to 'silently ignored' — safe but invisible; nowhere documented in the migration section.
- (contradiction-audit) core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1038-1040 — 'The existing `services.Advertisement` assumes Builder and Runtime' is stale: core/agents/services/advertisement.go:24-25 already provides a `CapabilityOnly` mode that suppresses BUILDER/RUNTIME for capability-only plugins (conditional capability assembly at advertisement.go:50-54).
