The justification for a provider-specific advertisement helper must be rewritten or the helper replaced by extending Advertisement.
- (contradiction-audit) core/docs/provider-plugins.md:957 vs :951 — the Sentry projection example places the same public DSN value in both the public file (NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN in error-tracking.env) and the secret file (SENTRY_DSN in error-tracking.secret.env), giving one value two contradictory classifications in a normative example.
- (contradiction-audit) core/docs/provider-plugins.md:227 (vendor table, Sentry management-credential row) — 'project read/write depending operation' is garbled; should read 'project read/write depending on operation'.
- (contradiction-audit) core/docs/provider-plugins.md:866-868 vs :486-488 and :528-529 — the text simultaneously states raw secrets 'do not transit the provider process' / provider 'never sees the captured bytes' and that 'the host may expose a secret value to the provider' when the manifest allows; the exception is nowhere reflected in the action vocabulary (395-403), security controls (1392-1422), or test matrix (581-601). One of the two statements must be corrected (see HIGH finding).
- (contradiction-audit) core/docs/provider-plugins.md:398 vs :1310-1313 — `provider.delete` is defined as 'Delete exactly one owned remote resource' while destroy plans name 'exact owned/adopted resources'; adopted-resource deletability is stated inconsistently.
- (contradiction-audit) core/docs/provider-plugins.md:291 vs :1179 vs :1440 — idempotency is owned by the provider ('idempotency strategy'; provider 'must use the operation/resource identity as the vendor idempotency key') and by the host broker ('request idempotency-key injection where declared') simultaneously.
- (contradiction-audit) Brief drift — provider-plugins-adversarial-review-brief.md:273-274 renders the lifecycle as 'Validate -> Observe -> Plan -> Approve -> Apply -> Project -> Doctor' (Project as a discrete post-Apply stage), while the proposal makes PROJECT_OUTPUT a plan action (provider-plugins.md:1122) and separately a host 'Commit' authorization moment; the brief's summary presents as settled a timing the proposal states three different ways.
- (contradiction-audit) Brief drift — provider-plugins-adversarial-review-brief.md:546-548 states an unconditional non-goal ('create production accounts, payment products, prices, tax rules... automatically') whereas the proposal's non-goal at provider-plugins.md:123 is conditional ('create Stripe products or prices without an explicit pricing specification') and provider-plugins.md:1645-1650 anticipates a later Stripe catalog resource. The brief's summary is stricter than the proposal it summarizes.
- (contradiction-audit) Verified-accurate load-bearing claims (no correction needed, recorded for other lanes): ScopedAuthorization fields AudienceID/CatalogDigest/RequestDigest/MaxUses/Caveats exist (core/policy/scoped_auth.go:79-99, core/policy/scoped_auth_v2.go:118-122, verification at scoped_auth_v2.go:337-369); WithPrincipal/WithoutPrincipal/WithoutSandbox/WithScopedAuthSecret/WithPermissionsCallback/WithProductionAdmission exist (core/agents/manager/loader.go:453,485,506,546,589,603); NewCeilingPDP strict empty-manifest deny exists behind requireManifest=true, with the caveat that the documented default during M4 rollout is permissive (core/policy/pdp_ceiling.go:48-66) so provider admission must pass true explicitly; UNCERTAIN receipt stage and Ed25519 signing exist (core/executionreceipt/receipt.go:224, receipt_test.go:5-21); the 1Password resolver is read-only via `op read` (core/configurations/secrets.go:32,124-132); the configuration package has no writer (no non-test Write path found in core/configurations — 'existing configuration loader is read-only' at provider-plugins.md:969 is accurate); toolbox-web enforces allowlist, exact-subdomain matching, redirect revalidation, 30s default timeout, and a MaxBodyBytes response cap (toolbox-web/server.go:17,23-26,33-98,90-98,248-252); `codefly agent ci` exists (cli/cmd/agents/ci.go:47); sha256 workspace-path isolation exists (cli/pkg/executionruntime/runtime.go:61-71); executionjournal uses bbolt (cli/pkg/executionjournal/journal.go); policy.MapExpander exists (core/policy/permissions.go:95-117); base/v0/failure.proto carries retryable and retry_after (core/proto/codefly/base/v0/failure.proto:157,169-170); current max agent kind is TOOLBOX=5 (core/proto/codefly/base/v0/agent.proto:26), so the brief's 'PROVIDER=6' is the correct next enum value.
- (contradiction-audit) Unverified by this lane (flagged for provider lanes, confidence LOW): the Resend documentation-inconsistency claim (provider-plugins.md:84-87 and 1770-1779, 'webhook verification documentation says create/retrieve/list may return the signing secret, while the list API example does not show it') was not checked against resend.com docs in this review; treat as an open question until the Resend lane fetches the official pages.

## 6. Codefly reuse matrix

Classifications follow the brief's Lane 2 taxonomy. Evidence is at the pinned revisions in the coverage appendix.

### Lane 2 reuse matrix (non-policy rows) — evidence at core d1be667, cli 701a6d4, toolbox-web bb134e4

| # | Capability (proposal row) | Classification | Key evidence | Smallest required extension |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Agent kind & identity (`resources/agent.go`, `base/v0/agent.proto`) | REUSE WITH SMALL EXTENSION | Registry-based kinds via `RegisterAgent` (core/resources/agent.go:46-57); enum ends at `TOOLBOX = 5` (core/proto/codefly/base/v0/agent.proto:9-27); toolbox precedent at core/resources/toolbox.go:22-26 | Add `PROVIDER = 6`, `ProviderAgent = "codefly:provider"`, `RegisterAgent` call, and a `Path()` branch (agent.go:160-177) |
| 2 | Agent routing / installation / local-latest | PARTIAL/UNWIRED | Four inconsistent kind switches: core/resources/agent.go:160-177; core/agents/manager/manager.go:80-86 (unknown kinds silently -> `modules/`); cli/cmd/agents/build.go:514-519 (unknown kinds -> `services/`); cli/cmd/agents/versions.go:498-502. Toolboxes are already mishandled by two of the four | Centralize kind->subdir mapping in one place consumed by all four sites + exhaustive-kind test (finding ADV-R1) |
| 3 | Agent release discovery & artifact verification | PARTIAL/UNWIRED | OCI blob-digest verify exists (core/agents/manager/store.go:189-242); Nix store env-gated (loader.go:680-688); GitHub fallback unverified plain `http.Get`+tar (downloader.go:58-80) auto-invoked by Load (loader.go:701-706); repo name hardcoded `service-%s` for all kinds (downloader.go:30); no signature or lock file anywhere | Kind-aware repo naming; checksum verification or disable auto-download for providers (ADV-R2); artifact-digest lock stays P5 as proposed |

#### Duplication / dependency-direction flags
1. Kind->directory logic duplicated 4x and inconsistent (rows 1-3); the toolbox kind proves additions rot — centralize before adding PROVIDER.
2. GitHub release repo mapping ignores kind entirely (`service-%s`, downloader.go:30) — a provider and a service sharing publisher/name share a release stream.
3. toolbox-web imports core, so 'extract from toolbox-web into a core broker' is a rewrite on the other side of the dependency arrow and of the trust boundary (guards currently run in the untrusted plugin process).
4. executionjournal's privacy helpers are unexported — either export them from a shared package or accept deliberate duplication in provider state; do not import the attestation-specific journal for provider desired/observed state.
5. Doctor's existing `provider_*` codes (secret backends) vs the proposal's `provider.*` diagnostic namespace is a latent operator-vocabulary collision inside a versioned JSON contract.

### Policy and permission primitives

| Primitive | Classification | Evidence | Smallest required extension |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| policy.Principal + manager.WithPrincipal | PARTIAL/UNWIRED | Type is REUSE AS-IS and validated/immutable (principal.go:30-155); manager binds it and re-checks expiry (loader.go:485,657-663; gateway.go:120). But no cli/ code constructs a Principal for provider/human-CLI use, and the CLI supervisor spawns agents with WithoutPrincipal (cli/pkg/engine/supervisor.go:143). Production principal binding exists only in core/toolbox/session and Mind. | Add CLI principal acquisition (from login token/CI service identity) and pass manager.WithPrincipal on every provider agent Load; add a provider agent kind so session-style binding applies. |
| manager.WithProductionAdmission | REUSE WITH SMALL EXTENSION | Validates enforcing sandbox + valid non-expired principal + permissions callback + >=32B scoped secret, order-independent (loader.go:603-632). Does NOT check CODEFLY_PDP_MODE or break-glass. | Extend admission to assert PDP mode==enforce and break-glass inactive (proposal invariant 10) and surface both in the receipt/doctor. |
| policy.PermissionPolicy + policy.NewCeilingPDP (manifest ceiling) | REUSE AS-IS | Ceiling intersect with role grant, empty-manifest→deny under RequireManifest (pdp_ceiling.go:86-131); glob matching exact/bare-*/trailing-* with mid-* rejected (permissions.go:415-498). | None to reuse; but mandate narrow production resource prefixes (workspace/env/binding) and reject `provider:<p>/*` grants at admission — matcher already supports the narrow trailing-* form since remote-id is last. |
| policy.GatewayEvaluator + ScopedAuthorization (v1 HMAC + v2 Ed25519) | REUSE AS-IS | Host-side evaluate+mint with CatalogDigest/RequestDigest/caveats and fail-on-backend-error (gateway.go:109-200); exact-match verify + required caveats (scoped_auth_v2.go:311-374); v2 Ed25519 wired in cli/pkg/gateway/prepared_mutation.go:477-499. | Add a per-broker-request minting/verification loop (one token per outbound HTTP request, or a plan-bound multi-use token with derived-action re-check) — the current one-token-per-RPC shape does not cover N HTTP requests inside one Apply. |
| manager.WithPermissionsCallback / PermissionsCallbackServer | DO NOT REUSE | REUSE AS-IS for principal binding only: principal is host-overridden and cannot be impersonated (callback.go:262-274). DO NOT REUSE for per-plan-action authorization because action and resource are taken verbatim from the plugin request body (callback.go:286-298, 380-389) — suitable for narrowing an already-outer-authorized Toolbox sub-op, unsuitable for authorizing provider mutations whose action must be derived from the plan+HTTP request. | Introduce host-derived (action,resource) from the approved plan and the actual outbound method/path; ignore provider-asserted action/resource for authorization. |
| Escalation (PDPDecision.RequireApproval + EscalationGrantor) and risk_levels→approval | PARTIAL/UNWIRED | Defined in core (escalation.go, pdp.go:42-48) and implemented by saas-starter host (module-saas-starter/host/host.go:181-445), but no core PDP emits RequireApproval (observability.go:224; rg finds none in non-test code) and EscalationGrantor is not wired into the CLI. RiskLevelOf exists (permissions.go:472) with no mapping to a RequireApproval decision. | Either wire a CLI interactive approval bound to the plan digest for high/critical actions, or scope v1 to role-grant-only and drop the approval-binding invariant. |
| policy.SaasPDP cache/revocation/fail-closed + policy observability | REUSE AS-IS | Fail-closed default, denies never cached, LRU positive cache, metrics (pdp_saas.go:35-211,297-338; observability.go). Revocation via TokenRevocationList (hardening.go) — but TRL is consulted only in core/toolbox/policyguard/guard.go:388, which providers don't use. | Consult the TRL and re-invoke SaasPDP before each provider mutation/commit so revocation is visible mid-Apply (denies are uncached, so no new invalidation channel is needed). |

**Narrative.** policy.Principal + manager.WithPrincipal + WithProductionAdmission are REUSE AS-IS as types/enforcement but PARTIAL/UNWIRED from the CLI (no CLI code binds a real principal; supervisor uses WithoutPrincipal). PermissionPolicy + NewCeilingPDP are REUSE AS-IS for the manifest-ceiling concept but the glob matcher only supports exact/bare-`*`/trailing-`prefix*` (permissions.go:489-498), a REUSE WITH SMALL EXTENSION if richer resource scoping is ever needed. GatewayEvaluator + ScopedAuthorization (v1 HMAC + v2 Ed25519, CatalogDigest/RequestDigest/MaxUses/caveats) are REUSE AS-IS and are the correct approval-binding substrate; v2 Ed25519 is already production-wired in cli/pkg/gateway prepared_mutation.go. The permissions callback (WithPermissionsCallback / PermissionsCallbackServer) is REUSE AS-IS for principal binding but DO NOT REUSE for per-plan-action authorization because it trusts plugin-supplied action/resource (callback.go:286-298). Escalation (RequireApproval + EscalationGrantor) is PARTIAL/UNWIRED: defined in core and implemented by saas-starter host, but no core PDP emits RequireApproval and there is no human-CLI escalation path. SaasPDP cache/revocation/fail-closed is REUSE AS-IS (pdp_saas.go). Policy observability (PDPMetrics/RecordDecision) is REUSE AS-IS. Break-glass, AllowAllPDP, PDPModeOff, WithoutPrincipal are the documented bypasses the proposal (invariant 10) says provider production admission must reject and keep greppable — that rejection is NEW; WithProductionAdmission validates sandbox/principal/callback/secret (loader.go:609-632) but does NOT check the break-glass env var or PDP mode.

### Sandbox and broker primitives

| Primitive | Classification | Evidence | Smallest required extension |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Web toolbox HTTP guards (proposal reuse row line 255; 'extract a host-mediated provider HTTP broker with credential injection') | DO NOT REUSE | The guard executes inside the untrusted plugin process: host allowlist lookup at toolbox-web/server.go:207-210, CheckRedirect closure at 76-85, LimitReader at 251-260, client with nil Transport at 76. Its allowlist arrives via the plugin's own env var (toolbox-web/cmd/toolbox-web/main.go:22-24) and the plugin holds OS-level open egress (toolbox-web/toolbox.codefly.yaml:26, honored verbatim by core/policy/permissions.go:202-203). It also permits http:// (server.go:204-206), sets any caller header verbatim (234-240), truncates rather than failing (251-260), never validates DNS/IPs, and inherits environment proxies through http.DefaultTransport (GOROOT/src/net/http/transport.go:46-57). | None as reuse. Build the broker as NEW host-side code in core (e.g. core/net/brokerguard + core/provider/broker) - direction is already correct since toolbox-web depends on core v0.2.23 (toolbox-web/go.mod:8) and core does not depend on toolbox-web. Afterwards, re-point toolbox-web at the shared guard (ideally making it descriptor-only with host-executed fetch so its network: open grant can be dropped). Treat toolbox-web as prior art, not a library. |
| Plugin sandbox enforcement on supported operating systems (proposal reuse row line 247, controls #4/#5) | PARTIAL/UNWIRED | core/runners/sandbox exists with two enforcing backends (sandbox.go:94-103) and is applied by the toolbox launcher (core/toolbox/launch/launch.go:140-152, 199-235), but every other agent load path opts out: core/services/agent.go:141-148, cli/pkg/engine/supervisor.go:142, core/sdk/sdk.go:162-168. Capacity is self-declared and unbounded by any host ceiling (core/policy/permissions.go:174-206); production admission checks only a non-native backend (core/agents/manager/loader.go:611-615) and a non-empty policy (core/resources/toolbox.go:186-197). macOS provides no read confinement (core/runners/sandbox/sandboxexec.go:100-124, 162-170) and Linux omits PID/IPC isolation and --clearenv (core/runners/sandbox/bwrap.go:107-138). CI never exercises the macOS profile (.github/workflows/go.yml:23). | A provider session analogous to core/toolbox/session that (1) mandates WithSandbox under a new host-owned per-kind capacity ceiling (network loopback/deny only, empty read_paths, write_paths limited to the per-spawn UDS dir), (2) mandates WithUDS, WithPrincipal, WithPermissionsCallback and WithProductionAdmission with WithoutSandbox/WithoutPrincipal unreachable, (3) builds cmd.Env from an allowlist instead of os.Environ(), (4) adds --unshare-pid/--unshare-ipc/--unshare-uts/--clearenv on Linux and real read denial on macOS, and (5) runs the sandbox_e2e suite on a macOS runner. |
| Authenticated UDS transport and per-spawn process authentication (proposal reuse row line 246) | REUSE WITH SMALL EXTENSION | Per-spawn private 0700 UDS directory with owner-PID-tagged name and crash sweeping (core/agents/manager/loader.go:744-765, core/agents/manager/sockets.go:34-65); 32-byte crypto/rand bearer required on every call (loader.go:768-778); callback socket 0600 with the spawn-time principal bound as the authoritative subject (core/policy/callback.go:161-172, loader.go:836-843); own process group plus pgid file (loader.go:718-721, 905-910). However UDS is opt-in (useUDS default false, loader.go:297; WithUDS at 416 has one non-test caller, core/toolbox/session/session.go:276) and TCP loopback is the default, which cannot work under Linux --unshare-net (core/runners/sandbox/bwrap.go:134-136). | Make UDS mandatory for provider spawns (no TCP fallback), declare Windows providers unsupported (loader.go:745, core/runners/sandbox/sandbox.go:94-103), and extend the sweeper to stale codefly-perms-*.sock files or move that socket inside the per-spawn 0700 directory (core/agents/manager/sockets.go:44, core/policy/callback.go:152-156). |
| Cassette record/replay testing (proposal reuse row line 260) | NEW | Searching core/ and cli/ for 'cassette' returns only documentation (core/docs/*), with no recorder, replayer, matcher or sanitizer implementation anywhere in the tree. | Implement recorder/replayer inside the new host broker so enforcement stays active during replay, with a deterministic matcher that normalizes host-injected non-deterministic values (Idempotency-Key derived from operation/attempt IDs, request IDs, dates) and canonicalizes Stripe form-encoded bodies; sanitize before any disk write and fail the recording if a secret-shaped field survives. |

## 7. Trust-boundary and permission analysis

### Lane 5 required artifact: allow/deny truth tables and the ambient-CRUD path

#### Legend
- **Manifest** = provider.codefly.yaml PermissionPolicy ceiling (CeilingPDP, permissions.go Allows)
- **Role** = saas-starter grant via SaasPDP.Backend.Decide
- **Scoped** = ScopedAuthorization exact-match (action/resource/audience/CatalogDigest/RequestDigest/caveats)
- **Plan** = action present in the exact approved plan (proposal moment 2/3)
- Effective decision is the INTERSECTION (proposal:322-334); any layer denies ⇒ DENY.

#### Table A — canonical action authorization (mutation: provider.delete on `provider:stripe/ws/local/billing/stripe.webhook-endpoint/we_123`)

| # | Manifest declares | Role grants | Scoped token | In approved plan | Backend reachable | Result | Enforced at |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | yes | yes | valid, exact | yes | yes | ALLOW | CeilingPDP→SaasPDP→scoped verify→broker plan-check |
| A2 | NO | yes | — | yes | yes | DENY (manifest-ceiling) | pdp_ceiling.go:113 |
| A3 | yes | NO | — | yes | yes | DENY (role) | pdp_saas.go:205-210 |
| A4 | yes | yes | expired/wrong digest | yes | yes | DENY (scoped) | scoped_auth_v2.go:319,340-345 |
| A5 | yes | yes | valid but action=provider.observe | yes(as delete) | yes | MUST DENY — derived-action mismatch | NEW broker derivation (not built) |
| A6 | yes | yes | valid | NO (not in plan) | yes | DENY (plan) | NEW per-request plan check (not built) |
| A7 | yes | yes | valid | yes | NO | DENY (fail-closed) | pdp_saas.go:297-311 |
| A8 | yes | yes | valid | yes | revoked mid-Apply | DENY at next action | NEW per-action re-check + TRL (not built) |

Rows A5, A6, A8 are the load-bearing NEW enforcement the proposal assumes but that no existing primitive provides. A5 is the confused-deputy: the callback (callback.go:286-298) would authorize the *claimed* provider.observe.

#### Table B — read vs remote-data vs sink writes (distinct actions required)

| Operation | Action needed | Also needs remote-data authz? | Separate from public? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Validate (offline) | provider.validate | no | n/a |
| Observe (remote read) | provider.observe | YES — reading the account/resource is itself authorized | n/a |
| Write public config | provider.project.public | no | YES |
| Persist secret via sink | provider.project.secret | no | YES — must be a distinct grant (proposal:402-403) |

Proposal correctly separates project.public from project.secret; the matcher supports these as exact actions. Gap: no primitive currently enforces that a provider.project.public grant cannot commit a SECRET-classified output — that is NEW host commit-time logic (proposal moment 4).

#### Table C — principal/authority context differences

| Context | Principal.Kind | Expiry re-check | Delegation cap | Approval channel |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Human CLI | human | Load + gateway (gateway.go:120) | n/a | none wired (finding MEDIUM) |
| CI | service (OrgID required, principal.go:139-141) | same | n/a | none |
| Mind/agent | agent (AgentID required) | same | MaxDelegationDepth=3 (hardening.go:272) | escalation via agent SDK + saas-starter grantor |

#### The ambient-CRUD path (one broad grant → remote CRUD)

```
Role grant: provider.delete on provider:stripe/*        (broadest expressible ceiling; matcher permits only trailing *)
        ∩ Manifest: provider.delete on provider:stripe/* (proposal:722 example)
        ⇒ globMatch prefix 'provider:stripe/' matches
          provider:stripe/<ANY-ws>/<ANY-env>/<ANY-binding>/<ANY-type>/<ANY-remote-id>
        ⇒ authority to delete EVERY Stripe resource in EVERY workspace/environment/binding
```
Root cause: (a) validateGlobPattern rejects mid-path wildcards (permissions.go:415-428) so operators cannot scope 'any binding within one environment'; (b) the doc's own manifest example models the broad form. Because remote-id is LAST in the resource path, a *narrow* ceiling `provider:stripe/<ws>/<env>/<binding>/<type>/*` IS expressible — the fix is to mandate that shape for production and reject `provider:<p>/*`/bare `*` grants at admission.

#### Safe-to-log vs must-redact (policy logs/receipts)
- SAFE: principal_id, principal_kind, org_id, agent_id, delegation_depth, action, redacted resource, plan/scoped-auth/approval/operation/attempt/receipt IDs, allow/deny/fail-closed outcome, decision latency (observability.go DecisionEvent).
- MUST REDACT / never present: principal_token (principal.go:211), vendor error bodies (do not map into PDPDecision.Reason; pdp_saas.go:297-311 embeds only internal err), webhook secrets/DSNs/API keys, provider request/response bodies.

### Enforcement-point table (Lanes 6 and 7)

Legend for OS coverage: **macOS** = `sandbox-exec` Seatbelt profile built by `core/runners/sandbox/sandboxexec.go`; **Linux** = `bwrap` argv built by `core/runners/sandbox/bwrap.go`; **host** = in-process Codefly (CLI) code, OS-independent. "None" means no mechanism enforces the claim today.

| # | Claim in the proposal | Mechanism that would enforce it | macOS coverage | Linux coverage | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Provider cannot read workspace files / projected `*.secret.env` (inv. 12/13, lines 638-646) | OS sandbox filesystem read policy | **None.** `(allow default)` + only 4 home denies (sandboxexec.go:100-124); read paths explicitly "advisory" (162-170) | **Enforced.** Only declared `--ro-bind` paths + `/usr,/lib,/bin,/sbin,/etc` visible (bwrap.go:107-131) | BLOCKER on macOS. Deny `file-read*` by default, or at minimum deny workspace `configurations/**`, `~/.codefly/**`, projection output dir |
| 2 | Provider cannot read `~/.codefly` incl. other bindings' provider state (proposal line 1248) | OS sandbox read policy | **None** (same as #1); deny-list entry `~/.config/codefly/secrets` is a dead path, real home is `~/.codefly` (core/resources/global.go:57) | **Enforced** (not bound into the sandbox) | BLOCKER on macOS; fix the dead deny-list entry regardless |
| 3 | Provider cannot write the workspace (inv. 19, control #14) | OS sandbox write policy | **Enforced.** `(deny file-write*)` + allowlist (sandboxexec.go:104-118) | **Enforced.** Only `--bind` write paths (bwrap.go:126-127) | Host-side ceiling missing: `write_paths` is self-declared by the manifest (see #7) |
| 4 | Provider has no direct external network (inv. 11, control #5) | OS sandbox network policy | **Enforced only if the manifest says so.** loopback → `(deny network*)` + localhost re-allow (sandboxexec.go:130-147); `open` → no rule at all | **Enforced only if the manifest says so.** `--unshare-net` for deny/loopback (bwrap.go:134-136) | HIGH: nothing rejects `network: open`; production admission accepts it (loader.go:611-615, toolbox.go:186-197; proven by toolbox-web/manifest_test.go:13-17) |
| 5 | Provider cannot reach other local services (implied by "loopback only for host comms", lines 638-641) | OS sandbox network policy | **Not enforced.** All loopback traffic allowed (sandboxexec.go:142-144) → local DBs, other agents, any local proxy reachable | **Enforced.** `--unshare-net` gives a private netns; host loopback unreachable | MEDIUM on macOS; providers need no TCP loopback at all if the host mandates `WithUDS` → prefer `NetworkDeny` + UDS grant |
| 6 | Provider gets no host secrets except handles (control #7, inv. 13, line 866) | Host: spawn environment construction | **None.** `cmd.Env = os.Environ()` (loader.go:722) | **None.** Same, plus no `--clearenv` | BLOCKER: allowlist the child environment; keep `CODEFLY_SCOPED_AUTHZ_SECRET` out of provider spawns (use Ed25519 v2) |
| 7 | Manifest declarations are a ceiling, not a grant (inv. 7) — for *capacity* | Host: per-kind maximum sandbox policy before spawn | **None** | **None** | HIGH: `policy.SandboxPolicy` is applied verbatim (permissions.go:174-206; launch.go:199-235). Add a provider ceiling: network ∈ {loopback, deny}, `write_paths` ⊆ per-spawn UDS dir, `read_paths` = ∅ |
| 8 | Provider cannot read other same-UID processes' secrets | OS sandbox PID/proc isolation | Not applicable in the same form (no `/proc`); `(allow default)` still permits broad process info | **Not enforced.** `--proc /proc` in the host PID namespace, no `--unshare-pid`/`--unshare-ipc` (bwrap.go:107-121) → `/proc/<pid>/environ` readable | HIGH (confidence MEDIUM): add `--unshare-pid --unshare-ipc --unshare-uts --clearenv` |
| 9 | Provider cannot exec helper binaries to bypass the broker (scenario 6) | OS sandbox exec + network policy | Exec **allowed** (`allow default`), but children inherit the profile → no egress under deny/loopback. Residual: `mach-lookup` open → DNS resolver / LaunchServices covert channels (inference, needs experiment) | Exec **allowed** (`/usr`,`/bin` ro-bound), children share the netns → no egress | MEDIUM: OS layer is inheritance-correct but exec is unrestricted; treat provider `exec` as a policy violation and rely on network isolation, not exec denial |
| 10 | Provider cannot impersonate another principal (inv. 6) | Host: callback binds spawn-time principal | **Enforced** (loader.go:836-843, core/policy/callback.go:130-134) | **Enforced** | No gap — this is genuine REUSE AS-IS |
| 11 | Provider A cannot reach provider B's callback socket or state | UDS path grants + socket modes | **Enforced** under a sandbox: narrow `network-*` regex per socket path (sandboxexec.go:154-159); 0600 socket (callback.go:167-172) | **Enforced**: fresh `/tmp` tmpfs, only `--bind-try` sockets visible | LOW: stale `codefly-perms-*.sock` never swept (sockets.go:44); name is PID+nanotime, not crypto-random |
| 12 | Host↔provider transport is authenticated and private (control #4) | Host: per-spawn UDS + bearer token | **Enforced only when `WithUDS`** — default is TCP loopback (loader.go:297, 745) | Same, and TCP loopback **cannot work** under `--unshare-net` | HIGH: mandate UDS for providers; no TCP fallback; Windows unsupported (loader.go:745, sandbox.go:94-103) |
| 13 | Only manifest-approved origins are contacted (control #6) | Host broker: origin allowlist | **N/A (host)** — no host implementation exists; the only allowlist is in-process in the plugin (toolbox-web/server.go:207-210) with `network: open` | **N/A (host)** — same | HIGH: broker is NEW code in core. In-process allowlists in a process with raw egress are unenforceable |
| 14 | Exact origin ⇒ exact TCP peer | Host broker: explicit `Transport.Proxy`, DNS resolve-and-pin, private-range dial guard | **N/A (host)** — precedent inherits `DefaultTransport` (`Proxy: ProxyFromEnvironment`, GOROOT/src/net/http/transport.go:46-57, 491-506) and never validates resolved IPs | **N/A (host)** — same | HIGH: pin proxy (nil by default), resolve once + pin IP, reject loopback/link-local/RFC1918/ULA/CGNAT unless declared; blocks DNS-rebinding SSRF from the trusted host |
| 15 | Provider cannot supply its own credentials (inv. 17, line 1435) | Host broker: header **allowlist** + URL-userinfo rejection | **N/A (host)** — precedent sets all caller headers verbatim (server.go:234-240) | **N/A (host)** — same | HIGH: denylist is insufficient — Go turns URL userinfo into `Authorization: Basic` (GOROOT/src/net/http/client.go:251-257) |
| 16 | Credentials never cross to an unapproved origin on redirect (line 1442) | Host broker: no redirects on credentialed requests | **N/A (host)** — precedent follows redirects to any allowlisted host (server.go:78-84) | **N/A (host)** — same | MEDIUM: Go forwards `Authorization` to subdomain matches; broker must not follow at all when credentialed |
| 17 | One-time secrets never reach the provider process (inv. 18, lines 505-530) | Host broker: capture before response hand-off | **N/A (host)** — no implementation | **N/A (host)** — no implementation | HIGH: provider-set `Accept-Encoding` defeats capture (Go does not decompress caller-requested gzip, GOROOT/src/net/http/transport.go:195-202) → broker must own `Accept-Encoding` and inflate under a ratio cap |
| 18 | Responses are bounded (line 653) | Host broker: fail-closed caps | **N/A (host)** — precedent truncates at 4 MiB and flags `truncated:true` (server.go:251-260) | **N/A (host)** — same | MEDIUM: truncation destroys an unrecoverable one-time secret after a successful mutation; need fail-closed caps + JSON depth/duplicate-key/value-length limits |
| 19 | Every remote mutation matches an exact approved plan action (inv. 8/22) | Host broker: derive action+resource from the parsed request, compare to plan, re-consult PDP | **N/A (host)** — no implementation, and no manifest method/path/body schema to intersect (lines 695-742 vs 1437) | **N/A (host)** — same | HIGH/protocol-breaking: bind `{id}` path params and declared body fields to the plan action; add per-action request budgets |
| 20 | Retries never duplicate a remote effect (inv. 24) | Host broker + provider idempotency identity | **N/A (host)** | **N/A (host)** | MEDIUM: Stripe prunes keys after ≥24 h and then treats a reuse as a new request → need a declared retry freshness window, else UNCERTAIN |

#### Summary answer to the brief's question

*"Prove or refute: a compromised provider cannot reach an unapproved origin or read workspace/secrets."*

- **Unapproved origin, Linux:** *plausibly true today* — `--unshare-net` is a real kernel boundary — **but only if** the host refuses `network: open` (row 4/7, no such check exists) and mandates UDS (row 12).
- **Unapproved origin, macOS:** *conditionally true* — Seatbelt `(deny network*)` is enforced under loopback/deny, but every loopback service is reachable (row 5) and `(allow default)` leaves out-of-process helper channels open (row 9); the package itself disclaims hostile-binary resistance (`core/runners/sandbox/doc.go:27-32`).
- **Workspace/secrets, Linux:** *true for the filesystem* (row 1/2), *false for other processes* (row 8, `/proc/<pid>/environ`), *false for the environment* (row 6).
- **Workspace/secrets, macOS:** **refuted** (rows 1, 2, 6) — everything the user can read, the provider can read.
- **Exfiltration:** even with all of the above fixed, the broker's manifest-derived origin belongs to the provider's own vendor, which for a third-party plugin is the attacker. Host-mediated egress constrains *credential theft*, not *data exfiltration* (see the BLOCKER on allowlisted-origin exfiltration).

#### Verdict on `toolbox-web`

**Do not extract. Reimplement as a host broker in `core`; leave `toolbox-web` alone functionally for now.**

- Dependency direction already permits it: `toolbox-web` → `core v0.2.23` (`toolbox-web/go.mod:8`); `core` has no dependency on `toolbox-web`. Putting the guard in `core` (e.g. `core/net/brokerguard` + `core/provider/broker`) keeps the arrow pointing the right way and lets `toolbox-web` consume it later.
- There is nothing substantial to extract: the whole guard is a map lookup (`server.go:207-210`), a `CheckRedirect` closure (`76-85`) and a `LimitReader` (`251-260`) — while ~90% of the broker's requirement list (credential handles, capture, proxy/DNS/IP pinning, TLS/CA policy, header allowlist, idempotency injection, rate-limit normalization, cassettes) does not exist anywhere.
- Separately, `toolbox-web`'s own posture should be fixed on its own merits: with `network: open` (`toolbox.codefly.yaml:26`) its in-process allowlist is decorative, and `core/policy/canonical.go`'s routing of `curl`/`wget` "to the Web toolbox (with domain allowlist)" therefore does not create the boundary it advertises. The correct fix is the same broker: make the toolbox a *request descriptor* and execute the fetch in the host, then drop `network: open`. OS-enforced per-destination egress is only partially achievable as an alternative (Seatbelt supports `(allow network-outbound (remote ip "1.2.3.4:443"))`-style predicates — the profile already uses the `remote ip` form at `sandboxexec.go:144` — but plain `bwrap` has no equivalent without `slirp4netns` or a local proxy, and pinned IPs drift with DNS).

Legend: **[RAW]** = surface holds raw secret bytes; **[REF]** = reference/handle/fingerprint only; **[NONE]** = surface provably never sees raw bytes under the proposal + current code. Citations are to current code where the step exists today, otherwise marked *(new P1)*.

#### Summary of processes that can see raw bytes across all traces
1Password `op` CLI (+vault), Codefly CLI host process (resolver cache, broker header build, capture buffer, projection writer staging), OS temp/staging + final owner-only secret files (local sink), service/build process environments and their children, docker daemon container config (containerized runs), TLS sessions, vendor servers, production secret sink (P5). **Never** (post-fix): provider agent process, plans, state, receipts, cassettes, journal. The two design-level breaches of that 'never' list found in this review: inherited spawn environment (loader.go:722) and read-path vendor responses carrying secrets (Resend list/retrieve, Sentry client-keys).

## 9. Lifecycle and failure analysis

### Phase assessment (Validate / Observe / Plan / Apply / Project / Doctor)

**Lane 4: protocol coherence and evolvability.** The phase model is fundamentally sound: no long-lived Configure session (each request carries ProviderContext) is the right call and matches both the restart-safe toolbox leaf precedent (toolbox.proto's stateless RPC surface) and Terraform/Pulumi lessons; Observe/Plan separation with offline deterministic Plan is cleanly achievable for all three initial providers (Stripe: diff desired webhook/events against observed endpoint list; Sentry: pure observe/project; Resend: domains+webhooks), and UNCERTAIN outcomes map onto the existing execution receipt stage (core/proto/codefly/execution/v1/execution.proto:22-23) and structured failures (base/v0/failure.proto:150-170 with retryable/retry_after). But the protocol section as written cannot be frozen, because it omits its own largest surface: the provider→host HTTP-broker request-descriptor channel, the checkpoint channel, and the output-commit channel are all host-side services the provider must call, none has a proto, a version, or negotiation rules — the document's 7-RPC table covers only the host→provider direction, yet Observe and Apply are unimplementable without the reverse direction, and the manifest's method/path/secret-capture declarations must syntactically match a descriptor schema that does not exist. Second, Apply's orchestration weight is wrong: a single Apply RPC in which the provider executes the whole plan and 'emits checkpoints' cannot satisfy invariant 25 (every externally visible effect durably checkpointed before the next mutation) or per-action revocation checks unless the transport is streaming-with-acks or, better, host-driven per-action execution — the simpler host-driven shape also converts 'provider must reject unplanned actions' from a promise into a construction. Third, approval stability is broken by design: the plan digest binds the observed-state digest while canonical observation is required to include volatile fields (rate-limit status, retry-after, request IDs, observation time), so every refresh invalidates approval. Remaining gaps: output placement is ambiguous (which RPC carries final typed outputs; whether observe-only bindings run a projection-only Apply), Doctor's remote half duplicates Observe, state versioning lacks a minimum-upgradable-from range, downgrade rules, and an opaque provider-data field for unknown-field survival, pagination/listing completeness is unmodeled although uniqueness decisions depend on it, RPC size bounds are unstated, and the document is internally inconsistent about whether the protocol is v0 or 1. None of this requires abandoning the shape; all of it must be resolved before v0 freeze, and the freeze/negotiate split is enumerated in the special artifact.

**Lane 16: error model, rate limits, retries, reliability.** The taxonomy reuse claim is accurate: core/proto/codefly/base/v0/failure.proto:12-175 already provides neutral FailureCode values (UNAUTHENTICATED=8, PERMISSION_DENIED=9, NOT_FOUND=4, RATE_LIMITED=18, TIMEOUT=16), a `retryable` bool (:158-159) and `retry_after` (:169-170), and executionreceipt supports UNCERTAIN (core/proto/codefly/execution/v1/execution.proto:22-23, core/executionreceipt/receipt.go:224) — REUSE AS-IS for the envelope. The reliability design around it, however, is materially under-specified: no single layer owns the retry loop (CLI coordinator, broker, and provider agent are all plausible readers of `retryable=true`), the taxonomy has no delivery-status dimension distinguishing 'request never sent' from 'sent, outcome unknown', and the proposal's own MaxUses=1 scoped authorization for mutations conflicts with its 'stable operation ID across transport retries' promise — a legitimate idempotency-keyed retry either exhausts the token or MaxUses is meaningless. Rate-limit handling is split between broker (header normalization) and provider (error translation) without an authority rule, so a provider that misclassifies a 429 or a post-send timeout as retryable can drive a generic retry layer into duplicate mutations, exactly what the brief forbids. Pagination completeness is unmodeled: Observe reports present/missing/inaccessible/ambiguous but has no 'enumeration truncated' state, and the broker's mandated smaller-than-4MiB body caps (toolbox baseline: toolbox-web/server.go:21) make truncation more likely, turning 'missing' into a duplicate CREATE. Offline behavior for Validate/Plan/UpgradeState is credibly enforced by sandbox+broker design and Tier-2 conformance ('offline RPCs truly have no network'), which is sound. Verdict: the error envelope reuse is real; the retry/ambiguity/ownership semantics must be specified in P1 before any provider is written (findings 1, 5).

**Lane 18: doctor, observability, metrics, supportability.** The claimed doctor foundation genuinely exists and is high quality: cli/cmd/doctor_workspace.go is strictly read-only with an explicit documented contract ('must not create, modify, or delete any file, and must not start agents, containers, or services', :92-95, :684-686), stable diagnostic codes with a breaking-change rule (:24-27), versioned JSON (:46), a 30s bound (:757), and a real-provider qualification record (cli/docs/doctor-workspace-qualification.md). But the proposal's plan to fold 12 provider sections including remote vendor calls into `codefly doctor workspace` contradicts that contract on three axes: it must start provider agents, it adds unbounded remote latency to a 30s local command, and it consumes shared vendor rate budgets — Resend's documented limit is 10 requests/second per team across all keys with 429 (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/introduction), so a CI doctor loop throttles concurrent real operations. Doctor mutation-safety is asserted (security control 9) but the only per-request broker check the proposal specifies re-validates against 'the approved plan', which Doctor does not have — the operation-kind→HTTP-method ceiling must be made normative (finding 2). The proposed diagnostic code list has no Codefly-policy-denied code (only vendor `provider.auth.rejected`), so scenario 40's operator cannot distinguish a missing role grant from a bad Stripe key, and the new `provider.*` codes collide with the existing doctor's `provider_*` codes which mean SECRET provider (doctor_workspace.go:37-40). Metrics: the existing PDPMetrics baseline is label-free counters (core/policy/observability.go:25-100) — safe — but P5 promises operation metrics with no label allowlist; binding names, workspace hashes, and remote IDs must be forbidden as labels. Receipt correlation IDs (operation/attempt/plan/receipt) are well designed; support-bundle safety relies on assertions about provider-authored free text with no host-side secret-shape scan (the scan is specified only for cassettes). Findings 2, 3, 4, 9, 10.

### Lane 10 required artifact: failure timelines (11 cases, proposal-as-written)

Legend for each case: **T** = timeline, **Outcome** = what the proposal-as-written produces, **Verdict** = whether UNCERTAIN + next-plan reconciliation suffices, or manual intervention is required, or the proposal specifies nothing.

Common substrate (verified): receipts journal enforces terminal-requires-durable-STARTED (cli/pkg/executionjournal/journal.go:219-224), one terminal stage per attempt (journal.go:205-211), immutable attempt binding (journal.go:225-232), and offers `IncompleteAttempts` for an append-UNCERTAIN-on-restart sweep (journal.go:432-470). Provider state is a separate per-binding bbolt store (provider-plugins.md:1248-1252). No transaction spans the two stores.

---

#### 1. Crash before any remote mutation (after approval, inside Apply)
**T:** ADMITTED receipt → STARTED receipt → crash before first broker send.
**Outcome:** No remote or local effect. Journal has STARTED without terminal. Next invocation's recovery sweep (implied at provider-plugins.md:2036, wiring unspecified) appends UNCERTAIN. Next Observe shows no resource; plan re-proposes the same CREATE; approval may still be valid if the plan digest is unchanged and TTL (provider-plugins.md:462-463) has not lapsed, otherwise re-approve.
**Verdict:** UNCERTAIN + reconciliation suffices. No manual intervention. Requires the (currently unspecified) mandatory entry-point recovery sweep.

#### 2. Crash after request send, before response
**T:** STARTED → broker sends POST (e.g. Stripe webhook create) → crash. Remote effect unknown.
**Outcome:** Proposal requires checkpoints only "immediately after each externally visible effect" (provider-plugins.md:1183); there is no required pre-send checkpoint, so after restart the host may have no durable record that a request was in flight, nor the idempotency key, nor the prospective resource ID. Sweep appends UNCERTAIN. Next Observe must discover whether the resource exists; a same-URL match is an unmanaged conflict (auto-adoption forbidden, provider-plugins.md:1293-1294).
**Verdict:** UNCERTAIN alone is insufficient. Recovery is semi-manual (approved IMPORT) and, for secret-bearing creates, destructive (REPLACE, since the secret was in the lost response). Required fixes: mandatory pre-send checkpoint {action, idempotency key, prospective ID}; explicit same-attempt same-key idempotent replay carve-out from the no-auto-retry rule (provider-plugins.md:1213-1214 vs 1224-1226); ownership stamp in vendor metadata.

#### 3. Crash after response, before checkpoint
**T:** STARTED → send → response received → (broker one-time capture path: parse → extract → sink persist → checkpoint reference → hand handle to provider) → crash somewhere before the action-completion checkpoint.
**Outcome:** Two sub-windows. (a) Crash before sink persist: remote resource exists, secret irrecoverably lost; explicitly covered — operation becomes UNCERTAIN, later actions stop, safe metadata checkpointed (provider-plugins.md:533-535). (b) Crash after sink persist + reference checkpoint (broker checkpoints "before allowing another mutation", provider-plugins.md:525) but before the provider's action result is checkpointed: secret is safe and its reference durable; remote ID recoverability depends on what the capture checkpoint contains (unspecified whether it includes the remote ID — it should).
**Verdict:** (a) UNCERTAIN + reconciliation leads to supply-secret or approved REPLACE — semi-manual, inherent to the vendor model. (b) suffices if the capture checkpoint is specified to include the remote resource identity; otherwise degenerates to case 2's manual import.

#### 4. Remote success + secret-sink failure
**T:** STARTED → create succeeds → broker capture extracts secret → sink Put/Commit fails.
**Outcome:** Explicitly specified: UNCERTAIN, no later action runs, safe response metadata checkpointed (provider-plugins.md:533-535). Remote ID is known (response arrived), so state can record the owned resource with secret-missing status. Sink preparation is required before the mutating request is sent (provider-plugins.md:540-542), which shrinks but does not eliminate this window.
**Verdict:** UNCERTAIN + reconciliation suffices structurally; the convergence plan is "supply or recover the missing secret" or approved REPLACE (provider-plugins.md:1217-1220). Human approval required for REPLACE; no undocumented gap.

#### 5. State commit + projection failure
**T:** Remote effects done and checkpointed → secret sink committed → public configuration projection fails local validation.
**Outcome:** Projection writer restores the prior output set (provider-plugins.md:983-984); the persisted one-time secret is explicitly NOT rolled back (provider-plugins.md:536-539 — correct: preserving the only copy beats fake atomicity). Listed as a canonical partial example (provider-plugins.md:1210). Next plan proposes only PROJECT_OUTPUT (action exists, provider-plugins.md:1122).
**Verdict:** Reconciliation suffices; rerun re-projects; no manual intervention. Unspecified detail: the terminal receipt classification for this attempt (FAILED with partial checkpoints vs UNCERTAIN) — should be FAILED-with-checkpoints since every remote outcome is known; the proposal never says.

#### 6. Projection success + terminal receipt failure
**T:** Everything succeeds → crash between the last local write and the terminal receipt append.
**Outcome:** Journal has STARTED without terminal; state and outputs are complete and digest-consistent. Sweep appends UNCERTAIN (journal.go:432-434 pattern). Next plan is provably no-diff.
**Verdict:** Reconciliation fully suffices; no manual intervention. Audit permanently shows UNCERTAIN with a follow-up empty plan as the effective success proof — honest and acceptable. Best-supported case.

#### 7. Concurrent plan/apply (two processes)
**T:** A plans, B plans (same prior digest) → A applies, state advances → B applies.
**Outcome:** B must be denied via prior-state/output digest mismatch (plan digest binds them, provider-plugins.md:1153-1157; "apply rejects stale observations and changed output/state digests", control 11 ~provider-plugins.md:1410) with diagnostic provider.plan.stale (provider-plugins.md:1570).
**Verdict:** Suffices ONLY IF the digest CAS executes atomically inside the inter-process lock's critical section at apply admission — the proposal specifies the lock in one clause (provider-plugins.md:1251-1252) and never the atomicity, hold duration, or read-only concurrency (bbolt exclusive open would make concurrent doctor/observe fail with a raw timeout, cf. journal.go:98). Specification gap; no manual intervention once specified.

#### 8. Stale approval
**T:** Plan approved → observation refreshed / endpoint changed / provider upgraded / sink changed → apply attempted.
**Outcome:** Approval binds plan digest and exact actions (provider-plugins.md:551-558); any bound-input change invalidates it; enforcement at plan admission and again per brokered request and per output commit (per-operation authorization moments 2-4, provider-plugins.md:434-446); TTL bounds delay (provider-plugins.md:462-463). Deny changes nothing (invariant 33).
**Verdict:** Suffices; re-plan/re-approve; no state involvement beyond the deny record. Residual weakness: "artifact digest when available" (provider-plugins.md:1150) means a same-version binary swap is invisible pre-P5 — see scenario 25.

#### 9. Remote deletion outside Codefly
**T:** User deletes the webhook in the Stripe dashboard → next Observe.
**Outcome:** Observation reports the resource "missing" (state enum at provider-plugins.md:1110); ownership record persists in state; plan proposes CREATE to converge on the stable address (provider-plugins.md:1216), producing a new signing secret whose sink write supersedes the old reference. Drift diagnostic provider.drift.detected available (provider-plugins.md:1567).
**Verdict:** Reconciliation suffices with normal CREATE approval; no UNCERTAIN; no manual intervention beyond approval. (Events lost while the webhook was absent are an application-level concern, out of provider-state scope.)

#### 10. Remote manual edit
**T:** User changes URL/event set in the dashboard → next Observe.
**Outcome:** Drift on provider-owned canonical fields (provider-plugins.md:1266) → UPDATE plan converging to desired (exactly the human plan example, provider-plugins.md:1503-1521). Fields outside the provider-owned set are ignored by construction.
**Verdict:** Reconciliation suffices. Residual: the provider-owned field set must be part of the versioned state schema, or an agent upgrade silently reclassifies what counts as drift (connects to case 11 and the downgrade finding).

#### 11. Provider upgrade mid-operation
**T:** Apply in flight → provider agent package replaced on disk → (a) running process continues, or (b) crash then recovery resolves the newer agent.
**Outcome:** (a) The spawned process is unaffected; the operation completes under the old code; state records the old agent identity/digest (provider-plugins.md:1265). (b) On recovery, sweep appends UNCERTAIN; the newer agent cannot resume the old plan — approval is invalidated by the upgrade (provider-plugins.md:556-558) and the plan digest binds agent identity (provider-plugins.md:1150); the host must run UpgradeState before the recovery plan (provider-plugins.md:1229-1233), which must migrate pending/partial/uncertain checkpoints (provider-plugins.md:1273) — only true if checkpoints are inside the versioned state schema, which the proposal never states.
**Verdict:** Reconciliation suffices once two things are specified: checkpoints are versioned state (migrated by UpgradeState), and the host refuses the reverse direction (older agent, newer state) fail-closed. Neither is currently written down.

---

#### Summary matrix

| # | Case | Specified? | UNCERTAIN + reconcile enough? | Manual steps |
|---|------|-----------|-------------------------------|--------------|
| 1 | crash pre-mutation | partial (sweep unwired) | yes | none |
| 2 | crash post-send pre-response | no (no pre-send checkpoint) | no | approve IMPORT; REPLACE for secrets |
| 3 | crash post-response pre-checkpoint | partial (533-535, 525) | mostly | supply secret / approve REPLACE |
| 4 | remote ok + sink fail | yes (533-542) | yes | approve REPLACE if secret unrecoverable |
| 5 | state ok + projection fail | yes (983-984, 536-539, 1210) | yes | none |
| 6 | done + no terminal receipt | pattern exists (journal.go:432-470) | yes | none |
| 7 | concurrent plan/apply | partial (lock atomicity unspecified) | yes, once CAS-under-lock specified | none |
| 8 | stale approval | yes (551-558, 434-446) | yes | re-approve |
| 9 | remote deleted externally | yes (1110, 1216) | yes | approve CREATE |
| 10 | remote manually edited | yes (1266, 1503-1521) | yes | approve UPDATE |
| 11 | provider upgrade mid-op | partial (556-558, 1229-1233; checkpoint versioning + downgrade unstated) | yes, once specified | re-plan/re-approve |

### Lane 11/12 required artifacts

#### A. Configuration projection: current capability vs proposed requirement

| Proposed writer/contract requirement | Current Codefly capability | Verdict | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Read generic named configurations, public+secret, per profile | Full: path-named env/yaml files, secret/ref classification by suffix | EXISTS | core/configurations/local_reader.go:55-148, 269-305 |
| Resolve secret references | 1Password only, read-only `op read`, per-load cache | EXISTS (read-only) | core/configurations/secrets.go:126-144, 171-216 |
| Host-owned atomic writer | Nothing — no write path in core/configurations | NEW | whole package; shell seed only (provider-common.sh:129-174) |
| Multi-file atomic rename of output set | Impossible as written (per-file rename; shared dir) | REDESIGN | local_reader.go:64; provider-common.sh:146-174 |
| Git-ignore verification of secret targets | Shell only (`git check-ignore`) | EXTRACT to Go | provider-common.sh:135-144 |
| 0600/0700 modes, owner-only temp staging | Shell only (`umask 077`, `install -m 600`) | EXTRACT to Go | provider-common.sh:129-133, 172-173 |
| CAS against approved output digest | Nothing; shell has `cmp` + `--force` | NEW | provider-common.sh:160-171 |
| PUBLIC/SENSITIVE/SECRET/browser/purpose classification | One bool: `ConfigurationValue.secret` | NEW proto or lossy | configuration.proto:8-15 |
| Sensitive-key defense in depth | Substring heuristic incl. `DSN`, `AUTH`, `TOKEN` | EXISTS (collides with public DSN) | core/resources/configuration.go:178-186; base_builder.go:868-880 |
| Contract identity `billing@1` w/ validation | No schema artifact anywhere; consumers bind bare names | NEW + undefined owner | service.codefly.yaml `workspace-configuration-dependencies`; core/resources/service.go:57 |
| Consumer purpose scoping (runtime vs build) | Whole-configuration-name granularity only | NEW contract rule needed | cli/pkg/orchestration/runner.go:273; env_variables_manager.go:489-513 |
| Ownership metadata | Unspecified location/git status | NEW + undefined | proposal writer invariant 9 |
| Writable secret sink | None (resolver read-only) | NEW (proposal admits) | secrets.go:126-144 |
| SDK injection unchanged by provider setup | Projection targets same files the loader reads → same runner injection path | VERIFIED SAFE | local_reader.go:64; runner.go:273 |

#### B. Endpoint/exposure-mode resolution trace (local-dogfood, billing webhook)

```
binding.spec.callback {service: auth-sidecar, endpoint: rest, path: /v1/billing/webhook}
  |
  v
[1] identity check: module+service+endpoint required (endpoint.proto:20-24)
    GAP: binding schema has no `module`; starter has auth-sidecar in 2 module trees
  |
  v
[2] local origin resolution — two sources, can disagree:
    (a) static: port = ToNamedPort(ws,mod,svc,name[+scope]) (port.go:117-132)
        -> deterministic, stable across restarts       [callback stability: OK]
    (b) flow-authoritative: daemon-reported bound addr (endpoints.go:100-124)
        -> containers/persisted flows may bind a DIFFERENT port
    FALLBACK IS SILENT (a) when daemon down  -> wrong-port registration path
  |
  v
[3] exposure mode:
    local-direct    -> loopback origin only (WorkOS browser redirect: works,
                       browser can reach localhost; no remote resource)
    local-forwarded -> Stripe CLI / Resend CLI forwards remote->loopback
                       * secret printed to terminal (Stripe) / temp webhook
                         destroyed on exit (Resend docs/cli)
                       * no principal, no receipts, no supervision in v1
                       * restart => stale projected secret [no doctor check]
    public          -> needs (env,svc,endpoint)->HTTPS origin resolver: DOES
                       NOT EXIST (Ingress consumed only by gitops render);
                       Stripe mandates public HTTPS + TLS1.2/1.3
    existing        -> operator-supplied remote ID + secret ref; observe-only
                       unless imported — coherent with ownership model
  |
  v
[4] plan digest binds resolved origins -> approval invalidated on change
    Apply precondition 'non-expired endpoint' UNDEFINED (no TTL, no
    mandated re-resolution) -> scenario 31 enforcement is unspecified
  |
  v
[5] webhook registered before app ready -> vendor retry absorbs
    (Stripe test mode: 3 retries over a few hours) -> bounded loss window;
    durable dedup receiver is application-side (starter), not Codefly
```

#### C. Scenario 18 classification truth table (Sentry DSN)

| Projection choice | Sink | Load result | Deploy (restricted) | Browser | Git risk |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DSN public, public file (correct; = starter error-tracking.env.example) | billing/public env | loads | passes only if key exempted from heuristic (`DSN` substring trips IsSensitiveKey → needs classification-aware exemption) | NEXT_PUBLIC_ inlined | none (DSN safe-public per Sentry) |
| DSN secret=true | *.secret.env | loads locally | HARD FAIL base_builder.go:868-880 | likely absent → silent no error-tracking | ignored file, none |
| DSN secret=true | *.secret.ref.env | REJECTED (plaintext, secrets.go:66-78) | n/a | n/a | none |
| AUTH_TOKEN public (under-classification) | public env | loads | fails restricted only; native runs fine | exposed if NEXT_PUBLIC_ named | COMMITTED — unrecoverable without rotation |

Conclusion: classification must drive BOTH file class and heuristic exemption; the writer needs a secret-shape scan on public outputs because the under-classification branch is the only unrecoverable one.

### Required adversarial scenarios (all 40)

Each of the brief's 40 scenarios is answered with expected behavior, the enforcement point, the durable evidence it leaves, and the recovery action that remains. "Expected" means what *should* happen; where the proposal as written does not specify a mechanism, that is stated explicitly rather than assumed. Scenario 18 was answered independently by two reviewers (configuration classification and Sentry); both answers are retained because they attack it from different sides.

### Scenario 1: Malicious provider advertises fewer capabilities than its binary tries to exercise [permissions]
- Expected: Undeclared actions/origins are denied regardless of what the binary attempts; the binary cannot itself reach the vendor.
- Enforcement point: Manifest ceiling (CeilingPDP, permissions.go Allows) intersected with the running catalog being a subset of the packaged manifest (proposal:745); actual egress performed by the host HTTP broker, not the binary (sandbox network=loopback, launch.go:184-215). The load-bearing enforcement is that the host — not the provider — makes the HTTP call and the PDP check.
- Durable evidence: DecisionEvent (observability.go) with manifest-ceiling deny reason; execution receipt ADMITTED/FAILED.
- Recovery: None needed — no remote effect. If the mismatch is a benign version drift, re-pin/re-install the provider so catalog==manifest. NOTE: proposal does not specify what happens if the binary calls an undeclared RPC not gated by the broker — that gap must stay closed by keeping all egress host-mediated.

### Scenario 2: Provider changes its runtime catalog after approval [permissions]
- Expected: Approval is invalidated; Apply refuses because the bound catalog digest no longer matches.
- Enforcement point: ScopedAuthorization.CatalogDigest exact-match at verify (scoped_auth_v2.go:340-341) and the plan digest binding catalog identity (proposal:1149-1160). The host must re-derive and compare the catalog digest before Apply.
- Durable evidence: Scoped-auth ID + CatalogDigest in the receipt; deny with 'catalog digest mismatch'.
- Recovery: Re-run Observe→Plan→Approve against the new catalog. This requires the provider Guard/verifier that consumes CatalogDigest — which is NEW for providers (only Toolbox sessions consume it today).

### Scenario 3: A provider asks the broker to call an allowed origin with an unplanned destructive path [sandbox-broker]
- Expected: The broker rejects the request before any bytes leave the host, because the concrete request does not correspond to an action in the approved plan - and the rejection must not depend on any provider-supplied claim about which action or resource the request represents.
- Enforcement point: Proposal-specified: broker execution re-check (lines 442-444) plus 'allowed method/path patterns from the provider manifest' (line 1437). Not sufficiently specified: the normative manifest (695-742) declares no method/path/query/body constraints, so there is no ceiling to intersect (contradiction with 1437); and method+path cannot distinguish Stripe update from delete-by-id or bind {id} to the approved remote ID (POST and DELETE /v1/webhook_endpoints/:id, docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints). Nothing in current Codefly code enforces any of this: the only HTTP guard is in-process in the plugin and checks hostname only (toolbox-web/server.go:200-210). Required point: host broker, deriving action and resource from the parsed request (path params, query, declared body fields), comparing to the approved plan action's remote ID, then re-consulting the PDP with that derived pair.
- Durable evidence: The proposal promises policy-decision logs with canonical action and redacted resource (570-580) and per-operation receipts (1189-1198), but no per-broker-request audit record. Required: one denial record per rejected request with operation/attempt ID, plan digest, derived action, derived resource, the declared template that failed to match, and the credential-handle ID that was not spent, plus per-origin request and byte counters so repeated probing is visible.
- Recovery: No remote recovery needed (nothing was sent). Fail the Apply as denied with a typed diagnostic, abort the remaining plan actions rather than continuing (a probing provider is evidence of compromise), emit a terminal FAILED receipt, quarantine or pin-deny that provider version, and surface the denial in provider doctor.

### Scenario 4: A provider supplies its own Authorization header [sandbox-broker]
- Expected: The broker rejects the request outright rather than silently stripping the header, and credential-handle constraints remain the only source of authorization material - including for the non-header variants of the same attack.
- Enforcement point: The proposal forbids caller-supplied Authorization, cookie and proxy headers (1435) and states invariant 17, which is necessary but insufficient: Go turns URL userinfo into Authorization Basic when no Authorization header is set (GOROOT/src/net/http/client.go:251-257), so a header-name denylist leaves an open channel, and the precedent implementation does the opposite of the requirement by setting every caller header verbatim (toolbox-web/server.go:234-240) with no credential-header test. Required point: host broker with a per-operation ALLOWLIST of settable headers (Content-Type, Stripe-Version, Accept), rejection of any URL containing userinfo, and request reconstruction from validated components instead of replaying the provider's raw URL.
- Durable evidence: A denial record naming the offending header key (never its value) or 'url-userinfo-present', with operation/attempt/plan IDs and provider audience plus version. The proposal does not currently require recording attempted header injection; it should be an explicit security event with its own metric so a compromised provider release is detectable across users.
- Recovery: No remote effect: abort the operation, emit FAILED, and treat repeated attempts as grounds to quarantine that provider version. Note that a provider seeking credentials does not need this attack today, because it inherits the host environment (core/agents/manager/loader.go:722) - fixing environment inheritance is a precondition for this scenario's answer to mean anything.

### Scenario 5: A provider tries an HTTP redirect to a credential-stealing origin [sandbox-broker]
- Expected: No credential is ever presented to any host other than the exact approved origin; the safest behavior is that credentialed requests do not follow redirects and a 3xx is returned to the provider as data.
- Enforcement point: The proposal specifies 'redirect revalidation' (652) and 'redirect denial when credentials would cross origins' (1442), which is ambiguous because it implies same-origin redirects are followed with credentials re-attached. Reused behavior would permit redirects to any allowlisted host (toolbox-web/server.go:78-84, tested only for the off-allowlist case at server_test.go:166-198), so with a multi-origin manifest a credential minted for origin A can be replayed to a subdomain of A, and Go itself forwards Authorization to exact/subdomain matches (pkg.go.dev/net/http Client.Do). Required point: host broker - CheckRedirect errors for any credentialed request; opt-in following only for declared idempotent GETs to the same origin and declared path prefix, with credentials re-derived and each hop re-authorized against the plan digest.
- Durable evidence: A receipt/diagnostic entry recording that a 3xx was received, the Location host (safe to record), whether it was followed, and confirmation that no credential was emitted on a second hop. Cassettes must record the 3xx as a distinct interaction so replay exercises the same path.
- Recovery: No remote mutation if the redirect was refused: return a typed diagnostic (e.g. provider.remote.redirect-refused) with the observed Location so the operator can fix the base URL. If a vendor genuinely needs redirects (Sentry's trailing-slash conventions are plausible - documented paths end in '/' at docs.sentry.io/api/requests/, but the 301 behavior is unverified, LOW confidence), the provider must declare canonical trailing-slash paths instead of relying on redirect following, and the behavior must be qualified in a live-acceptance cassette.

### Scenario 6: A provider tries proxy environment variables or a subprocess to bypass the broker [sandbox-broker]
- Expected: Neither path yields egress: proxy variables must not influence the broker, and a subprocess must inherit the same OS confinement as its parent and therefore reach no external host.
- Enforcement point: Subprocess: partially enforced today - confinement is inherited by children (core/toolbox/launch/sandbox_e2e_test.go TestE2E_OSSandbox_InheritedByChildProcesses) and NetworkDeny defeats curl (core/runners/sandbox/sandbox_test.go:116-140). But the provider can exec arbitrary binaries: macOS emits '(allow default)' with no process-exec restriction (sandboxexec.go:100) and Linux ro-binds /usr,/bin,/sbin (bwrap.go:118-120) - curl and vendor CLIs are startable, they simply have no network under deny/loopback. Two residual gaps: (i) macOS NetworkLoopback allows all loopback traffic ('(allow network* (remote ip "localhost:*"))', sandboxexec.go:142-144), so the provider or its child can reach every local service (databases, other agents, any local proxy), whereas Linux --unshare-net isolates the namespace (bwrap.go:134-136); (ii) on macOS '(allow default)' leaves mach-lookup open, so out-of-process helpers (the DNS resolver, LaunchServices/'open') are plausible covert-egress channels - inference requiring an experiment, supported by the package's own disclaimer (core/runners/sandbox/doc.go:27-32). Proxy: not enforced at all - the proposal never mentions proxies, and a client built like the precedent inherits DefaultTransport with Proxy: ProxyFromEnvironment (GOROOT/src/net/http/transport.go:46-57, 491-506), so host-side HTTP_PROXY/HTTPS_PROXY silently reroutes credentialed traffic. The provider cannot set the host's environment, so the proxy risk is an ambient-environment confused-deputy issue, but the provider does inherit and can read those variables (loader.go:722), telling it whether the operator's traffic is interceptable.
- Durable evidence: Required: (a) receipts and doctor output record the sandbox backend, network policy and unix-socket grants actually applied for the spawn (available from sandbox.Backend(), core/runners/sandbox/sandbox.go:83); (b) the broker records the pinned destination IP and whether any proxy was used. Note OS-level denials are not delivered to the host as structured events, so absence of evidence is not evidence of absence.
- Recovery: Give the broker an explicit Transport with Proxy nil (or an approved, plan-digest-bound proxy). For the subprocess path, impose the host-owned provider capacity ceiling (loopback or deny only, no write paths), prefer NetworkDeny with only the per-spawn UDS grant since providers need no TCP loopback once the host mandates WithUDS (loader.go:416, core/toolbox/session/session.go:276), and on macOS narrow the profile so loopback is not blanket-allowed. Treat an observed subprocess exec by a provider as a policy violation surfaced in diagnostics; never rely on the OS layer as the only line.

### Scenario 7: Provider claims a different principal in the permission callback [permissions]
- Expected: Ignored; the spawn-time principal is authoritative.
- Enforcement point: PermissionsCallbackServer.WithPrincipalProvider returns the bound principal and overrides the request's principal_id (callback.go:262-274; loader.go:836-838); proven by TestE2E_Authorizer_PluginCannotImpersonate (doc.go:221).
- Durable evidence: DecisionEvent principal_id equals the bound principal, not the claimed one.
- Recovery: None — impersonation is structurally prevented. CAVEAT: only the principal is overridden; action/resource are still trusted from the plugin (see HIGH finding on confused-deputy), so this scenario is closed but the adjacent action/resource-lying scenario is NOT.

### Scenario 8: A human's permission is revoked after Plan but during Apply [permissions]
- Expected: Apply stops at the next action, checkpoints, and returns partial/denied without further mutation.
- Enforcement point: SaasPDP never caches denies (pdp_saas.go) + a per-action re-check + TokenRevocationList (hardening.go). The re-check loop does NOT exist for providers today (see HIGH finding) — GatewayEvaluator mints one token per RPC and no provider Guard consults the TRL.
- Durable evidence: Partial/UNCERTAIN receipt with the last completed action and a deny DecisionEvent for the halted action.
- Recovery: Operator restores the grant (or accepts the partial state); next Plan observes remote state and converges. Requires the NEW per-action re-authorization; without it the remaining actions would proceed until token TTL.

### Scenario 9: A CI principal reuses a scoped authorization in another environment [permissions]
- Expected: Denied — the token is bound to the originating environment/binding and audience.
- Enforcement point: ScopedAuthorization AudienceID + caveats (CaveatEnvironment/CaveatTenantID via well_known_caveats.go) with required-caveat exact match (scoped_auth_v2.go:365-372; well_known_caveats.go:147-224). MaxUses=1 for mutations (proposal:454). This works ONLY if the host sets the environment caveat as Required and the verifier enforces it — a NEW provider-side verifier.
- Durable evidence: Caveat-mismatch deny; scoped-auth ID + environment caveat recorded.
- Recovery: Mint a fresh authorization for the correct environment. Gap: nothing today forces the provider path to set/require the environment caveat; the proposal must mandate CaveatEnvironment + CaveatResourceBinding as required for every provider mutation token.

### Scenario 10: A Mind agent delegates authority beyond the human grant [permissions]
- Expected: The agent can act only within the intersection of manifest ceiling, its own role grant, and the delegated human grant; deep/opaque chains are capped.
- Enforcement point: Delegation chain recorded on Principal (principal.go:81-103); MaxDelegationDepth default 3 (hardening.go:272-326); escalation binds EXACTLY the requested (action,resource,principal) and does not elevate the role permanently (escalation.go:24-29, 63-73). Intersection is the effective-decision model (proposal:322-334).
- Durable evidence: delegation_chain + grant_id in the identity map and receipts (principal.go:214-225); DelegationDepth in DecisionEvent (pdp_saas.go:334-336).
- Recovery: None if correctly denied. Risk: nothing prevents an agent principal from being granted a broad provider.* role directly; the intersection only helps if the human grant and manifest ceiling are actually narrow (see ambient-CRUD finding). Depth cap defends against chain-hiding, not against a directly over-broad agent grant.

### Scenario 11: A local Stripe configuration points at a live account [stripe]
- Expected: Setup/observe classifies the credential and account as live before any mutation is planned; the plan (if produced at all) displays 'Account: acct_… (live)'; policy for local* environments denies every mutating action and the shared-key escape hatch; the operation ends as a clean deny with zero remote/local/state/sink changes (invariant 33). The proposal specifies the policy layer (provider-plugins.md:1448-1464 live-guard, scoped-auth caveat 'observed account ID/mode' at :456-457) but does NOT specify the detection mechanism, and the mechanism it implies — observing mode on stripe.account — does not exist: the Account object has no livemode attribute (https://docs.stripe.com/api/accounts/object), and the provider never sees the raw key prefix because it holds only an opaque handle (provider-plugins.md:465-491).
- Enforcement point: Must be: (1) host-side key classification at secret-resolution time (sk_live_/rk_live_ vs sk_test_/rk_test_ prefixes per https://docs.stripe.com/keys) bound as an account-mode caveat into the credential handle and plan digest; (2) PDP caveat check at plan admission and again at every brokered request; (3) defense-in-depth: broker verifies livemode=false on webhook-endpoint responses (the only API-level mode authority) and halts to UNCERTAIN on mismatch. Today's script enforces this solely via the ^sk_test_ regex at stripe.sh:135-136.
- Durable evidence: Policy deny decision log entry with account-mode caveat outcome; plan digest embedding account mode; deny receipt correlating principal, binding, and the caveat mismatch — none containing the key itself (fingerprint only).
- Recovery: None required remotely (denied before mutation). User swaps the input-configuration secret reference to a sandbox key and re-runs; doctor should name the exact remediation ('local-dogfood requires a test/sandbox credential'). Gap to fix: the proposal must add the host classification step, otherwise there is nothing for the caveat to check.

### Scenario 12: A Stripe webhook create succeeds but the response is lost [state-recovery]
- Expected: Apply halts; the operation terminates UNCERTAIN; no later plan action runs; safe metadata is checkpointed (provider-plugins.md:533-535, 1213-1214). The next plan Observes, finds an endpoint at the planned URL, and — because URL-match auto-adoption is forbidden (provider-plugins.md:1293-1294) — proposes an explicit, approval-requiring IMPORT of a 'uniquely discoverable' resource or a REPLACE (provider-plugins.md:1215-1222). Because the signing secret existed only in the lost response, convergence ultimately requires supplying an existing secret reference or a destructive REPLACE.
- Enforcement point: Host apply loop (checkpoint-per-effect rule, provider-plugins.md:1183) and the UNCERTAIN rule; ownership rules at next plan; approval for IMPORT/REPLACE. Gap: no mandatory pre-send checkpoint of {action, idempotency key, prospective ID} and no same-attempt idempotent-replay carve-out, so the host may not even know a request was in flight (see HIGH finding).
- Durable evidence: STARTED receipt in the journal (terminal-requires-STARTED enforced at cli/pkg/executionjournal/journal.go:219-224), UNCERTAIN terminal receipt, and whatever checkpoints committed; the plan digest and approval record.
- Recovery: UNCERTAIN + reconciliation is NOT sufficient as written: recovery is semi-manual (operator confirms identity, approves IMPORT, then supplies a secret or approves destructive REPLACE). With the recommended pre-send checkpoint + same-key idempotent replay + ownership stamping, it becomes automatic in most cases.

### Scenario 13: Stripe webhook secret captured but configuration projection fails [secrets]
- Expected: Remote webhook exists; secret bytes are already committed to the sink; projection writer validation fails. The persisted secret is retained — the proposal explicitly forbids silently rolling back a persisted one-time secret — the writer restores the prior output set (writer invariant 12), the operation terminates partial/FAILED (not UNCERTAIN: all remote and sink effects are known), and the next plan proposes a PROJECT_OUTPUT-only action sourcing the existing sink reference with no remote mutation.
- Enforcement point: Host-owned projection writer (atomic install + rollback-on-validation-failure) and the no-rollback-of-persisted-secret rule in the capture section; checkpoint sequence in the execution journal.
- Durable evidence: Journal checkpoint containing the sink reference and fingerprint; state entry with remote endpoint ID and output digests; FAILED receipt (cli/pkg/executionjournal + core/executionreceipt provide stages and signing).
- Recovery: Rerun plan/apply: converge by projecting from the stored sink reference. Gap: this requires the reference checkpoint to have committed before projection started — ordering the proposal implies ('checkpoints the persisted reference before allowing another mutation') but does not state relative to projection; and re-projection must not require re-reading the secret from the vendor (it cannot, for Stripe). Specified adequately overall; ordering sentence should be made explicit.

### Scenario 14: Secret sink fails after the remote webhook exists [secrets]
- Expected: Sink was Prepared and authorized before the POST was sent (explicitly required). Stripe returns 200 with the secret; sink Put/Commit fails. Broker discards the secret bytes from memory, the operation becomes UNCERTAIN, no later action runs, and safe response metadata (endpoint ID, no secret) is checkpointed.
- Enforcement point: Broker capture pipeline (steps 3 and 7) plus the explicit rule 'if capture or durable persistence fails after the remote mutation, the operation becomes UNCERTAIN and later actions stop'.
- Durable evidence: UNCERTAIN receipt; checkpoint with remote endpoint ID and capture-failure diagnostic; state marks the resource owned-with-unknown-secret.
