- Recovery: Next plan observes the endpoint exists but its secret is unrecoverable from state. Options per proposal: supply an operator-provided secret reference, recover from the vendor if the API allows (whether Stripe returns the secret on retrieve was NOT confirmed by the fetched create-endpoint doc — open question for live acceptance), or an explicitly approved REPLACE (delete+recreate) yielding a fresh secret. Gap: the sink transaction's durability semantics between Put and Commit are unspecified (see MEDIUM sink finding), so 'failed' vs 'actually persisted' may itself be ambiguous.

### Scenario 15: Resend returns a different signing-secret shape than documentation [resend]
- Expected: The broker's declared capture (POST /webhooks, pointer /signing_secret) misses the relocated/renamed field. Correct behavior: broker detects a secret-shaped value (whsec_ pattern class) outside any declared capture pointer, withholds the response from the provider, marks the operation UNCERTAIN/BLOCKED with a capture-mismatch result distinct from 'absent', and checkpoints sanitized metadata. The proposal as written does NOT specify this: capture is defined only 'for a matching successful response' (provider-plugins.md:516), and the Resend section's 'tolerate its absence' rule (provider-plugins.md:1774-1775) makes a shape change indistinguishable from genuine absence, so the raw whsec_ value passes to the provider process while the run reports success. Shape variance is not hypothetical: the current script already handles two shapes ('.signing_secret // .data.signing_secret', resend.sh:206), Resend has no API version header (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/introduction), and the docs internally disagree — verification prose says create/retrieve/list all return the secret (https://resend.com/docs/webhooks/verify-webhooks-requests) while the list example omits it (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/webhooks/list-webhooks).
- Enforcement point: Codefly host HTTP broker (capture engine + secret-pattern scan before logging/recording/provider delivery). The provider cannot be the enforcement point — it is the party being protected against. Proposal currently specifies enforcement only for pointer-matching responses; the mismatch case is unspecified (gap).
- Durable evidence: Execution receipt with capture-result = PRESENT_UNDECLARED/mismatch; checkpointed sanitized response metadata (status, safe headers, content-type — no body); sanitized cassette recording the new shape for offline analysis; provider state showing webhook ID with secret-reference = unresolved.
- Recovery: Webhook exists and works; recover the secret via an explicit approved secret-recovery action against GET /webhooks/{webhook_id} — current docs example shows signing_secret in retrieve (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/webhooks/get-webhook) — with capture-to-sink under provider.project.secret; if retrieve also fails to yield it, operator supplies a secret reference (proposal provider-plugins.md:1777) or plans an approved webhook replacement. Vendor qualification: P4 live acceptance must record actual create/retrieve/list/update response shapes with a dedicated account before the capture manifest is finalized (proposal provider-plugins.md:2289-2291 already requires this).

### Scenario 16: A Resend sending-only key is accidentally used for management [resend]
- Expected: Validate is offline and cannot detect it (both key kinds are re_-prefixed; resend.sh:138's regex proves the format is indistinguishable, and list-api-keys exposes no permission field — https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/api-keys/list-api-keys). First remote call in Observe (GET /domains or GET /webhooks) returns restricted_api_key 401 'This API key is restricted to only send emails' (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/errors). Expected: provider translates this by error NAME into a structured credential-scope-insufficient failure with rebind remediation; Plan never runs; nothing is mutated; the binding stays untouched (invariant 33). Gap the proposal must close: translation keyed on HTTP status misclassifies (restricted=401 but invalid_api_key=403, inverted from convention), and the reverse mistake — full_access key bound as runtime — produces no remote error at all, so the promised warn/reject (provider-plugins.md:1785) has no vendor signal without a deliberate probe, which the credential-handle purpose/path binding currently forbids.
- Enforcement point: Vendor-side rejection (Resend) is the only hard enforcement for the sending-key-as-management direction; Codefly's enforcement is honest classification (provider error translation, host-owned broker metadata) plus doctor. For the full_access-as-runtime direction, enforcement must be a manifest-declared read-only scope-qualification probe under provider.doctor authority — currently missing from the proposal's credential model (gap).
- Durable evidence: Receipt/doctor entry with sanitized vendor error code restricted_api_key, the credential-handle fingerprint (never the key), and the remediation issued; no plan digest and no state change recorded.
- Recovery: Operator rebinds the management input to a full_access key and reruns; no remote or local cleanup is needed because the failure occurred read-only and fail-closed. For the reverse direction: doctor probe detects full_access on the runtime handle, environment policy warns (local) or rejects projection (production) per provider-plugins.md:904-907; recovery is creating a domain-restricted sending_access key (management mutation, out of v0.1 managed scope — a MANUAL_ACTION with exact instructions).

### Scenario 17: Sentry setup token accidentally projected as a runtime/build token [secrets]
- Expected: Host refuses any projection whose resolved underlying secret is the MANAGEMENT/setup credential when the output's declared purpose is RUNTIME or BUILD, unless an explicit allow-shared-credential opt-in is present and visible in the plan and receipt. Sentry P3 exit gate requires the setup token absent from runtime output by default.
- Enforcement point: Output-commit authorization (the proposal's authorization moment 4) — but as specified, enforcement is 'the provider must warn' plus optional production policy, which is advisory. Real enforcement requires the host to compare the resolved reference identity/fingerprint of the projected value against the declared purpose of the input it originated from; this comparison is not specified anywhere. Downstream, ConfigurationValue has no purpose field (core/proto/codefly/base/v0/configuration.proto:8-15), so consumption-time enforcement is impossible without either a proto extension or separate configuration names for build-only secrets.
- Durable evidence: Plan shows projected keys with fingerprints and any shared-credential opt-in; output-commit receipt; policy decision log with deny/allow outcome.
- Recovery: Disconnect the projection, rotate the exposed setup token in Sentry (rotation workflow unspecified in the proposal), re-project the correct reference, redeploy consumers so old process environments (which held the token) are replaced. The proposal specifies the intent but not the enforcement mechanism — see MEDIUM purpose-separation finding.

### Scenario 18: Sentry DSN over-classified as secret, or a true secret under-classified as public [config-endpoints]
- Expected: The DSN must project as PUBLIC and browser-exposable (Sentry: 'DSNs are safe to keep public because they only allow submission of new events', https://docs.sentry.io/concepts/key-terms/dsn-explainer/), landing in the public error-tracking.env exactly as the starter does today (module-saas-starter/configurations/local-dogfood/error-tracking.env.example:4 NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN). Over-classified (secret=true): with a reference-only sink the loader rejects the plaintext DSN outright (core/configurations/secrets.go:66-78 via local_reader.go:377-385) and the whole error-tracking configuration fails to load for every consumer; with a plain .secret.env sink the value loads locally but restricted deployment rendering hard-fails later (core/agents/services/base_builder.go:651-653, 868-880 — and IsSensitiveKey matches the 'DSN' substring regardless of the flag, core/resources/configuration.go:178-186), and browser delivery depends on the Next.js agent's handling of secret-flagged values, risking silently disabled browser error tracking. Under-classified (true secret like SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN marked public): it is written to the public env file, which is a committed file class — the writer's gitignore verification is specified only for secret targets — and injected into every consumer's process env. NOTE: the proposal's own Sentry projection example commits the over-classification error (DSN/org/project in the secret file), copied from the starter's secret.env.example, against Sentry's official classification.
- Enforcement point: Proposed: provider manifest output classification checked against the contract schema at plan admission; writer sink-selection rules. Actually existing today: only file placement convention, the reference-only plaintext rejection, IsSensitiveKey log redaction, and restricted-rendering hard-fail. Missing: any check that browser-exposable values reach a browser-visible key (NEXT_PUBLIC_ naming is pure convention), and any secret-shape scan of public outputs before they land in a committed file.
- Durable evidence: The plan must display each output's classification and target file class (public / secret / secret-ref) so a reviewer can catch misclassification before Apply; projection receipt records contract version and output digests. Today no equivalent surface exists.
- Recovery: Over-classification: fix classification in a provider release, re-plan, re-project — self-healing. Under-classification into Git: Codefly cannot recover it; the token must be rotated at Sentry and Git history purged manually. Because one direction is unrecoverable, the writer must gain a preventive secret-shape check on public outputs (see HIGH finding on classification).

### Scenario 18: A Sentry DSN is over-classified as secret and becomes unavailable to the browser, or under-classified and exposes a true secret [sentry]
- Expected: Over-classification arm: the provider emits the public DSN with classification SECRET / browser-exposable=false. Expected behavior is a hard, named diagnostic — the error-tracking@1 contract declares NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN as a required browser-public key, so a plan that cannot satisfy it must surface BLOCKED/MANUAL_ACTION, not a green run. As proposed, this is NOT what happens: classification is provider-declared (provider-plugins.md:919-921,927) and the host maps SECRET to secret=true without contract cross-checking, so the DSN lands only in the secret sink, NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN is absent from the public projection, and the frontend silently initializes without error tracking (instrumentation-client.ts guards on DSN presence). The proposal even leans toward over-classification itself: it redacts the public DSN as sensitive (provider-plugins.md:1523-1525) and writes it into error-tracking.secret.env (:957), while official Sentry docs state DSNs are safe to keep public (dsn-explainer). Under-classification arm: the provider emits a secret-bearing value (SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN, or dsn.secret from the keys response, which the official list-keys endpoint returns alongside dsn.public) as PUBLIC/browser-exposable. Expected behavior is rejection before the projection writer runs. As proposed, only manifest review and IsSensitiveKey log redaction stand in the way — redaction protects logs, not the projected file — so the secret is written to the Git-tracked public env file and compiled into the browser bundle.
- Enforcement point: Required enforcement point (currently unspecified): the host projection writer validating provider-declared per-output classification against a host/ecosystem-owned per-key classification in the error-tracking@1 contract schema, with the packaged provider manifest (provider-plugins.md:1084,1095-1096) as a secondary ceiling and IsSensitiveKey redaction as log-only defense in depth. The proposal specifies the provider-side declaration and the manifest ceiling but assigns no component the authority to overrule a provider's classification with contract knowledge; that is the gap this scenario exploits in both directions.
- Durable evidence: The approved plan and execution receipt must record, per output key: contract ID/version, declared classification, browser-exposure flag, target file class (public vs secret), and the output digest (plan digest already binds output target and current digest, provider-plugins.md:1157). For the under-classification arm, Git history of the public env file plus the receipt's key/classification record prove what leaked and when; the secret's safe fingerprint (provider-plugins.md:923) identifies which credential must be rotated without printing it. For the over-classification arm, the receipt shows the browser key was never projected, distinguishing misclassification from a consumer bug.
- Recovery: Under-classification: revoke/rotate the leaked material at Sentry (revoke the auth token; rotate the client key via project settings — rotation/revocation is officially supported per dsn-explainer), purge the value from Git history, re-run plan/apply to reproject, and have doctor confirm the old fingerprint no longer appears in any projection. Over-classification: no remote action is needed (Sentry v0.1 is observe-only); correct the contract/provider classification, re-plan, re-project. In both arms doctor must be able to detect the condition afterward: a contract-required browser key missing from the public projection, and any contract-declared secret fingerprint present in a public file. The proposal specifies none of these doctor checks today.

### Scenario 19: Two developers apply the same binding against one sandbox account [state-recovery]
- Expected: State is per-machine local (provider-plugins.md:1248, 1256-1258: no team backend until P5), so neither developer's state sees the other's ownership. Developer B's Observe finds A's endpoint at the same callback URL; per the ownership rules and the Stripe acceptance list ('existing same-URL endpoint is an unmanaged conflict', provider-plugins.md:1659), B's plan is BLOCKED with an unmanaged-conflict diagnostic rather than creating a duplicate or auto-adopting.
- Enforcement point: Provider ownership classification at plan time; host approval gate for any IMPORT. Nothing prevents both developers from each explicitly importing the same remote ID on their own machines — there is no cross-machine lease or arbitration; the only cross-machine signal is optional vendor-side ownership metadata (provider-plugins.md:1610), which is observed but never enforced as a claim.
- Durable evidence: Each machine's own state, plans, and receipts; the remote resource itself; no shared record.
- Recovery: Safe-by-default (blocked, no mutation) but requires human coordination: one developer imports, or each uses a distinct binding/account. If both import, subsequent updates ping-pong (each converges drift toward its own desired state) with no detection. The proposal should document this limitation explicitly and recommend per-developer sandbox accounts; a lease/stamp mechanism is future work. UNCERTAIN is not involved; manual coordination required.

### Scenario 20: Two CLI processes apply different plans concurrently [state-recovery]
- Expected: One apply proceeds; the other is denied before any remote effect with provider.plan.stale (provider-plugins.md:1570), because its plan digest binds prior-state and observed-state digests (provider-plugins.md:1153-1154) that no longer match, and 'apply rejects stale observations and changed output/state digests' (security control 11, provider-plugins.md:1410-1411).
- Enforcement point: Inter-process lock on the binding state store (provider-plugins.md:1251-1252) plus digest CAS at apply admission. Gap: the proposal never states the CAS occurs atomically inside the lock's critical section, nor the lock's hold duration across a long remote Apply, nor read-only concurrency behavior — bbolt's exclusive per-open lock would make concurrent observe/doctor fail with a raw timeout (bolt.Options{Timeout} pattern at cli/pkg/executionjournal/journal.go:98). See MEDIUM finding.
- Durable evidence: The winning apply's ADMITTED/STARTED/terminal receipts; the loser's deny diagnostic and (ideally) a denied-admission receipt; state digests before/after.
- Recovery: The denied process re-runs Observe/Plan and seeks fresh approval. UNCERTAIN not involved. Sufficient once lock+CAS atomicity is specified; as written the atomicity is an implementation accident.

### Scenario 21: A workspace moves to another filesystem path [state-recovery]
- Expected: As written: nothing detects the move. The path sha changes (pattern: cli/pkg/executionruntime/runtime.go:62-78, Abs+Clean only, no EvalSymlinks), Codefly opens a fresh empty state tree, Observe reports formerly owned resources as existing unmanaged objects, and the plan proposes CREATE duplicates or BLOCKED conflicts. The old state (ownership, secret references, uncertain checkpoints) is silently orphaned. The proposal specifies nothing for this case.
- Enforcement point: None exists — that is the HIGH finding. Required: EvalSymlinks canonicalization, recorded canonical path inside the state root, fail-closed mismatch diagnostic, and an explicit relocation command; longer term a stable workspace identity (core/resources/workspace.go:40-75 currently has only Name).
- Durable evidence: The orphaned old state directory under the old sha (still on disk, undiscoverable without recomputing the old path hash); receipts in the journal keyed by the old workspace target.
- Recovery: Manual and undocumented: recompute the old path's sha and move/rename the state directory, or re-import every owned resource by exact remote ID and re-supply secret references. UNCERTAIN + reconciliation does not apply; this is pure state-address loss and requires manual intervention under the proposal as written.

### Scenario 22: Provider state is deleted while remote resources remain [state-recovery]
- Expected: Equivalent to a fresh binding: Observe finds the remote webhook; auto-adoption by URL is forbidden, so it is classified an unmanaged conflict; plan is BLOCKED or proposes explicit IMPORT by exact remote ID (provider-plugins.md:1291-1295). Locally projected configuration and owner-only secret files live in the workspace configuration tree, not the state directory, so they typically survive; the migration/adoption flow (provider-plugins.md:1949-1962) preserves existing secret files/references during import.
- Enforcement point: Ownership rules at plan time; approval for IMPORT; destroy remains impossible until re-import because deletion requires exact recorded ownership (brief invariant 23; provider-plugins.md:1305-1314).
- Durable evidence: Receipts survive if the provider journal lives outside the deleted state tree — the proposal never says where provider receipts are stored relative to provider state (the execution journal precedent lives under a separate ~/.codefly/execution/gateways/<sha> tree, cli/pkg/executionruntime/runtime.go:72-77); this should be made explicit so evidence survives state loss. Surviving projected config files carry ownership metadata (provider-plugins.md:986-989).
- Recovery: Manual: operator runs import for each resource with the exact remote ID and supplies the existing secret reference (the sink file still holds the webhook secret). Reconciliation machinery helps (no-diff plan after adoption proves convergence) but human identification and approval are required. Acceptable, and correctly conservative.

### Scenario 23: A user manually edits the remote webhook (URL/events changed in the vendor dashboard) [testing-migration-roadmap]
- Expected: Next Observe returns the canonical observed fields (URL, enabled events, status — provider-plugins.md:1100-1114, stripe.webhook-endpoint observed fields at 1602-1610). Plan, computed offline from desired + prior state + observation, emits UPDATE actions converging provider-owned fields; doctor reports provider.drift.detected (provider-plugins.md:1567). Any previously approved plan is invalidated because the observed-state digest is bound into the plan digest (provider-plugins.md:1148-1161) and Apply rejects stale observations (control 11, provider-plugins.md:1410-1411). Gap the proposal leaves open: it never defines the owned-field set for stripe.webhook-endpoint, so whether a manual description edit or an operator-added extra event is reverted, ignored, or reported-only is unspecified (the brief's Lane 13 asks exactly this; no answer exists in the doc).
- Enforcement point: Plan-digest binding of the observation + Apply freshness precondition (provider-plugins.md:1168-1173); per-action policy admission of the corrective provider.update on the exact resource; broker method/path enforcement on the corrective request.
- Durable evidence: Provider state's canonical observed fields and last observation time/digest (provider-plugins.md:1266-1273); the new plan and its digest; ADMITTED/STARTED/terminal receipts for the corrective apply (provider-plugins.md:1189-1198).
- Recovery: Operator reviews the drift in the displayed plan and approves the corrective UPDATE (or explicitly accepts the remote change by editing desired state). Required change: the Stripe provider spec must enumerate owned vs ignored fields before P2 acceptance so 'converge' has defined semantics.

### Scenario 24: A user deletes the remote webhook outside Codefly [testing-migration-roadmap]
- Expected: Observe classifies the owned resource as 'missing' (presence taxonomy at provider-plugins.md:1109-1110); Plan proposes CREATE to recreate it (recovery menu at provider-plugins.md:1216-1222: converge via stable identity / import / replace / manual). The recreation returns a NEW one-time signing secret, captured by the broker into the prepared sink (provider-plugins.md:516-534) and re-projected; a subsequent plan is empty. The stale sink value and projected STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET must be replaced — but the secret is declared write-once (provider-plugins.md:1615-1616, 924) and the proposal defines no write-once replacement semantics, so this routine path can dead-end in UNCERTAIN or silently overwrite (my MEDIUM finding). Running product processes also still hold the old secret until configuration re-injection (scenario-33 adjacency; not addressed here by the proposal).
- Enforcement point: Observe presence classification (provider agent) + host approval of the high-risk CREATE; sink prepared and permission-checked before the mutating request (provider-plugins.md:532-535); checkpoint of the persisted reference before any further mutation (capture step 7).
- Durable evidence: State retains the old remote ID, ownership record, and old secret fingerprint; the new plan digest, capture checkpoint, sink reference, and receipts record the recreation; old vs new secret fingerprints are correlatable without exposing values (provider-plugins.md:566-580).
- Recovery: Approve the recreation plan; after apply, re-run doctor to confirm webhook verification with the new secret; restart/re-inject consumers of billing configuration. Required change: define write-once-per-resource-generation sink semantics (finding above) so the replacement is authorized by the new plan rather than improvised.

### Scenario 25: A provider agent is upgraded between Plan and Apply [state-recovery]
- Expected: Apply is denied and the approval invalidated: 'upgrading the provider ... invalidates approval' (provider-plugins.md:556-558) and the plan digest binds 'provider agent identity and artifact digest when available' (provider-plugins.md:1150). The operator re-plans and re-approves under the new agent, after any required UpgradeState steps.
- Enforcement point: Host comparison of agent identity/digest against the approved plan digest at apply admission, before any credential or broker access. Gap: 'when available' — before the P5 artifact-digest lock (provider-plugins.md:2232-2234), a locally rebuilt binary with the same version string produces the same plan digest, so a same-version binary swap between Plan and Apply is undetected in P1-P4. Acceptable for local dogfood only; must be stated as a known local-mode limitation, and the digest must become mandatory plan-digest input whenever it is computable (local builds can hash the binary).
- Durable evidence: Deny diagnostic plus receipts binding agent identity/digest; state records prior agent identity and artifact digest (provider-plugins.md:1265).
- Recovery: Re-plan/re-approve; no remote or local mutation occurred (invariant 33). For an upgrade landing mid-Apply (binary replaced on disk while the spawned process runs): the running process is unaffected; a crash-then-recover sequence resolves the newer agent, which must not resume the old plan — the digest mismatch enforces re-planning, and checkpoints must be migrated by UpgradeState, which requires checkpoints to be part of the versioned state schema (currently unstated — see downgrade finding). Reconciliation suffices once that is specified.

### Scenario 26: An old agent reads newer state [state-recovery]
- Expected: Should fail closed with provider.schema.incompatible (the diagnostic exists, provider-plugins.md:1574) before the agent receives prior state. The proposal specifies only the upgrade direction ('Before planning with newer code, the host calls UpgradeState', provider-plugins.md:1229-1231) and is silent on downgrade — the host has both signals (agent's supported state schema version via GetProviderInformation, provider-plugins.md:1092; state's recorded schema version, provider-plugins.md:1264) but no stated rule.
- Enforcement point: Must be the host coordinator, comparing versions before constructing ProviderContext; never the agent (a schema-N agent cannot be trusted to safely detect schema-N+1 data it may partially parse).
- Durable evidence: State schema version field; the deny diagnostic; per-upgrade-step backups (provider-plugins.md:1239) are the only restore artifact and their retention is unspecified.
- Recovery: Upgrade the agent back, or restore the automatic pre-upgrade backup. Requires the fail-closed rule and backup retention to be specified in P1 (currently a MEDIUM finding); as written, behavior is undefined and silent ownership corruption is possible. Manual intervention (version choice) is inherently required; the guardrail is what is missing.

### Scenario 27: A cassette accidentally contains a live token or webhook secret [secrets]
- Expected: Should be prevented at three layers: recorder strips broker-injected Authorization headers and all manifest-declared secret fields before any bytes reach disk; a commit-time check 'fails if a secret-shaped field remains'; human review of committed cassettes. Replay-only default means no test can silently go live and record.
- Enforcement point: Broker cassette recorder/sanitizer (new P1 code — no broker cassette infrastructure exists today; the only cassette in the workspace is the LLM help test, cli/cmd/codefly-help/main_test.go:74-89) plus CI scanning plus review.
- Durable evidence: Sanitized cassette diff in the PR; CI failure artifact when the scanner fires.
- Recovery: If a live secret lands in Git anyway: rotate the credential immediately at the vendor, purge Git history, re-record the cassette. The proposal specifies none of this — no leak runbook, no rotation procedure, and the 'secret-shaped' heuristic will miss unprefixed token formats (Sentry tokens). Required: sanitize from the manifest's declared secret-field list (deterministic) rather than shape heuristics alone; see LOW cassette finding.

### Scenario 28: The policy backend is unavailable [permissions]
- Expected: Every decision denies (fail-closed); no remote mutation, local state, config, or secret-sink write occurs.
- Enforcement point: SaasPDP.failClosedDecision denies on backend error (pdp_saas.go:297-311, FailClosed defaults true); GatewayEvaluator returns err and never allows on backend failure (gateway.go:104-108, 148-153); callback 5xx→deny (callback.go:408-413, 493-507). CRITICAL CAVEAT: fail-closed only holds once a PDP is actually consulted; the CLI supervisor path today has no PDP (WithoutPrincipal), so absence of wiring is fail-OPEN, not fail-closed.
- Durable evidence: FailClosedTotal metric + Warn 'PDP fail-closed' log (observability.go:202-205); admitted-but-failed receipt.
- Recovery: Retry once the backend is reachable; no compensation needed because no effect occurred (invariant 33). The proposal must ensure the provider coordinator always consults a PDP so 'unavailable' means deny, not skip.

### Scenario 29: The permission callback socket is unavailable mid-operation [permissions]
- Expected: Every subsequent Authorized()/sub-operation check fails closed as a deny.
- Enforcement point: callbackAuthorizer returns (false, reason, err) on dial/timeout/non-200 (callback.go:400-419); disabledAuthorizer denies when the socket env is unset (callback.go:426-432); callbackPDP maps error→deny (callback.go:493-507). The UDS is 0600 and per-spawn (callback.go:168, 146-155).
- Durable evidence: Deny DecisionEvent with 'permissions callback unreachable'; receipt shows the operation halted.
- Recovery: Re-establish the callback (respawn/re-load) and re-run; because Apply must re-check before each mutation, a mid-Apply socket loss halts before the next mutation — provided the NEW per-action re-check exists. Without it, a token already minted for the RPC would let the current CallTool proceed even though sub-operation checks fail closed.

### Scenario 30: A crash occurs after the last remote effect but before the success receipt [state-recovery]
- Expected: The journal holds ADMITTED and STARTED with no terminal stage; provider state holds checkpoints for all completed actions. On the next provider invocation for that binding, a recovery sweep appends an UNCERTAIN terminal receipt (the journal is built for exactly this: IncompleteAttempts at cli/pkg/executionjournal/journal.go:432-470, with the append-UNCERTAIN-on-restart pattern documented at journal.go:432-434, and one-terminal-per-attempt enforced at journal.go:205-211). The next plan re-Observes and, since every effect was checkpointed and remote state matches desired, converges to a no-diff plan.
- Enforcement point: Journal invariants (terminal-requires-durable-STARTED, journal.go:219-224) plus a coordinator-run sweep on the binding before accepting new work. The sweep wiring is NEW host work the proposal implies ('integrate execution receipts and uncertain recovery', provider-plugins.md:2036) but never specifies as a mandatory entry-point step; who runs it and when must be stated since the provider coordinator is a short-lived CLI process, not a daemon.
- Durable evidence: STARTED receipt; complete state checkpoints; the appended UNCERTAIN terminal; the subsequent empty plan (the strongest available proof that the operation actually succeeded).
- Recovery: UNCERTAIN + reconciliation fully suffices; no manual intervention. The audit trail permanently records UNCERTAIN rather than SUCCEEDED for the attempt — correct and honest, with the follow-up no-diff plan as the success evidence. This is the best-supported scenario in the whole lane.

### Scenario 31: Public ingress changes after the webhook plan is approved [config-endpoints]
- Expected: The plan digest binds 'resolved endpoint identities and origins' and approval is invalidated by 'changing an endpoint' (proposal, Plan hashing list and Approval semantics); Apply requires 'non-expired observation and endpoint preconditions'. Expected: at Apply admission the host re-resolves the ingress origin, detects the mismatch against the plan-bound origin, denies with provider.plan.stale, sends zero broker requests, and requires re-plan/re-approval.
- Enforcement point: Host plan-digest compare plus an Apply-time endpoint-precondition re-check. Two gaps: (a) 'non-expired' is never defined — no TTL, and re-resolution at Apply is implied but not mandated; (b) no ingress→origin resolver exists today at all (Environment.Ingress is consumed only by orchestration cloning and gitops k8s rendering — core/resources/environment.go:79-84,134, cli/pkg/gitops/module_bundle.go:142,166; the scripts require a hand-passed --webhook-origin, provider-common.sh:123-127), so the machinery that would detect the change is NEW P1 work. A residual TOCTOU remains between the Apply-time recheck and the broker request carrying the URL: an ingress change mid-Apply yields a remote webhook pointing at a dead origin.
- Durable evidence: Denial receipt recording the stale-precondition reason plus both origins (plan-bound and re-resolved); provider state stores the origin applied to the remote webhook so the next Observe reports URL drift.
- Recovery: Re-plan (new origin → new plan digest → new approval), Apply issues an UPDATE of the webhook URL — supported by both Stripe and Resend webhook APIs. The mid-Apply race resolves as ordinary drift at the next Observe/Doctor; doctor's 'endpoint and ingress resolution' section must include callback-origin match to make this visible without waiting for missed events.

### Scenario 32: A local forwarded webhook arrives before the backend is ready [config-endpoints]
- Expected: Stripe CLI forwards the event to the loopback callback; the service is not listening; delivery fails with connection refused; the failure is recorded vendor-side and retried — Stripe test environment: 'Event deliveries created in a test environment are retried three times over the course of a few hours' (https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks). Events are eventually delivered when the flow is up, subject to at-least-once and out-of-order semantics the application must absorb. The proposal specifies NOTHING here: no readiness sequencing between provider setup, forwarder, and `codefly run` (setup step 10 deliberately prints the run command AFTER apply), and the forwarder is unsupervised in v1, so this window is a designed-in property, not an accident.
- Enforcement point: None in the provider system — enforcement is entirely application-level: the starter's durable webhook receiver with signature verification and deduplication (the Resend acceptance list explicitly exercises 'duplicated, and out-of-order events... against the starter's durable receiver'). Vendor retry policy bounds the window: three retries over a few hours in Stripe test mode means a backend down longer than the retry schedule PERMANENTLY loses those test events — with no Codefly-side record.
- Durable evidence: Vendor dashboard delivery attempts and the application's webhook receipt table. Codefly has no durable evidence: the forwarder is outside receipts in v1 (a consequence of the companion deferral).
- Recovery: Start the flow; vendor retries drain the pending events within the retry budget; for events past the budget, trigger re-send from the vendor dashboard or re-run the product action. Required additions: the dogfood runbook must state the bounded-retry loss window, and provider doctor should include a callback listening probe — the primitive already exists (Reachable, cli/cmd/common/endpoints.go:148-158; --require-up, cli/cmd/endpoint/endpoint.go:89-91).

### Scenario 33: Disconnect removes local projection but runtime processes still hold old credentials [config-endpoints]
- Expected: Disconnect removes/disables the projection files, retains provider state and remote resources, and 'reports what remains' (proposal, Deletion section). Reality on the existing runtime: configuration is injected as process environment at service start (cli/pkg/orchestration/runner.go:273 → core/resources/environment_variables_manager.go:279-296,489-513) with no reload channel, so every running consumer keeps STRIPE_API_KEY et al. in memory until flow restart — and the remote key remains fully valid at the vendor under deletion-policy retain. Expected behavior per the proposal text is therefore correct but dangerously incomplete: it reports remote remainder, not runtime remainder.
- Enforcement point: None exists and none is proposed — the proposal specifies nothing about running consumers, restart, or credential validity at disconnect. The needed enforcement points are: disconnect-time flow inspection (enumerate running services whose dependency set includes the removed configuration name, derivable from service.codefly.yaml workspace-configuration-dependencies), and a doctor projection/runtime-skew check.
- Durable evidence: Disconnect receipt with the projection-removal digest. Nothing records which processes still hold values or that the vendor credential remains live; the receipt should additionally record 'remote credential remains valid; N running consumers hold projected values' to make the report honest.
- Recovery: Restart the flow to purge injected values; if the operator's intent was access removal, rotate or revoke the runtime key at the vendor (the only real revocation — Codefly cannot reach into vendor key validity under retain) or run explicit destroy. Doctor should flag the skew until restart occurs.

### Scenario 34: Destroy sees an unmanaged remote resource with the same URL [testing-migration-roadmap]
- Expected: Destroy observes current remote state, then builds a destructive plan naming only exact owned/adopted resources by remote ID; it 'refuses ambiguous or unmanaged resources' and 'deletes only resources allowed by delete-owned' (provider-plugins.md:1306-1314). A same-URL endpoint that is not in state as owned/adopted is classified 'unmanaged conflict' (provider-plugins.md:1286-1295 — URL/name/email matching is explicitly insufficient for adoption, and by the same rule for deletion) and is excluded from the plan, reported for manual review. If the owned resource itself no longer exists remotely, its DELETE resolves as already-absent (NOOP) — the provider must not fall back to URL matching to find a substitute victim.
- Enforcement point: Three layers: (1) provider plan formation restricted to state-recorded owned/adopted IDs; (2) host per-action authorization of provider.delete against the exact typed resource including remote ID (provider-plugins.md:399, 439-446 — no unbounded wildcard at apply time); (3) broker execution — but note the credential handle and broker check are specified at path-template granularity (provider-plugins.md:478-481, 448-451), so exactness depends on the host deriving/verifying the concrete request path from the approved resource ID rather than trusting the provider descriptor; this is implied but not an explicit requirement (my LOW finding demands it as an acceptance criterion).
- Durable evidence: The approved destructive plan digest enumerating exact remote IDs and ownership; receipts for each delete (ADMITTED/STARTED/terminal); terminal state recording the surviving unmanaged resource as an unmanaged conflict with its remote ID.
- Recovery: None required for the unmanaged resource — it is intentionally retained; the operator inspects it manually (dashboard or a later explicit import-then-destroy). If the user actually wanted it removed, the path is explicit import by exact ID followed by a new destroy plan.

### Scenario 35: A provider returns a very large or malicious JSON response [sandbox-broker]
- Expected: Bounded host memory and CPU; fail closed rather than truncate; no unparsed or partially parsed body reaching logs, cassettes or diagnostics; and where the response was supposed to carry a one-time secret, an explicit UNCERTAIN outcome with a named manual recovery instead of silent loss.
- Enforcement point: The proposal specifies bounded sizes and timeouts (653), 'smaller default body caps' (1439) and that capture 'bounds and parses the response before it can be logged or recorded' (522), but not fail-closed versus truncate, JSON parser limits, duplicate-key handling, or the required type of a captured value. The reuse precedent truncates and flags truncated: true (toolbox-web/server.go:251-260, pinned by server_test.go:142-164) with a 4 MiB cap (line 21) and no header-size, redirect-count or JSON-depth limits. Required point: host broker, before any logging/metrics/cassette write - per-operation byte cap with hard failure, response header cap, explicit JSON limits profile (max depth, max tokens, max string length, duplicate-key rejection), and capture requiring the pointed value to be a JSON string of declared maximum length.
- Durable evidence: A checkpoint recording that the mutation request was sent, the vendor status code, the safe request ID and the capture-failure reason (oversize, unparseable, duplicate key, wrong type), plus an UNCERTAIN terminal receipt. No body bytes may be recorded, and the cassette entry must be either fully sanitized or absent.
- Recovery: No automatic retry, since the remote effect may already exist (invariant 24). The next plan must observe remote state, locate the created endpoint by stable identity, and offer: adopt/import with an operator-supplied secret reference; an explicitly approved REPLACE (delete owned plus recreate) to obtain a fresh secret; or a MANUAL_ACTION to roll the secret in the vendor dashboard. For Stripe the webhook signing secret is returned only at creation, so a lost capture cannot be recovered by re-reading - REPLACE or a dashboard action are the only paths, and the Stripe reference design must say so explicitly.

### Scenario 36: A provider API begins returning a new field that looks secret [secrets]
- Expected: Under the current design: the broker only captures/strips declared fields, so the new undeclared secret-looking field flows raw into the provider process, may appear in provider-chosen observation output, and enters cassettes on the next recording (where the shape heuristic may or may not fire). State rules forbid full response bodies but observed fields are provider-selected.
- Enforcement point: Runtime: none specified — this is a gap. Test-time: cassette sanitizer shape check and human review on re-record; API-version pinning (Stripe-Version) bounds drift for Stripe but Sentry/Resend have no equivalent version header per the proposal's own vendor table.
- Durable evidence: Cassette diff on re-recording shows the new field; doctor/drift diagnostics could surface schema-unknown fields if implemented.
- Recovery: Ship a provider+manifest update declaring the field (capture-suppress or classify); rotate any value that leaked to logs/cassettes. Required change (part of the BLOCKER capture finding): broker strip-unknown/allowlist projection of response fields for declared resource types, or at minimum a broker-side sensitive-shape scrub analogous to resources.IsSensitiveKey (core/resources/configuration.go:178-186) applied to response field names before forwarding to the provider.

### Scenario 37: A provider needs pagination to determine whether an existing resource is unique (Resend webhook by endpoint) [resend]
- Expected: Observe must page GET /webhooks with the documented cursor parameters (limit, after — 'You can only use either after or before parameter, not both', https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/webhooks/list-webhooks) until has_more=false, within a bounded page budget, before asserting uniqueness or absence of the endpoint URL. Plan then emits NOOP (owned ID matches), CREATE (provably absent), or — for an endpoint-matching webhook that is not in state — MANUAL_ACTION/explicit import, never URL-based auto-adoption (proposal ownership rule). If pagination cannot complete (mid-listing 429 from the team-shared 10 req/s budget, or page-budget exhaustion), Observe must return an incomplete-observation failure and Plan must refuse to run on it. The proposal specifies none of this: no pagination contract exists in the broker request-description model, no completeness flag exists in observation or the plan digest, and duplicate-endpoint recovery is written for Stripe only. The current script demonstrates the failure concretely: resend.sh:169-181 matches the endpoint against a single unpaginated page and ignores has_more. Compounding: POST /webhooks has no idempotency support (keys cover only POST /emails and /emails/batch, https://resend.com/docs/dashboard/emails/idempotency-keys), so a duplicate CREATE cannot be prevented vendor-side, and lost-response recovery is itself list-based — inheriting the same pagination hole.
- Enforcement point: Provider observation logic (must request all pages) + host broker (must permit declared bounded pagination on the allowed path and bind observation completeness into the plan digest so an incomplete observation cannot admit an Apply). Both halves are currently unspecified (gap).
- Durable evidence: Observation digest including page count and completeness flag inside the plan digest inputs; cassette recording the full paged sequence; receipt recording refusal reason when observation was incomplete.
- Recovery: If a duplicate was created (TOCTOU between Observe and Apply, or a pre-fix truncated listing): next Observe with complete pagination surfaces both webhooks; Plan reports the unowned duplicate as a conflict requiring explicit import or explicit destroy of the owned one — deletion via DELETE /webhooks/{webhook_id} (https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/webhooks/delete-webhook) only under exact ownership and destructive authorization. Vendor qualification: default and maximum page sizes are undocumented and must be measured in P4 live acceptance; until qualified, the provider must always paginate explicitly with a fixed declared limit.

### Scenario 38: A retry crosses the vendor's idempotency retention window [stripe]
- Expected: Within the window: the host retries the identical POST with the same operation-derived Idempotency-Key; Stripe replays the original status+body (including the one-time `secret` — 'saving the resulting status code and body of the first request', https://docs.stripe.com/api/idempotent_requests), the broker performs the declared secret capture on the replayed body, and the operation converges with exactly one endpoint. Past the window (keys removable 'after they're at least 24 hours old'): automatic same-key retry is forbidden because it would create a duplicate; the operation stays UNCERTAIN and the next cycle is Observe → list endpoints (≤16 total, single page) → identify the possibly-created endpoint by the Codefly metadata ownership stamp → explicit import + MANUAL_ACTION secret recovery (Workbench 'Click to reveal', https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks) or a planned REPLACE. The proposal acknowledges key expiry (provider-plugins.md:1222-1226) and forbids blind retry of ambiguous outcomes (invariant 24) but specifies neither the window policy, nor send-time checkpointing, nor the in-window replay-recovers-the-secret affordance, nor metadata stamping as the discovery key.
- Enforcement point: HTTP broker (owns idempotency-key injection per provider-plugins.md:1436-1441 and the retry budget); Apply freshness preconditions; UNCERTAIN state machine halting later actions; ownership rules forbidding URL-only adoption (provider-plugins.md:1285-1295).
- Durable evidence: Pre-dispatch checkpoint containing operation/attempt IDs, the idempotency-key identity, and the original request send timestamp (required to compute replay safety — currently missing from the proposal's checkpoint contents); UNCERTAIN receipt; safe Stripe request IDs; post-recovery import/replace plan and receipts.
- Recovery: ≤ conservative window (recommend ≤20h from checkpointed send-time, since 24h is a pruning-eligibility lower bound, not a replay guarantee): automatic same-key replay, secret re-captured to the prepared sink, state converges. > window: re-observe; if a stamped endpoint exists, explicit import plus dashboard secret reveal or approved replace (new secret, projection update); if two same-URL endpoints exist, unmanaged-conflict handling with explicit selection; deletion of the surplus endpoint only via an explicit destructive plan against the stamped, owned ID.

### Scenario 39: A production secret is successfully persisted but state commit fails [secrets]
- Expected: Operation becomes UNCERTAIN and halts. The secret exists in the production sink; the only local record is the journal checkpoint of the persisted reference — the proposal orders that checkpoint before 'allowing another mutation' but does not order it against provider state commit, so the reference may or may not be durably recorded.
- Enforcement point: Journal checkpointing (bbolt, cli/pkg/executionjournal/journal.go) and the UNCERTAIN classification; sink-side audit trail of the production backend.
- Durable evidence: UNCERTAIN terminal receipt; journal checkpoint with sink reference (if it committed); the production sink's own versioning/audit log.
- Recovery: Rerun: the plan must rediscover the persisted secret. With the current sink contract (Prepare/Put/Commit/Rollback only, no Lookup, no deterministic addressing) this is impossible programmatically — the operator must manually locate the secret in the production sink or force a REPLACE that rotates it. Required change: deterministic sink keys plus Lookup and idempotent Put-or-adopt (see MEDIUM sink finding); with that, recovery is: Lookup by derived address, adopt reference into state, second plan is a no-diff.

### Scenario 40: Doctor runs with a principal allowed to inspect configuration but not provider account metadata [reliability-ux-doctor]
- Expected: Doctor degrades to a partial report rather than failing or escalating: local static sections (binding schema validity, agent installed/version-compatible, input configuration presence, endpoint/ingress resolution, projection contract and digest, state/lock health) execute normally; remote sections (provider authentication and scope, account/environment mode, remote resource health, drift) are skipped, each carrying a policy-denial diagnostic naming the missing action (provider.observe); no provider credential handle is minted and no vendor request is attempted; the report status distinguishes 'partially checked' from 'not ready'; exit code reflects that distinction. THE PROPOSAL SPECIFIES ALMOST NONE OF THIS: it declares provider.doctor low-risk and lists 12 doctor sections, but defines no partial-report semantics, no policy-denied diagnostic code (the proposed code list has only vendor-side provider.auth.rejected / provider.auth.scope-insufficient), and no statement of whether a policy denial fails the whole doctor. Findings 3 and 4 require these to be defined in P1.
- Enforcement point: Dispatch-time PDP evaluation (proposal 'Per-operation authorization' step 1) evaluates provider.doctor and, separately, provider.observe for the remote subset BEFORE the agent is spawned with credential handles — the handle-minting step is the choke point: no provider.observe grant, no management-credential handle, so the broker structurally cannot inject a credential even if the provider requests one. The broker independently denies any outbound request lacking a scoped authorization (step 3 analogue for reads). PDP backend failure fails closed per invariant 26.
- Durable evidence: PDP decision events with deny outcome recorded through the existing policy observability path (core/policy/observability.go:161 RecordDecision increments DeniesTotal; DecisionEvent carries principal, action, resource); operation/receipt correlation IDs binding the doctor invocation to the denials; the doctor JSON report itself, persisted by the caller, containing the skipped-section markers with the (to-be-added) provider.policy.denied code. No remote or local state change exists to evidence — invariant 33 holds because nothing past dispatch ran.
- Recovery: Operator requests the provider.observe role grant (or an operator-policy caveat widening) from a grantor, or re-runs doctor under an authorized principal; re-run completes the remote sections. No cleanup is required — the denied path minted no handles, sent no requests, and wrote no state. Residual gap: until finding 4's code is added, the operator cannot distinguish this from a vendor credential failure, so the practical recovery today would likely be misdirected at the Stripe key.

## 10. Provider-by-provider validation

### Stripe

### Lane 13 artifact — Stripe v0.1 verification matrix, field-ownership table, and credential/output model

#### A. Proposal-claim verification table (all via current official docs, fetched 2026-07-30)

| # | Proposal claim (Stripe v0.1) | Verdict | Official evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Webhook endpoint CRUD via API (observe/create/update/import/destroy) | CONFIRMED | POST /v1/webhook_endpoints, POST /:id, GET /:id, GET list, DELETE /:id — https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints |
| 2 | Signing secret returned only at creation (API) | CONFIRMED | `secret`: "Only returned at creation." — https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/object; update response contains no secret — https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/update |
| 3 | …and therefore unrecoverable | PARTLY DISPROVEN | Dashboard/Workbench: "Click to reveal" + "Invalidate secret key" (expire now or delay ≤24h; multiple secrets active during overlap) — https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks. API-unrecoverable, dashboard-recoverable ⇒ MANUAL_ACTION, not forced replacement |
| 4 | Idempotency keys where supported; retry safety | CONFIRMED w/ bounds | All POSTs accept keys; GET/DELETE "idempotent by definition"; keys removable "after they're at least 24 hours old"; replay = original status+body (incl. 500s); param mismatch ⇒ error; concurrent ⇒ not saved, retryable — https://docs.stripe.com/api/idempotent_requests |
| 5 | Replay recovers the one-time secret in-window | CONFIRMED (inference from "saving the resulting…body of the first request"; qualify in live acceptance) | same URL as #4 |
| 6 | Explicit API version pin; must not inherit account default | CONFIRMED | Per-request `Stripe-Version` header; endpoint `api_version` at creation overrides account default for event payloads; current version 2026-07-29.dahlia — https://docs.stripe.com/api/versioning |
| 7 | Endpoint api_version updatable | DISPROVEN | Update params: url, enabled_events, description, disabled, metadata only — https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/update ⇒ version change = REPLACE ⇒ new secret |
| 8 | Sandbox/live observable on stripe.account | DISPROVEN | Account object has no livemode — https://docs.stripe.com/api/accounts/object. Mode authority = key prefix (host-side; sk_/rk_ test/live — https://docs.stripe.com/keys) + livemode on webhook-endpoint objects |
| 9 | Restricted runtime key practical | UNVERIFIED FROM DOCS | RAKs: rk_test_/rk_live_, per-resource None/Read/Write, Dashboard-only creation; permission categories not publicly enumerated — https://docs.stripe.com/keys/restricted-api-keys. Needs live-acceptance qualification |
| 10 | Stripe CLI forwards to loopback; provider does not create remote endpoint | CONFIRMED | `stripe listen --forward-to`; CLI whsec printed at listen start and distinct from endpoint secrets; `--load-from-webhooks-api` optional — https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks, https://docs.stripe.com/stripe-cli/use-cli. CLI login key valid 90 days |
| 11 | Event set update converges | CONFIRMED | enabled_events updatable; compare as a set (unordered), `['*']` special-cased — https://docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/update |
| 12 | Duplicate/lost-response discovery viable | CONFIRMED w/ gaps | List endpoints; ≤16 endpoints per account (https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks) ⇒ single page; `metadata` supported at create ⇒ ownership stamp possible (script sets none today) |
| 13 | Test-mode webhook delivery for dogfood | CONFIRMED w/ caveat | Test env retries: "three times within a few hours" (vs 3 days prod) ⇒ local receiver downtime loses events; manual resend via CLI ≤30 days — https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks |

#### B. Field ownership: `stripe.webhook-endpoint`

| Field | Owner | Plan semantics | Drift handling |
|---|---|---|---|
| url | Codefly (derived from resolved endpoint/ingress) | UPDATE | remote change ⇒ drift, plan reconverges |
| enabled_events | Codefly (spec) | UPDATE, set-comparison, canonicalize sorted | drift ⇒ reconverge |
| api_version | Codefly, **create-only** | **REPLACE** on change (new secret) | never inherits account default |
| description | Codefly (ownership marker text) | UPDATE | reconverge or ignore per policy |
| metadata (codefly/* keys) | Codefly (binding/workspace/operation stamp) | UPDATE | codefly-namespaced keys reconverge; foreign keys **ignore** |
| status / disabled | Codefly | UPDATE | remote disable ⇒ drift warning |
| secret | Remote (write-once) | never in state/plan; sink reference + fingerprint only | rotation = dashboard roll (MANUAL_ACTION) or REPLACE |
| id, object, created, livemode, application | Remote | read-only; id = stable identity; livemode = guard cross-check | ignore |

`stripe.account`: wholly observed (id, charges_enabled, details_submitted, safe display subset); never managed; **no mode field** — mode comes from host key classification + response livemode.

#### C. Credential roles and minimum scopes

| Role | Form | Provenance | Minimum scope (docs-verifiable) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Management | rk_test_/rk_live_ RAK, or sandbox sk_test_ locally | Dashboard-only creation (MANUAL_ACTION) | Webhook-endpoint write + account read — **category names not publicly enumerated; qualify live** | never projected to runtime by default |
| Runtime | RAK | Dashboard-only | writes for customers/checkout/portal/subscriptions — **qualify live** | projected as STRIPE_API_KEY (SECRET/RUNTIME) |
| Webhook verification | whsec_ (endpoint or CLI-listen secret) | broker one-time capture, or host safe input (local-forwarded) | n/a | SECRET/WEBHOOK_VERIFICATION; CLI secret ≠ endpoint secret |
| Browser | pk_test_/pk_live_ publishable | dashboard | n/a | absent from billing@1 — correct for hosted Checkout; needed if Stripe.js/Elements ever added |
| Build | none in v0.1 | — | — | matches proposal |

#### D. Output classification (billing@1)

PUBLIC: BILLING_PROVIDER, STRIPE_API_BASE, STRIPE_API_VERSION. SECRET: STRIPE_API_KEY (RUNTIME), STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET (WEBHOOK_VERIFICATION). Browser-exposable: none. Unsafe in plans/state/logs/cassettes: sk_/rk_ values, whsec_ values, full webhook-create/replayed bodies (contain `secret`), Authorization headers, acct_ IDs and account business_profile contact fields (sanitize per proposal's cassette placeholder list), event payloads (customer/email data).

#### E. Local dogfood and live acceptance

Dogfood viable: dedicated sandbox (isolated account, own keys, sk_test_ prefix), shared-key escape hatch, `stripe listen --forward-to` with no remote endpoint, `--events` filtering recommended; caveat: test-mode delivery retries only 3× within hours; CLI login key expires after 90 days (companion supervision should diagnose re-login).

Live acceptance must qualify (docs cannot): (1) RAK permission categories sufficient for management observe+webhook CRUD and for runtime product calls; (2) GET /v1/account under a scoped management RAK; (3) idempotent replay carrying `secret` and broker capture on replay; (4) practical replay horizon vs the ≥24h pruning floor; (5) metadata stamp round-trip; (6) 16-endpoint quota error shape; (7) sandbox account-ID behavior vs legacy test mode.

#### F. Unresolved vendor ambiguity

1. RAK permission category enumeration is Dashboard-only, not in public docs.
2. Idempotency retention upper bound: "at least 24 hours old" is an eligibility floor, not a replay guarantee — pick a conservative window and qualify.
3. Whether the CLI-listen whsec is stable across sessions is undocumented.
4. Sandbox-vs-test-mode account identity details (distinct acct_ per sandbox) not explicit on the sandboxes overview page.

**Lane 13 conclusion.** The Stripe v0.1 design is substantially confirmed against current official docs: webhook-endpoint CRUD exists exactly as assumed (POST /v1/webhook_endpoints create/update, GET retrieve/list, DELETE), the signing secret is 'Only returned at creation' via the API (docs.stripe.com/api/webhook_endpoints/object), idempotency keys apply to all POSTs with replay of the original status+body and pruning 'after they're at least 24 hours old' (docs.stripe.com/api/idempotent_requests), per-request Stripe-Version pinning is real, and endpoint-level api_version overrides the account default for event payloads (docs.stripe.com/api/versioning). The Stripe CLI 'listen --forward-to' forwarding model matches the proposal's local-forwarded mode: it does not create a remote endpoint and its whsec is CLI-specific, distinct from any registered endpoint secret. Two material gaps survive falsification attempts: (1) the proposal's `stripe.account` observed resource claims to observe 'sandbox/live mode', but the Stripe Account object has no livemode attribute and the provider never sees the raw key prefix (it holds only an opaque credential handle), so the load-bearing local-live guard has no specified detection mechanism — it must be explicitly assigned to the host at credential-resolution time (key-prefix classification of sk_/rk_ test vs live) plus livemode cross-checks on brokered responses; (2) webhook api_version is create-only and cannot be updated, so changing the pinned API version on a managed endpoint is a REPLACE that mints a new signing secret, which the Stripe section's declared capability set (create/update/import/delete, no replace) and the human plan example ('STRIPE_API_VERSION: 2025-… → 2026-02-25.clover' with 'secret: unchanged') both get wrong. Secondary confirmed facts the design should absorb: idempotent replay within the retention window re-returns the original body including the secret (a safe lost-response recovery path the proposal does not exploit), the Dashboard can reveal and roll endpoint secrets (so 'secret unrecoverable' is API-only, and MANUAL_ACTION recovery exists), webhook endpoints support metadata (usable as the Codefly ownership stamp; today's script sets none), accounts are capped at 16 webhook endpoints, and restricted keys (rk_test_/rk_live_, per-resource None/Read/Write) are dashboard-created only with no public enumeration of permission categories, so the restricted-runtime-key story needs live-acceptance qualification rather than documentation citation. Compared with module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/stripe.sh, the proposal is a strict improvement: the script enforces the live guard purely via a ^sk_test_ regex (which would also reject legitimate rk_test_ restricted keys), sends no Idempotency-Key, sets no api_version, stamps no metadata, effectively adopts existing endpoints by URL match, and projects the management key as the runtime key — all behaviors the proposal correctly replaces or gates. Overall: Stripe fits the proposed contract well; the two HIGH findings must be resolved before P2 but neither requires a protocol redesign because REPLACE and host-side credential classification are already representable.

### Sentry

### Lane 14 artifact — Sentry v0.1 claim-verification matrix and scenario 18 trace

#### A. Claim-by-claim verification of the proposal's Sentry section

| # | Proposal claim (core/docs/provider-plugins.md) | Verdict | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Token model: 'Sentry API keys are legacy; provider schemas prefer scoped auth tokens' (:1711) | CONFIRMED | https://docs.sentry.io/api/auth/ — 'API keys are a legacy means of authenticating. They will still be supported but are disabled for new accounts.' Modern access is `Authorization: Bearer` auth tokens. |
| 2 | Setup credential 'project-read scope' suffices for project discovery (:1675, :899) | CONFIRMED | GET `/api/0/projects/{org}/{project}/` requires one of project:read/write/admin — https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/retrieve-a-project/ |
| 3 | Same scope suffices for client-key (DSN) discovery (:1690-1695) | CONFIRMED | GET `/api/0/projects/{org}/{project}/keys/` requires project:read/write/admin — https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/list-a-projects-client-keys/ |
| 4 | `org:ci` is the build-purpose scope for releases/source maps (:901, :1677) | CONFIRMED | https://docs.sentry.io/api/permissions/ — 'Use the org:ci scope for CI and deployment workflows, including uploading source maps, creating releases, and managing code mappings.' Note `project:releases` also still exists for release endpoints. Token-type nuance: Organization Auth Tokens (sntrys_) carry a fixed non-editable CI permission set and are shown only once (https://docs.sentry.io/account/auth-tokens/); scope introspection of an arbitrary token is not documented. |
| 5 | Public DSN is browser-exposable public data (:29-32, :951, :1709) | CONFIRMED | https://docs.sentry.io/concepts/key-terms/dsn-explainer/ — 'DSNs are safe to keep public... they do not allow read access to any information'; abuse mitigations are IP blocking and DSN rotation/revocation. The proposal nonetheless redacts it as sensitive (:1523-1525) and files it in secret.env (:957) — internal contradiction. |
| 6 | Regional/self-hosted origins are a real Sentry requirement (:30, :1673, :1926) | CONFIRMED (requirement) / GAP (mechanism) | https://docs.sentry.io/api/ — API is v0; 'US region is hosted on us.sentry.io', 'DE region is hosted on de.sentry.io'. But the broker's exact package-time origin allowlist (:650, :707-708, :1088) has no specified path for a binding-supplied self-hosted origin. |
| 7 | Rate limits: 'per caller+endpoint limits and concurrency headers' (:230) | CONFIRMED | https://docs.sentry.io/api/ratelimits/ — per unique caller+endpoint fixed windows plus concurrent-request limits; headers X-Sentry-Rate-Limit-{Limit,Remaining,Reset,ConcurrentLimit,ConcurrentRemaining}; docs warn polling triggers limits quickly. |
| 8 | Project creation deferred from v0.1 for team/default-rule/delete reasons (:1697-1701, :2281-2285) | DEFERRAL SOUND, RATIONALE STALE | https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/create-a-project-for-an-organization/ — POST `/api/0/organizations/{org}/projects/`; no team required; auto-creates a personal team (side-effect resource); `default_rules` boolean (default true); org:write needed if member project creation disabled. |
| 9 | No inbound webhook needed for basic SDK ingestion (:221-223) | CONFIRMED (by omission) | None of the consulted setup/auth/project/key docs involves an inbound callback; ingestion is SDK→DSN. |
| 10 | Client-key observation is 'safe' fields only (:1690-1695) | INCOMPLETE | The same endpoint returns keys[].secret and keys[].dsn.secret beside dsn.public (list-keys doc). No proposal rule strips them from observation/logs/cassettes (contrast Resend rule :1778). |

#### B. Scenario 18 data-flow trace (Sentry setup token → public DSN → browser)

```text
[user] --hidden prompt/op:// ref--> [host secret resolver]
   --mint handle (purpose=setup, scope=project:read, origin=<resolved sentry origin>)-->
[provider-sentry Observe] --request descriptor: GET /api/0/projects/{org}/{proj}/keys/-->
[host HTTP broker] --inject Bearer <setup token>; enforce origin/method/path-->
[sentry API] --200: [ {id, isActive, rateLimit, secret*, dsn:{public, secret*, ...}} ]-->
[broker sanitize: MUST strip keys[].secret, keys[].dsn.secret BEFORE log/cassette]  <-- unspecified today
[provider canonical observation: key id, isActive, dsn.public only]
[Plan: PROJECT_OUTPUT NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN (classification: PUBLIC, browser=true)]
                                  ^-- classification authority gap: provider-declared,
                                      contract error-tracking@1 has no pinned per-key floor/ceiling
[host projection writer] --public file error-tracking.env (Git-tracked)--> [frontend bundle]
                        --secret file error-tracking.secret.env (0600, ignored)--> [backend/build]
Failure arms:
  over-classified  : DSN routed to secret file only -> NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN absent ->
                     instrumentation-client.ts silently no-ops -> green run, no browser error tracking
  under-classified : dsn.secret or SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN declared PUBLIC/browser ->
                     written to Git-tracked env + JS bundle; log redaction does not apply to files
```

#### C. Brief section-10 provider-validation fields — Sentry

- **Confirmed API capabilities**: Bearer auth-token model (user tokens, org tokens sntrys_, internal integrations); GET project and GET client keys under project:read; cursor pagination on keys; POST org-level project creation with auto personal team and default_rules; per caller+endpoint rate limits with X-Sentry-Rate-Limit-* headers; v0 API; regional hosts us.sentry.io / de.sentry.io; DSN rotation/revocation.
- **Disproven/stale assumptions**: public DSN treated as sensitive-by-default in CLI output (:1523-1525) contradicts vendor guidance; project-creation deferral rationale cites a team-parameter model the API no longer has; the 'safe fields only' framing of key observation omits that the same response carries legacy secret material.
- **Credential roles and minimum scopes**: setup = auth token with project:read (personal token or internal integration; whether an sntrys_ org token can serve this path is unqualified); build = org:ci (org auth token sntrys_ is the vendor-recommended artifact; fixed scopes, shown once, not introspectable); project-management (deferred) = project:write, plus org:write when member creation is disabled; runtime = public DSN only, no token.
- **Managed and observed resources**: v0.1 manages nothing remote; observes sentry.project and sentry.client-key; import is 'project reference' only. Smallest future non-webhook mutations, in pressure order: client-key create/rotate (returns public DSN, no one-time secret), then project creation (side-effect personal team, conditional scope escalation).
- **Output classification**: NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN, NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT, SENTRY_DSN, SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT, SENTRY_ORG, SENTRY_PROJECT — all PUBLIC (DSN browser-exposable per vendor); SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN — SECRET, purpose=build, never browser, never runtime-by-default; keys[].secret / keys[].dsn.secret — SECRET, must never leave the broker boundary.
- **Local dogfood**: no forwarder, no ingress, no webhook; controlled browser+backend events into a dedicated test project plus release/environment correlation is achievable read-only+SDK-side; the existing script's single-token flow (sentry.sh:161,203) must NOT be ported — it conflates setup and build purposes, the exact anti-pattern the proposal's purpose separation targets (scenario 17 precursor).
- **Live acceptance requirements**: qualify sntrys_ org-token capability against project:read endpoints; qualify keys list ordering stability and multi-key behavior; qualify wrong-region behavior (sentry.io vs us/de origin for a region-pinned org); verify keys-response sanitization in recorded cassettes; verify rate-limit header normalization on 429/concurrency rejection.
- **Unresolved vendor ambiguity**: org-token endpoint coverage (no documented scope-introspection endpoint); keys list sort order stability; whether region-pinned orgs receive errors or redirects on the wrong regional host (docs only promise latency differences); self-hosted version skew of the v0 API surface.

#### D. Lane 14 verdict on the brief's closing question

Sentry **half-validates** the abstraction. Real pressure delivered: non-webhook shape, observe-only management mode, browser-exposable output classification, binding-supplied regional/self-hosted origins, three-way credential-purpose separation, no-forwarder local story — and the two genuine design gaps this review found (origin-ceiling model, contract classification authority) were both surfaced *by* Sentry's requirements, proving the counterexample earns its place. Deficiencies hidden: zero coverage of Apply, idempotency, checkpoints, UNCERTAIN outcomes, one-time capture, secret sinks, destroy — so the mutation half of the protocol freezes validated only by two webhook-CRUD providers. Keep Sentry second; re-scope its acceptance toward what it can pressure (origin policy, contract-pinned classification, multi-key ambiguity, keys-response hygiene) and stop counting it toward mutation-contract validation.

**Lane 14 conclusion.** The Sentry v0.1 section is factually the strongest-verified provider section in the proposal: every checkable vendor claim held against current official docs (auth tokens over legacy API keys, project:read as the minimum scope for both project retrieval and client-key listing, org:ci as the documented CI/source-map/release scope, us.sentry.io/de.sentry.io regional origins, API v0, per-caller+endpoint rate limits with X-Sentry-Rate-Limit-* concurrency headers). Deferring project creation is correct, though the stated rationale is stale: the current endpoint POST /api/0/organizations/{org}/projects/ no longer requires a team and instead auto-creates a 'personal team' side effect, which is itself a good reason to defer. However, Sentry exposes two real design gaps the proposal has not closed. First, Sentry is the 'required design case' for regional/self-hosted origins (provider-plugins.md:1926) yet the broker/manifest model only supports an exact, package-time origin allowlist (provider-plugins.md:650,707-708,1088) — there is no specified mechanism for admitting a binding-supplied origin, and this must be fixed in the P1 manifest/credential-handle schema, not in P3. Second, the DSN classification story is internally contradictory (public browser file at :951, secret env file at :957, redacted-as-sensitive at :1523-1525) and classification authority rests on provider-declared outputs rather than the host-owned error-tracking@1 contract, which is exactly the over-/under-classification failure of scenario 18; the under-classification side is sharpened by the fact that the official client-keys list response carries legacy secret material (key `secret`, `dsn.secret`) one JSON pointer away from `dsn.public`. On the brief's closing question: Sentry only half-validates the abstraction. It genuinely pressures observation, projection, browser classification, multi-origin, and three-way credential-purpose separation (and the current script's conflation of setup and build token at sentry.sh:161/203 proves the purpose-separation feature is needed), but it exercises zero mutation machinery — no Apply, no idempotency, no checkpoints, no UNCERTAIN outcomes, no one-time capture, no destroy, no secret sink. Both mutation-bearing providers (Stripe, Resend) are webhook-endpoint CRUD, so invariant 35's claim that the protocol is 'designed against all three providers before being declared stable' is true for the read/project half and false for the mutation half. Sentry should stay second, but its acceptance criteria must be re-scoped to the things it can actually pressure (origin policy, contract-pinned classification, multi-key ambiguity, keys-response secret hygiene), and the protocol-freeze claim must be honest that mutation semantics are validated only against webhook-shaped resources.

### Resend

### Lane 15 artifact — Resend v0.1 provider validation (brief output section 10 format)

#### Confirmed API capabilities (all via current official docs, fetched 2026-07-30)

| Capability | Status | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Base URL `https://api.resend.com`, Bearer auth, HTTPS-only | CONFIRMED | https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/introduction |
| `full_access` vs `sending_access` key permissions | CONFIRMED | https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/api-keys/create-api-key |
| `domain_id` restriction — sending keys only | CONFIRMED (restriction applies **only when** permission is `sending_access`) | same |
| Key introspection (permission/domain of an existing key) | **DOES NOT EXIST** — list returns only `id`,`name`,`created_at`,`last_used_at` | https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/api-keys/list-api-keys |
| Webhook CRUD | CONFIRMED: `POST /webhooks`, `GET /webhooks/{id}`, `GET /webhooks` (paginated `limit`/`after`/`before`, `has_more`), `PATCH /webhooks/{id}` (`endpoint`,`events`,`status` enabled/disabled), `DELETE /webhooks/{id}` (`deleted: true`) | api-reference/webhooks/* pages |
| Signing secret in responses | create: **yes** (`signing_secret: whsec_...`); retrieve: **yes** (example); list: **no** (example) vs prose claiming yes; update/delete: no | create-webhook, get-webhook, list-webhooks, verify-webhooks-requests |
| Svix signing (`svix-id`/`svix-timestamp`/`svix-signature`), per-endpoint keys | CONFIRMED | https://resend.com/docs/webhooks/verify-webhooks-requests |
| At-least-once, unordered delivery; dedup by `svix-id`; retries 5s/5m/30m/2h/5h/10h | CONFIRMED | https://resend.com/docs/webhooks/introduction |
| Rate limit 10 req/s **per team, all keys**; 429; headers `ratelimit-limit/remaining/reset`, `retry-after` (IETF draft-06) | CONFIRMED | https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/rate-limit |
| Idempotency keys | **`POST /emails` and `POST /emails/batch` ONLY**, `Idempotency-Key` header, 24 h retention, 409 on payload mismatch/concurrency | https://resend.com/docs/dashboard/emails/idempotency-keys |
| No API version header (calendar versioning planned) | CONFIRMED | api-reference/introduction |
| `User-Agent` required, else 403 | CONFIRMED | api-reference/introduction |
| Error model: `restricted_api_key`=401, `invalid_api_key`=403, `validation_error`=400/403/422, `rate_limit_exceeded`/quota=429 | CONFIRMED — map by **error name**, not status | https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/errors |
| Domain verification prerequisite + statuses `not_started/pending/verified/partially_verified/partially_failed/failed/temporary_failure`; regions us-east-1/eu-west-1/sa-east-1; per-record DNS status | CONFIRMED | dashboard/domains/introduction, api-reference/domains/get-domain, errors (`invalid_region`) |
| CLI `resend webhooks listen` registers temporary webhook, streams, cleans up on exit; `--forward-to` retains Svix headers; `--port` default 4318 | CONFIRMED existence; **tunnel provision CONTRADICTORY** (docs example: user tailnet URL + \"any tunnel can be used\"; CLI 2.0 blog: CLI \"handles the tunnel\"); temp-secret exposure **undocumented** | https://resend.com/docs/cli, https://resend.com/blog/resend-cli-2 |

#### Disproven / stale assumptions

1. Script assumption \"existing webhook's signing secret cannot be retrieved\" (resend.sh:184) — contradicted by current `GET /webhooks/{id}` example. Proposal already flags this correctly.
2. Proposal's implication (matrix :1923) that the Resend CLI is a Stripe-CLI-equivalent transport — unproven; primary sources conflict on tunnel provision.
3. Any assumption that broker idempotency-key injection protects webhook management — disproven; idempotency covers email sending only.

#### Credential roles and minimum scopes

| Role | Scope | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Management | `full_access` | Required for domains/webhooks/api-keys; `sending_access` on these paths → `restricted_api_key` 401 |
| Runtime (send) | `sending_access` + `domain_id` | Domain restriction available only for sending keys; **not introspectable after creation**; token returned once at create |
| Webhook verification | `whsec_` Svix secret | Per-endpoint; returned at create (and per docs at retrieve); classification SECRET |
| Build/browser | none | No build credential; nothing browser-exposable |

#### Managed vs observed resources
