- Managed: `resend.webhook` (create/update incl. enable-disable/import/delete verified as endpoints). No uniqueness constraint on endpoint URL → duplicate-safe logic must be client-side over **complete paginated listing**.
- Observed: `resend.domain` (status/region/capabilities/DNS-record readiness), account reachability. Domain verification is operator-owned DNS work → MANUAL_ACTION territory, correctly out of managed scope.

#### Output classification (`email@1`)
Public: `EMAIL_PROVIDER=resend`, sender identity, API base. Secret: runtime sending key (reference), webhook signing secret (reference). Nothing browser-exposable.

#### Required secret-retrievability capability/result model (replacement text — the assignment's required deliverable)

```yaml
# packaged manifest, per resource type
resource: resend.webhook
secret-outputs:
  - name: webhook-verification-secret
    purpose: WEBHOOK_VERIFICATION
    retrievability: UNKNOWN_QUALIFY_LIVE   # RETRIEVABLE | CREATE_ONLY | UNKNOWN_QUALIFY_LIVE
    locations:                              # every path that may carry the secret, incl. GETs
      - { method: POST,  path: /webhooks,      pointer: /signing_secret, phase-disposition: CAPTURE_TO_SINK }
      - { method: GET,   path: /webhooks/{id}, pointer: /signing_secret,
          phase-disposition: { observe: REDACT_REPORT_PRESENCE, secret-recovery: CAPTURE_TO_SINK } }
      - { method: GET,   path: /webhooks,      pointer: /data/*/signing_secret, phase-disposition: REDACT_REPORT_PRESENCE }
    token-patterns: [ "whsec_[A-Za-z0-9_=-]+" ]   # broker fails closed on matches outside declared locations
```

Broker per-response result (bound into checkpoint/receipt):
`CAPTURED | ABSENT | REDACTED_PRESENT | PRESENT_UNDECLARED (fail closed → BLOCKED/UNCERTAIN) | SINK_FAILED (UNCERTAIN)`.

Plan rule: a binding whose webhook exists but whose secret reference is unresolved must emit an explicit `SECRET_RECOVERY` action (requires `provider.project.secret`) or `MANUAL_ACTION` — never an implicit read-time capture, never silent \"absent\".

#### Local dogfood
Sending works with no inbound callback (verified domain + sending key). Webhook lifecycle locally requires either public HTTPS ingress (managed webhook, `public`/`existing` modes — sound) or the Resend CLI (`local-forwarded` — **blocked on live qualification**: tunnel mechanics and temp-webhook secret exposure; forwarded events are signed with the temp endpoint's key, so the projected managed-webhook secret will not verify them). Starter receiver is real and dedup-correct (resend_webhook.go: svix verify + SvixID dedup; migration 84).

#### Live acceptance requirements (dedicated team, explicit opt-in)
1. Record actual create/retrieve/list/update/delete response bodies; resolve list-secret prose-vs-example contradiction; pin capture manifest from evidence.
2. Measure default/max page size of `GET /webhooks`; verify `has_more` cursor traversal.
3. Verify POST /webhooks status code (200 vs 201) and whether HTTPS endpoints are enforced.
4. Qualify `resend webhooks listen`: tunnel provision, temp webhook registration/cleanup (assert via full listing before/after), and signing-secret surfacing.
5. Behavioral scope probe: sending key on `GET /domains` → `restricted_api_key` 401; full-access key on same → 200 (drives the warn/reject path).
6. Exercise 429 and confirm `retry-after`/`ratelimit-*` headers on live responses.
7. Duplicate-create TOCTOU: create webhook out-of-band between Observe and Apply; assert conflict handling, then exact-ownership `DELETE` cleanup of test resources only.

#### Unresolved vendor ambiguity (do not resolve by assumption)
- Signing secret on **list**: prose yes, example no.
- CLI tunnel: docs (BYO tunnel) vs CLI 2.0 blog (built-in).
- Temp-webhook secret exposure during CLI forwarding: undocumented.
- Page-size defaults, POST /webhooks status code, HTTPS-endpoint enforcement: undocumented.

**Lane 15 conclusion.** The Resend v0.1 section (core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1735-1815) is substantially accurate against current official docs and is the best-verified of the three vendor sections: full_access vs sending_access exists exactly as claimed, domain_id restriction applies only to sending_access keys, webhooks are Svix-signed (svix-id/svix-timestamp/svix-signature) with documented at-least-once and out-of-order delivery, the default limit is 10 req/s per team with 429 plus IETF draft-06 ratelimit-* and retry-after headers, domain verification is a hard sending prerequisite with seven documented statuses, and the signing-secret documentation ambiguity the proposal flags is real. However the section under-specifies four things the current docs make unavoidable: (1) per current examples the signing secret is returned on plain GET retrieve, so secret-bearing responses occur on the read-only Observe/Doctor path, which the proposal's mutation-framed one-time capture mechanism (provider-plugins.md:495, 516) does not cover; (2) the capture mechanism is fail-open on shape variance — a moved/renamed signing_secret field silently misses the declared JSON Pointer and the raw whsec_ value flows to the provider process; (3) webhook listing is cursor-paginated and POST /webhooks has no idempotency support (idempotency keys cover only POST /emails and /emails/batch), so uniqueness determination and lost-response recovery both require a pagination contract the protocol lacks; (4) Resend provides no API-key introspection (list-api-keys returns neither permission nor domain_id), so the acceptance criterion 'management and sending-only scopes are distinguished' is satisfiable only by behavioral probing, which the credential-purpose model does not currently authorize, and the reverse error (full_access bound as runtime) is remotely undetectable. The proposal correctly identifies that the existing script's 'secret cannot be retrieved' assumption is stale, correctly demands live qualification instead of assumption, and correctly separates management/runtime credentials that the current script conflates (resend.sh:230 projects the management key as the runtime key). The section needs an explicit secret-retrievability capability/result model, a pagination/observation-completeness contract, and a defined local-forwarded secret story (the Resend CLI's tunnel and temp-webhook-secret mechanics are ambiguous between the docs page and the CLI 2.0 announcement) before P4 acceptance criteria are testable. None of this rejects Resend as the third provider — it is a genuinely useful counterexample — but two of the gaps (capture-miss fail-open, read-path secrets) are protocol-level and must land in the P1 foundation, not in P4.

## 11. Testing gap analysis

**Lane 19 conclusion.** The five-tier design (pure contract, recorded cassettes, provider conformance, opt-in live acceptance, starter dogfood) is structurally correct and its permission negative matrix (provider-plugins.md:583-600) is one of the strongest parts of the proposal. However, the two load-bearing test infrastructures are misrepresented as extensions of existing tooling. (1) 'codefly agent ci' conformance is hardcoded to kind codefly:service (cli/cmd/agents/ci.go:427-428; codefly:module already requires --skip-conformance, ci.go:422-425) and its conformance model is 'generate a service via Builder.Create, then run the workspace CI gate' (ci.go:554-579, 662-694) — none of which applies to a leaf-protocol provider agent; only the manifest/source/build/audit/drift stages and the GOWORK=off isolation pattern (ci.go:476-509) are reusable. Provider conformance is a new harness. (2) No cassette recorder/replayer/sanitizer exists anywhere in core/, cli/, or toolbox-web/ Go code — the pattern exists only as documentation citing the external mind-server repo (core/docs/testing.md:135-150). What 'NO MOCKS' should mean here: Tier 0 exercises pure functions with zero HTTP; Tier 1 replays recorded real-vendor bytes through the real production broker code path with enforcement active, failing hard (never falling back to live) on a cassette miss, with recording an explicit credentialed opt-in; hand-written fake Stripe/Sentry/Resend servers or in-process fake clients are never acceptance evidence; and the broker, PDP, sandbox, and sink in conformance tests must be the production implementations, never stubs. Symmetrically, default CI (every PR) must run only Tiers 0-2 so no merge depends on live vendor availability; Tier 3 runs scheduled/opt-in against dedicated accounts and is the only tier permitted to refresh cassettes, with every refresh a reviewed diff. Remaining gaps: the sanitizer's 'secret-shaped field remains' failure criterion is unspecified; deterministic replay matching versus broker-injected idempotency keys/timestamps is unaddressed; the neutral conformance provider's remote fixture is undefined; and the platform matrix (Linux CI + macOS) needed to actually prove sandbox network denial is not specified.

Tier key: **T0** pure deterministic contract test (no network) · **T1** recorded real-API cassette replay through the production broker · **T2** provider conformance suite (`codefly agent ci` provider mode) · **T3** opt-in live vendor acceptance (dedicated sandbox/test accounts) · **T4** full SaaS starter dogfood. Verdict standard: **YES** only if a specified or clearly-implied test would actually *fail* when the invariant is violated; **WEAK** if the tier exercises the code path but a violation could still pass, or the required fixture/runner is unspecified; **NONE** if no proposed test can fail on violation.

Two structural facts constrain every verdict below:

- **No hostile-provider fixture is specified anywhere in the proposal.** The P1 test-infrastructure list (`core/docs/provider-plugins.md:2041-2050`) and the T2 conformance steps (`core/docs/provider-plugins.md:1862-1877`) test a *well-behaved* neutral provider; the P1 exit gate "a provider binary cannot directly write workspace files or reach an unapproved external origin" (`core/docs/provider-plugins.md:2061-2062`) is unprovable without a binary that tries. Invariants 2, 11, 12, 13, 17, 18 all need one.
- **All CI is Linux-only.** Every workflow in every repo runs `ubuntu-latest` (`core/.github/workflows/go.yml:23`, `cli/.github/workflows/go.yml:16,105`, `toolbox-web/.github/workflows/ci.yml:17,50`). Sandbox enforcement is per-OS (`bwrap` on Linux, `sandbox-exec` on macOS — `core/agents/manager/loader.go:421-422,887-888`); macOS-specific sandbox e2e tests exist (`core/toolbox/launch/sandbox_e2e_loopback_darwin_test.go`) but never run in CI. Every OS-enforced invariant is therefore at best half-verified without a macOS runner.

|---|---|---|---|---|

| 31 | Second identical run: no diff | T2, T3, T4 | **YES** | Conformance step 10 (`provider-plugins.md:1875`); acceptance no-op plans for all three providers. |
| 32 | Scripts become shims only after parity | — | **WEAK** | Migration rule (`provider-plugins.md:1940-1948`); no test that a shim contains no API/output logic. Trivial static test, unspecified. |
| 33 | Denied op changes nothing | T0, T1, T2 | **YES** | Explicit bullet (`provider-plugins.md:599-600`); replay-only cassettes make any post-denial request a hard failure. |
| 34 | Attribution without secret exposure | T0, T2 | **YES** | Receipt conversion tests (tier 0) + receipt surface in capture fixtures; safe-correlation field list (`provider-plugins.md:566-580`). |
| 35 | Protocol designed vs all three providers before stable | T2 (cross-provider matrix) | **WEAK** | The cross-provider matrix (`provider-plugins.md:1908-1931`) is a real suite but is sequenced *after* v0 freeze (P3/P4 "can start after provider v0 is frozen by Stripe", `:2090,2119`) — the gate it is supposed to enforce is already contradicted by the roadmap; no test can fire before the later providers exist. |

**Score: 20 YES · 12 WEAK · 3 NONE (2, 4, 13).**

### Closing list — no credible test as proposed, ranked by risk, with smallest fix

1. **Invariant 2 + 13, Scenario 6 (compromised binary: direct egress, workspace write, secret-store reach, proxy-env/subprocess broker bypass).** Highest risk: this is the design's central threat claim and its P1 exit gate, with zero specified falsifying test. *Smallest fix:* one hostile conformance agent in the T2 suite that attempts (a) direct outbound TCP, (b) a workspace file write, (c) `exec op`/`curl`, (d) `HTTPS_PROXY` egress — asserting each attempt fails under the sandbox on **both** Linux and macOS.
2. **Invariants 11/12/19 on macOS.** Sandbox enforcement is per-OS; every claim is CI-verified only on Linux. *Smallest fix:* add one `macos-latest` job to `core/.github/workflows/go.yml` running the existing `core/toolbox/launch` darwin sandbox e2e tests plus the hostile fixture above.
3. **Scenario 33 (disconnect leaves running processes holding old credentials).** Silent credential-lifetime gap in production. *Smallest fix:* T4 assertion that after `provider disconnect`, `codefly doctor` reports live consumers of the removed projection (or restarted services fail closed).
4. **Scenario 38 (retry crosses vendor idempotency retention window).** Duplicate remote mutation risk that live tests cannot practically reach. *Smallest fix:* T0 clock-injected retry-policy test asserting retries past the declared retention window are refused and classified `UNCERTAIN`.
5. **Scenario 21 (workspace moved).** State is keyed on canonical-path SHA (`provider-plugins.md:1248`), so a move silently orphans state and re-plans creation — a duplicate-resource generator. *Smallest fix:* T0 test that a state lookup with prior-state markers under a different path digest fails loudly with a relink diagnostic instead of returning empty state.
6. **Scenario 19 (two developers, one sandbox account).** *Smallest fix:* T3 test running two applies from two distinct state directories against one account; assert second sees an unmanaged conflict, not adoption.
7. **Invariant 1 (app never depends on provider agents).** *Smallest fix:* T0 dependency-lint test over starter service manifests/go.mod/package.json rejecting any `provider-*` reference.
8. **Invariants 4 and 30 (no provider-specific authz; no provider conditionals in core/CLI).** *Smallest fix:* T0 static grep tests (deny-list of provider names in coordinator packages; allow-list of authz imports in the provider host path).
9. **Invariant 10 (`--yes`/`--force`).** *Smallest fix:* CLI-level T0 test that `--yes` on a critical-risk plan action still returns `require-approval`.
10. **Invariant 28 (no literal ports/URLs).** *Smallest fix:* T0 schema-validation test rejecting numeric ports and absolute URLs in binding `spec` callback fields.
11. **Invariant 32 (shims).** *Smallest fix:* T0 static test asserting shim scripts contain no `curl`/HTTP/output-writing statements.
12. **Invariant 35 (protocol frozen only after three providers).** Not test-fixable — it is a governance gate the roadmap itself contradicts (v0 freezes at Stripe, `provider-plugins.md:2090,2119`); flag for the required-changes section rather than the test plan.

## 12. Required design changes

Ordered and minimal. Each item names the finding IDs it discharges. Anything not listed here is either already sound or deferrable without protocol consequence.

### 12.1 Change before issue creation (P0 — proposal edits, no code)

These seven items either pin a contract that P1 freezes or invalidate a claim the roadmap is sized against. All are document changes.

1. **Decide the third-party provider question and record it as the sizing decision.** State explicitly whether external authors publishing provider agents is a near-term requirement. If it is not, evaluate the trusted in-host engine alternative in section 7's alternative sketch on its merits and record why it was rejected; if it is, keep the current architecture and accept that the sandbox/broker/handle layers are foundation work rather than hardening. Every downstream P1 estimate depends on this. *(Discharges the Lane 1 sizing finding; makes invariants 2, 11–14, 17 either load-bearing or vacuous by decision rather than by accident.)*

2. **Define origin admission.** Replace "the broker derives exact origin ceilings from the packaged manifest" with a two-part model: manifest-declared origin *patterns or defaults*, plus an explicit binding-level origin admission path that lands the resolved origin in the plan digest and the credential handle. Sentry regional (`us.sentry.io`, `de.sentry.io`) and self-hosted origins are already v1 inputs, as are the Stripe and Resend API base inputs, so this is a v1 correctness bug, not a future concern. Add the SSRF guard requirements at the same time (resolve-once-and-pin, reject loopback/link-local/RFC1918/ULA/CGNAT unless explicitly declared, explicit `Transport.Proxy` rather than `ProxyFromEnvironment`). *(Convergent HIGH across Lanes 1/23, 7, 14.)*

3. **Resolve the three contract-pinning contradictions.** (a) Delete the manifest-gated raw-secret escape hatch, or restate the absolute "raw secrets never transit the provider process" claims as conditional and name the exact conditions — the two cannot both stand. (b) Choose one projection mechanism: a `PROJECT_OUTPUT` plan action, a host commit moment, or a post-Apply setup step; say which one observe-only bindings such as Sentry use. (c) Reconcile the sink `Prepare/Put/Commit/Rollback` transaction with the rule that a persisted one-time secret is never rolled back — the durable-at-`Put` semantics the capture path requires are incompatible with the transaction as written.

4. **Correct the reuse table.** Label as new work: the HTTP broker (`toolbox-web` contributes a map lookup, a `CheckRedirect` closure, and a `LimitReader`; its guards run inside the untrusted process with `network: open`), cassette record/replay (no implementation exists in any repo — `go-vcr` appears only as an unused `cli/go.sum` entry), CLI-side permission wiring including principal acquisition (zero call sites in `cli/`), execution-receipt production for ordinary CLI commands (wired only behind the gateway daemon's opt-in `--governed-execution`), and provider conformance (`codefly agent ci` hard-requires kind `codefly:service` and its model is generate-a-service-then-run-the-workspace-gate).

5. **Extend one-time capture to read paths and make matching fail closed.** Generalize capture declarations to read operations with array-wildcard pointers and a sink-less "suppress/redact" disposition; require that when a rule's method and path match but the response cannot be parsed and captured as declared, the response is blocked rather than forwarded; require that the provider always receives re-serialized canonical JSON, never the original bytes; make the broker own `Accept-Encoding`; and drive the cassette sanitizer from the same declared field list. *(Discharges the capture BLOCKER and the fail-open HIGH; also fixes duplicate-key and raw-body side channels.)*

6. **Restate the isolation claims to what the backends enforce, per platform.** The invariants that say a provider cannot read the workspace or secrets are false on macOS today and must either be scoped to Linux, made conditional on new read-deny rules, or dropped. Add the host-side sandbox *capacity ceiling* — the manifest is currently applied verbatim, so `network: open` passes production admission (proven by `toolbox-web`'s own manifest test) and invariant 11 has no enforcement point. Add environment allowlisting for provider spawns.

7. **Add the missing protocol surface to the protocol section.** The provider→host directions (broker request descriptor, checkpoint, output commit) must be specified as part of the same versioned unit as the seven host→provider RPCs, and `Apply` must be restated as host-driven per-action execution or streaming-with-acked-checkpoints — a single opaque `Apply` cannot satisfy checkpoint-before-next-mutation or per-action revocation. Also fix the plan digest to bind only the *material* part of the observation, or every refresh invalidates approval.

### 12.2 Change during the foundation (P1)

8. Centralize agent-kind routing into the `RegisterAgent` table with fail-closed defaults before adding `PROVIDER`, and fix the existing TOOLBOX mis-routing found in the process (`cli/cmd/agents/build.go:514-519` installs toolboxes under `services/` while `core/resources/agent.go:168-169` expects `toolboxes/`). Make GitHub repo/asset naming kind-aware; today it is hardcoded `service-<name>` for every kind.
9. Define manifest-in-artifact packaging with digest binding. The security-load-bearing `provider.codefly.yaml` has no distribution channel in any current artifact format, and the GitHub download path performs no verification at all (`core/agents/manager/downloader.go:59-70`). Either verify or disable auto-download for providers.
10. Build host-side action/resource derivation in the broker, per-outbound-request authorization, and a CLI approval path. Reject `provider:<name>/*` and bare `*` resource grants at production admission — because remote ID is last in the resource path, the correctly narrow form is already expressible, but the proposal's own manifest example models the ambient-CRUD form.
11. Create the generic output contract schemas (`billing@1`, `email@1`, `error-tracking@1`) as versioned artifacts with an assigned owning repository, and extend classification beyond `ConfigurationValue.secret`'s single bool so purpose, browser-exposability, and provenance survive projection. Add a secret-shape scan on public outputs — under-classification is the only unrecoverable branch.
12. Specify the state and recovery details the timelines expose: a mandatory pre-send checkpoint carrying action, idempotency key, and prospective resource ID; a same-attempt same-key idempotent replay carve-out from the no-blind-retry rule; ownership stamping into vendor metadata where the vendor supports it (Stripe webhook endpoints support `metadata`); digest compare-and-swap executed inside the lock at apply admission; checkpoints inside the versioned state schema; and fail-closed refusal when an older agent meets newer state.
13. Add an `(env, service, endpoint) → public HTTPS origin` resolver. `Environment.Ingress` is consumed only by gitops rendering today; no such resolver exists, and Stripe requires publicly accessible HTTPS endpoints, so `public` exposure mode cannot ship without it.
14. Specify the operability contracts the CLI currently lacks: exit codes distinguishing no-diff, diff-present, denied, approval-required, uncertain, and partial; plan-file portability (the digest binds prior state, which is machine-local, so a plan cannot currently move between CI runners); and a retry-ownership rule naming exactly one layer that owns the retry loop, with a delivery-status dimension separating "never sent" from "sent, outcome unknown."
15. Keep provider doctor checks out of `codefly doctor workspace`'s 30-second read-only local contract, or split remote provider checks into an explicitly opt-in bounded command. Resolve the `provider_*` (existing: secret backends) versus `provider.*` (proposed: external providers) diagnostic-code collision before the JSON contract freezes.

### 12.3 Safe to defer

- Remote encrypted team state, writable production secret sink, artifact digest lock enforcement, DR, key rotation, and multi-user concurrency — all correctly deferred to the production-mutation gate, provided Gate B (production observe) is defined so nothing production-facing ships against the local-dogfood bar.
- Sentry project creation. Deferring is right, though the stated rationale is stale: the current endpoint no longer requires a team and instead auto-creates a "personal team" side effect, which is a better reason to defer than the one given.
- Provider SDK portability beyond Go, uninstall-with-active-bindings, and non-JSON capture.
- Companion forwarder principals and receipts, provided the P2/P4 acceptance criteria state the stale-secret-on-restart story explicitly.

### 12.4 Reject or replace

- **Reject** the claim that provider setup needs a long-lived stateful `Configure` session. The proposal already rejects it; this review confirms that decision against the Terraform and Pulumi precedents and the existing stateless toolbox leaf pattern.
- **Reject** truncate-and-flag response handling for the broker. Truncating a successful mutation response can destroy the only copy of a write-once secret; caps must fail closed.
- **Reject** header denylisting. It must be an allowlist: Go converts URL userinfo into `Authorization: Basic` when no Authorization header is set, so a denylist is bypassable by construction.
- **Reject** following redirects on credentialed requests at all.
- **Replace** the 13-command CLI surface with the 8-command surface in section 9's operability artifact, which preserves every automation and debugging path.
- **Replace** the five-issue decomposition with the eight-issue structure in section 13.

## 13. Recommended roadmap and GitHub issue map

**Lane 20 (migration) conclusion.** The migration design is directionally safe: no path in the proposal silently adopts or deletes a remote resource. URL-only matches become unmanaged conflicts requiring explicit endpoint-ID selection (provider-plugins.md:1961-1962, 1293-1295), deletion defaults to retain, disconnect is separated from destroy, and the projection writer refuses to overwrite unowned differing configuration (writer invariant 7). This matters because the current scripts do exactly what the proposal forbids: stripe.sh matches an existing webhook by URL+enabled status (module-saas-starter/scripts/setup/stripe.sh:168-176), persists no remote ID or state anywhere, and projects the management sk_test_ key as the runtime STRIPE_API_KEY (stripe.sh:215-226). Consequences the proposal underspecifies: (a) adoption step 4 'propose an import plan for exact remote IDs' has no source for those IDs since scripts never recorded them — Observe must present same-URL candidates with their IDs for operator selection, and behavior with multiple same-URL endpoints is undefined; (b) ownership metadata location is unspecified — if stored beside configuration files, the still-live legacy scripts (setup_install_pair, provider-common.sh:146-175, which installs files directly with --force override) can clobber it during the coexistence window; it must live in provider state; (c) the script-shim is not behavior-preserving — --provision-webhook maps to a binding-level lifecycle change, --skip-remote-validation has no equivalent (setup always observes), --force is replaced by plan/adoption — the proposal must declare which flags hard-fail with guidance; the four non-migrated scripts (workos/posthog/otel/turnstile) keep sourcing provider-common.sh, so shims must not break shared helpers; (d) old CLIs silently drop the new provider-bindings environment field because all resource YAML goes through permissive yaml.v3 Unmarshal (core/resources/marshalling.go:62-69) — no error, no diagnostic, just a missing capability; a minimum-CLI-version signal is needed. Rollback to scripts is possible without remote-state loss but leaves provider state claiming ownership of files the script rewrote — a diagnosed conflict, which is the correct failure mode.

**Lane 21 (production readiness) conclusion.** The proposal defines exactly two readiness gates — 'complete for local dogfood' and 'complete for production' (provider-plugins.md:2209-2240) — while separately permitting read-only production operation before a writable sink exists ('read-only production providers may run; production providers may project already existing secret references', provider-plugins.md:1010-1015). That combination creates an unnamed third state, 'production observe', with no mandatory feature list, which the brief explicitly requires and rejects collapsing. Production observe is not low-stakes: it spends a real management credential against a live account and must therefore require validated non-expired principals, scoped-auth-v2-only admission, artifact-digest-verified agents, proven fail-closed PDP, and receipt retention — none of which the local-dogfood gate requires. The gap is sharpened by wiring status: every policy composition primitive the gates depend on exists in core (WithPrincipal, WithScopedAuthSecret, WithPermissionsCallback, WithProductionAdmission at core/agents/manager/loader.go:485, 546, 589, 603) but has zero call sites in the CLI (rg across cli/ -g '*.go' returns none), so every gate item is new integration work, not existing production behavior. Reusable substance does exist for the evidence layer: Ed25519-signed receipts (core/executionreceipt/receipt.go:26, 85-131) and a bbolt journal with UNCERTAIN terminal semantics (cli/pkg/executionjournal/journal.go:22, 432, 796-808) are implemented and CLI-wired via the execution runtime/dispatcher (cli/cmd/daemon_execution.go). P5 additionally bundles the entire Gate-C infrastructure (remote state, writable sink, digest lock, DR) with GA operability/documentation, making the single 'production' milestone both vague and unreviewably broad. Required: the explicit three-gate model in the 'special' artifact, with Gate B added to the Definition of done before any production observe is enabled.

**Lane 22 conclusion.** The dependency logic of the roadmap is sound — foundation before providers, Stripe freezes protocol v0, Sentry and Resend then proceed in parallel, hardening research overlaps — and the tension with invariant 35 ('designed against all three before declared stable') is resolved acceptably because the cross-provider matrix (provider-plugins.md:1908-1931) withholds 'stable' until all three pass. But the five-issue structure is not executable as written. Issue 1 spans two repositories (core, cli) and at least eight separable deliverable families — proto/kind/routing, the security-critical HTTP broker + credential handles + one-time capture, the coordinator/state/projection/CLI/doctor stack, and the conformance+cassette test infrastructure — far too large for safe review, especially when the broker/capture surface is the component most in need of focused adversarial review. Issue 5 bundles Gate-C production infrastructure with GA documentation. The structure also names no owning repositories, omits the three new provider repos, omits the module-saas-starter migration work hiding inside issues 2-4, and omits operational prerequisites (dedicated Stripe sandbox, Sentry test org, Resend account with a DNS-verified domain — a lead-time item) that gate the acceptance evidence of issues 2-4. Most important structural gap: the generic output contracts (codefly.dev/configuration/billing@1, email@1, error-tracking@1) referenced by bindings, manifests, projections, and conformance exist nowhere in code (rg for 'output-contract|codefly.dev/configuration' over core/ and cli/ returns nothing; the starter has only informal env-file conventions, e.g. setup_scripts_test.go:180-190) and no roadmap deliverable creates or assigns ownership of them — yet they are the contract that must freeze before P2-P4 can run in parallel. Replacement: eight issues (see 'special'), preserving the same serial spine while making the broker separately reviewable and adding the contract-registry deliverable to the foundation.

### Recommended GitHub issue map (replacement for the proposed 5-issue structure: 8 issues)

The serial spine is preserved (foundation → Stripe freezes v0 → Sentry ∥ Resend → hardening). The change: the foundation splits into three reviewable trust-boundary-scoped issues plus a conformance-harness issue, and hardening splits Gate-C infrastructure from GA operability. The proposed 5-issue structure fails on: Issue 1 unreviewably large (spans core+cli and eight deliverable families, including the most security-critical surface), no owning repos named anywhere, no contract-registry deliverable, no vendor-account provisioning, Issue 5 mixing production-safety infrastructure with documentation.

**Contract that must freeze before parallel work (end of Issue F1):** `provider.proto` v0 (RPCs + ProviderContext + plan/checkpoint/output messages), `provider.codefly.yaml` manifest schema (permissions, api-origins, secret-captures, projections, sandbox), canonical plan serialization/digest algorithm, resource-address syntax (`provider:<p>/<ws>/<env>/<binding>/<type>/<id>`), provider state schema v1 + UpgradeState semantics, credential-handle constraint schema, and the generic output contract schemas `billing@1` / `email@1` / `error-tracking@1` with an assigned owning repo.

---

#### F1 — Provider agent kind, protocol v0, manifest, and contract registry
- **Repos:** core (proto, resources, agents SDK), cli (cmd/agents routing: build/install/list/versions/publish; agent ci build-path routing with conformance explicitly deferred to F4)
- **Objective:** freeze every cross-issue contract surface.
- **Dependencies:** P0 decisions only (name, deletion default, credential purposes, sink direction, digest-lock timing).
- **Contract inputs:** P0 outcomes.
- **Deliverables:** `Agent.PROVIDER` enum + `RegisterAgent(ProviderAgent, ...)` (pattern: core/resources/service.go:28-31); `codefly/services/provider/v0/provider.proto`; provider SDK/base helpers + typed response builders; manifest parser + catalog-subset validation; canonical digest functions; **versioned generic contract schemas billing@1/email@1/error-tracking@1 seeded from the starter's current env conventions**; routing tests for install path `~/.codefly/agents/providers/...`.
- **Invariants:** manifest is a ceiling not a grant; empty manifest = deny-all under strict admission; no provider-specific branch in generic code.
- **Tests:** pure Tier-0 (schema, canonicalization, digest, manifest subset, contract validation); routing tests.
- **Dogfood evidence:** none (no runtime yet).
- **Acceptance:** generated bindings compile in core and cli; a sample manifest failing subset validation is rejected with a stable diagnostic; second serialization of the same plan yields an identical digest.
- **Non-goals:** broker, coordinator, any vendor code.

#### F2 — Provider HTTP broker, credential handles, one-time secret capture, cassette recorder/sanitizer
- **Repos:** core (new package; extracts/generalizes toolbox-web guard primitives — exact-origin, redirect revalidation, timeouts, response caps)
- **Objective:** the complete host-mediated egress boundary, reviewable in isolation as a security artifact.
- **Dependencies:** F1 (manifest origin/path/capture declaration schema). **Runs in parallel with F3.**
- **Contract inputs:** manifest schema; credential-handle constraint schema; sink `Prepare/Put/Commit/Rollback` interface (defined here or in F1, implemented locally in F3).
- **Deliverables:** request-descriptor validation; origin/method/path-template enforcement with host-side concrete-path derivation from approved resource IDs; forbidden caller Authorization/cookie/proxy headers; redirect/timeout/size policy; opaque credential-handle injection with purpose/origin/TTL/use-count checks; idempotency-key injection; declarative JSON-Pointer capture into a prepared sink with checkpoint-before-next-mutation; cassette recorder + deterministic matcher (normalizing injected idempotency keys/timestamps/request IDs) + fail-closed sanitizer whose deny-set derives from manifest capture declarations and credential purposes; replay executes the identical enforcement path with no live fallback.
- **Invariants:** brief invariants 11, 14, 17, 18; capture cannot be redirected, broadened, or retargeted by the caller; a raw captured secret is unreachable from the provider process and from every logging/cassette/diagnostic surface.
- **Tests:** the full Lane-7/Lane-9 attack suite (redirects, host confusion, header smuggling, oversized/malicious JSON, wrong-field capture attempts, unapproved-ID-in-valid-template denial); sanitizer red-team fixtures; replay-rejects-out-of-policy-recording test.
- **Acceptance:** dedicated security review sign-off; capture fixture proves raw one-time secret never crosses the broker boundary; recording the same logical operation twice yields byte-identical cassettes.
- **Non-goals:** vendor semantics, coordinator, CLI.

#### F3 — Provider coordinator: bindings, permission composition, state, projection, CLI, doctor
- **Repos:** cli (primary), core (receipts glue, local file sink implementation)
- **Objective:** the host lifecycle around F1's protocol and F2's boundary.
- **Dependencies:** F1; F2 interfaces (can develop against them before F2 completes).
- **Contract inputs:** frozen proto/manifest/digest/contract schemas; sink interface.
- **Deliverables:** environment `provider-bindings` parsing + validation (+ old-CLI silent-ignore documented and doctor-surfaced); first CLI wiring of WithPrincipal / ceiling PDP / GatewayEvaluator / scoped auth / WithPermissionsCallback / WithProductionAdmission (note: zero existing call sites — this is integration, not reuse); four-moment authorization (dispatch, plan admission, broker execution, commit); bbolt state under `~/.codefly/providers/<ws-sha>/<env>/<binding>/` with inter-process locking, upgrade orchestration, and ownership metadata **stored in state, not the workspace**; projection writer (path/symlink/gitignore/mode/atomic/CAS invariants, write-once-per-generation secret semantics); local owner-only file sink; execution receipts for provider operation kinds; `codefly provider` command group + JSON output; doctor sections; three-gate production admission (see table below).
- **Invariants:** brief invariants 1, 5-10, 19-33; denied operation changes nothing anywhere.
- **Tests:** complete permission intersection/negative matrix (provider-plugins.md:583-600); revocation-mid-apply; stale plan; concurrent apply locking; crash timelines; writer attack suite.
- **Acceptance:** every mutation attributable and admitted through existing PDP primitives; a denied apply leaves remote/local/config/sink untouched (verified by fixture).
- **Non-goals:** vendor providers; remote state; production sink.

#### F4 — Neutral conformance provider, reference fixture server, and `agent ci` provider mode
- **Repos:** cli (cmd/agents ci), core or new `provider-conformance` repo
- **Objective:** the P1 exit-gate proof mechanism. (New harness — the existing conformance stage is service-only, cli/cmd/agents/ci.go:427-428, and its generate-service/workspace-gate model does not apply.)
- **Dependencies:** F1, F2, F3.
- **Deliverables:** Codefly-owned loopback **reference fixture server** as a normative contract artifact (idempotency, 429/retry-after, pagination, one-time-secret return, lost-response); neutral provider exercising the full lifecycle; the 12-step Tier-2 flow in `codefly agent ci`; golden plan/state/output fixtures; zero-broker-request assertions for Validate/Plan/UpgradeState; platform matrix (linux/amd64 CI + darwin/arm64) for sandbox network/write denial; misbehaving-provider suite (undeclared action, unapproved origin, principal-claim spoofing, direct dial, workspace write).
- **Acceptance = P1 exit gate:** validate → observe → plan → apply idempotent resource → project public+secret → no-diff rerun → import → disconnect → destroy, all receipted, on both platforms.
- **Non-goals:** vendor behavior.

#### S1 — Stripe provider vertical slice + starter migration (freezes protocol v0)
- **Repos:** `provider-stripe` (new), module-saas-starter, cli/core only for bug fixes (no provider-specific branches — conformance matrix enforces this)
- **Dependencies:** F4 green. **Ops prerequisite (start immediately):** dedicated Stripe sandbox account + opt-in CI credential channel + cleanup automation.
- **Deliverables:** P2 list (provider-plugins.md:2066-2078) plus: owned-vs-ignored field policy for stripe.webhook-endpoint; import candidate-selection UX (same-URL endpoints listed with IDs; exact-ID entry required); shim for scripts/setup/stripe.sh with per-flag policy (--api-key-file/--env-file translate; --provision-webhook/--skip-remote-validation/--force hard-fail with exact replacement instructions); dogfood runbook.
- **Tests/dogfood evidence:** Stripe acceptance list (provider-plugins.md:1653-1664); cassettes; live sandbox tier; full starter checkout/portal/webhook/reconciliation dogfood; no-diff rerun; no secret/port/raw-response in state/plan/logs/receipts/Git.
- **Acceptance:** P2 exit gate verbatim + protocol v0 formally frozen (any change after this reopens F1).

#### S2 — Sentry provider (observe/project-only + credential-separation validation)
- **Repos:** `provider-sentry` (new), module-saas-starter. **Dependencies:** S1 v0 freeze. **Ops prerequisite:** dedicated Sentry test org/project. **Parallel with S3.**
- **Deliverables/acceptance:** P3 list and exit gate (provider-plugins.md:2088-2115); contract pressure items are the acceptance evidence (projection-only first-class, no-callback first-class, browser-exposable output, setup credential absent from runtime).

#### S3 — Resend provider (second webhook + credential-scope validation)
- **Repos:** `provider-resend` (new), module-saas-starter. **Dependencies:** S1 v0 freeze. **Ops prerequisite (long lead — start during S1):** dedicated Resend account + DNS-verified test domain + CI credentials.
- **Deliverables/acceptance:** P4 list and exit gate (provider-plugins.md:2119-2147); the signing-secret retrievability qualification cassette must be recorded before plan semantics are finalized; capability/result model for uncertain secret retrieval (per brief Lane 15), not a hardcoded doc assumption.

#### H1 — Production mutation readiness (Gate C infrastructure)
- **Repos:** core, cli. **Dependencies:** research parallel from S1; completes only after S1-S3 expose real requirements. 
- **Deliverables:** remote encrypted shared state + locking; writable production secret sink (backend selected in P0/P1); artifact digest lock enforcement; state backup/restore + DR; provider removal protection; state-migration workflow; key rotation; multi-user concurrency; policy templates; compatibility tests across previous two protocol/agent versions. GA operability items (drift-only CI command, scaffold, author guide, generated docs, metrics, support bundle) ride as a second milestone within H1 and split out only if review load demands.
- **Acceptance:** P5 exit gate (provider-plugins.md:2168-2173) + the Gate B and Gate C rows below all verified.

**Parallelism summary:** F1 → (F2 ∥ F3) → F4 → S1 → (S2 ∥ S3 ∥ H1-research) → H1. Ops prerequisites for S1/S2/S3 start as soon as each predecessor issue starts.

---

### Three-gate production-readiness table

The proposal defines only Gate A and Gate C (provider-plugins.md:2209-2240) while separately allowing read-only production operation (provider-plugins.md:1010-1015). Gate B must be added to the Definition of done.

| Mandatory feature | A: Local dogfood ready (end S1/S2/S3 per provider) | B: Production observe ready (MISSING from proposal — must be defined) | C: Production mutation ready (end H1) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Provider kind/build/install/resolve as first-class agent | required | required | required |
| Pinned semantic agent version | required | required | required |
| Artifact digest verification | not required | **required** | required + lock file enforced |
| Validated non-expired principal bound at spawn | required (human CLI) | **required, no fallback paths** (WithoutPrincipal/AllowAllPDP/shadow rejected independently) | required |
| Scoped authorization | v1 per-spawn acceptable; PDP-callback fallback allowed read-only | **v2 Ed25519 only; fallback disabled** | v2 only; MaxUses=1 for mutations |
| PDP availability + fail-closed proven | required (matrix tests) | **required with live backend + revocation checks per sub-operation** | required |
| Sandbox: no external network, no workspace writes | required, both platforms | required | required |
| HTTP broker: exact origin/method/path, credential handles, no caller auth headers | required | required, **read-only method policy: no mutating request admissible, no armed capture** | required, full |
| One-time secret capture + prepared sink before mutation | required (local file sink) | n/a (no mutation, no capture) | **required with writable production sink** |
| Local bbolt state + inter-process lock | required | required (local cache non-authoritative; no ownership mutation) | superseded by remote encrypted shared state + locking |
| Remote encrypted team state | not required | not required | **required** |
| Plan digest + approval binding, --yes cannot manufacture authority | required | required (approval for any high-risk read policy) | required + escalation with grantor attribution |
| Live-account guard | required: local env mutations restricted to sandbox/test accounts; plan displays observed account/mode | required: observed account/mode displayed and policy-checked | required + break-glass as distinct governed receipted operation |
| Projection writer (atomic, CAS, gitignore, ownership) | required | required (projection of existing references only) | required |
| Secret sink for write-once outputs | local owner-only file sink | **creation of write-once secrets must fail before mutation** (no mutation admitted anyway) | writable production sink, prepared+authorized pre-request |
| Execution receipts (admitted/started/terminal, Ed25519) | required (core/executionreceipt exists; wire to provider ops) | required + **retention policy** | required + retention, correlation, support bundle |
| UNCERTAIN handling + no blind retry | required | n/a (read-only) | required + recovery runbooks + DR tested |
| Doctor: binding/auth/scope/account/drift sections | required | required (scope + account safety focus) | required + state/lock/sink health |
| No-diff second run | required | required (observe/plan stable) | required |
| Conformance + cassettes + live acceptance green | required per provider | required | required across previous two protocol/agent versions |
| State upgrade/backup/restore, provider removal protection | not required | not required | **required, tested** |
| Multi-user concurrency + key rotation | not required | not required | **required** |

Key rationale for Gate B: production observe spends a real management credential against a live account. Its threat model is credential misuse and unattributable access, not partial mutation — hence digest-verified binaries, principal validation with no fallback, fail-closed PDP, and a broker that cannot express a mutating request are mandatory even though state/sink machinery is not.

## 14. Open decisions

Each decision below is genuinely unresolved after this review — it needs a product choice or an experiment, not more analysis.

### 14.1 Are third-party-published provider agents a near-term requirement?

- **Options.** (a) First-party only for the foreseeable future → adopt the trusted in-host engine (section 7's alternative) and treat sandbox/broker/handles as later hardening. (b) Third-party publication is a real goal → keep the proposed architecture and accept the isolation work as foundation.
- **Recommendation.** Answer this explicitly in P0. If the honest answer is (a), take (a): retrofitting a trust boundary under shipped third-party code is far harder than building it first, but building it for code you author and review is a large cost against a threat you have not yet accepted.
- **Consequence.** Determines roughly half of P1's scope and nearly all of its security-review load.
- **Latest responsible phase.** P0. Everything downstream is sized by it.
- **Evidence needed.** A product statement, not an experiment.

### 14.2 How are operator-supplied API origins admitted?

- **Options.** (a) Manifest declares origin *patterns*, host validates the concrete origin against them. (b) Binding-level explicit origin admission recorded in the plan digest and credential handle. (c) Both — patterns for defaults, admission for anything outside them.
- **Recommendation.** (c). Sentry regional and self-hosted origins plus the Stripe/Resend API-base inputs are v1 requirements, and the SSRF surface demands that the *host* — not the manifest — make the final call.
- **Consequence.** Changes the manifest schema and the credential-handle constraint schema, both frozen in P1.
- **Latest responsible phase.** P0 decision, P1 implementation. Cannot wait for P3 where Sentry lands.
- **Evidence needed.** None; the design choice is clear once the requirement is acknowledged.

### 14.3 Does the Resend webhook signing secret survive retrieval, and on which calls?

- **Options.** (a) Assume the documented create-and-retrieve behavior. (b) Model retrievability as a runtime-qualified capability with an explicit result state.
- **Recommendation.** (b), unconditionally. The vendor documentation is internally inconsistent on this point, and the proposal is right to refuse to resolve it by assumption. The plugin must express "secret present", "secret absent", and "secret retrievability unknown" as distinct observed results.
- **Consequence.** Affects plan semantics (whether a rotation is a REPLACE) and the capture declaration set.
- **Latest responsible phase.** Before P4 plan semantics are finalized, but the capability shape must exist in P1.
- **Evidence needed.** A recorded live-acceptance cassette against a dedicated Resend account with a DNS-verified domain. This is the long-lead operational prerequisite — start provisioning during the Stripe slice.

### 14.4 Which writable secret sink ships first, and does local file-based projection meet the bar?

- **Options.** (a) Owner-only ignored files for local, defer production sink entirely. (b) Pick a production backend now and implement both against one interface.
- **Recommendation.** (a) for local dogfood, but choose the production backend before the sink interface freezes — the one-time-capture path requires durable-at-`Put` semantics that a transactional `Prepare/Commit/Rollback` interface cannot provide, and that constraint must be baked into the interface rather than discovered later. Note that the only existing secret backend, the 1Password resolver, is read-only (`core/configurations/secrets.go:137-144`), so there is no writable sink of any kind today.
- **Consequence.** Gates all production mutation.
- **Latest responsible phase.** Interface in P1; implementation at the production-mutation gate.

### 14.5 Does Stripe restricted-key scoping actually support the intended setup/runtime split?

- **Options.** (a) Rely on restricted keys for the runtime credential. (b) Accept a shared key locally with an explicit escape hatch and require restricted keys only in production.
- **Recommendation.** Qualify by experiment before committing. Restricted keys are dashboard-created only with no public enumeration of permission categories, so the split cannot be verified from documentation.
- **Consequence.** Determines whether the credential-purpose separation the proposal promises is achievable for Stripe or merely aspirational.
- **Latest responsible phase.** During the Stripe slice, before its acceptance criteria are signed off.
- **Evidence needed.** A live read-only qualification against a dedicated Stripe sandbox account.

### 14.6 How is the local-live guard actually detected?

- **Open question rather than a choice.** The proposal assigns account-mode observation to the provider via a `stripe.account` resource, but the Stripe Account object exposes no `livemode` attribute and the provider holds only an opaque credential handle, so it cannot inspect the key prefix. The guard must move to the host at credential-resolution time (classifying `sk_`/`rk_` test versus live) plus `livemode` cross-checks on brokered responses. Confirm this reassignment in P0, since it moves a load-bearing safety check across the host/provider boundary.

### 14.7 Who owns the retry loop?

- **Options.** CLI coordinator, broker, or provider agent.
- **Recommendation.** The host coordinator, with the broker performing no retries of its own and the provider only classifying errors. Add a delivery-status dimension separating "never sent" from "sent, outcome unknown," and reconcile the `MaxUses=1` scoped-authorization rule with legitimate idempotency-keyed retries — as written, a valid retry either exhausts the token or `MaxUses` is meaningless.
- **Consequence.** Wrong answer here produces duplicate remote mutations, which is the failure the proposal most wants to avoid.
- **Latest responsible phase.** P1, before any provider is written.

### 14.8 Is `codefly doctor workspace` the right home for provider checks?

- **Options.** (a) Fold provider sections in as proposed. (b) Keep the existing 30-second read-only local contract intact and put remote provider checks behind an explicitly opt-in bounded command.
- **Recommendation.** (b). The existing doctor commits in writing to not starting agents and to a local latency bound; provider checks would start agents, add unbounded remote latency, and consume shared vendor rate budgets (Resend's documented limit is 10 requests/second per team across all keys). Also resolve the `provider_*` versus `provider.*` diagnostic-code collision inside that versioned JSON contract.
